## Design system gaps
Items the design system did not define and the functional default that was used instead.
- Spacing scale: DS uses Tailwind steps without tokens. Added `--space-0..6` = 2/4/6/8/12/16/24px. (Batch 01)
- Type tracking unit: DS gives unitless values (+0.4, −0.1); used px. (Batch 01)
- Shell metrics: DS says headers ~36–44px. App bar 40px (`--appbar-height`), panel header 32px (`--panel-header-height`). (Batch 01)
- Splitters: 1px `--hairline` line (`--splitter-line`), 6px invisible hit area (`--splitter-hit`), `--accent-dim` on hover/drag. (Batch 01)
- Tooltips: `--surface-4` background, hairline border, overlay shadow, caption type, shortcut in mono tertiary, 400ms delay (`--tooltip-delay`). (Batch 01)
- Theme attribute: DS is dark-only and names no switch; `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` is what the theme bridge watches. (Batch 01)
- Toggle switch: 28×16 track (`--control-default` × `--space-5`), 12px thumb; on = `--accent` track, `--on-accent` thumb. (Batch 01)
- Icon sizes: `--icon-size` 14px, `--icon-size-sm` 12px (DS range 11–16, 13–14 typical in toolbars). (Batch 01)
- Tokenized DS prose values: `--hover-wash` (menus "bg-white/[0.04]"), `--disabled-opacity` 0.6, brand gold stops `--brand-gold-from/-to/-hover`, `--on-brand-gold`. (Batch 01)
- Panel surfaces: DS has no NLE panel mapping. App bar and panel headers `--surface-1`, panel bodies `--bg`, timeline and preview wells `--bg-sunken`. (Batch 01)
- Brand mark: DS says "bold letter" but caps weights at 600; used 600. (Batch 01)
- Modal metrics: DS prose "max-w-sm/md/lg" and "body p-5" tokenized as `--modal-width-sm/md/lg` (384/448/512px) and `--modal-padding` (20px, not on the spacing scale). (Batch 03)
- Start screen: column `--start-width` 560px, recent-project thumbnail `--recent-thumb-height` 40px at 9:16; brand mark 32px (`--control-comfortable`) with `stat` type; title in `display` type. (Batch 03)
- Save dot colors: saved `--success`, saving `--info`, unsaved `--accent`, error `--danger`, read-only `--text-muted`; 6px dot in a 16px hit target. (Batch 03)
- Read-only banner: 32px strip on `--surface-2` under the app bar, hairline bottom, `--info` lock icon, caption text, compact secondary "Allow editing". (Batch 03)
- Secondary button: `--surface-3` + hairline, hover `--surface-4` + primary text (DS allows surface-1 or surface-3). Drop target: dashed `--accent-dim` outline on `--accent-wash`. (Batch 03)
- Toasts (the DS "Dynamic Island" gives only material and radius): top center, `--appbar-height` + `--space-3` from the top, `--toast-width` 360px, at most 4 stacked, spring entry; tone icon `--info`/`--success`/`--danger`; body-strong message + caption detail (3 lines max); 2px (`--space-0`) `--accent` progress bar on a `--hairline` track; durations info 4 s, success 3 s, error 7 s; progress toasts stay until done. (Batch 04)
- Media cards: grid columns `minmax(--media-card-min 112px, 1fr)`, `--space-3` gaps; 16:9 thumbnail well (`--media-thumb-aspect`) on `--bg-sunken` with the kind icon (24px, `--text-muted`) as placeholder; duration badge `--type-badge` (10px mono; DS chips are 9.5-10px) on `--surface-3`, radius-sm; selected = `--accent` border + 1px `--accent-wash` ring and an accent kind icon; hover border `--surface-5`. Kind icons: Film, AudioLines, Image. (Batch 04)
- Media list view: 24px sticky header (overline columns, the active one primary with a chevron), 28px rows with hairline separators, hover `--hover-wash`, selected `--accent-wash`; thumbnail column `--media-list-thumb-width` 40px; minimum width 24 x `--control-default` with horizontal scroll. (Batch 04)
- Compact Select (24px, caption type) for panel header strips; empty media state: 28px muted icon, title type, caption hint, primary "Import media". (Batch 04)
- Preview canvas: outside the frame `--bg-sunken` (same as the well), frame edge a 1 px `--hairline` (one device px per CSS px, rounded), margin around the frame at Fit `--preview-margin` = `--space-5` (16px). The frame interior is the project background. (Batch 05)

- Trimming II (Batch 14; the DS has no edit visuals): junction band `--timeline-junction-zone` 6px centered on a cut between abutting clips (spec §6.10), each half within its clip's trim zone. Cursors (24px SVG, 2px `--text-primary` stroke over a 4px `--bg-sunken` outline, hotspot at the center): roll `][` with outward arrows, slip `|<->|` (two bars, a double arrow between), slide `<-||->` (two bars, arrows outward), rate stretch = the trim bracket with a second chevron at the arrow's tip. Ghost edges per edited edge: `--accent`, `--danger` only on the side that sits on its media bound (a roll's left or right half); a slipped clip shows both edges, a slid clip none (its neighbors' edges mark it). Edit point brackets (§6.7 "red/orange from DS"): `]` + `[` on the cut in `--danger` for a roll edit point, the one rippled side in `--accent` (the DS has no orange); `--timeline-trim-edge-width` spines over the clip band, `--space-1` serifs at the top and bottom. Speed badge (§6.4 `2x`): the sync badge's chip with the text in `--text-secondary`, left of the sync badge. Tooltips: roll and slide `Δ +00:00:15`; slip `Δ +00:00:05 · In 00:03:15` (the new source in point); rate stretch `Δ · new duration · 0.5x`, `Max speed 4x` / `Min speed 0.25x` in `--danger` at the limits.

- Hover-scrub (Batch 20, spec §12 "a thin progress line"; the DS has no scrub visuals): the line is the timeline playhead (`--playhead` = `--live`, the DS "scrub red"; `--playhead-width` 1px) across the thumbnail, kept inside it at both ends; the duration badge shows the scrub position's timecode instead (same `--type-badge` chip, the session's timecode format); the picture is the LOD1 thumbnail nearest the position (LOD0 while that sheet loads). List rows scrub their thumbnail cell the same way (no badge there).
- Source range bar (Batch 20, spec §12 "a range bar on the card"): a `--space-0` (2px) `--accent` bar along the thumbnail's bottom edge from the in to the out point (an open end runs to the media's edge); gold marks the set state.
- Usage chip and unused state (Batch 20, spec §12 "usage count on hover", "'unused' dim state"): a `--type-badge` chip on `--surface-3` in `--text-secondary`, radius-sm, `--space-1` in from the thumbnail's top-left, shown while the card is hovered: "Unused" or "Used N×" (N counts clips, so a video with its audio is 2, as the list's Used column). An unused card's picture is at `--disabled-opacity` (full while it is scrubbed).
- Media drag (Batch 20, spec §6.14 "a small DOM element" off the timeline): the timeline drag tooltip's chip (`--surface-4`, hairline border, radius-sm, caption type in `--text-primary`, `--space-1` / `--space-2` padding) floating with `--shadow-overlay` at `--z-overlay`, `--space-3` right of and below the pointer, at most 7 x `--control-default` wide (ellipsis); it reads the media name or "N items". Cursors: `grabbing`, `copy` over the preview, `not-allowed` over a refused drop. Over the timeline: the clip drag's ghosts, new rows, hatching and insert line (Batch 11 tokens); the tooltip reads `start · length` (mono, `mm:ss:ff`) with the snap name. Files dragged from the OS: the insert line (`--accent`, arrow) at the drop frame alone (their length is unknown before the import).
- Preview overlays (Batch 21, spec §7.5/§7.6; the DS has none), drawn on a 2D canvas over the preview: safe-zone masks `--preview-mask` (`--bg-sunken` at 55%: a navy scrim, never black) over the platform UI areas with their names (caption type, `--text-secondary`, `--space-2` in) when they fit; the safe rectangle dashed in `--preview-guide` (white 72%) with `--preview-dash` (`--space-1`) dashes; title-safe dashed in `--preview-guide`, action-safe dashed in `--preview-grid`; grid lines and the custom grid in `--preview-grid` (white 34%, the overwrite hatch's white); the center cross `--preview-cross-size` (`--control-compact`, 24 px) across in `--preview-guide`; the pixel grid `--preview-pixel-grid` (white 12%). All lines 1 device px on device pixels ("lines are white-alpha hairlines").
- Preview rulers (Batch 21, spec §7.5 "thin rulers in px"): `--preview-ruler-size` 16 px (`--space-5`) strips along the canvas top and left on `--surface-1` with a hairline edge toward the picture, major ticks the full height and minor ticks `--preview-ruler-tick` (`--space-1`) in `--border`, labels in project px in the mono font at the overline size, `--text-tertiary`, the left ones turned to read upwards; majors (1-2-5 steps) at least the widest label (the frame's long edge, measured) plus `--space-3` apart, as on the timeline ruler.
- Menu buttons (Batch 21; the DS gives dropdowns as floating menus only): a value dropdown (Zoom, Quality) takes the compact Select's look (`--surface-1`, `--border`, radius-sm, caption type in `--text-primary`, a 12 px chevron in `--text-tertiary`; open = the DS input focus: `--accent-dim` border + `--accent-wash` ring); an icon dropdown (Overlays) the IconButton's (pressed gold when an overlay is on). The menu is the context menu shell (glass, hairline, overlay shadow, radius-lg) with two new row kinds: section headings (overline type, `--text-tertiary`) and a stepper (label, then `-` value `+` with 16 px buttons and the value in mono `--text-primary`); toggle rows stay open and show their on state as the DS active row (accent text on `--accent-wash`). The menu opens at the trigger's bottom edge (no gap), as the timeline toolbar's overflow menu.
- Fullscreen preview (Batch 21; the DS has no fullscreen): the panel fixed over the window on `--z-fullscreen` (80: under `--z-overlay`, so menus, tooltips, toasts and dialogs stay above); its header and footer float over the picture in the menu material (glass: "blur is a privilege of floating things"), and fade out (`--duration-standard`) together with the cursor after `--preview-chrome-hide-delay` (2 s) without pointer movement; the meter column stays. Fullscreen button: lucide `Maximize` / `Minimize` (pressed while fullscreen).
- Preview header (Batch 21, spec §13/§17): Zoom (value dropdown), Fit (lucide `Expand`, icon only now), Quality (value dropdown), Overlays (lucide `Grid3x3`), Snap (lucide `Magnet`, the timeline's toggle mirrored), Smart guides (lucide `RulerDimensionLine`), Fullscreen, Export frame; the dev pool select reads `768 MB`. The footer's timecode is the timeline toolbar's editable field, sized to its text; the status strip's quality chip reads what the preview renders now (`Auto: full`, `Auto: reduced`, `1/2: half`).

- Canvas selection and transform gizmo (Batch 22, spec §7.2/§7.3; the DS has no gizmo): selected layers outlined in `--accent` at `--gizmo-line-width` 1 px (one device px or more), the hovered one in `--accent-dim`; the box is the layer's visible quad (turned with it) or, for several layers, their axis-aligned group box in `--accent` with each member outlined. 8 square handles `--gizmo-handle-size` 8 px (`--space-3`) filled `--text-primary` with a 1 px `--accent` edge, square on screen, hit square `--gizmo-handle-hit` 12 px (`--space-4`); edge handles only on sides at least `--gizmo-min-edge` (28 px, `--control-default`) long on screen. Rotate zones `--gizmo-rotate-zone` 16 px (`--space-5`, spec §7.3) outside each corner with a 24 px SVG rotate cursor (a quarter arc with an arrow at each end, `--text-primary` 2 px over a 4 px `--bg-sunken` outline, `crosshair` fallback); resize cursors are the browser's, chosen by the handle's direction on screen to 45°; `move` over the box. Anchor marker `--gizmo-anchor-size` 12 px (`--space-4`): a ring half that size and a cross in `--text-primary` over a 3 px `--bg-sunken` outline, `crosshair` over it with Alt. The 100% scale snap: the box sides through the dragged handle in `--accent` at `--gizmo-snap-width` 2 px (= `--timeline-trim-edge-width`) while the snap holds. Marquee: `--accent-wash` fill and a 1 px `--accent-dim` edge (the timeline's). Readout near the cursor in the timeline's drag tooltip style (`--surface-4`, hairline, radius-sm, caption mono `--text-primary`, `--space-1` / `--space-2` padding, `--space-3` up and right of the pointer, below it at the canvas top). Drag dead zone `--canvas-drag-threshold` 4 px (= `--timeline-drag-threshold`, spec §6.8). The canvas menu uses the context menu shell (no new values).
- Smart guides (Batch 23, spec §7.4; the DS has none): guide lines 1 px `--accent` (`--canvas-guide-width` = `--gizmo-line-width`, one device px or more), across the whole frame for the frame, thirds and safe targets, from the dragged box to the layer for a layer; distance spans in `--accent` with `--canvas-guide-cap` (6 px, `--space-2`) end caps across them; distance chips centered on the span: `--accent` fill, `--on-accent` text in the mono font at the caption size, radius-sm, `--space-1` / `--space-0` padding (Figma's colored pill, gold as the DS's selection color); snap threshold `--canvas-snap-threshold` 6px (the spec's number, as `--timeline-snap-threshold`).
- Proxy chip and progress (Batch 24, spec §12 "proxy badge", §9.5; the DS has no progress visuals): a `PX` chip in the card thumbnail's top-right corner (`--space-1` in, `--surface-3`, radius-sm, `--type-badge`), `--text-tertiary` while pending with the share done after it (`--space-1` apart) and a `--space-0` (2 px) `--accent` bar along its bottom edge, `--success` when ready, `--danger` when failed, none when there is no proxy; the list's Proxy column reads None (muted), Queued or the percentage (mono), the proxy size (mono), Failed (`--danger`). The Project section's switch cell is `--control-default` tall.
- Properties sections (Batch 25; the DS has no inspector): a section is a `--properties-section-header` (28 px, `--control-default`) header button (overline title in `--text-tertiary`, a 12 px chevron turned -90 degrees while collapsed; hover `--hover-wash` with `--text-secondary`) over the label / control grid (caption labels in `--text-secondary`, `--space-2` / `--space-4` gaps, `--space-4` sides), sections set apart by hairlines. "N clips" / "N tracks" sits in the panel header in caption type, `--text-tertiary`.
- Number field scrub and mixed values (Batch 25): the field's inner label (text, or a 12 px lucide icon: RotateCw for rotation, Droplet for opacity) is the scrub handle, `--text-tertiary` at rest, `--text-primary` hovered, `--accent` while it scrubs, with the field's edge in the DS input focus (`--accent-dim` + `--accent-wash` ring) and `ew-resize` over the whole page; a `--field-scrub-threshold` (3 px) dead zone. A mixed value is an empty field with a `--` placeholder in `--text-muted`; a mixed switch has its thumb halfway on the off track.
- Quick-action row (Batch 25, spec §17): compact IconButtons `--space-0` apart wrapping over as many lines as needed, groups set apart by a 1 px `--hairline` rule `--icon-size` tall with `--space-1` margins, on `--surface-1` under the panel header with a hairline below; the speed chips are text-only IconButtons (caption type). Lucide icons: Shrink (Fit), Maximize2 (Fill), Focus (Center), FlipHorizontal2, FlipVertical2, RotateCcw (Reset), TrendingUp / TrendingDown (fades), Snowflake (Freeze), ZoomIn (Punch zoom), VolumeX (Mute), BookmarkPlus / BookmarkCheck (presets), Blend (Dissolve), Moon / Sun (dips), CopyPlus (Duplicate), Trash2 (Delete). The scale lock is Link2 (pressed: gold) / Unlink2.
- Color field and picker (Batch 25; the DS has no color control): a `--control-default` square swatch on `--picker-checker` (a `--surface-5` / `--surface-3` checkerboard of `--space-3` cells) with a `--border` edge (`--accent-dim` hovered or open) before the hex field. The picker is a popover in the menu material (glass, hairline, overlay shadow, radius-lg), `--picker-width` (8 x `--control-default`, 224 px) wide with `--space-3` padding and `--space-2` gaps: a `--picker-square-height` (140 px) saturation/value square (the hue under `--picker-light` / `--picker-dark` gradients), `--picker-strip-height` (12 px) pill strips for the hue (`--picker-hue`) and the alpha (over the checkerboard), `--picker-thumb-size` (12 px) rings, `--picker-thumb-ring` (`--space-0`, 2 px) thick, in `--text-primary` over a 1 px `--bg-sunken` edge (the gizmo markers' look), a row with the preview swatch, the hex field and the eyedropper (Pipette), the R G B A number fields, and the recent colors as label-style swatches over a hairline. `--picker-light` (#fff), `--picker-dark` (#000) and `--picker-hue` are the picker's own functional colors, not design colors.
- Rows of later batches (Batch 25): the control dimmed (`--disabled-opacity`, the DS disabled state), a wrapper carrying the tooltip that names the batch in dev builds.
- Crop handles (Batch 26, spec §7.3 crop mode; the DS has no crop visuals): corner brackets and edge bars inside the visible box, arms `--crop-handle-length` 16 px (`--space-5`; at most a third of the side on screen), a `--crop-handle-width` 2 px (`--space-0`) `--text-primary` stroke over a `--bg-sunken` outline 1 px wider on each side (the canvas cursors' look), turned with the layer; edge bars only on sides at least `--gizmo-min-edge` long on screen; the scale handles' hit squares and resize cursors; no rotate zones. The whole media's box: a 1 px `--accent-dim` dashed outline (`--preview-dash` dashes); the visible box keeps the `--accent` selection outline. The readout uses the gizmo readout's chip: `L 480 · 1440 × 1080` (the crop of the moved edges and the visible size, media px).
- Crop tool chip (Batch 26; the DS has no tool chip): the menu material (glass, hairline, overlay shadow, radius-lg) centered `--space-3` below the canvas top; lucide `Crop` in `--accent` (the active tool), "Crop" in caption type `--text-primary`, a 1 px `--hairline` rule `--icon-size` tall, then compact IconButtons Reset (RotateCcw) and Done (Check with its text).
- Crop section (Batch 26): Left / Right and Top / Bottom field pairs (inner labels L R T B, unit px or %), Units as two text chips (pressed gold), Aspect as the scale lock's Link2 / Unlink2 button, then compact secondary buttons Crop to frame (lucide `Frame`) and Reset (RotateCcw) `--space-2` apart; the crop mode toggle (lucide `Crop`, a compact IconButton, pressed gold) at the right end of the section header, `--space-2` from the edge.
- Speed section (Batch 27; the DS has no speed control): the Speed % field with a 12 px lucide `Gauge` scrub label, and the duration link after it as the scale lock's Link2 / Unlink2 IconButton (pressed gold), `--space-1` apart (`.withAction`). Duration is the Clip section's mono timecode field. Ripple is the DS switch (the Batch 01 toggle) for the Build's "ripple checkbox" (the DS has no checkbox). A freeze frame's speed field is disabled, with a caption hint in `--text-muted` under it (`.hint`). Freeze frame is a compact secondary button (lucide `Snowflake`).
- Freeze frame badge (Batch 27, spec §6.4 label bar badges): lucide `snowflake` at `--icon-size-sm` in `--text-secondary`, the link and keyframe icon badges' look, in place of the speed chip on a freeze frame's label bar.
- Text section (Batch 28; the DS has no text controls): rows of the properties grid. The content field is the DS input as a multi-line area (`--surface-1`, `--border`, radius-sm, body type, `--text-content-height` = 4 body lines plus padding, resizable, the DS input focus). The font field takes the Select's look with the family name drawn in its own font. Weight is a Select with the Italic IconButton after it (`.withAction`). Fill, background mode, case and box mode are text-chip IconButtons (pressed gold, the speed chips' look); alignment and vertical anchor are icon IconButtons (lucide TextAlignStart / Center / End, AlignVerticalJustifyStart / Center / End). Stroke, shadow and background are DS switches that reveal their rows. Number fields scrub on icon labels (ALargeSmall size, Droplets blur, SquareRoundCorner radius, MoveHorizontal spacing, MoveVertical line height) or letters (W, X, Y).
- Font picker (Batch 28, spec §11 "searchable list with live previews and sections"): the menu material (glass, hairline, overlay shadow, radius-lg), `--font-picker-width` (10 x `--control-default`, 280 px), under the field like the color picker. A DS search input (`--control-default`, lucide Search) over the list (at most `--font-picker-list-height`, 12 rows, hairline top). Section headings in overline type `--text-tertiary`; rows `--control-default` tall, the family at the stat size in weight 450 in its own font, hover `--hover-wash`, the current family as the DS active row (accent text on `--accent-wash`); a star button per row (`--text-muted`, shown on hover, `--accent` filled for a favorite); notes in caption type `--text-tertiary`; "Show system fonts" as a caption action row (lucide MonitorDown).
- Missing font (Batch 28, spec §10.1 "missing font badge"): in the Text section a chip after the font field (`--danger-wash` fill, `--danger` text, lucide TriangleAlert at `--icon-size-sm`, `--type-badge`, radius-sm, `--control-compact` tall); on the timeline a label bar icon badge, lucide `triangle-alert` at `--icon-size-sm` in `--danger`.
- Dev text button (Batch 28): a compact IconButton (lucide TextCursorInput) before the timeline toolbar's dev stats toggle, dev builds only. Removed in Batch 29 (`+ Title` / `+ Subtitle` are enabled).
- Inline text editing (Batch 29; the DS has no caret, text selection or editor):
  - Caret: a `--text-caret-width` (2px = `--space-0`) `--accent` bar as tall as the font's em box, on the overlay canvas; it blinks every `--text-caret-blink` (530 ms) while the editor has the focus, stays steady without it, and hides while text is selected.
  - Selection highlight: `--text-selection` (the accent at 40%) behind the glyphs and over a background box, in the text's raster.
  - IME composition: underlined 1 device px in `--accent` along each line's em box bottom.
  - The edited box keeps its `--accent` outline, without handles or hover; the cursor is `text` over it.
  - The editing textarea is transparent, with no selection color and no focus ring (`PreviewPanel.module.css` `.inlineText`).
  - Text box gizmo: corner handles and the left and right side handles only (the handle look of Batch 22).

- Text presets (Batch 30; the DS has no preset library):
  - Presets tab: a tool row (Global / Project as two chips in a `--surface-1` track, on = `--surface-4`; the search field at `--control-compact` height), the Media tab's card grid (`--media-card-min`, `--media-thumb-aspect`, `--surface-1`, hairline, radius-lg; hover `--surface-5`, pressed `--accent-dim`), the name under the thumbnail at caption type. Thumbnails are the preset rendered by the text engine on the card background with a `--space-2` margin, no other backdrop.
  - Card badges in the thumbnail's top-left corner: `--surface-3` chips with an `--accent` lucide Star (favorite), Type (default title) or Subtitles (default subtitle) icon at `--icon-size-sm`.
  - Library folder strip at the bottom of the Global scope: `--surface-1`, a top hairline, a FolderOpen icon, caption text in `--text-secondary` (`--danger` on an error), compact icon buttons (FolderSync, FolderX) or a compact secondary `Library folder` button.
  - Save as preset's name field: the DS input at `--control-compact` height in a row under the quick row (or under the Presets tool row), `--surface-1`, a bottom hairline.
  - Favorite chips in the timeline toolbar: the text chips of IconButton on `--surface-3`, at most `--preset-chip-max-width` (96px) wide with an ellipsis, `--space-1` after the add buttons.
- Text animations (Batch 31; the DS has no animation controls):
  - Animation section (after Text): an In and an Out row of text chips (None and the 12 animations), the IconButton text chips (caption type, pressed gold) wrapping `--space-0` apart (`.chips`); Duration and Stagger number fields in seconds (unit `s`, two decimals) scrubbing on lucide Timer / ChevronsRight labels; Easing as the DS Select (Auto (the animation's own), Linear, Ease in, Ease out, Ease in-out). A duration longer than half the clip gets the caption hint (`.hint`, `--text-muted`): "Plays in 2.00 s: half the clip". Fields of a row without an animation are dimmed (`--disabled-opacity`).
  - Timeline badge: lucide `sparkles` at `--icon-size-sm` in `--text-secondary` on the label bar of a text clip with an in or out animation (the link and keyframe badges' look).
  - The blur of `blurIn` is content, not UI: a Gaussian sigma of 0.2 font sizes at the start, capped at 16 texture px in the renderer (spec §10.4 "cap the blur radius").
- Captions (Batch 32; the DS has no subtitle tools):
  - Captions tab: a tool row (the search field at `--control-compact`, the cue count in caption type `--text-tertiary`, tabular figures) over the cue list. Rows `--cue-row-height` (48px: two body lines and their padding) with a hairline under each: the in and out timecodes stacked in `--type-mono` `--text-tertiary` (`--text-secondary` when selected) in a `--cue-time-width` column (80px, the toolbar timecode field's width), the text as a borderless two-line field that takes the DS input look on hover (`--border`) and focus (`--surface-1`, `--accent-dim` border, `--accent-wash` ring), the word count as a `--type-badge` chip on `--surface-3`. Hover `--hover-wash`, selected `--accent-wash` (the media list's), keyboard focus a 1px inset `--accent-dim` ring, the cue under the playhead a `--space-0` `--playhead` bar on the row's left edge (the scrub red). Empty state: the Media tab's (Captions icon, title, caption hint) with a primary `Cue at playhead` and a secondary `Import SRT/VTT` button.
  - Captions header (spec §17): compact IconButtons, lucide Plus (+ Cue), FileUp (Import), FileDown (Export), Paintbrush (Style), Mic (Transcribe, disabled until Batch 33), Eraser (Clear all).
  - Caption style section (after Clip, or after Track): Position as text chips (Bottom, Center, Top, Custom; pressed gold), Y / Max width / Max lines number fields scrubbing on lucide ArrowUpDown / MoveHorizontal / Rows3 labels, Highlight as text chips (None, Color, Box, Scale, Both) with the caption hint under them (`.hint`), the Active and Other words color fields, then the Text section's style rows (no vertical anchor).
  - Toolbar `+ Caption track`: disabled once the project has its caption track (tooltip says so). Timeline: a cue's label shows its text, lines joined by spaces.
  - Content values (not UI): the default caption text is Inter 800 at 64 px (for a 1080 px short side), white with a 6 px black outline and a soft shadow, wrapping at 900 px, 2 lines; the default highlight is yellow (1, 0.84, 0.04) over white; Bottom puts the caption's bottom edge at 78% of the frame height (the Subtitle's), Top its top edge at 18%; the scale highlight grows a word by 1.15 about its middle; the highlight box pads the word's advance and em box by 0.12 / 0.06 font sizes.

## Global deviations
Decisions that diverge from docs/EDITOR_SPEC.md, with the reason. Each one also appears under its batch.
- The design system file is `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` (repo root), not `DESIGN_SYSTEMS.md` as the docs say. Every reference to `DESIGN_SYSTEMS.md` means this file. Reason: the real filename, kept as-is per NEXT_BATCH_SETUP §0.
- Styling uses plain CSS Modules + CSS custom properties, not the Tailwind named in `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`. Reason: spec §3.1 (binding) forbids Tailwind; DS values are kept verbatim as tokens. Approved with the Batch 01 plan. (Batch 01)
- Node engine is `>=22.12` (spec says nothing; scaffold said 20). Reason: Vitest 5 requires it. (Batch 01)
- Track heights live only in the document (`Track.height`, §5.2), not in session state as §5.3 also lists. Reason: one source of truth, and §5.2 types are required verbatim. (Batch 02)
- Undo restores the selection from before the undone command, redo the one after it (§6.19's wording is ambiguous). (Batch 02)
- Backups in `history/` are named with UTC timestamps (`project-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.json`, then `-2`, `-3`... for saves within one second). Reason: local time can run backwards at a daylight-saving fall-back, and the rotation (oldest name first) would then delete the new backups. (Batch 03)
- `edit.delete` (Delete/Backspace) is also bound in the `media` scope. Reason: §12 asks for Delete in the media panel while the §18 table lists only timeline and preview; §12 is the more specific rule. (Batch 04)
- Media items are created with their applicable analysis `pending` (§9.1); the IdleQueue consumer picks them up and queues placeholder jobs without writing the document (no undo steps). (Batch 04)
- The sample index is built from the moov's sample tables (read by `readMp4`, mdat never read), not by streaming the whole file through mp4box extraction (§9.2, Batch 06 Build). For the MP4/MOV files the app accepts, the moov already lists every sample's offset, size, dts, cts and sync flag; streaming would read gigabytes of mdat for nothing. Fragmented MP4 (samples in moof boxes) is refused by the probe since Batch 04. (Batch 06)
- Seeks feed the keyframe straight into the running decoder, with no `flush()` and no `reset()` (§9.2 says "flush, feed from the sync sample"). Measured in Chrome 155 on an RDNA2 GPU: 11 frames of 4K HEVC took ~110 ms fed straight on, and 300-600 ms after a flush or a reset + configure (1080p H.264: 32-55 ms against 137-152 ms). Frame requests feed only what their target needs (`feedUntil`), so the decoder is idle when the next seek comes. (Batch 06)
- Image clips may run past their media's `durationUs` (`validate.ts`): a still has `durationUs: 0` and §6.14 gives images 5 s. Video and audio clips stay bound by the media. Animated images (loop or hold) are Batch 34's. (Batch 07)
- Timeline zoom limits (§6.2 "fit-to-content down to 0.01 px/frame, up to 400") are applied only when the user zooms or scrolls, never on document changes, so an undo that shortens the content never moves the view. The zoom-out limit is Fit (content + 2 s); a view already below it is never pushed further out. (Batch 07)
- Session `timeline.scrollY` is relative to the resting divider position (the divider rests at half the track area height; video tracks stack up from it, audio tracks down, §6.2) and may be negative; session.json accepts negative values. (Batch 07)
- Commands carry a 10th invalidation flag, `composite` (§3.4 lists nine): the rendered frame changes but nothing the timeline draws does. `clip.update` patches that touch only `transform`, `opacity` or `crop` carry `composite` instead of `clips`, so the timeline never redraws for them (Batch 08 acceptance 4). (Batch 08)
- Default transform on insert is Fit for every media with a size, not only when the aspect differs: same-aspect media of another size fill the frame exactly (scale 1 when the sizes match). "1:1 scale otherwise" taken literally would show 4K phone footage at twice the 1080x1920 frame. (Batch 08)
- Media time 0 is the first frame: the compositor asks for `firstCts + sourceTime` (edit lists are still not applied, Batch 06), and picks the frame time nearest to it (§5.1). (Batch 08)
- The frame rate can change only while the project has no clips and no markers; resolution changes use "keep". Reason: the §15 conversions (fps ratio, "Scale layout") belong to Batch 42 (this batch's "Do not build yet"), and an unconverted fps change breaks the media-clip duration invariant. (Batch 03)
- Video frames are uploaded at most at the project's long edge, scaled on the GPU while uploading (§9.3 names no upload size). Reason: the preview never draws more of a frame at Fit or 100%, and 4K HEVC decodes faster scaled on upload (70 frames/s, against 56 copied at 4K). Zoom above 100% gets larger uploads in Batch 21. (Batch 09) Batch 21: uploads follow the preview's render scale above 100% (see the Batch 21 detail deviations).
- MP4/MOV audio decodes with WebCodecs `AudioDecoder` in decode.worker, not `decodeAudioData` on the main thread (§14). Reason: `decodeAudioData` needs the whole file in memory (gigabytes for 4K phone video), cannot decode a range (so no 60 s segments past 10 min), and ignores the edit list (AAC priming: phone audio up to ~48 ms late). Other audio formats use `decodeAudioData`; past 10 minutes they still decode whole (Batch 45 follow-up). (Batch 10)
- The audio scheduler starts one `AudioBufferSourceNode` per clip (per 60 s segment and loop pass) at most 200 ms ahead, in 50 ms steps, playing to the clip's end, instead of a source per 50 ms chunk (§8). Reason: chunk joins must be sample-exact and each risks a click; seeks, pauses and edits still stop a source with the 10 ms ramp. (Batch 10)
- At 1x the playback clock starts (and re-anchors after a seek) the audio render lead + 20 ms ahead (~70 ms on the dev machine), so the first samples are heard together with the first frame. (Batch 10)
- P6 pinning covers only the playback windows ahead of the playhead (up to 15 frames) and what the preview draws, not every frame within ±1 s. Reason: ±1 s at 60 fps is ~1 GB of 1080x1920 frames, over the 768 MB budget; frames just played stay in the LRU. (Batch 09)
- Insert drops (Mod, §6.8) split the clips crossing the insert frame on every unlocked track and shift the right parts with the rest (Premiere's insert with sync locks on), by the moved block's span. Locked tracks never shift and nothing lands on them, so the only insert refusal ("insert collisions are reported by a toast") is a locked destination or source track, reported by an error toast as in overwrite mode. (Batch 11)
- Mod+click toggles the clip alone (§6.7 "add without linked partner"), so a second Mod+click removes it. Alt on an already-selected clip keeps the selection for Alt+drag; Alt+click without a drag selects only that clip. A click on empty space clears the selection on release (a press there may start a marquee). (Batch 11)
- Overwrite trims and splits partition keyframes by frame with no boundary keyframes (eases stay opaque until Batch 35). (Batch 11)
- During a drag the S key is read by the drag's window key listener (capture phase) and stopped there, never reaching the KeyDispatcher (§3.6). Reason: hold-to-invert snapping (§6.9) must work while Shift, Alt or Mod is held, and Shift+S and Mod+S are other commands (linked selection, save). Key-ups pass on; outside drags S goes through the dispatcher. (Batch 12)
- Q / W (§6.10) use one edit point: among the clips under the playhead on targeted, unlocked tracks, the latest start (Q) or the earliest end (W). The clips with their edge there and their linked partners are trimmed to the playhead, and the ripple shifts every unlocked track (the §6.10 ripple rule), limited by collisions. Clips spanning the edit point (a music bed) are left alone; clips after them on their track can limit the ripple. Clips that start at the playhead are not trimmed (W would delete them). Reason: one edit point keeps all tracks in sync, and every track is targeted until the Batch 16 toggles, so trimming each targeted track's own clip would cut a music bed's head. After Q the playhead moves to the edit point, where the frame it showed now is, so playback continues on the same picture (Premiere); W leaves it. (Batch 13)
- Ripple trims: the edit point is the dragged clip's old edge, and on a track holding a trimmed clip (a linked partner) that clip's edge; clips at or after it shift by the change in duration. An in-point ripple keeps the clip's start, so clips on other tracks starting at or after it shift with its content. Locked tracks neither move nor limit: clips never change tracks in a trim, so "collisions with locked clips" (§6.19) cannot occur. (Batch 13)
- The sync badge shows the start offset to the link partner, or the end offset when the starts match (§6.10: an Alt-trim of one side "produces a sync offset badge"; a source-time offset stays 0 after a trim). Batch 16 ("Move into sync") settles the rule. (Batch 13)
- Keyframes follow the source on in trims of video and audio clips only; images, text and solids keep theirs on the clip start (§6.10 names media on and text off; a still has no moving source). Keys out of the new range are kept so a trim back restores them. The "keyframes follow source" setting is Batch 42's settings panel. (Batch 13)
- A trim drag pauses playback, as a ruler scrub does: the preview shows the trimmed edge frame. While the pointer is over the timeline, the view reads Mod on window keydown/keyup only to switch to the ripple cursor; it never handles or stops a key (§3.6). (Batch 13)
- Edit point selection (§6.7) lives in its own session channel (`SessionStore.editPoint`: the left and right clip of a cut and the side: `both` for a roll, `left` / `right` for a ripple trim), not in `Selection`, and is never persisted. Reason: §3.3 (binding) fixes the selection kinds to clips/track/cue/none. Selecting an edit point clears the clip selection; selecting clips (any non-empty selection) clears the edit point. (Batch 14)
- Junction hits (§6.4, §6.10): the trim zones lie inside each clip (§6.4), so where clips abut the left 8 px of the cut are the left clip's out point and the right 8 px the right clip's in point; §6.10's sentence ("the left 8 px belongs to the right clip's in point") is read as the same layout described from the cut. The 6 px band centered on the cut is the junction: a plain drag there still trims the side under the pointer (Batch 13 behavior), Mod+Alt drags roll it, and a click selects its edit point (a roll edit point; with Mod or Ripple mode the ripple side under the pointer). (Batch 14)
- The split preview (§6.10 "split view with both sides"; Batch 14 implementation note "a compositor option `previewSplit`") shows two whole frames side by side, each fitted in its half of the canvas, not the left half of one frame and the right half of the other. Reason: Premiere's trim monitor; a 9:16 frame cut in half hides half of each picture. It is an override field of the preview (`previewSplit: { leftFrame, rightFrame }`): two `Compositor.evaluate` calls, one `LayerTextures.resolveSplit` pass and one `Renderer.drawPanes` render pass. (Batch 14)
- The renderer maps each layer to target px on the CPU (pane viewport x layer matrix) and its view uniform maps target px to clip space; each draw record carries its scissor (the frame within its pane, or the pane for the border). Reason: one render pass draws several panes with different viewports (§9.4 names one draw list per frame). (Batch 14)
- Slip direction follows Premiere: dragging the clip right brings earlier source frames in (the content moves with the pointer); `slip(doc, clipId, delta)` takes the source delta (positive = later frames). Keyframes of slipped video and audio clips stay on their source frames ("keyframes follow source"). (Batch 14)
- Slide with a gap on a side: the gap shrinks or grows instead of a neighbor trimming, bounded by the clip beyond it (or frame 0). Linked partners slide with their own abutting neighbors. A rolling edit rolls the partners' cut too; a partner with no clip against it trims alone, bounded by its neighbor. (Batch 14)
- Rate stretch keeps `in`/`out`, sets `speed = speed * oldDuration / newDuration` (exact for 2x stretches: 0.5), limits speed to 0.25x..4x (§14), scales keyframe frames proportionally (rounded) and clamps fades. Images, text, solids and captions have no speed: the Rate stretch tool trims their edge. Rate stretches never ripple. (Batch 14)
- The preview footer's timecode readout is read-only (§7.8: "Timecode readout (editable)"). Reason: the timeline toolbar's timecode field (Batch 07) is the editable playhead field, with the same parser and keyboard jumps; Batch 09's Spec refs take §7.8 for the transport buttons only. Batch 21, which builds the rest of §7.8, makes the readout editable with `bindTimecode`. (Batch 09) Resolved in Batch 21: the readout is an input bound with `bindTimecode`.
- Gap selection (§6.7) lives in its own session channel (`SessionStore.gap`: track, start, end), not in `Selection`, and is never persisted. Reason: §3.3 (binding) fixes the selection kinds to clips/track/cue/none. A gap, a clip selection and an edit point exclude each other; an edit that moves or closes the gap clears it. (Batch 15)
- Ripple with gaps on several tracks (Ripple delete, Close gap, Close all gaps; §6.11, §6.19): each track with a gap to close closes it by its own length. The other unlocked tracks move with the ripple: a clip there moves by the most any one of those tracks has closed before its start (in the gaps ending at or before it), so gaps on different tracks apart in time never add up and the clip stays in sync with the content beside it. A clip that would collide (it would move more than the clip before it plus the free space between them) limits the gaps ending before it to the largest safe amount (they then close that far, left to right), and a toast names it. Locked tracks neither move nor limit. Ripple delete closes the deleted clips' spans only (touching deleted clips form one span; a gap that was already beside them stays). Reason: one shift for every track cannot close "each gap by its own amount" (acceptance 2) when two tracks lose different lengths; this is the Batch 13 ripple rule (spanning clips stay, clips after them limit) applied per gap. (Batch 15)
- `G` with no gap selected closes one gap: on each targeted unlocked track the gap under the playhead, or the gap right after the clip under it; of those, the one that ends first (the lowest track on a tie). Reason: §6.11 says "the gap"; closing every track's gap there would also move a title far away on a nearly empty track. (Batch 15)
- Close all gaps (Compact, `Shift+G`) closes every gap from frame 0 to each targeted unlocked track's last clip in one command; untargeted unlocked tracks follow the ripple. "On the selected tracks" waits for track selection (Batch 16). (Batch 15)
- The Razor cuts on the press (no drag), the clicked clip and, with Linked selection on, its linked partners; Shift+press anywhere in the track rows cuts every unlocked track at the frame. The frame snaps to every candidate (the playhead included) while snapping is on. (Batch 15)
- Label colors are drawn in Batch 15 (minimal: flat washes of the body and label bar), though Batch 17 lists them too. Reason: Batch 15's clip menu has the swatches and a pick must show. Clicking the checked swatch removes the label (no ninth "none" swatch). (Batch 15)
- `edit.delete` and `edit.rename` are registered once, in `app/cutController.ts`; in the media scope they call the media panel's remove and rename (one command id per action, §3.6). (Batch 15)
- The sync badge (spec §6.12 `+12` / `-12`) and "Move into sync" measure sync by source for clips of the same media (a video and its own audio): the frames between where each clip's source time 0 would play. An Alt-trim of one side (a J or L cut) keeps the same source time at the same frame, so it leaves no badge, against §6.10's "produces a sync offset badge"; a move or a slip of one side does. Clips of different media (linked by hand) compare starts (§5.2). This settles the Batch 13 rule (start, else end offset). Reason: J and L cuts are the common use of Alt-trims, and a badge on them would offer a "Move into sync" that breaks real sync. (Batch 16)
- Track heights stay in the document only (Batch 02 deviation), but behave as session state (§5.3, the Batch 16 note "heights are session state"): they are written with `DocumentStore.amend` (no undo step) and undo/redo keep the current heights of the tracks that survive (`carryTrackHeights`). Reason: one source of truth; an undo of a reorder puts whole track objects back and would otherwise bring old heights with it. (Batch 16)
- A click on a track header selects the track (Shift or Mod toggles it in a track selection, Mod+Shift selects its clips); it does not toggle targeting as §6.7 also says. Reason: §6.1 gives the header its own target toggle, and a click that flipped targeting would untarget a track every time it is selected for the properties panel. (Batch 16)
- Track selection is `Selection.kind = "track"` with an optional `trackIds` set (in selection order; the first is the one the properties panel shows); clip selections have none. session.json keeps it; restores drop tracks that no longer exist. (Batch 16)
- Compact on given tracks (`Shift+G` on the selected tracks, or the targeted ones): every given track closes its own gaps, so one with no gap stays as it is (it neither follows nor limits the others); the other unlocked tracks follow the ripple. Reason: the Batch 15 review's open point (a targeted track with no gap limited the others; any gap made it ignore them). (Batch 16)
- Groups: selecting any member selects the group with its members' linked partners while Linked selection is on (clicks, marquee, right-click); Alt+click and Mod+click still take one clip. Deleting a member deletes the group (§6.12); split pieces keep the group (so the group spans both halves). Clips on locked tracks never join or leave a group. (Batch 16)
- `Mod+L` links only a set with at least one audio clip and one clip of a video track (video, image, text or solid), each on its own track, and unlinks every member of the link groups the selection touches. `Mod+Alt+Up/Down` cycles (wrapping) the selected tracks, else the tracks of the selected clips, else every track; the toolbar's Track height sets every track to the height after the tallest one present. (Batch 16)
- Thumbnail sheets (§9.6): 16 thumbnails per sheet (4 x 4 cells of 160 px tall frames), `cache/thumbs/<mediaId>/<lod>-<sheet>.webp` plus `index.json` (version, source LOD, source file stamp, layout, the sheets written). The in-memory LRU keeps at most 256 sheets **and** 192 MB (`MAX_BYTES`): 256 sheets of 16:9 thumbnails would be ~740 MB of bitmaps. Sheets on screen and a card's first sheet are never evicted. (Batch 17)
- Filmstrip level (§6.4 "the LOD matching pxPerFrame", §6.17): the coarsest level whose spacing is at most a tile's source span, so every tile gets its own frame (LOD2 when even 5 frames are more than a tile). A tile shows the frame at its left edge's source time. While its sheet is not in memory a tile shows the same tile from another level in memory (coarser first, then finer), and the body color only when none is: zooming in shows the coarser thumbnails repeated, not blank bodies, until the denser sheet arrives (acceptance 1 "placeholders"; Premiere does the same). (Batch 17)
- Thumbnail decodes yield to the preview as well as to playback and drags (§3.5 "low priority, cancellable"): a sheet decode starts only after the preview has sent no frame to its decoder for 250 ms (`PREVIEW_QUIET_MS`), and a running one is cancelled when it does (then made again). Reason: one hardware decoder serves both; without it the Batch 15 split check's preview step to frame 119 took 265-325 ms instead of the recorded 104-139 ms (108-124 ms with it). (Batch 17)
- Media cards of videos show the first cell of their LOD0 sheet (§9.6 sprite sheets), not a separate poster: the decode worker's one-frame video poster path is removed and `cache/thumbs/<id>.poster.webp` is written for images only. A video card shows its kind icon until LOD0 sheet 0 exists (made first after import, from the IdleQueue: not during playback). (Batch 17)
- Waveforms (§6.4 "min/max peaks, mirrored around the center line"): each column draws the larger of |min| and max over the channels, mirrored, on a linear scale; columns narrower than a level-0 bucket interpolate between bucket centers (a smooth envelope at deep zoom, not steps). Gain is drawn at 0 dB (Batch 37) and the fade wedges come with fades (Batch 36). (Batch 17)
- Peaks stay in memory for every audio-bearing media once its peaks job has read or built them (one read per session; a pyramid is ~1.5 KB per second of stereo), instead of loading per visible clip with an LRU; the timeline moves the media it shows to the front of the queue. Media that leave the project drop theirs. (Batch 17)
- Clips with `enabled = false` or `volume.muted` are dimmed and hatched (§6.4), but nothing sets either yet: the clip menu's Enable/Disable row (§6.16) and the properties `enabled` switch (Batch 25) are not built here. Text clips show their name in the body until `TextContent` exists (Batch 28). (Batch 17)
- `AnalysisScheduler` hands media to their jobs (peaks, thumbnails) and no longer queues placeholder idle tasks. (Batch 17)
- Track menu (§6.16): "Add video track above/below, Add audio track" on video headers; on audio headers "Add video track, Add audio track above/below"; "Delete empty tracks" keeps the first video and audio track when every track of their kind is empty; "Lock all tracks" becomes "Unlock all tracks" when all are locked; "Mute all others" mutes the other tracks of the clicked one's kind. A locked track can be reordered and renamed (its clips are unchanged); deleting it is refused. (Batch 16)
- Scrollbars (§6.1) take their 12 px from the track area (bottom and right), so the divider rests 6 px higher than before; the header column runs down to the bottom strip. The horizontal bar spans the content end plus 25% of the view (the furthest a view can scroll), or the view itself where it already reaches further. A zoom handle drag moves the proportional thumb's end (the visible share of the span) by the pointer's travel with the view's opposite edge fixed (scroll clamps apply); the drawn thumb is at least 36 px, so at deep zoom its end and the proportional one differ by a few px, and the bar re-maps live (the fixed end's thumb moves by ~1 px during a drag). A press on a trough brings the thumb's middle under the pointer and drags on (no paging). (Batch 18)
- Space+drag (§6.6) against Space = Play/Pause (§18): a plain Space press arms the pan only while playback is stopped and the pointer rests on empty track space (an empty or gap hit, no text input focused); the KeyDispatcher never sees it, a press then pans, and a release with no pan in between plays (on key-up). Everywhere else, and while playing (a pause is never delayed), Space plays/pauses on key-down as before. (Batch 18)
- Hand tool (H): a press anywhere in the track area pans both axes, clips included, and selects nothing; the ruler still scrubs and the headers act as headers. Middle drags pan everywhere with any tool, the ruler included. Pans stop follow playhead like any manual scroll. (Batch 18)
- Zoom to selection (`Shift+\`, §6.2): the selected clips (a track selection: the clips on those tracks), else the selected gap, else the two clips of the selected edit point; the span fills the view but 10% of its width on each side, centered, within the zoom limits (Fit at most) and the scroll range; nothing selected does nothing. Vertical scroll is left as it is. (Batch 18)
- Keyboard zoom steps (`=`, `-` and the toolbar's zoom buttons, which run the same commands) ease over `--duration-micro` (120 ms) with `--ease-out`, the session taking each frame's view; a step during a step builds on its target, and any other view change (wheel, scroll, pan, Fit, the slider) ends it where it is. Fit, Zoom to selection and Center playhead stay immediate. (Batch 18)
- The timecode format (§5.1 `mm:ss:ff` / `mm:ss.mmm`) is session state (`timecodeFormat`, kept in session.json; §5.3 does not list it, it is per project like the other display toggles). The toggle is a chip before the preview's timecode (`view.toggleTimecodeFormat`); the toolbar field (typing included), the durations, the preview readouts and the ruler labels follow (§6.3; in milliseconds the labels between seconds are `.mmm`). Drag tooltips stay `mm:ss:ff`: edits are frame-exact. (Batch 18)
- Every §18 key is bound (`keymap.ts` already listed them all); keys whose command a later batch builds are listed with that batch in `core/keyboard/planned.ts`: dev builds show "<name>: coming in Batch NN" on the key-down (not on repeats), production builds consume the key silently as before. (Batch 18)
- End (Go to end, Batch 09 follow-up) stays at the content end, one frame after the last clip (Premiere's sequence end). Reason: inserts at the playhead (double-click a card; the append workflow of Batch 20) then land after the content instead of overwriting its last frame; Left reaches the last frame. (Batch 18)
- The out point is stored as the range's exclusive end (`O` at frame F stores F + 1; `Shift+O` goes back to F, the range's last frame): the frame on screen when O is pressed stays in the range (Premiere), and the out point snaps, navigates (Up/Down) and is drawn on the boundary like a clip end. A point set on the wrong side of the other clears the other (Premiere); handle drags keep at least one frame. (Batch 19)
- In/out stay session state (§3.3, §5.3 binding): not undoable. Lift and Extract clear the range and put the playhead on the in point (Premiere); an undo brings the clips back, not the range. (Batch 19)
- Lift / Extract act on [in, out) with the in point defaulting to 0 and the out point to the content end when only one is set (neither: an info toast). Extract closes the range on every targeted unlocked track (empty parts too) with the Batch 15 ripple rule: untargeted unlocked tracks follow, a clip spanning the range there stays, a collision limits the ripple and a toast names the clip. Right pieces of a clip spanning the range are relinked as a split does (pieces cut together share a new group; a lone one is unlinked). Each runs as one transaction. (Batch 19)
- Loop (§6.13): with loop on and a range set, every play (Space, J/L) loops the range and starts on its first frame (backward: its last) when the playhead is outside it; without a range, the whole timeline. Play in to out plays the range once and stops on its last frame, or loops it while loop is on; with neither point set it plays the whole timeline. A playhead moved by someone else while looping (Home) plays on from there and wraps at the out point. (Batch 19)
- One marker per frame: `M` on a frame that has one opens that marker's inline editor (Premiere's second M) instead of adding a duplicate. New markers are green (Premiere's default). (Batch 19)
- Marker selection lives in its own session channel (`SessionStore.marker`), not in `Selection` (§3.3 fixes the selection kinds), and is never persisted; it excludes a clip selection, an edit point and a gap. A press on a marker selects it at once (as a clip press does, so the properties panel changes before a drag, P1); `Delete` removes the selected marker. (Batch 19)
- Ruler hits: markers (the ruler's bottom band) and the in/out handles (its upper part) come before the playhead head; elsewhere the ruler scrubs as before. The ruler menu's Add marker adds at the right-clicked frame (`M` adds at the playhead). (Batch 19)
- `Shift+M` / `Mod+Shift+M` (§6.13) are extra keys of the §18 marker jumps (`timeline.nextMarker` / `prevMarker`, timeline scope, next to Shift+Down / Shift+Up). (Batch 19)
- The in/out wash and handles are drawn on the overlay (one fill per frame), so a handle drag never redraws the content canvas; markers stay on the content canvas (P3 lists them there). (Batch 19)
- Double-click on a media item is a real insert at the playhead (§6.14 "or `,` for insert"): every unlocked track opens the clip's length there (the §6.8 insert, clips crossing the playhead split), replacing Batch 07's stand-in that searched the first free span after the playhead and overwrote nothing. `.` overwrites. Both move the playhead to the end of what was placed (§6.14 says it for insert; overwrite does the same in Premiere and keeps the append workflow). Several selected items go back to back. The new clips are selected (every media placement selects what it added). (Batch 20)
- `,` and `.` (§6.14, not in the §18 table) are keymap bindings in the media scope (`media.insertAtPlayhead`, `media.overwriteAtPlayhead`: the selected media); over a hovered card they take that card. While a card with a duration is hovered, I, O and Alt+X set or clear its source range (§12) ahead of the timeline's in/out keys (a capture-phase listener; stills let them through). (Batch 20)
- The source out point is the hovered time itself, the range's exclusive end: I at 2 s and O at 5 s take [2 s, 5 s), a 3 s clip (acceptance 2). Hover positions are project frames (0..the media's whole frames), so ranges are frame-exact on the timeline. The timeline's O stores F + 1 instead (Batch 19: the frame on screen stays in). A point on the wrong side of the other clears the other; an in point with less than a frame after it and an out point at 0 are ignored. Source ranges are session state (`sourceRanges` by media id, in session.json, pruned when media leave), not undoable. (Batch 20)
- A media drag lands on the lane under the pointer on its side of the divider, for both kinds: a video dropped on V2 puts its audio on A2, one dropped on A2 its picture on V2; missing lanes up to that one are created (V3, A2...). It uses the clip move's rules (§6.8: overwrite, Mod inserts, a locked target refuses with the move's toast) and snaps the block's leading or trailing edge. The drag takes the media selection when the pressed card is in it (in selection order), else the card alone. (Batch 20)
- A drop on the preview canvas (and Add to canvas, at the frame center) never overwrites (§6.14 "the topmost video track (or a new one)"): the topmost video track when it is unlocked and free over the whole drop, else a new track above it; a video's audio goes on the first audio track free there, else a new one. The picture's position is the drop point (rounded project px from the frame center, Fit scale as any insert). (Batch 20)
- Files dropped from the OS onto the timeline (§6.14 "one transaction"): the import and the clips are one undo step ("Drop files"): `MediaImporter.importFiles` takes a `place` callback run inside the transaction that adds the media. While the files are dragged the overlay shows only where they will start. A drop refused on a locked track keeps the imported media (the import happens before the placement). (Batch 20)
- The cross-panel media drag is pointer-based (the Batch 20 Build choice): a press on a card becomes a drag past the 4 px dead zone, then the media list captures the pointer; the timeline view gets the drag through `dragMedia` / `leaveMediaDrag` / `endMediaDrag` and draws it as a `mediaDrop` gesture (auto-scroll, S and Mod work as in clip drags); the page rects of the timeline overlay and the preview canvas are read once at the start (P9). (Batch 20)
- Detail on zoom (§7.1 BINDING): the render scale is the view scale up to 100%, above it `min(zoom, source detail)` (source px per project px of the most detailed visible media layer, crop and rotation counted). Below the view scale the visible part of the frame (not the whole frame) is rendered offscreen at that scale and blitted as one texture layer; the 4096 px cap applies to that offscreen part and to frame uploads. Reason: the whole 1080x1920 frame at 400% is 4320x7680 (over 4096 and ~130 MB), and capping the whole frame at 4096 would show a fitted 4K source at 60% of its pixels; the visible part at the render scale is never larger than the canvas. A render at the view scale itself is drawn straight onto the canvas (the same pixels without the copy). (Batch 21)
- Quality modes (§7.5, §8) without proxies (Batch 24): "reduced" is the full render scale capped at the project's own pixels with frames uploaded at the project's long edge (what playback always did). Auto renders reduced while playing or scrubbing and for 300 ms after, then full (frames the pool holds smaller are decoded again at the detail size); Proxy is always reduced until Batch 24; 1/2 and 1/4 render (and upload) at half / a quarter of the reduced scale while playing, as Auto otherwise; Full is always full. The status strip shows the mode and what the preview renders now. (Batch 21)
- Frame uploads follow the preview: the project's long edge times max(1, render scale), at most 4096 px and the device limit (the Batch 09 long-edge rule generalized). A frame decoded again for detail replaces the smaller cached texture in the pool (the frame service replaces a cached frame only with a bigger upload). (Batch 21)
- Preview view (§7.1): the pan is the project point at the canvas center (an offset from the frame center in project px, clamped so a frame edge can come to the center), so a resize keeps it there; Fit is always centered, plain wheel and drags pan only when zoomed; free zoom 5%-3200%; menu levels and Mod+0/1/2 zoom around the canvas center. The Hand tool's drag also pans the preview, and Space arms a pan only over the canvas while stopped and zoomed (a tap plays, as on the timeline). (Batch 21)
- Overlays (§7.6): title-safe 80% inside action-safe 90% (the spec writes "title-safe/action-safe (90/80%)"; title-safe is the inner one by convention); no separate frame-border item (the §7.1 frame edge is always drawn); "3x3 squares" are square cells a third of the short side with the middle cell centered, custom N is N x N cells (2-16); safe-zone numbers in `render/preview/safeZones.ts` come from the platforms' ad templates as measured by third-party tools (they publish no organic-post spec). Rulers run along the canvas edges (the frame edges are off screen when zoomed), labeled in project px. Overlays hide during the split trim preview. (Batch 21)
- Fullscreen (§7.8 "fills the window") is the panel fixed over the browser window, not the browser Fullscreen API; not persisted. Esc cancels a running gesture first, then leaves fullscreen. (Batch 21)
- Preview chrome placement (§13 header list, §17 groups): Zoom, Fit, Quality, Overlays, Snap, Guides, Fullscreen and Export frame sit in the panel header (Fit moved there from the footer); the footer keeps the readouts, the transport, Mute, Master volume, Loop and Play in/out. Zoom, Quality and Overlays are menu buttons over the context menu shell (not native selects): the zoom value is written by a subscription, so a wheel zoom never renders React (P1), and overlay toggles keep the menu open. (Batch 21)
- A scope binding whose command is not registered yet gives way to the global binding of the same key (KeyDispatcher `isAvailable`): the preview's arrow keys step frames until Batch 22's canvas nudges exist. (Batch 21)

- Canvas layers (§7.2, §6.12): muted video tracks stay selectable on the canvas though out of the composite ("still editable"); hidden and locked ones are skipped. Text clips join the canvas in Batch 28. (Batch 22)
- Canvas clicks (§7.2): Alt+click cycles through the layers under the pointer and selects the cycled one alone; Mod+click toggles a layer alone; plain and Shift clicks take its group and linked partners as on the timeline. A press inside the gizmo box moves the whole selection (a click there selects the top layer); Alt over the anchor marker drags the anchor, and a click there is the Alt+click. (Batch 22)
- Gizmo math (§7.3): moves in whole project px (nudges 1 / 10 px), scale results unrounded, rotation at 0.1°; scale stops at 1% (no flip by dragging past the opposite handle). Several layers share an axis-aligned group box (turned with a group rotation while it runs); a non-uniform group scale of a turned member scales it along its own axes (no shear). The 100% snap "flash" is the dragged handle's sides in --accent while it holds; the anchor marker shows with Alt held over the canvas. A canvas drag pauses playback. (Batch 22)
- Bring forward / Send backward (§7.2) also pass a locked, hidden or muted neighbour track (a new track past it), like an occupied one; several clips move one by one. (Batch 22)
- A scope binding whose handler declines also gives way to the global binding of the key (KeyDispatcher `candidates`): the preview's arrows nudge the selected layers and step frames when none is selected on the canvas. `app.cancel` is registered by canvasController (timeline gesture, canvas gesture, then fullscreen). (Batch 22)
- Smart guides toggle (§7.4, §13 "Guides toggle"): with Smart guides off, other layers and equal spacing are no snap targets and Alt shows no measurements; the frame (center, edges, thirds) and the safe edges still snap with the Snap toggle and always draw their lines (§0 "every snap is visible"). (Batch 23)
- Canvas snap targets (§7.4): the thirds while the grid overlay shows Thirds (the Overlays menu's Thirds is the "thirds" toggle); the platform safe rectangle and, while shown, the title-safe and action-safe rectangles; other layers by the axis-aligned bounds of their visible quads (a turned layer's bounding box), taken from the picture the compositor draws: layers on locked tracks are targets and measure neighbours though not selectable, muted and hidden ones are not (out of the picture). (Batch 23, locked tracks since its fix)
- One snap state (§7.4): the S toggle and hold-S also govern the §7.3 100% scale snap; Shift's 45° rotation snap stays (an explicit modifier), and rotation has no other snap. Anchor drags do not snap. (Batch 23)
- Canvas snapping math (§7.4): a snapped axis lands exactly on its target, which can be a fractional project px (unsnapped axes stay whole px, Batch 22); a scale snaps the dragged edge only while the box is axis-aligned (no turn or a quarter turn, group boxes), a uniform corner along the nearer target's axis with the other following the aspect; equal spacing is the middle case only (the box centered in the room between two layers in line with it, no other such layer between them), not the extension of a series. (Batch 23)
- Canvas guides (§7.4): every target the moved box meets exactly is shown (also on an axis Shift holds), a layer line spans the box and every layer on it with the distance to the nearest one on each side (once per layer), and a distance an equal-spacing gap already shows is not repeated. Measure mode (Alt, no drag, Smart guides on) measures each side to the nearest layer overlapping the selection across it, else to the frame edge (negative when it overhangs); a group measures from its box. Distances read whole px, else one decimal. (Batch 23)
- Proxy settings (§11 Project section, §9.5) are global app settings (IndexedDB `settings.v1`: `proxyAuto`, `proxyRule`, `proxyShortEdge`), like the autosave delay: §5.2's `Project` type is kept verbatim, and which videos are too heavy is a property of the machine. The on/off switch governs automatic proxies only (videos with no proxy decision get one when the rule picks them); jobs already pending still run, and Generate proxy works with it off. (Batch 24)
- Proxy jobs resume by starting over (§9.5 "resumable"): an MP4 being muxed cannot be continued, so on open the `.part` (and the browser's `.crswap` swap file) of an interrupted job is deleted and its pending video is made again from the first frame (acceptance 3 allows "or restarts cleanly"). They pause while the IdleQueue is paused: playback, and timeline drags and scrubs too (the preview's scrub decodes come first); export is `TODO(batch 43)`. (Batch 24)
- Auto quality (§7.5, §8, §9.5): proxies while playing and scrubbing; the full frame is asked for at once when playback pauses or a scrub or trim ends (the proxy frame stays until the full one is decoded), not 300 ms later as §8 words it; a scrub held still counts as paused after 300 ms (§7.5). The "fell behind by more than 2 frames" switch applies where it can change something: Auto already plays the proxy of every video that has one, so a video with no proxy decision that falls behind gets one queued, and 1/2 and 1/4 (which decode the full file) switch a video that falls behind to its proxy for the rest of the playback; Full never switches. (Batch 24)
- Proxy encoding (§9.5): H.264 Main first, then High and Constrained Baseline when the encoder has no Main profile; frames scaled by successive 2x bilinear halvings on OffscreenCanvas (the Build's "pick the faster that keeps quality": 37-52 fps against 13-19 fps for one high-quality canvas or bitmap resize and for the encoder's own scaling, 4K60 HEVC to 960x540 here). Pixels stay in coded orientation; the source's rotation goes into the proxy's track matrix. The proxy's sample index is cached as `cache/index/<id>.proxy.bin`. (Batch 24)
- Export frame (§9.5 "PNG export at full resolution regardless of the quality mode") decodes each video's exact full frame at its own size, or at twice its size on the frame when drawn smaller than half its size (the renderer filters bilinearly without mipmaps), at most 4096 px; it no longer draws the textures last shown. (Batch 24)
- Field scrubs (§11 "commands coalesce while scrubbing") write ephemeral values (`app/liveEdit.ts`: transforms, opacities, the background while the color picker is dragged) that the preview and the gizmo draw, and commit one command on release, instead of dispatching coalesced commands during the scrub. Reason: P2 (binding) keeps gestures out of the document, so a scrub neither autosaves nor renders the app bar; one undo step per gesture either way. Arrow-key steps coalesce (`properties.<prop>`, 300 ms). (Batch 25)
- Transform fields (§11): position in project px from the frame center as stored (0 = centered; typing 100 sets exactly 100), scale W / H in % with an aspect lock between them (closed unless a selected layer is scaled unevenly; locked, the other axis follows by the same factor; 1% to 10000%), rotation in degrees, anchor in % of the layer; opacity in %. Anchor edits keep the picture in place (the position follows), as the gizmo's Alt anchor drag (§7.3). (Batch 25)
- Clip section times (§11): End is the exclusive end (Start + Duration). Start trims the in point (the end stays), End and Duration the out point, Source in / out the in / out point by the typed source time at the clip's speed; regular trims (no ripple) with linked partners while Linked selection is on, clamped by the media and the neighbors with the keyboard trims' toasts. With several clips each is trimmed to the typed time, one undo step. (Batch 25)
- Multi-selection (§11 "shared properties"): Transform and Opacity show when any selected clip is a layer and edit the layers (a linked audio partner is skipped); the name field renames every selected clip; a mixed switch sits halfway and a click turns them all on. (Batch 25)
- Resolution changes go through the conversion flow's placeholder (`changeResolution`) and scale the layout (positions per axis, scale by the smaller ratio, locked tracks too), replacing Batch 03's "keep" (the Batch 25 Build). The frame rate still changes only without clips or markers until Batch 42. The Project section's default durations, default transition duration and cache size / Clear cache are disabled rows naming Batch 42 (its settings panel; §11 lists them). (Batch 25)
- Duplicate (`edit.duplicate`, Mod+D in the timeline and preview scopes) copies the primary clip alone, unlinked and ungrouped, onto its own track in the next free span after it, and selects the copy (the Batch 25 single-clip command). The canvas menu's Duplicate row stays a placeholder (Batch 39: +20 px). (Batch 25)
- The one-click transforms are `transform.fit/fill/center/flipH/flipV/reset` for the quick row and the canvas menu (one id per action, §3.6) on every selected layer, not only those at the playhead; `canvas.resetTransform` is gone. The clip menu's Enable/Disable (`clip.toggleEnabled`, §6.16) is built with the Clip section's switch, ahead of Batch 41's TODO: Disable while any selected clip is enabled. (Batch 25)
- The color picker's recent colors are a global app setting (`recentColors` in IndexedDB `settings.v1`), like the proxy settings. (Batch 25)
- Crop to frame (§11) keeps the transform: the visible area is the frame when the layer covers it, else the largest area with the frame's aspect inside the part of the frame the layer covers (Fill then fills the frame with it exactly); turned layers get the largest such rectangle centered in the turned frame, then inside the media, with the aspect of the nearest quarter turn. (Batch 26)
- Crop mode (§7.3, Build "crop tool chip"): entered from the Crop section header or by a double-click on a video or image layer (selected alone), shown by a chip over the canvas (Crop · Reset · Done); one layer at a time; it ends on Esc (after a running gesture), Done, a double-click on the layer, a selection that is not that layer alone, or the clip leaving or locking. Body drags still move the layer; Alt is the symmetric crop (no anchor marker or measure mode); no rotate zones. (Batch 26)
- The crop aspect lock is the Crop section's toggle, shared with the canvas handles; Shift inverts it during a handle drag. It keeps the current visible aspect in media px (an edge moves the other axis around its center, a corner scales from the opposite corner or, with Alt, the center). (Batch 26)
- Crop edges snap to the frame's edges, center and thirds (thirds always, not only while the Thirds grid shows), while snapping is on and the layer is axis-aligned; no other layers or safe zones. (Batch 26)
- Crop units are media px of the media as shown or % of its side (px by default, per session, not persisted); a crop always leaves at least 1 media px on each axis. A crop handle drag writes `liveEdit.crops` like a Crop field scrub, so the fields follow it live. (Batch 26)
- Clip speed (§11, §14): kept exactly as asked (0.25x..4x). Linked (default), `in`/`out` stay and the duration follows, rounded. Unlinked, the duration stays and `out` follows; media shorter than that is clamped with a toast (§6.19). Without ripple, a slower clip stops at the next clip with its out point brought in (the speed stays). Ripple shifts what follows as a ripple trim of the out point does (a left shift stops at the first overlap, leaving a gap). The Speed section's link and Ripple are per session, both on by default; Ripple is the DS switch. Speed field scrubs apply once on release (no live retiming, P1/P2). The section's Duration is a rate stretch when linked, a trim otherwise. "Speed..." (§6.16) opens the section on its field. (Batch 27)
- Freeze frames (Batch 27 Build) are video clips with `speed = 0`: `in` is the frozen source time, `out` one project frame later; only video clips may have speed 0. Freeze frame always splits the clip's linked partners and ripples every unlocked track by 2 s from the playhead (clips spanning it stay). It acts on the selected video under the playhead, else the topmost targeted visible one. Freezes trim like images, never slip, stretch as a trim, and split into two holds. (Batch 27)
- Punch zoom multiplies the right pieces' `scale` / `scaleY` by the app setting `punchZoomScale` (1.2, 1.05..3) around their anchor. It splits linked partners while Linked selection is on, and acts on the selected layers under the playhead, else the topmost video or image. A clip starting at the playhead is zoomed whole. `punchZoomAnimated` is reserved (false) until Batch 35. (Batch 27)
- Text (Batch 28): the box lives in `TextContent` (`{ value, box }`); `Gradient` (`{ from, to }`, vertical) is defined for text fills. The vertical anchor (and the alignment) name the edge that stays in place when a text's box changes size: text edits move the position to keep it. Rasters are drawn at the render scale times the layer's own scale, rounded up to 2^(1/8) steps, clamped to 0.25..4, at most 4096 px. Glyph hit areas are padded by 4 project px and include the background boxes; text snaps with its content box. The Text section follows Clip. Rasterization runs on the main thread in the preview render. System fonts are listed on a click; only the faces used are copied into `fonts/` (with `fonts.json`). Bundled fonts carry latin and latin-ext only. Details under the batch. (Batch 28)
- Text UX (Batch 29): `+ Title` / `+ Subtitle` go on the topmost video track when it is free there, else on a new top track; a Title made with a text clip selected sits 40 px under it, a Subtitle always in the lower third (bottom edge at 78%). Inline editing draws the caret on the overlay from the rasterizer's geometry and the selection highlight in the raster; Esc commits like Mod+Enter; one "Edit text" step; the properties panel and popovers keep an edit open. Text box corners size the font (and every px value), sides set a fixed box width; no top or bottom handles. Details under the batch. (Batch 29)

- Text presets (Batch 30): project presets are files in `presets/text/<id>.json` (spec §9.7 and the batch), not `Project.textPresetsLocal` (§5.2: kept, always empty); preset changes are not undoable (only Save as preset's link and Apply are commands). Sizes are stored for a 1080 px short side and scale with the project. `TextPreset` adds `applyPosition` and `updatedAt`; applying replaces style, box and animations and keeps content, duration and position (unless Apply position). The library folder sync is a union (the newer copy wins). The Presets header keeps New from selection, Import and Export; the Global / Project toggle and the search sit in a tool row under it. Details under the batch. (Batch 30)
- Text animations (Batch 31): an animation's `duration` is the whole animation, every unit's delay included; without a `stagger` the units overlap (each takes 2.5 delays). Each plays over at most half the clip (in and out never overlap); the stored value stays as typed. Out animations play the units last first and keep the direction their name says (slideUp out moves up). Whole-text animations move the layer raster; per-unit ones draw an atlas cell per unit over a raster of the background boxes alone (at the leading unit's opacity); a typewriter reveals graphemes by count, cut out of its line atlas cells. Eases: linear, easeIn, easeOut, easeInOut (absent: the animation's own). Presets store animations in milliseconds. Text clips within 5 s of the playhead are rasterized ahead. A text edited inline shows at rest; the gizmo and hit testing use the text at rest. Details under the batch. (Batch 31)
- Captions (Batch 32): `captionTracks[trackId]` holds the style only (the cue ids are the track's `clipIds`, one source of truth); cues are selected as clips (kind `clips`; the `cue` kind is never made). Word timings are frames relative to the cue start. Cues never overlap: a cue move stops at the cues that stay, an Alt-drag copy goes to the nearest free spot within its length (else the drop is refused), a cue's trims are regular (no push, Ripple mode or not); insert moves and ripples from other tracks still shift (and split) cues to keep sync. The cue list types into a live draft committed as one "Edit caption" step; Enter splits by the share of characters (at the first right word with word timings), Shift+Enter breaks the line, Esc reverts. Import replaces the track's cues; export picks SRT or VTT from the file name; VTT inline timestamps are read as word timings. A cue wraps at the max width and draws smaller (to half size) to keep within the max lines. The highlight is drawn from two word atlases (the other words, the active word), the box mode with square corners until Batch 34. Details under the batch. (Batch 32)

## Follow-ups
Small things discovered during a batch that belong to a later batch. Format: `- [from Batch NN -> Batch MM] description`.
- [from Batch 00 -> Batch 01] `npm run check` and `npm run lint` do not exist yet; Batch 01 creates them along with the Vite + React scaffold. (done in Batch 01)
- [from Batch 01 -> Batch 02] `KeyDispatcher.onAction` only logs (`app/services.ts`); route it to the command registry. (done in Batch 02: `ActionRegistry`)
- [from Batch 01 -> Batch 03] Persist splitter positions per project (session.json); localStorage `layout.v1` stays the global fallback. (done in Batch 03)
