- [from Batch 01 -> Batch 05] Unsupported screen should also trigger when `navigator.gpu` exists but no adapter can be acquired. (done in Batch 05: startup adapter check in `main.tsx`)
- [from Batch 01 -> Batch 09] Pause/resume the `IdleQueue` from playback (and from gestures from Batch 11 on). (done in Batch 09: the playback engine pauses it while playing; gestures in Batch 11)
- [from Batch 01 -> Batch 42] Chrome reserves Ctrl+N, Ctrl+T and Ctrl+W in a normal tab; pages cannot preventDefault them, so `project.new` (Mod+N) opens a browser window instead. Decide on an alternative binding or document the installed-app (PWA window) route.
- [from Batch 02 -> Batch 03] `DocumentStore.load(project)` (replace the document, clear the history); persist `SessionState` + the selection snapshot (arrays) in session.json; default tracks for a new project (`createProject` makes none); keep `meta.modifiedAt` current on save. (done in Batch 03)
- [from Batch 02 -> Batch 07] Default `timeline.pxPerFrame` in `DEFAULT_SESSION` is a placeholder (4). (done in Batch 07: kept 4 px/frame = 120 px/s at 30 fps, documented)
- [from Batch 02 -> Batch 16] Selection kind `track` has no track id field (§3.3 shape kept as written); add one with track selection. Link-group offsets are not validated (allowed by §5.2); the sync badge needs an offset query. (done in Batch 16: `Selection.trackIds` for kind `track`; `linkOffset` is the offset query, by source for one media)
- [from Batch 02 -> Batch 34] `validate.ts` applies the media in/out rules to image clips too; decide the rule for stills.
- [from Batch 02 -> Batch 41] `validate.ts` requires a media clip's `mediaId` to exist in `project.media`; "Remove, keep offline clips" must keep the media record (marked missing) or relax this check.
- [from Batch 03 -> Batch 04] `media/` exists in every project; link-mode media (`file.handleKey`) should store its file handles through `project/kv.ts` (`idbKeyValueStore`), next to the recent-project handles. (moved to Batch 41: Batch 04 only stubs reference mode)
- [from Batch 03 -> Batch 25] The Project section is a plain section: make it collapsible (state in `collapsedSections`) with the properties framework; `TextField` has no scrub mode. (done in Batch 25: every section is collapsible with its state in `collapsedSections`; number fields scrub on their label)
- [from Batch 03 -> Batch 40] Mod+S does nothing while a text input or a dialog has focus (inputs own their keys, the dialog scope is isolated), so Chrome's "Save page" dialog opens. Decide whether Mod+S is preventDefault-ed everywhere.
- [from Batch 03 -> Batch 04] The 10,000-dispatch perf test (`src/core/store/DocumentStore.test.ts`, limit 300 ms) hit 332 ms best-of-3 in one full `npm run check` while 32 jsdom test files ran in parallel (56–90 ms alone). Run perf tests in their own sequential pass (or a separate Vitest project) so CPU contention cannot turn an unattended run red. (done in Batch 04: `*.perf.test.ts` run in the "perf" Vitest project after the unit tests)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 06] The decode engine builds on `readMp4` (sample table, description, rotation) and `ByteSource`; persist the sample index in `cache/index/`; add cancellation by priority class (a scrub cancels pending prefetches of the same media) to `WorkerHost`/`WorkerPool` (only per-request cancellation exists). (done in Batch 06: `demux/sampleIndex*.ts` + `cache/index/<id>.bin`; the class cancellation lives in `FrameService`, which aborts the superseded scrub and the prefetches of the media through the existing per-request cancel messages)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 10] The audio probe decodes whole non-MP4 files with `decodeAudioData` (resampled to 48 kHz, so the source rate is unknown; long files cost memory): reuse the decoded-audio cache / segmented decode. The peaks job replaces the `AnalysisScheduler` placeholder; decide how `analysis.peaks` becomes "ready" without an undo step per media. (done in Batch 10: the probe's buffer goes into the decoded-audio cache, so a file is decoded once; MP4 audio decodes by range in decode.worker; peaks jobs replace the placeholder and set "ready" through `DocumentStore.amend`, with no undo step. Non-MP4 audio over 10 minutes still decodes whole: Batch 45 follow-up)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 15] `edit.delete` and `edit.rename` are registered in `app/mediaController.ts` and only log outside the media scope: route the timeline/preview scopes to clip delete/rename there (or split the handler by scope). (done in Batch 15: both are registered in `app/cutController.ts`, which calls the media panel's actions in the media scope)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 17] Card posters (first frame, `cache/thumbs/<id>.poster.webp`) give way to the sprite sheets; the analysis placeholder marks `analysis.thumbs`. (done in Batch 17: video cards show their LOD0 sheet's first cell; `ThumbnailStore` marks `analysis.thumbs` ready after LOD0 and LOD1)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 20] Media panel: hover scrub, drag to the timeline, "unused" dim state and usage count on hover; double-click on a card thumbnail inserts (the name keeps double-click rename). (double-click insert done in Batch 07, on cards and list rows) (done in Batch 20: hover-scrub on cards and list rows, drags out of the panel, the unused dim state and the usage chip)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 24] The list view's Proxy column shows "None". (done in Batch 24: None, Queued or the percentage, the proxy size, Failed)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 34] Stills have `durationUs: 0` (no natural duration); animated GIF/WebP durations come from `ImageDecoder`, and the frame list (`frameDurationsUs`) is returned by the probe but not persisted.
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 41] Reference import mode: the `importMode` app setting exists (no UI) and falls back to copy; store handles through `project/kv.ts`; posters of reference media; "Remove and delete clips" and the media-file deletion checkbox (disabled / absent now); `media.remove` refuses media used by clips; bins, search, filter chips, missing media.
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 46] After an import every new card is selected (gold borders on all of them): review against "gold is used sparingly". The list view needs horizontal scroll below ~670 px.
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 07] `src/core/scheduler/FrameScheduler.ts` has `TODO(batch 02)` (map document `affects` flags onto view flags), which points at a finished batch; the timeline view is the first document subscriber, so do the mapping there and retag the TODO. (found in the Batch 04 review) (done in Batch 07: `TimelineView` maps document `affects` to `timelineContent`; the TODO is now a comment)
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 08] Remove `render/preview/ImageHarness.ts` and the `setLayers` entry point of `PreviewView` (the compositor's draw list replaces them). `WebGpuRenderer.draw` builds a `DrawRecord` array and uniform objects per call; make it allocation-free once the draw list is a reused struct array. Frame textures go into `TexturePool` under `(mediaId, lod, timeUs)`. (done in Batch 08: harness and `setLayers` removed; `draw` fills a pooled record array and packs uniforms in place)
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 09] `TexturePool` pinning exists per key (`pin`); pin the frames within ±1 s of the playhead (P6, `TODO(batch 09)` in the pool). (done in Batch 09: the engine pins its window frames ahead of the playhead until passed, the preview what it draws; not all of ±1 s, see Global deviations)
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 21] The header Zoom select (Fit/25-400%, session `preview.zoom`) is the Batch 05 stand-in: pan, wheel zoom at the cursor, Mod+0/1/2 (`canvas.zoom*` are in the keymap but unregistered), the §7.1 detail-on-zoom offscreen render, quality modes. 100% = one project px per device px (see the batch deviations). The canvas CSS size follows the fractional panel size; use `devicePixelContentBoxSize` if exact backing pixels matter. (done in Batch 21)
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 44] Export frame lives in the preview header (`preview.exportFrame`, `exportFrame()` in `app/previewController.ts`: picker first, then `PreviewView.renderFrame()` at project size, PNG); move or mirror it in the Export tab.
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 46] The dev pool-budget select and the GPU stats toggle show in dev builds only (`import.meta.env.DEV`); §13 wants the pool memory in the status strip behind a dev toggle.
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 06] `WebGpuRenderer.uploadImageBitmap`/`uploadVideoFrame`: destroy the new texture when `copyExternalImageToTexture` throws (closed source) before rethrowing (P13). (found in the Batch 05 review) (done in Batch 06: `WebGpuRenderer.copyIn`)
- [from Batch 05 -> Batch 08] `PreviewStatsOverlay` can run before the preview render it reports when `previewOverlay` is invalidated first in a frame (`FrameScheduler` orders views by flag invalidation order): it then shows `renders` one behind. Make the overlay invalidate itself from the preview's render, or have the scheduler render `previewOverlay` views after `preview` ones. (found in the Batch 05 review) (done in Batch 08: `FrameScheduler` renders flags in a fixed order, content before overlay)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 08] Remove `render/preview/VideoHarness.ts`, the `ScrubBar` of `PreviewPanel.tsx` (and its `.scrub*` CSS), `PreviewView.clearLayers` and `pickPreviewMedia` with the image harness. The compositor asks `frameService.getFrame(mediaId, sourceTimeUs, { priority })` and pins what it draws. Frame times are the container's cts in us with no edit list applied (a file with a composition offset starts after 0; `positionAt` maps earlier times to the first frame): decide how media time maps to cts there. (done in Batch 08: all removed; media time 0 = the first frame's cts, Global deviations)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 08] A `getFrame` result can be approximate since the Batch 06 fixes: when the decoder cannot produce the frame, the result is the nearest one it did, with that frame's time and pool key. The compositor draws it but must not treat it as the frame at the requested time (ask again on a later render). (found in the Batch 06 fixes) (done in Batch 08: `LayerTextures` draws it and asks again once the layer wants another frame)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 09] Uploads cost decoder throughput: `copyExternalImageToTexture` from a hardware frame took 1080p H.264 from 2.9 ms/frame (frames closed) to 7.3 (one reused texture) and 8.5-11.5 (a new texture per frame), and 4K HEVC from 11 to 21-29 ms/frame (headless Chrome 155, RDNA2). For playback, recycle evicted frame textures of the same size instead of create + destroy, and consider `importExternalTexture` for the frame on screen. Use `frameService.prefetch` windows rather than one request per frame (a request round trip costs 15-30 ms per frame). Hardware decoders stall when the app holds many of their frames (20 held frames hung one); the session's stall fallback frees its ring, but keep windows small. (done in Batch 09: spare frame textures are recycled, frames are scaled to the project size on upload, playback uses 2-frame prefetch chunks, windows take their frames out of the ring and idle sessions free their ring after 1 s)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 09] `VideoDecodeSession.output` (`src/media/decode/VideoDecodeSession.ts:487-497`) drops a leading picture of the current run only while its timestamp is still pending. When a superseded run's output has a timestamp that is also pending in the new run, it prunes every pending entry at or before it (line 489). A leading picture of the new run that comes out after that lands in the ring as a valid frame. Track pending timestamps per run. (found in the Batch 06 re-review, not reproduced) (done in Batch 09: superseded runs are tracked apart from the current one, with a test that fails on the old bookkeeping)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 24] Exact-frame latency deep in a GOP is bound by the hardware decode rate (~10 ms per 4K HEVC frame at 84 Mbps: median 593 ms 41-59 frames past a keyframe in 1 s GOPs). Proxies (540p, a keyframe every 15 frames) are the way to P8's 50 ms. `lod: "proxy"` has no file yet: `frameSource` in `app/previewController.ts` returns null (`TODO(batch 24)`), and its sample index needs its own cache name. (done in Batch 24: the frame source opens `cache/proxies/<id>.mp4` of ready proxies with their own `cache/index/<id>.proxy.bin`; scrubbing a 4K60 HEVC clip draws the exact proxy frame in 17-34 ms)
- [from Batch 06 -> Batch 41] `frameSource` resolves copy-mode media only (`TODO(batch 41)`: media referenced by a file handle).
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 08] Inserted clips get the identity transform (scale 1 = native size, anchor at the center): Batch 08's "default transform on insert: Fit" goes into `mediaClip` in `src/timeline/edit/insertMedia.ts` (`TODO(batch 08)`). (done in Batch 08: `defaultTransform`)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 09] Follow playhead (§6.2 paging during playback) belongs to playback. Keyboard jumps (End, Up/Down, the timecode field) do not scroll the view to the playhead yet. (done in Batch 09: `TimelineView.follow`/`reveal`, `pageToFrame`)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 11] A click selects only the clicked clip, Shift+click toggles (`clickClipSelection`): the linked partner is not selected (session `linkedSelection` is ignored), and a plain click inside a multi-selection replaces it (Batch 11 must keep it for drags). Mod/Alt variants and the marquee too. (done in Batch 11: `clickSelection` with partners and Shift/Mod/Alt; a press on a selected clip defers the click to the release; marquee)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 12] Ruler scrubbing with Shift should snap to edit points and markers while snapping is on (`TODO(batch 12)` in `TimelineView.onPointerDown`). (done in Batch 12: `TimelineView.scrubAt`, every candidate but the playhead)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 16] Track headers draw only the kind glyph and name: toggles, double-click rename and track selection are `TODO(batch 16)`; header clicks do nothing. The Track height toolbar button stays disabled. (done in Batch 16: every header toggle, double-click rename, track selection, Track height button)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 17] Clips show only body, label bar, kind glyph and name: badges, label colors, filmstrips and waveforms are `TODO(batch 17)`. The per-kind clip colors are design gaps to revisit with the label colors. (done in Batch 17: filmstrips, waveforms, label colors with them, link/keyframe badges, disabled hatch, text/solid/image bodies; the per-kind colors stay)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 18] No scrollbars yet (horizontal with zoom handles, vertical): vertical scrolling is wheel-only, and in a project with many video tracks the top ones sit above the resting divider until the user scrolls up. Zoom to selection stays disabled. The timecode field ignores invalid input silently (no feedback) and uses mm:ss:ff only. (done in Batch 18: both scrollbars with zoom handles, Zoom to selection, the field marks invalid input and follows the format toggle)
- [from Batch 07 -> Batch 46] The timeline toolbar's Zoom group (Batch 01 priority 1) is the first to overflow into "...": at a 1600 px window the slider and zoom buttons sit in the overflow menu (they fit at 1920 px). On an empty or very short project the zoom-out limit is Fit (content + 2 s), so the view cannot go back beyond about 2 s once zoomed in past that. Review both.
- [from Batch 03 -> Batch 42] fps conversion and "Scale layout" in the Project section (`project.setFrameRate` refuses projects with clips or markers); recent-project thumbnails (`RecentProject.thumbnail` is null); the autosave delay moves to the settings panel (stored in IndexedDB `settings.v1`); the history list reads backup names with `backupSortKey` (UTC, `-N` suffix).
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 09] Playback evaluates each frame through `Compositor.evaluate` + `LayerTextures.resolve` (add a `playback` priority pass, no stand-in churn). The cut warming of `LayerTextures.warm` (edge frames of the clips within 2 s of the playhead) is the scrub side of P7; the lookahead scan extends it. Transport buttons, duration and fps badge go into the preview footer (`TODO(batch 09)` in `PreviewPanel.tsx`); the footer timecode is read-only (the timeline toolbar field is the editable one). (done in Batch 09; the footer timecode stays read-only, the toolbar field is the editable one)
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 24] Exact frames deep in long GOPs miss Batch 08's 100 ms: 1080x1920 H.264 with 2 s GOPs took 108 and 133 ms 59 frames past a keyframe (hardware decode ~2 ms/frame; 1 s GOPs stayed at 47-98 ms). Proxies are the fix. (done in Batch 24: scrubs use the proxy (15-frame GOPs); the full frame after a pause still takes the hardware decode rate, recorded under Batch 24)
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 26] One-click Fill next to the default Fit (§6.19, §11); `defaultTransform` in `timeline/edit/insertMedia.ts` holds the Fit math. (done: Fill in the quick row and the canvas menu since Batch 25; Batch 26 adds Crop to frame, so Fill reframes landscape footage without overflow)
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 41] Undecodable or missing media draw nothing (a console warning; `TODO(batch 41)` in `LayerTextures.fail`): the labeled gray offline slate.
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 44] Export frame (and later the export) draws the last resolved draw list, which may hold stand-ins while frames load: export must wait for exact frames. (Export frame done in Batch 24: `PreviewView.renderFrame` waits for exact full frames through `LayerTextures.resolveExact`; the export pipeline remains)
- [from Batch 08 -> Batch 09] Before the compositor runs per playback frame, remove the per-render allocations it still has: `sinCos` returns an array (`render/compositor/Compositor.ts:41-48`), `deviceSize`/`frameTransform` return new objects (`render/preview/viewport.ts`), and `keyId` builds strings (`render/preview/LayerTextures.ts:74`). Guard `FLAG_ORDER` in `core/scheduler/FrameScheduler.ts` so a new `InvalidationFlag` cannot be left out (it would never render). (found in the Batch 08 review) (done in Batch 09)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 10] Audio runs on `audioClock.context` (`TODO(batch 10)` in `audio/AudioClock.ts`); mute and the master volume knob go next to the transport (`TODO(batch 10)` in `PreviewPanel.tsx`). The P1 milestone was checked here once (0 commits in 30 s of playback); re-check it with audio playing. (done in Batch 10: the audio graph and scheduler run on the clock's context; mute and the master volume knob sit before loop and Fit; P1 with audio playing: 0 commits in 330 s)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 11] The `IdleQueue` has one pauser (the playback engine). Count pausers once gestures pause it too (`TODO(batch 11)` in `IdleQueue.ts`). (done in Batch 11: named pausers, "playback" and "gesture")
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 19] Play in/out (`transport.playInToOut`, Mod+Shift+Space, the transport button) and the in/out loop (`TODO(batch 19)` in `playbackController.ts` and `PreviewPanel.tsx`). (done in Batch 19: `PlaybackEngine.playInToOut`, the Play in/out button beside Loop; loop wraps the in/out range)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 21] Video frames are uploaded at most at the project's long edge (`uploadSize` in `previewController.ts`, `TODO(batch 21)`). Zoom above 100% needs larger uploads. (done in Batch 21)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 21] The preview footer's timecode readout is a read-only `span` written by `bindPreviewReadouts` (`app/previewController.ts`). §7.8 wants it editable: make it an input bound with `bindTimecode` (`app/timelineController.ts`), as in the timeline toolbar. (found in the Batch 09 review) (done in Batch 21)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 24] Auto quality: `PlaybackEngine.autoProxy` flags media whose frames were late on more than 2 frames in a row (`TODO(batch 24)`). Switch those to proxies for the rest of the playback, and request the full-quality frame 300 ms after a pause (§8). 4K60 HEVC at Full is at the limit of the development machine: median 1.08% dropped when rested, 3-15% when hot. (done in Batch 24: Auto plays proxies, 1/2 and 1/4 switch a media that falls behind to its proxy, one without a proxy decision gets one queued; the full frame is asked for at once when playback pauses)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 21] While the Preview panel is active, the arrow keys (with or without Shift) and Ctrl+0/1/2 do nothing: they are bound to preview-scope actions (canvas nudge, preview zoom) that are not registered yet, and the dispatcher picks the scope binding before the global one (frame step). Let an unregistered scoped action fall through to the global binding until Batches 21/22 wire them. (found while writing the user guide) (done in Batch 21)
- [from Batch 09 -> Batch 21] After a level is picked in the preview **Zoom** dropdown (native select), the select keeps focus and every shortcut, Space included, is ignored until the user clicks elsewhere. - [from Batch 09 -> Batch 18] **Go to end** / End moves to the content end, one frame after the last clip, so the preview shows only the background. Decide whether it should land on the last frame of content. (found while writing the user guide) (done in Batch 18: decided to keep End at the content end, see Global deviations)
- [from Batch 04 -> Batch 41] User-facing text mentions batch numbers: the Remove media dialog says "removing used media comes with Batch 41" and the disabled button tooltip says "Comes with the advanced media panel (Batch 41)". Use neutral wording. (found while writing the user guide)
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 16] Track headers draw only mute and solo, and only on audio tracks (`geometry.headerToggles`, `drawContent.ts`, `TODO(batch 16)`): video-track mute, target, lock and hide, and the full header UI. Mute/solo are `track.update` commands; give them their own command ids with the header actions. (done in Batch 16: all toggles per kind; `track.target/lock/mute/solo/hide` command ids)
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 17] Waveforms read the peaks files (`cache/peaks/<id>.bin`, `readPeaksFile` in `media/cache/peaks.ts`; `TODO(batch 17)` in `media/PeaksJobs.ts`). Level n covers 256 << n frames per bucket; pick the level closest to one bucket per CSS px. (done in Batch 17: `PeaksJobs.get`, level closest to one bucket per CSS px)
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 37] Clip gain curves (fades, keyframes, rubber band) go on the clip GainNode per scheduled chunk with `setValueCurveAtTime` (`TODO(batch 37)` in `audio/scheduler/AudioScheduler.ts`). Every other gain change uses `ramp`/`rampTo` in `AudioMixer.ts`: a linear ramp needs an explicit start point (see the Batch 10 click fix). Audio scrub can play grains from `decodedAudio` segments.
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 38] Silence detection runs in analysis.worker from the peaks (`TODO(batch 38)` in `analysis.worker.ts`).
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 43] The export mixdown runs the same scheduling against an `OfflineAudioContext` (`TODO(batch 43)` in `AudioMixer.ts`); the monitor gain stays out of it.
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 45] Non-MP4 audio (mp3, aac, wav, ogg) over 10 minutes decodes whole with `decodeAudioData`: a 60 min stereo file is ~1.4 GB of PCM, over the 512 MB budget, and files over 1 GB are refused. Segment WAV by byte ranges and MP3/AAC by frames.
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 45] Audio is aligned with the frame's draw: video drew 0-17.5 ms after the audio was heard (mean 8.7 ms) in the clap test. The display's presentation delay (about one vsync) comes on top and was not measurable headless. Measure on a real display (slow-motion phone video of a clap test) and delay the audio if needed.
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 16] A track removed while its clips sound is cut off with no ramp. `AudioScheduler.onDocument` calls `mixer.updateTracks` first, and that disconnects the removed track's node at once (`audio/AudioMixer.ts`, `updateTracks`), before the voices ramp out. Undoing an insert that created the audio track while it plays stops the sound abruptly. Ramp the track (or its voices) out and disconnect after `RAMP_SECONDS`. (found in the Batch 10 review) (done in Batch 16: `updateTracks` ramps a removed track to 0 and disconnects it after the ramp)
- [from Batch 10 -> Batch 45] Closing a project sends no `closeAudio`: `DecodedAudio.reset()` keeps decode.worker's parsed audio tracks (up to 8, with their `File`s) of the closed project (P13). `DecodedAudio.atMostStereo` mixes `decodeAudioData` output with more than 2 channels down on the main thread in one pass; move it off the main thread or slice it. (found in the Batch 10 review)

## Dependencies
Why each dependency exists (Batch rule 8).
- `react`, `react-dom` 18.3.1: UI chrome (spec §3.1). (Batch 01)
- `lucide-react`: icon set named by the design system. (Batch 01)
- `vite`, `@vitejs/plugin-react`, `typescript`: dev/build (spec §3.1). (Batch 01)
- `vitest`, `jsdom`: unit tests; jsdom for the DOM-dependent core tests (keyboard dispatcher, focus scopes, theme bridge). (Batch 01)
- `eslint`, `typescript-eslint`, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`: `npm run lint`, including the color-literal rule of §3.7. (Batch 01)
- `@types/react`, `@types/react-dom`, `@types/node`: typings. (Batch 01)
- `immer` 11: `produceWithPatches`/`applyPatches` for commands and undo (spec §3.3 names it). (Batch 02)
- `idb-keyval` 6: IndexedDB for recent-project directory handles and global settings (spec §3.1 names it). (Batch 03)
- `mp4box` 2.4.1: MP4/MOV demux (spec §3.1 names mp4box.js). Imported only by `decode.worker` (190.6 kB worker chunk; the main bundle does not contain it). (Batch 04) Since Batch 17 also by `analysis.worker`, which opens its own video sessions for the thumbnail sheets (205.83 kB chunk, was 3.88 kB).
- `@webgpu/types` 0.1.74 (dev): TypeScript declarations of the WebGPU API (`GPUDevice`, `GPUTexture`...), which `lib.dom` does not ship. Types only, nothing bundled. (Batch 05)
- `mp4-muxer` 5.2.2: the proxy's MP4 (spec §3.1 names mp4-muxer; Batch 43's export will use it too). Streams its bytes (`StreamTarget`, chunked, no fast start) from `proxy.worker` only (226.31 kB worker chunk with mp4box; the main bundle does not contain it). Upstream marks it superseded by Mediabunny; it is kept because the spec names it. (Batch 24)
- Bundled content fonts (Batch 28): not an npm dependency. 34 woff2 files (Inter, Montserrat, Nunito, Oswald, Unbounded, Playfair Display, Caveat, JetBrains Mono; latin and latin-ext, `wght` variable, 1.4 MB) copied with their SIL OFL licenses from the Fontsource 5.3.0 packages (`npm pack`) into `src/render/text/fonts/`; spec §3.1 asks for "a curated bundled set (variable fonts, open licenses)". Built as hashed assets, loaded only when a text uses them.

## Batch log

### Batch 00: Setup
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes: created /next-batch command, PROGRESS.md and CLAUDE.md; moved EDITOR_SPEC.md, BATCHES.md, NEXT_BATCH_SETUP.md into docs/; initialized git. Later (after Batch 01): applied docs/AUTO_BATCH_SETUP.md: `auto` mode and step 7b in /next-batch, /review-batch command, `scripts/run-batches.sh`, `needs_human` field.
- deviations:
  - design system path is `DESIGN_SYSTEM.md` (see Global deviations)
  - `scripts/run-batches.sh` differs from AUTO_BATCH_SETUP §3 in three places. (1) Batch numbers are read in base 10, because PROGRESS.md stores "01", which bash would treat as octal (so "08" and "09" would fail). (2) Completion is compared as a number, because "02" != "2" as strings, which would stop the run after the first batch. (3) A run that only applied review fixes is detected by a `batch-NN: fixes` HEAD commit, not by grepping the whole file for `fix-attempt: done`, because that grep matches any earlier batch and would skip the retry and could loop forever.
  - /next-batch Step 3 says outright that `auto` mode does not stop after the plan, so it does not contradict the "Then STOP" line.
  - Added `.gitattributes` (`*.sh text eol=lf`): `core.autocrlf` is true on this machine, and a CRLF checkout would break the script in bash.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 00 -> Batch 01] add `lint` and `check` scripts
- design gaps: none
- pending manual checks: none
- docs: none (development setup only: build commands, scripts and project notes; nothing a user sees, so no user guide page or What's new entry; checked in the Batch 17 docs run, 2026-10-15)

- status: done

ESLint color-literal rule on `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` plus `scripts/check-css-colors.mjs` for stylesheets; only `src/styles/tokens.css` may hold colors. Verified: a hex, a template-literal hex and an `rgba(` in a `.ts` file fail lint; a hex in a `.module.css` fails the CSS check.

  - `FrameScheduler` + `IdleQueue`; keymap with the full §18 table as data (101 command bindings), `KeyDispatcher` (scope first, then global; inputs own their keys; textEditing/dialog isolated; hold keys suppress repeats and dispatch key-up; layout-aware key match with physical-code fallback), `FocusScopeTracker` (`data-scope` on panels).
  - UI primitives: IconButton (24px, tooltip with keymap shortcut), Tooltip (single DOM-driven bubble, no React state), Toggle, Select, static NumberField, ContextMenu shell, `useDismiss`.

  - Unsupported-browser screen checking WebGPU, WebCodecs (6 classes), FSA (2 pickers).
  - Measured: 60 unit tests (9 files). Build 182.8 kB JS (59.7 kB gzip), 13.4 kB CSS. Headless-Chrome run over CDP: layout exact at 1920×1080 (left 1306/1920, preview 614×1040, rows 520/520, media 640) and 1600×900 (1088/1600, 512×860, 430/430, 533); no page overflow; splitter minimums hold at exactly 280/320/300/220/220; positions survive reload; 0 React commits during splitter drags and toolbar hover; toolbar fits with nothing hidden at 1600 (1074px), hides toggles + zoom at 1280; every scope resolution logged correctly; typing in an input fires nothing; Theme values correct and re-read on `data-theme` change; no console errors.

### Batch 02: Document model, time math, commands and undo
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - `core/document`: `types.ts` (§5.2 verbatim + `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, `JsonValue`), `time.ts` (exact integer `frameToUs`/`usToFrame`, `formatTimecode` `mm:ss:ff` / `mm:ss.mmm` with hours only when needed, `parseTimecode` with `+30`, `-15`, `+2.`), `validate.ts` (every §5.2 invariant; `DocumentInvariantError` names track and clip), `project.ts` (`createProject` defaults 1080×1920, 30/1, 48 kHz), `fixtures.ts` (test builders only).
  - `core/commands`: `Command` (`id`, `label`, `coalesceKey?`, `affects`, `run(draft, ctx)`), `CommandContext` (`playhead`, `selection` snapshot, `newId`), `UndoStack` (patches, inverse patches, selection before/after, 300 ms coalescing by key, sealed after undo/redo, limit 1000, labels), `basicCommands.ts` (`track.add`, `track.remove`, `clip.add`, `clip.remove`, `clip.update`), `ActionRegistry` (action id -> handler; keys and buttons both call `run`).
  - `core/store`: `DocumentStore` (`getState`, `dispatch(cmd, {selection?})`, `subscribe(listener, {affects?})`, `transaction(label, fn)` with nested rollback, `undo`/`redo`, `history` store; dev validation after every commit, undo and redo; invalid results throw and leave the document untouched), `SessionStore` (§5.3 shape; `state` and `selection` on separate channels; `setPlayhead`, `setSelection`), `selection.ts` (Set in memory, array snapshots), `useStoreSelector` (`useSyncExternalStore` + memoized `Object.is` bail-out).
  - `timeline/model/queries.ts`: per-track index (start-sorted clips, `maxDuration`, `end`) cached per (clips record, track) so it is rebuilt only for new document versions; `clipAtFrame`, `clipsAtFrame`, `clipsInRange` (binary search bounded by max duration), `neighbors`, `trackGaps`, `gapAt`, `trackEnd`, `contentEnd`, `editPoints`, `next/prevEditPoint`.
  - App: `services.ts` creates `sessionStore`, `documentStore` (empty project) and `actions`; `history.undo`/`history.redo` registered; `KeyDispatcher.onAction` routes registered ids to `actions`, unregistered ids are still logged and consume the key. App bar Undo/Redo are enabled from `documentStore.history`, with "Undo <label>" tooltips.
  - Measured: 149 unit tests in 19 files (+89 tests). 10,000 trivial dispatches with dev validation on: ~62 ms warm when run alone (raw Immer alone is ~39 ms); the test warms up and takes the best of 3 runs because the other test workers compete for the CPU: 83–113 ms best, while single runs during the full parallel suite reached 206–305 ms (limit 300; a single cold run once failed at 305 ms). Fuzz: 3 seeds × 1,000 random raw ops (994–998 real changes each, 38–55 clips on 5–7 tracks), validated after every op, undone to a deep-equal original and redone to a deep-equal final; plus 1,000 interleaved ops/undos/redos. 29.97 round-trips every frame of 1 hour; every frame of 10 minutes round-trips format→parse at 24/29.97/30/60 in both modes; 10:00:00 at 29.97 is frame 17983 (600.033 s, zero drift). Build 205.2 kB JS (67.9 kB gzip; immer adds ~8 kB gzip), 13.4 kB CSS.
  - Headless Chrome (CDP) on the dev server: Undo/Redo start disabled; after a dispatch Undo enables with "Undo Rename project"; Ctrl+Z undoes, Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y redo, button clicks do the same; Ctrl+Z in a text input does not reach the document; no console warnings or errors. Batch 01 regression at 1600×900: no overflow, panels 1088 / 512×860 / 430+430 / media 533, same as Batch 01.
- deviations:
  - Track heights: §5.3 lists them as session state, but §5.2 has `Track.height` in the document and this batch requires §5.2 exactly. Kept only in the document; `SessionState` has no track heights. Splitter positions stay in `layoutStore` (per-project persistence is a Batch 03 follow-up).
  - Undo selection: §6.19 says "restored to what it was after the undone command", which is ambiguous. Undo restores the selection from before the command and redo the one after it (callers pass `{ selection }` to `dispatch` when a command changes it, e.g. delete). A selection made after the command is replaced on undo. Ids that no longer exist are dropped.
  - "Set playhead" and "set selection" are session functions (`setPlayhead`, `setSelection`), not document commands: session state is never undoable (§3.3).
  - Later-batch types (`LabelColor`, `EaseId`, `Gradient`, `Transition`, `TextContent`, `TextStyle`, `TextAnim`, `TextPreset`, `CaptionTrackData`) are opaque JSON placeholders with `TODO(batch NN)`.
  - Functional defaults where the spec is silent: `RGBA` channels 0..1; timecode seconds are wall-clock seconds, so 29.97 shows no drift; plain digits are frames; in frames mode `.` and `:` both separate right-aligned fields, in ms mode `.` starts decimal seconds; undo history limit 1000 steps; new video tracks go above the other video tracks, caption tracks above all video, audio tracks at the end.
  - Validation adds checks implied by the spec: integer frames and microseconds, `start >= 0`, `speed > 0`, media existence, unique track ids, a clip on exactly one track, clip record keys equal ids, video tracks before audio tracks, `captionTracks` keys are caption tracks. Link-group offsets are allowed by §5.2, so they are not errors.
  - `Store.get()` renamed to `getState()` so all stores share the `{ getState, subscribe }` shape `useStoreSelector` reads.
  - The follow-up's "command registry" is `ActionRegistry` (action ids), to keep it apart from document `Command`s.
  - Removed the one-line placeholders `core/commands/index.ts`, `core/document/index.ts`, `timeline/model/index.ts` now that the folders have real modules.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 02 -> Batch 03] `load(project)`, session.json, default tracks, `modifiedAt`; [from Batch 02 -> Batch 07] default `pxPerFrame`; [from Batch 02 -> Batch 16] track selection id, link offset query; [from Batch 02 -> Batch 34] image in/out rule; [from Batch 02 -> Batch 41] offline clips vs media-existence check
- design gaps: none

### Batch 03: Project folder, persistence, start screen
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - `project/`: `fs.ts` (`FsDir`: readFile/writeText/remove/list/dir/permissions; `fromHandle` wraps FSA, one writable stream per write, closed or aborted at once), `memoryFs.ts` (in-memory FsDir used by every project test: logs write attempts before the permission check, injectable failures, monotonic mtimes), `layout.ts` (§9.7 tree, `ensureFolderLayout`), `projectFile.ts` (`serializeProject` stamps `meta.modifiedAt`/`appVersion`; `parseProjectFile` = JSON + shape check + `migrateProject` through the `MIGRATIONS` hook + `validateProject`, always on), `sessionFile.ts` (state, selection snapshot, layout ratios; field-by-field sanitizer, never throws), `history.ts` (names, rotation 20), `saveProject.ts` (tmp, backup of the previous file, real write, delete tmp, rotate), `Autosaver.ts` (1 s debounce, 5 s max wait, single flight, `saveNow`/`flush`/`setReadOnly`/`setDelay`, status store; failures set `error` and log), `SessionSaver.ts` (every 10 s when changed, plus flush), `permissions.ts` (`ensureAccess`: readwrite, asking only from `prompt`, else read, else none; `retry` re-asks denied ones), `recovery.ts`, `openProject.ts` (`openProjectFolder`, `createProject`), `ProjectSession.ts` (loads document + session + per-project layout into the stores; autosave, session saves, recent entry name/modified, flush on `visibilitychange`, `close()`), `recentProjects.ts` + `kv.ts` (idb-keyval), `appSettings.ts` (autosave delay), `projectPresets.ts`.
  - Core: `DocumentStore.load` (validates, clears history and selection, notifies every flag), `layoutStore.setRatios`, `projectCommands.ts` (`project.rename`, `project.setResolution`, `project.setFrameRate`, `project.setBackground`), `color.ts` (hex <-> RGBA), `defaultTracks` (V1 video, A1 audio).
  - App: `projectController.ts` (`appView` store; new/open/recent/drop/retry flows, one at a time; errors end in a dialog; actions `project.save`, `project.new`, `project.open`; any closing dialog hands the keyboard back to the global scope), `StartScreen` (hero New project, Open project, recent list with remove, folder drop with a DOM-attribute highlight), `DialogHost` (new project form, recovery offer, permission retry, errors) on a new `ui/Modal` (dialog focus scope, focus in, back to the trigger on close), `ReadOnlyBanner`, `AppBar` (click-to-rename name, save dot written to the DOM from the status store with hover details and click to save/retry/allow editing, live badge), `ui/Button`, `ui/TextField`. Properties: `ProjectSection` when nothing is selected (name, resolution presets + custom, fps, background hex, autosave delay).
  - Measured: 236 unit tests in 29 files (+87). Build 246.0 kB JS (80.95 kB gzip, +13 kB gzip), 25.1 kB CSS.

Splitter drag: 0 React commits; the dragged layout lands in session.json and comes back on reopen with localStorage cleared.
  - Batch 01/02 regression at 1600x900: panels 1088 / preview 512x860 / 430+430 / media 533, no overflow; Ctrl+K in the timeline still logs `[cmd] edit.split`; undo/redo as above.
- deviations:
  - History backup names are UTC (see Global deviations).
  - Frame rate locked once clips or markers exist; resolution changes are "keep" (see Global deviations).
  - The autosave interval of §11 is a global app setting (IndexedDB `settings.v1`), not document data: §5.2 settings are verbatim and the interval is not per-project. Choices 1/2/5/10 s, default 1 s (§15). It sets the debounce; the max wait is max(5 s, delay).
  - Mod+S always writes (and backs up), even with no changes, so "forces a save" is literal; saves within one second keep distinct backups (`-2`, `-3`).
  - New project: picker first, then a dialog (name prefilled with the folder name, preset, fps), in §15's order. The picked folder becomes the project folder; a folder that already has project.json is refused ("open it instead"). Other files in the folder are left alone.
  - Permission flow: readwrite is requested only when the browser state is `prompt`; when it is not granted the project opens read-only if read access is granted (requested the same way); with no access a dialog offers "Try again", which also re-asks denied permissions. "Allow editing" (banner or dot) re-asks write access.
  - Recovery offer: only when project.json.tmp is newer than project.json, differs from it, and parses and validates. Stale or invalid tmps are deleted (when writable). Escape / X on the offer leaves the tmp alone; the next save replaces it. Restoring loads it as the document (undo history cleared) and autosaves it, so the replaced project.json goes to history/.
  - Recent projects are keyed by project id (a copied folder with the same id replaces the entry), newest first, max 12; the path hint is the folder name (FSA exposes no paths).
  - Opening another project (Mod+N / Mod+O while one is open) reads the new folder first and closes the current one (flushing saves) only when that succeeded.
  - `meta.modifiedAt` / `meta.appVersion` are stamped into the saved file by `serializeProject`, not by a command (no undo step, document untouched).
  - Expected open failures (no project.json, no permission) are logged with `console.info`; unexpected ones with `console.error`.
  - A dropped folder may open read-only if Chrome does not count the drop as a user gesture for the permission prompt; the banner's "Allow editing" then asks with a click.
  - `tsconfig` lib adds `DOM.AsyncIterable` (`FileSystemDirectoryHandle.entries()`).
  - Removed the placeholder `src/project/index.ts`.

Repeat the reopen of step 2 but answer the edit prompt with "Don't allow" (allow viewing if asked): the read-only banner shows and the dot is grey; rename the project and wait 15 s: the files in the folder keep their modified times and history is unchanged. Click "Allow editing", Allow: the banner goes away and the rename is saved.

- review: rejected
  1. `src/ui/TextField.tsx:31-43` (commit mode): Enter calls `commit()` and then `blur()`. The `onBlur` handler (line 59) belongs to the same render and still sees the typed `draft`, so it commits a second time. Escape calls `setDraft(null)` and then `blur()`, so the same stale `onBlur` commits the typed text instead of reverting it. That breaks the component's own contract (lines 5-6) and spec §11 ("Esc reverts"). A jsdom probe confirmed it: type "typed", Enter -> `onCommit` called twice; Escape -> `onCommit("typed")` once. Effects in this batch: in the Project section, Escape in Name (`src/panels/properties/ProjectSection.tsx:70`) or Background (`:89`) renames the project or changes the color instead of reverting. Enter in Background dispatches `setBackground` twice, and each call assigns a new object (`src/core/commands/projectCommands.ts:77`), so one edit leaves two undo steps. A probe confirmed this: after one undo the background still has the new color. That breaks "one command per gesture" (§4 P2). Fix: use a ref to commit at most once and to cancel on Escape, as `ProjectName` already does (`src/app/AppBar.tsx:64,74-79,91-95`). Add a regression test (jsdom + `createElement` in a `.test.ts`).
  2. `src/ui/NumberField.tsx:36-44` has the same defect. The component is from Batch 01, but this batch is the first to wire it to document commands. Escape in the new W/H fields (`src/panels/properties/ProjectSection.tsx:78-79`, Custom resolution) dispatches `setResolution` with the typed value instead of reverting (probe: type 720, Escape -> `onChange(720)`). Enter calls `onChange` twice. Fix it the same way as item 1.
  3. `src/app/projectController.ts:97-110` + `:79-80`: `openDir` reads the folder before `switchTo` -> `closeCurrent()` flushes the open project. Mod+O on the folder that is already open, before the pending autosave has run (likely with the 10 s autosave delay), loads the stale project.json into the editor. The flush writes the newer document, but the editor still shows the stale one, and the next edit's autosave overwrites the newer file (it survives only in history/). Fix: flush the current session (`await current?.flush()`) before `openProjectFolder` in `openDir`.
  - Checked and fine: `npm run check` green (typecheck, lint, 236/236 tests in 29 files, build 245.99 kB JS / 80.95 kB gzip). Each of the 5 acceptance items has unit tests on the in-memory FS plus pending manual checks with steps. Nothing from "Do not build yet" (media copy, templates, fps/resolution conversion) was built. No color/font/size literals outside tokens.css (1px hairline borders, the DS input ring and `strokeWidth={2.5}` on hero/CTA icons are DS values). Every TODO has a `(batch NN)` tag. React is used only for chrome, the save dot and drop highlight are written to the DOM, and no media or canvas work was added.

### Batch 04: Media import, probe, media panel basics, worker infrastructure
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - Workers (§3.5): `media/workers/protocol.ts` (methods `probe`, `poster`, `decodeImage`; request ids; priorities user/playback/prefetch/background; a `cancel` message; errors as name + message; transferables collected from results), `WorkerHost` (worker side: priority queue then arrival order, concurrency 2, an AbortController per request; a cancelled request always answers AbortError and its late result is closed), `WorkerPool` (main side: one worker per kind, created on the first request; abort -> cancel message + immediate AbortError; a crashed worker rejects its requests and is recreated next time), `decode.worker.ts`. Vite builds workers as ES modules.
  - Bytes (§9.2): `ByteSource` (Blob range reads through `slice().arrayBuffer()`, 4 MB chunks, in-flight reads shared, fresh copies returned) over a `ChunkCache` byte-LRU of 64 MB shared by every source of the worker.
  - Probe (§9.1): `mp4Probe.ts` feeds mp4box with 1 MB ranged reads and jumps where mp4box asks, so moov-at-end files never read their mdat. It gives codec strings, the avcC/hvcC/av1C/vpcC description, coded size, rotation from the tkhd matrix (`rotation.ts`), durations from the sample tables through the new `time.ts` `ticksToUs`, fps from the first 120 cts deltas (`fps.ts`: the mode, averaged over its cluster, snapped to the nearest standard rate within 0.03%, else timescale/mode), and the audio codec/channels/rate. `imageProbe.ts` (worker): ImageDecoder for GIF/WebP animations, else createImageBitmap. Main thread: `audioProbe.ts` (`decodeAudioData` on an OfflineAudioContext, for mp3/aac/wav/ogg) and `svgProbe.ts` (size from width/height or the viewBox; poster through a sized canvas). `testMp4.ts` is a test-only MP4 writer (there is no ffmpeg on this machine), used by the unit tests and to wrap real WebCodecs output in the browser checks.
  - Posters: `decode/poster.ts` (worker) decodes the first sync sample with a one-shot VideoDecoder, draws it upright into an OffscreenCanvas (320 px long side) and returns a transferred ImageBitmap plus a WebP blob. `media/PosterStore.ts` follows the document: new media (import, open, undo) get a poster from `cache/thumbs/<id>.poster.webp` (decoded in the worker), or one regenerated and written back (not when read-only); removed media have their bitmap closed; cards observe one id, and (since the review fixes) `PosterPainter` draws them in the FrameScheduler loop.
  - Import: `media/mediaKinds.ts` (the 14 §9.1 extensions, a MIME fallback for pasted images; WebM refused), `project/mediaFiles.ts` (safe, case-insensitive unique names; `copyIntoMedia` streamed with progress and cleaned up on failure; project-relative `resolveFile`/`writeFileAt`/`removeFileAt`), `FsDir.getFile`/`FsDir.writeFile` (`blob.stream()` piped into the writable through a byte counter), `media/MediaImporter.ts` (classify -> refuse -> probe 3 at a time -> copy one at a time -> one `media.add`; imports are serialized; an aborted import removes its copies and adds nothing). Document: `core/commands/mediaCommands.ts` (`media.add`, `media.remove`, `media.rename`, `mediaUseCounts`); `validate.ts` checks MediaItems (integer us and sizes, quarter-turn rotation, positive rational fps, relPath inside the project).
  - `media/AnalysisScheduler.ts` is the IdleQueue consumer: one idle task per media with pending analysis, cancelled when the media goes away, queued again on undo.
  - App: `app/mediaController.ts` handles the per-project importer, posters and analysis, attached by `projectController` and detached before the project closes. It registers the actions `media.import` (Mod+I, header button, empty-state button; `showOpenFilePicker` with multiple), and `edit.delete` / `edit.rename` in the media scope. It handles drops of files and folders (recursive; handles are requested inside the event), image paste, and the refusal/progress/failure toasts, and owns the `mediaPanel` store (selection, inline rename, remove confirmation) and the `posterStore`. Also new: `ui/toasts.ts` + `app/ToastHost.tsx` (the Dynamic Island) and `RemoveMediaDialog` in `DialogHost`.
  - Media panel:
    - Header actions: sort select + direction, grid/list, Import.
    - Grid of cards: poster canvas, duration badge, kind icon, name with double-click/F2 inline rename, and a meta row (display size and fps, or the codec).
    - List view: thumbnail, name, duration with ms, resolution, fps, codec, audio, proxy, used. Click a column to sort, again to reverse.
    - Click / Mod+click / Shift+click selection by event delegation, an empty state, and the drop highlight through a data attribute.
    - The tab, view, sort and direction persist in session.json.
  - Measured: 340 unit tests in 46 files (+93), plus the perf project. Build 280.84 kB JS (92.42 kB gzip, +11.4 kB), 33.2 kB CSS, decode.worker 190.56 kB.
  - Headless Chrome 155 (CDP) on the dev server: OPFS folders stand in for picked folders, and the media are synthesized in the page with WebCodecs (hardware HEVC and H.264 encoders) and wrapped by `testMp4.ts`. 25 + 11 + 3 checks, all passing:
    - iPhone-style .mov (HEVC 1920x1080, rotation-90 matrix, timescale 600, AAC track): 3,000,000 us, 1920x1080, rotation 90, 30/1, hvc1.1.6.L120.b0 + mp4a.40.2. The card shows "1080×1920 · 30 fps"; the poster is upright portrait (180x320) and not black.
    - Android-style .mp4 (H.264 720x1280, 90 kHz, jittered deltas with dropped frames, moov at the end): duration exact to the tick sum (4,166,678 us), 30/1, rotation 0.
    - PNG 640x480 (still, no badge); MP3 (200 hand-made silent frames) 5,224,479 us against 5,224,490 us expected; SVG 200x100 (poster drawn, not cached); animated GIF (3 x 500 ms) 1,500,000 us.
    - Every file is copied byte-exact into media/; 3 WebP posters land in cache/thumbs/; one undo step "Import 5 media files".
    - notes.txt: toast "Can't import notes.txt / Unsupported file type"; no media, no file, no undo step. A drop from disk (CDP drag with real files) imports dropped.png and refuses readme.txt by name. A pasted PNG is imported.
    - Click/Ctrl+click selection; double-click rename + Enter commits; F2 + Escape cancels. Delete opens the "Remove media" dialog with "Remove and delete clips" disabled; Remove from project, then Ctrl+Z restores the media and its poster (from the cache). Delete in the timeline scope does nothing to media. List view columns and the duration sort work; the view persists in session.json.
    - 500 MB import (10 files of 50 MB) took 2.1-15.5 s, with all bytes copied and the progress toast shown. PerformanceObserver "longtask": none in 5 runs. Event-loop lag 12-25 ms (3 runs). requestAnimationFrame gaps were 21-98 ms in 4 runs and 216 ms in one run with no long task (headless frame scheduling while the browser process takes in 500 MB of blob data).
    - Reload: all 15 media come back, cached posters are reused (files untouched), and a deleted poster file is regenerated. Reopening the same project re-attaches the mounted cards to the new poster store.
    - React commits, counted through the DevTools hook: 0 while dragging files over the panel, 0 for poster arrivals, 0 while hovering cards.
    - Batch 01-03 regression at 1600x900: preview 512x860, media 533x430, timeline 1088x430, no overflow. Ctrl+K in the timeline logs `[cmd] edit.split`; Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z undo and redo; project create/open/reload as above. No console errors (apart from the dev server's favicon 404).
- deviations:
  - Delete in the media scope, and analysis "pending" from creation: see Global deviations.
  - SVG is probed and postered on the main thread, because workers cannot parse or rasterize SVG (createImageBitmap refuses SVG blobs). Its poster is not cached (cheap to redraw).
  - Audio-only files that are not MP4 use `decodeAudioData` on the main thread, as the batch asks; the browser decodes off-thread. `.m4a` goes through mp4box in the worker. For decodeAudioData formats the probe reports the source sample rate as 0 (the audio is resampled).
  - `MediaItem.width/height` are the coded size and `rotation` applies on top; the panel shows the displayed size (1080×1920 for an iPhone portrait clip).
  - A video item's `durationUs` comes from its video track (the audio track can differ by a few ms); audio-only items use the audio track.
  - Stills have `durationUs: 0` and no duration badge (Batch 34 decides the still-clip rule).
  - Media names are the original file names, extension included. Rename changes the display name only, never the file.
  - The "Remove media" dialog has its second option disabled and no file-deletion checkbox (Batch 41 lists that checkbox). Media used by clips cannot be removed yet: the command refuses and the dialog disables the button.
  - Media panel selection lives in `mediaPanel` (an app store), not in the SessionStore selection (clips/track/cue), and is not persisted. `SessionState.mediaPanel` gains `view` and `descending`; `MediaSort` gains the list columns (resolution, fps, codec, audio, used).
  - Reference mode is stubbed as the `importMode` app setting (IndexedDB `settings.v1`, no UI); "reference" logs a message and copies.
  - The poster is the first sync sample (the first frame). A poster that fails (e.g. a codec the browser cannot decode) is tried once per session, and the card keeps its kind icon.
  - The progress toast appears for imports of 32 MB or more. Paste imports images only, and only when the media panel or nothing owns the keyboard.
  - Imports run one at a time so two batches never pick the same file name.
  - Perf tests moved to `*.perf.test.ts` in a "perf" Vitest project (node environment, no file parallelism); `npm test` runs the unit project, then the perf project.
  - `Select` gained a compact size for header strips.
  - Removed the placeholders `src/media/{bytes,decode,probe,workers}/index.ts`.

- review: rejected
  1. `src/panels/media/PosterCanvas.tsx:14-25`: each card draws its poster into its visible `<canvas>` directly from the `PosterStore.observe` callback (`drawImage` at line 24), outside the `FrameScheduler`. Spec §3.4 (BINDING) allows nothing to render outside the scheduler loop except React chrome, and `src/core/scheduler/FrameScheduler.ts:1-2` says "Nothing else draws canvases". The Batch 04 notes describe the direct draw ("cards observe one id and draw into their own canvas") but log no deviation. This also matters for later batches: Batch 17 moves cards to sprite sheets and Batch 20 adds hover scrub, which redraws these canvases on every pointer move, and both would build on this pattern. Fix: the observer only marks the card dirty, and a `RenderView` registered with `scheduler` (a new `InvalidationFlag`, e.g. `mediaThumbs`) draws the dirty cards, so a burst of poster arrivals is drawn at most once per frame. Or, if direct drawing is intended, write the deviation and its reason here.
  2. `src/media/workers/WorkerPool.ts:83-85`: when a `result` arrives for an id that is no longer pending (aborted and already rejected by `onAbort`), it is dropped without closing its transferables. The worker closes late results only when the `cancel` message arrives before the handler finishes (`src/media/workers/WorkerHost.ts:166-168`). If the worker has already posted the result, the transferred `ImageBitmap` reaches the main thread and is never closed, which breaks P13 ("Memory of closed resources is explicit"). This can happen today when a project closes while posters are loading (`PosterStore.dispose` -> `controller.abort()`, `src/media/PosterStore.ts:90`). From Batch 06 on, the same path leaks `VideoFrame`s when scrubs cancel frame requests, and unclosed frames stall the decoder. Fix: in `receive`, when there is no pending entry and `message.type === "result"`, call `close()` on every item of `transferablesOf(message.value)` that has one. Add a `WorkerPool.test.ts` case that aborts after the host has posted the result and asserts the value's `close` was called.
  3. `src/media/mediaKinds.ts:46`: `BY_EXTENSION[ext]` is a lookup on a plain object literal, so extensions that name `Object.prototype` members are accepted: `classifyFile("clip.constructor", "")` returns the `Object` function and `classifyFile("clip.__proto__", "")` returns `Object.prototype` instead of null (confirmed with a temporary Vitest probe). Such a file is not refused. `createProbeFile`'s switch (`src/media/MediaImporter.ts:25`) matches no case and returns `undefined`. The importer then copies the file, `mediaItemFrom(undefined, ...)` (line 179) throws a TypeError, and the whole import is rolled back. The user sees "Import failed / Cannot read properties of undefined" instead of a toast that names the file, and the valid files picked or dropped with it are not imported (Acceptance 3). Fix: `Object.hasOwn(BY_EXTENSION, ext)` (or a `Map`), plus a `mediaKinds.test.ts` case for `.constructor` and `.__proto__`.
  - Checked and fine: `npm run check` green (typecheck, lint, 340/340 unit tests in 46 files + 1 perf test, build 280.84 kB JS / 92.42 kB gzip, decode.worker 190.56 kB). Acceptance: (1) `mp4Probe.test.ts` (iPhone-style HEVC .mov with rotation 90, timescale 600 and AAC; Android-style H.264 VFR with the moov at the end and the mdat never read), `fps.test.ts`, `rotation.test.ts`, `mainProbes.test.ts` (mp3 duration from the decoded length), `MediaImporter.test.ts` "copies every file into media/ and adds them in one undo step", plus manual check 1; (2) manual check 2 (Performance panel, no long task over 50 ms), with the CDP measurement in the notes; (3) `MediaImporter.test.ts` "refuses unsupported files by name and creates nothing for them" plus manual check 3 (except the names in item 3); (4) `PosterStore.test.ts` "reads cached posters on reopen and regenerates missing or damaged ones" plus manual check 4. Do not build yet: the sample table stays in memory (no `cache/index/`), only one poster frame is decoded (animated GIF/WebP frames are decoded once for the frame list the Build asks for), and there are no sprite sheets, peaks, proxies, bins, hover scrub or drag to the timeline. Literals: the new CSS holds only 1px hairline borders, the DS input ring, 0/1 opacities, layout percentages and a local z-index of 1/2; the new sizes are tokens in `tokens.css`, logged as design gaps. Every TODO carries a `(batch NN)` tag that names the right batch. §3/§4: probe, posters and cached-poster decoding run in `decode.worker` and return transferred bitmaps; the main-thread `decodeAudioData` is what the batch asks for, and SVG on the main thread is a logged deviation. Durations go through `ticksToUs`, and fps is a rational of integers checked by `validate.ts`. Import, rename and remove are one command each. The drop highlight is a data attribute, and poster arrival does not touch React state.
- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-14)
  1. Posters are drawn in the FrameScheduler loop. New `src/panels/media/PosterPainter.ts` (framework-free `RenderView`, registered once under the new `InvalidationFlag` `mediaThumbs`): `attach(canvas, posters, id)` observes the PosterStore, and each arrival only records the latest bitmap for that canvas and calls `scheduler.invalidate("mediaThumbs")`; `render` draws every dirty canvas once per frame. A bitmap that went away, or was closed before the frame (0 x 0), hides the canvas instead of drawing. `PosterCanvas` only hands its element to the app singleton `posterPainter` (`app/mediaController.ts`). This is also the view Batch 17 (sprite sheets) and Batch 20 (hover scrub) build on (TODOs in the file). `src/panels/media/PosterPainter.test.ts` (5 tests, real `FrameScheduler` with fake frames): nothing is drawn before the frame; three arrivals for two cards give one draw each, with the latest bitmap; an existing poster is drawn on the frame after attach; null and closed bitmaps hide the canvas; detach drops a pending draw; dispose unregisters.
  2. `WorkerPool.receive` closes the transferables of a `result` whose request is no longer pending (aborted or failed), through `closeTransferables`, which moved from `WorkerHost.ts` to `protocol.ts` so both sides share it. `WorkerPool.test.ts` "closes the bitmaps of a result that arrives after its request was aborted": the result is delivered after the abort and the cancel message, and the bitmap's `close` is called once (a stubbed `ImageBitmap` class, since jsdom has none). A late error is ignored.
  3. `mediaKinds.ts`: the extension table is a `Map`, so `constructor`, `__proto__` and the other `Object.prototype` names are unknown types. `mediaKinds.test.ts` (5 prototype names, plus a MIME of "constructor") and `MediaImporter.test.ts` (`clip.constructor` and `clip.__proto__` are refused by name in the refusal list, and `good.mov` from the same pick is imported).

### Batch 05: WebGPU renderer foundation
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - Built across two runs: the first run left `render/gpu` and `mat3`/`drawList` uncommitted (they typechecked and their tests passed); the second run checked them and built the rest.
  - `render/gpu`:
    - `GpuDevice`: one GPUDevice for the app. On loss (including `device.destroy()`) it asks for a new adapter and device at once, then retries after 50/200/500/1000/2000 ms. It reports `generation`, `restores` and `lastRecoveryMs`.
    - `Renderer` interface (`draw(list, target, viewport?)`, `createTexture`, `uploadVideoFrame` (closes the frame), `uploadImageBitmap`, `resize`, canvas/offscreen targets, `readBitmap`, `subscribe` "lost"/"ready").
    - `WebGpuRenderer`: three WGSL pipelines (textured quad with per-layer matrix, opacity, crop UV rect and flips; two-texture dissolve for Batch 36; solid color) with premultiplied alpha blending. One uniform buffer at 256-byte dynamic offsets, a bind group per texture made at upload (none per frame), mip chains rendered level by level (`mipmaps.ts`), a scissor to the frame, the border drawn outside it, and readback with padded rows and un-premultiply (`readback.ts`). Uploads use `copyExternalImageToTexture`. Matrices are 3x3 affine in project px (`render/mat3.ts`); a view uniform maps them to clip space.
  - `media/cache/TexturePool.ts`: a byte-budget LRU keyed `(mediaId, lod, timeUs)`, with `imageKey(mediaId)` for static mipmapped images. Counted pins, `setBudget`, `delete`/`deleteMedia`/`clear`, stats and a change listener. Every texture leaving the pool is destroyed (P13). The default budget is 768 MB (P6). The pool is emptied on device loss.
  - `render/preview/`:
    - `viewport.ts`: Fit letterbox with the margin, edges on whole device px; numeric zoom centered; contain fit; device size.
    - `PreviewView`: the WebGPU canvas at devicePixelRatio. It follows the layout store (preview panel size), session zoom, document `settings`, Theme and DPR (matchMedia), and the renderer's "ready". It renders only on the scheduler's `preview` flag and resizes the backing store inside `render`. `renderFrame()` draws to an offscreen target at project size and reads it back.
    - `PreviewStatsOverlay`: the dev text, written in the scheduler loop.
    - `ImageHarness` (temporary): the image selected in the Media panel is decoded full size in `decode.worker` (`decodeImage`, at most `maxTextureSize`; SVG through `svgPoster` on the main thread), uploaded mipmapped into the pool, pinned, and drawn contained in the project frame. It is uploaded again after a device loss and dropped when its media is removed.
  - `core/theme/cssColor.ts` turns token strings (hex, rgb/rgba) into RGBA for GPU colors. Theme gains `layout.previewMargin`.
  - App:
    - `app/previewController.ts`: GPU, renderer, pool and preview singletons; the harness per open project (attached and detached by `projectController`); `preview.exportFrame`; `__editor` on `globalThis` in dev builds (`__editor.device.destroy()`).
    - `main.tsx` requests the adapter at startup and shows the unsupported screen when there is none (Batch 01 follow-up).
    - Preview header: dev pool budget (768/64 MB) and GPU stats toggle, Zoom select (Fit, 25, 50, 100, 200, 400%), Export frame.
  - Measured:
    - 392 unit tests in 55 files (+52 tests, +9 files), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 310.23 kB JS (102.36 kB gzip, +29.4 kB / +9.9 kB gzip), 33.6 kB CSS; decode.worker unchanged.
  - Headless Chrome 155 (CDP, real AMD RDNA2 adapter) on the dev server: 25 acceptance + 8 regression checks, all passing:
    - Mipmaps: a 4000x3000 PNG (left half a 1 px checkerboard, then black, then white) at Fit (448x336 device px): checker std dev 0 (mean 128), black/white edge 0 intermediate px. The same view without mipmaps (control) has std dev 42.4.
    - Zoom: at 100% the image is 1080 px wide (1:1), std 0 and a crisp edge. At 400% the checker resolves (std 43.8).
    - Letterbox: project background black inside the frame above the image; `--bg-sunken` outside.
    - Resizing: 20-step splitter drag = 20 resize events, 20 renders, 0 React commits. 5 size changes inside one frame = 1 render. Two window resizes = 2 renders. Idle 1 s = 0 renders. At DPR 2 the backing store doubles (1343x1656 for 671.6x828 css px).
    - Device loss: `device.destroy()` twice: new device in 35-39 ms (recovery 18-33 ms), texture re-uploaded by 69-108 ms, image redrawn by 104-184 ms.
    - Pool: 6 images at 768 MB = 137.3 MB. Switching to 64 MB evicts down to 61.0 MB (the evicted 4000x3000 handle reports `valid: false`). Selecting the 61 MB image evicts everything unpinned; switching away evicts it. Five 15.3 MB images keep 4 (61.0 / 64.0 MB). The overlay reads "pool 4 tex · 61.0 / 64.0 MB · pinned 1 · evicted 8".
    - Export path: `renderFrame()` gives a 1080x1920 bitmap with exact black/white/gray pixels, PNG 48 kB.
    - Removing the media clears the preview and drops its texture; undo brings it back. A 1920x1080 project re-fits the image (pillarboxed); undo restores.
    - No adapter (requestAdapter stubbed to null): the unsupported screen names "A WebGPU adapter".
    - Regression: layout at 1600x900 (preview 512x828 body); header has no overflow; import posters; undo/redo of import; the zoom select writes session zoom; reopening the project clears the preview, keeps one canvas, and brings back posters; Ctrl+K in the timeline logs edit.split; no console errors or warnings (apart from Chrome's "powerPreference is ignored on Windows" note and the expected "device lost (destroyed)" warnings).
- deviations:
  - 100% zoom = one project pixel per device pixel (not per CSS px), so 100% is pixel-exact on HiDPI screens; the spec does not say which.
  - The harness draws into the project frame (`settings.width x height`, 1080x1920 by default) rather than a fixed 1080x1920, so it also follows resolution changes.
  - The zoom choice is the session's `preview.zoom` (persisted in session.json), shown as a compact select in the preview header; the frame stays centered (no pan) until Batch 21.
  - Export frame is a header button now (the batch's "dev button"), registered as action `preview.exportFrame` with no shortcut. The save picker opens first so the click's user activation is still valid.
  - Renderer device events are "lost" and "ready" ("ready" on the first device too), so views draw their first frame and re-upload from one event.
  - Images are decoded at most `maxTextureSize` px on the long side (8192 on this device). Larger images are scaled down by the worker.
  - The dev controls (pool budget, stats toggle) and `__editor` exist in dev builds only.
  - CDP's DPR emulation fires no matchMedia change, so the DPR check was paired with a resize. The matchMedia path is a manual check.
  - Removed the placeholders `src/render/gpu/index.ts`, `src/render/preview/index.ts`, `src/media/cache/index.ts`.

(2) One render per resize: pending manual check 2, plus the CDP counts in the notes (20 resize events = 20 renders, 0 renders while idle). `PreviewView` renders only on the scheduler's `preview` flag and resizes the backing store inside `render`. The overlay shows count, bytes, pinned and evicted.

The `Renderer` interface; three WGSL pipelines with premultiplied (one, one-minus-src-alpha) blending; one layer uniform buffer at 256-byte dynamic offsets, with bind groups made when a texture is created (none per frame); `copyExternalImageToTexture` for bitmaps and frames (the frame is closed in `finally`); 3x3 affine matrices with a view uniform to clip space; the offscreen target with padded readback and un-premultiply; the Export frame button.

Nothing decodes or uploads video frames, and there is no text or gizmo. The Zoom select is the batch's "dev dropdown" (a logged deviation: it is a session value and also shows in production builds).

The canvas, the stats text and the harness are framework-free and draw only from `FrameScheduler` flags (`preview`, `previewOverlay`). No document command was added (zoom is session state). Images are decoded in `decode.worker` (SVG on the main thread is the Batch 04 deviation), and the bitmap is closed after upload (P5, P13). Pool keys are integer microseconds (`STILL_US` = -1 for stills). The 768 MB default budget evicts by destroying textures (P6, P13). Panel sizes come from the layout store (P9).

    1. `src/render/preview/PreviewStatsOverlay.ts:28`: the comment says the overlay runs after the preview render it reports. But `FrameScheduler.frame` (`src/core/scheduler/FrameScheduler.ts:59-63`) orders views by the order their flags were invalidated. When a new image loads, `pool.put` (`src/render/preview/ImageHarness.ts:111`) invalidates `previewOverlay` before `draw` -> `setLayers` (line 90) invalidates `preview`. The overlay then writes the old `renders` count and stays one behind until the next invalidation. Dev only; the splitter-drag check is not affected, because only `preview` is dirty there.
    2. `src/render/gpu/WebGpuRenderer.ts:167-168` and `176-177`: when `copyExternalImageToTexture` throws (a closed `ImageBitmap` or `VideoFrame`), the texture just created is neither returned nor destroyed, so its GPU memory waits for garbage collection (P13). No caller can reach this today. `uploadVideoFrame` gets its first caller in Batch 06.
