### Batch 06: Video decode engine and frame delivery
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - Protocol (§3.5): `media/workers/protocol.ts` gains a `progress` message (one request can post several frames before its result), the frame priorities `scrub > playback > idle`, and the methods `openVideo`, `requestFrame`, `prefetch`, `closeVideo` and `decodeStats`. `WorkerHost` handlers get an `emit`; a progress value posted after a cancel is closed in the worker. Frame methods are "immediate": they start at once instead of waiting for the two queued slots, because each session orders its own jobs. `WorkerPool.request` takes `onProgress`, and progress that nobody takes is closed.
  - Sample index (§9.2, §9.1): `media/demux/sampleIndex.ts` holds the track in typed arrays: offsets, sizes, dts, cts, durations and sync flags in decode order. Its lookups are display order (by cts), `frameAt`/`positionAt` (binary search), `keyFor` and `feedUntil`. `keyFor` gives the sync sample to start from, one GOP earlier for a leading picture of an open GOP. `feedUntil` is the last decode index the decoder needs to show a frame (B-frames). `sampleIndexFile.ts` stores it in `cache/index/<mediaId>.bin`: magic, version, the source's size and lastModified, codec, description, size and rotation, then the 8-byte-aligned arrays. A mismatched or damaged file reads as null, and the index is rebuilt.
  - `media/decode/VideoDecodeSession.ts` (worker), one per video and lod:
    - Jobs run one at a time, by priority; a prefetch yields to a frame request.
    - A seek feeds the keyframe straight into the configured decoder (see Global deviations). Chunks go in dts order, outputs are matched by cts timestamp, and frames before the target are not delivered.
    - Sequential requests continue without a seek. A frame request feeds only up to `feedUntil` of its target; a prefetch feeds ahead through its range.
    - Backpressure: at most 8 chunks in flight and `decodeQueueSize` below 4.
    - An 8-frame ring serves backward steps, and callers get clones. The end of the track flushes.
    - A decoder that holds frames longer than the content needs (no output for 100 ms while idle) gets one more chunk each time, remembered for the session, and its ring is freed.
    - Leading pictures decoded without their previous GOP are dropped.
    - A decoder that fails before the session ever produced a frame marks the video undecodable (`UnsupportedCodecError`).
    - Scrubs: the keyframe first when the target is 4 or more frames past it, then the newest decoded frame at most every 33 ms across scrubs, once a scrub has waited 16 ms. A fast drag supersedes every request before it completes, so these are what move on screen.
    - `createVideoDecoder` wraps WebCodecs behind `DecoderLike`; `testDecoder.ts` is the test fake.
  - `media/decode/DecodeSessions.ts` (worker registry):
    - `open` loads the cached index or builds it (handing the bytes back for caching) and checks `VideoDecoder.isConfigSupported`. It answers frame times (display order, us), codec, coded size, rotation, `decodable` and `indexFromCache`.
    - `requestFrame` / `prefetch` map times to frames; `close`; stats.
    - A decoder closes after 10 s without requests, and at most 6 are live (LRU among idle ones). The index stays, so a later request only creates a decoder.
    - Wired into `decode.worker.ts`.
  - `media/decode/FrameService.ts` (main thread), `getFrame(mediaId, timeUs, { lod, priority, signal, onApproximate })`:
    - The time snaps to the frame shown at it, and a pool hit answers without the worker. Otherwise the worker's frame is uploaded and closed at once (P5, P13) and put in the `TexturePool` under `(mediaId, lod, frameTimeUs)`. A cached texture is never replaced, because it may be on screen.
    - Stand-ins: the nearest cached frame (new `TexturePool.nearest`) is shown synchronously, inside the call, then any closer frame from the worker.
    - A scrub supersedes the previous scrub of the same media and cancels its prefetches.
    - At most 4 requests per media at the worker; the rest wait, most urgent first.
    - A worker that lost its sessions is reopened once.
    - `prefetch`, `release(mediaId)`, and `attach(source)` per project (file, read and write of `cache/index/`; read-only projects skip the write).
    - Stats: approximate and exact latency (last, p95 of 64), requests, pool hits, superseded, failed, frames received, open frames.
  - `WebGpuRenderer`: a failed external copy destroys its new texture (Batch 05 follow-up). `uploadVideoFrame` also submits the queue at once, so the decoder gets its picture buffer back sooner (measured 25-35% faster decoding while uploading).
  - Preview:
    - `VideoHarness` (temporary) follows the Media panel selection and opens the video. A native range over the frame positions asks `getFrame` with scrub priority on each input and draws stand-ins, then the exact frame, upright through `rotatedContainTransform` (§9.3: rotation in the layer transform). It pins the shown frame and redraws after a device loss.
    - The bar, readout and status are DOM written by the harness, so React mounts `ScrubBar` once.
    - Unsupported codecs show "Can't decode HEVC (hvc1…) in this browser" and disable the bar.
    - `ImageHarness` and `VideoHarness` share `pickPreviewMedia`, and each clears only its own layers (`PreviewView.clearLayers`).
    - The dev stats overlay adds two lines of decode stats (worker counters polled every 500 ms while shown) and the open VideoFrames on each side.
    - `app/previewController.ts` owns `frameService` and `videoHarness`; media leaving the project release their sessions and textures.
  - Also: `codecLabel` moved to `media/mediaKinds.ts`; `sampleIndexPath`/`INDEX_DIR` in `project/mediaFiles.ts`; the `media/demux/index.ts` placeholder removed; the `TODO(batch 06)` comments retired.
  - Measured:
    - 447 unit tests in 59 files (+55 tests, +4 files), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 323.10 kB JS (106.36 kB gzip, +12.9 / +4.0 kB), 34.64 kB CSS, decode.worker 204.53 kB (+14.0 kB).
  - Headless Chrome 155 (CDP, AMD RDNA2, dev server, OPFS project), test media and results:
    - Media were made in the page with WebCodecs and wrapped by `testMp4.ts`, each frame showing its index as 12 black/white stripes, read back through `renderFrame()`:
      - `uhd60.mp4`: HEVC 3840×2160 at 60 fps timestamps, 360 frames, a keyframe every 60, 63.4 MB ≈ 84 Mbps.
      - `fhd60.mp4`: H.264 1920×1080 60 fps, 360 frames, 1 s GOPs, 24.2 MB.
      - `portrait.mov`: HEVC 1920×1080 30 fps with the rotation-90 matrix.
    - Acceptance 1, 40 random jumps per video. Wrong frames: 0/80. A frame was drawn in the first animation frame in 38/40 per video; the misses are the first scrub of a just-opened video, which waits for its keyframe.

      | Exact latency | 1080p H.264 | 4K HEVC |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | Median | 38 ms | 181 ms |
      | 0-15 frames past a keyframe | 18 ms | 29 ms |
      | 16-40 frames | 38 ms | 206 ms |
      | 41-59 frames | 125 ms | 593 ms |
      | p95 | 170 ms | 679 ms |

    - Acceptance 1, drags with one bar move per animation frame:

      | Animation frames with a new image | 1080p | 4K |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | Forward, 1 frame per move | 181/181 | 145/181 (max 4 frames without one) |
      | Forward, 3 frames per move | 59/60 | 15/60 (max 8; the decoder runs at ~100 fps) |
      | Backward, 1 frame per move | 82/110 (max 2) | 50/110 (max 11) |
      | Backward, 4 frames per move | 32/61 (max 5) | 23/61 (max 8) |

      The exact frame came 0-28 ms (1080p) and 0-168 ms (4K) after the drag stopped. React commits during drags: 0.
    - Seek cost by GOP depth, through `FrameService`, empty pool, at depths 0/10/30/59: 1080p 39/34/88/169 ms, 4K 40/116/298/580 ms. Raw hardware decode is ~2.4-3 ms per 1080p frame and ~9.6-11 per 4K frame.
    - Acceptance 2: frames 0..300 through `getFrame` (playback) cost 1 seek per video, with every frame time right and 0 open frames on the main thread.
    - Acceptance 3: 18 switches among the three videos (with scrubs) kept at most 3 live decoders, 0 open VideoFrames on the main thread, and 24 in the worker (three rings). 10.6 s after the last request: 0 decoders and 0 open frames on both sides (5 idle closes).
    - Acceptance 4: with `cache/index/` emptied, the first opens parsed 3 and wrote 3 `.bin` files (12 KB for 360 frames). After a reload, all 3 came from the cache, 0 parsed, and the three opens took 17 ms together.
    - Rotation: `portrait.mov` fills the 1080×1920 frame turned clockwise (coded top-left at the top-right), and frames 0, 45, 119 and 31 are exact.
    - Unsupported: Chrome's `isConfigSupported` accepts a 16000×16000 HEVC and a 4:4:4 RExt HEVC; their decoders refuse at configure. The bar shows the danger chip "Can't decode HEVC (hvc1.1.6.L153.90) in this browser" and disables itself, and later requests fail at once with no decoding.
    - Device loss (`device.destroy()`) with frame 100 on screen: redrawn exactly 64 ms later.
    - Regression:
      - Image and video selections take turns (the bar hides for images).
      - Removing media closes its sessions (3 → 2), and undo brings them back.
      - Posters for imports.
      - No header overflow at 1584×749.
      - Only the expected console warnings (device lost; no poster for the undecodable files).
- deviations:
  - The sample index from the moov tables, and seeks with no flush or reset: see Global deviations.
  - Acceptance 1 is met only near a keyframe. Exact frames within 80 ms (1080p) / 120 ms (4K) hold for forward drags, ring and pool hits, and targets up to ~25 (1080p) / ~10 (4K) frames past a keyframe; the medians are 38 ms and 181 ms. Deeper in a 1 s GOP the time is the hardware decode rate times the distance (about 10 ms per 4K frame here). Stand-ins (the nearest cached frame within the same call, the keyframe, then the newest decoded frame) keep the picture moving meanwhile. Proxies (Batch 24, P8's "50 ms on proxy") are the spec's answer; follow-up added.
  - Progressive delivery goes beyond "keyframe first, then exact": the nearest cached frame is shown at once (P8 wording), and a scrub waiting longer than 16 ms also gets the newest decoded frame, every 33 ms at most.
  - Class cancellation is done in `FrameService` (superseded scrub, prefetches of the scrubbed media) with the per-request cancel messages, not with a group cancel in `WorkerHost`/`WorkerPool`.
  - Frame methods skip the worker's two-slot queue ("immediate"); each session orders its jobs by priority.
  - At most 6 live decoders (the spec gives no number), and the decoder idle close is 10 s (P13).
  - A decoder that fails before any frame came out marks the video undecodable, because `isConfigSupported` alone missed real refusals. The error badge is in the preview's dev bar, not on media cards.
  - A frame request feeds only what its target needs; the spec's "at most 8 in flight" and `decodeQueueSize` limits apply on top.
  - `uploadVideoFrame` submits an empty command list after the copy (see notes).
  - `lod: "proxy"` is accepted end to end, but there are no proxy files yet (the frame source returns null).
  - The dev scrub bar shows in production builds too, like the Batch 05 image harness: it is the only way to see video before Batch 08.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 06 -> Batch 08] remove the video harness and scrub bar, the compositor uses FrameService, edit lists / media time; [from Batch 06 -> Batch 09] upload cost (recycle textures, importExternalTexture), prefetch windows, decoder pool stalls; [from Batch 06 -> Batch 24] deep-GOP latency needs proxies, proxy frame source and index name; [from Batch 06 -> Batch 41] frame source for referenced media
- design gaps: dev scrub bar, `--danger-wash`, stats overlay width (details in Design system gaps)

The scrub bar appears at the bottom of the preview. Drag it: the picture follows (stand-ins first), the status ends on "exact N ms", and a portrait iPhone clip is upright. Note the "frame ms" line: approx stays near 0; exact depends on how far the frame is from a keyframe (phone GOPs are often 1-2 s).

The stats "seeks" line grows by 1.

  4. Reload the page and reopen the project. Select a video: the stats read "index cached 1 parsed 0" (the first open after an import reads "parsed"). In Explorer, `cache/index/` holds one `.bin` per opened video.
  5. React DevTools Profiler, recording while dragging the scrub bar: no commits.
  6. A video your machine cannot decode (e.g. HEVC on a PC without HEVC decoding): the bar shows the red "Can't decode HEVC (…) in this browser" chip and is disabled.

- review: rejected
  1. `src/media/decode/VideoDecodeSession.ts:143` and `:350-356`: false stalls, and their effect never wears off.
     - The stall check is `now - lastProgressAt >= STALL_MS`. Only `output` sets `lastProgressAt` (line 452). It starts at 0, and `seek()` and `feed()` never reset it when the decoder gets new work.
     - Scrub jobs always wait in 16 ms slices (`APPROXIMATE_WAIT_MS`, line 350), because `DecodeSessions.requestFrame` always passes an approximate callback.
     - So a scrub that comes more than 100 ms after the session's last output counts a stall as soon as the decoder spends 16 ms on a frame with an empty queue. That covers the first scrub of a video and any scrub after a pause in a drag. A 4K HEVC keyframe, or the first frame of a new decoder, takes that long.
     - Each false stall frees the backward-step ring (line 355) and adds 1 to `extraInFlight` for the rest of the session (line 354). From then on, `canFeed` (lines 402-403) lets every frame request feed one more chunk past its target and keep one more chunk in flight.
     - Seeks reuse the running decoder without a flush (Global deviations), so the next seek first waits for every extra chunk: about 10 ms per frame at 4K. Exact-frame latency (Acceptance 1) gets worse the longer the editor is used, and the measurements in the notes come from short sessions.
     - Confirmed with a temporary Vitest probe. The probe decoder took chunks at once and gave its first output 17 ms later, with the clock at 5 s. It showed `stalls: 1` after the first scrub and `stalls: 2` after a second scrub 2 s later. A later playback request to frame 200 then fed 2 chunks past its target.
     - Fix: count a stall only after STALL_MS without output since the decoder last got work. Set `lastProgressAt = now()` in `seek()` and after each `decoder.decode()` in `feed()`, or measure from the start of the wait. Add that probe as a test, asserting `stalls: 0` and no chunk fed past the target.
  2. `src/media/decode/VideoDecodeSession.ts:361-363`: `canContinue` can return the wrong frame, marked exact.
     - It checks that the target's start key was fed (`key <= nextFeed`) and that the target is still pending. It does not check that the current run started at or before that key.
     - A leading picture of an open GOP (RASL after a CRA, the `keyFor` case at `src/media/demux/sampleIndex.ts:131-135`) is in flight in a run that started at its own CRA. `canContinue` continues that run instead of seeking from the earlier GOP. `output` then drops the picture as a leading picture (lines 458-464). The loop returns `nearest()` (line 340), which is the CRA or the frame after it, and `src/media/decode/DecodeSessions.ts:113` marks it `approximate: false`.
     - How it happens: in a backward drag across a CRA, one move seeks to the CRA's GOP, and the next move lands on a RASL frame while it is still decoding.
     - Confirmed with a temporary probe on the open-GOP index of `VideoDecodeSession.test.ts`, extended to 12 samples so the track does not end (I0 P2 B1 | CRA4 RASL3 P5 P6...). A scrub to position 5 was aborted, then a scrub to position 3 asked for 100000 us. It got the frame at 133333 us (the CRA), with 1 seek in total.
     - This breaks the batch goal ("frame-accurate") and the contract of `FrameResult.timeUs`, "at or before the requested time" (`src/media/decode/FrameService.ts:37`). The dev bar shows the wrong frame as "exact". x265 encodes open GOPs by default.
     - Fix: in `canContinue`, also require `timeUs >= this.runStartUs` (a leading picture of the current run cannot be reached by continuing), so the request seeks from `keyFor`'s earlier GOP. Add the probe as a test. Also mark a delivered frame whose timestamp is not the target as approximate in `DecodeSessions.requestFrame`; the `nearest()` fallback for frames the decoder drops reaches the same path.
  - Checked and fine:
    - Evidence: `npm run check` is green (typecheck, lint, 447/447 unit tests in 59 files plus the perf test, build 323.10 kB JS / 106.36 kB gzip, decode.worker 204.53 kB). Both probes were deleted and none of the reviewed files changed.
    - Acceptance 1 is not met as written. Only targets up to about 25 (1080p) / 10 (4K) frames past a keyframe are within 80/120 ms: medians 38/181 ms, p95 170/679 ms. The deviation is written down with its reason: the raw hardware decode rate (about 10 ms per 4K frame) times up to 59 frames in 1 s GOPs, which Batch 24's proxies address. It is accepted as written, apart from item 1, which makes it worse. Stand-ins are covered by `VideoDecodeSession.test.ts` (keyframe first, newest decoded frame while waiting, stand-ins during a superseding drag), by `FrameService.test.ts` ("shows the nearest cached frame at once, then a closer keyframe, then the exact frame"), and by manual check 1.
    - Acceptance 2: `VideoDecodeSession.test.ts` "decodes frames 0..300 in order with a single seek", plus manual check 2.
    - Acceptance 3: `DecodeSessions.test.ts` "switching between three videos keeps three decoders and leaks no frames" and "closes a decoder and its frames after 10 s without requests", plus manual check 3.
    - Acceptance 4: `DecodeSessions.test.ts` (cache hit on the second open, re-parse on a size/lastModified mismatch), `sampleIndex.test.ts` (file round trip, damaged or foreign files read as null), `FrameService.test.ts` (no write-back of a cached index), plus manual check 4.
    - Build list: everything is present. The sample index and `cache/index/<id>.bin` are built from the moov tables (a deviation with a sound reason). `VideoDecodeSession` covers configure from the description, the sync-sample seek, drop-until-target, sequential continuation, the `decodeQueueSize` and in-flight limits, the 8-frame ring and rotation as metadata. `DecodeSessions` adds the idle close and the LRU cap. `FrameService` covers `getFrame`, `prefetch`, `scrub > playback > idle`, superseding, 4 requests per media and stats. Also present: progressive delivery, the dev scrub bar, `isConfigSupported` and the error chip.
    - Do not build yet: no compositor, playback clock or audio. `lod: "proxy"` passes through, but the frame source returns null (`TODO(batch 24)`).
    - Literals: the new CSS uses tokens only, plus the DS 1px hairline border, a local z-index of 1 and `max-width: 50%`. `--danger-wash` lives in `tokens.css` (a logged gap). The new TS has no color or size literals.
    - §3/§4 (React and drawing): React mounts `ScrubBar` once, and the harness writes the bar's DOM. The preview draws only through `setLayers` and the `preview` flag, and the stats only through `previewOverlay`. No document command was added.
    - §3/§4 (time and media): times are integer microseconds (`ticksToUs` rounds). Demux and decode run in `decode.worker`, and the main thread passes only a `File` or `Blob` there. `FrameService.receive` closes every `VideoFrame` in `finally`, and `WorkerPool` closes progress nobody takes and late results.
    - TODOs: every new TODO has a `(batch NN)` tag (08, 24, 41), and the `TODO(batch 06)` comments are gone.
  - Observation (not blocking): `FrameService.release` (`src/media/decode/FrameService.ts:235`) sends `closeVideo` while an `openVideo` for the same media may still be running in the worker. `openVideo` has no signal (line 274) and starts at once. The worker's close then finds nothing, and the open registers its session afterwards (`src/media/decode/DecodeSessions.ts:99-100`). That session (index, `File`, `ByteSource`; no decoder or frames) stays until the worker dies. This happens when media is removed, or the project closes, while a video is still opening. Pass a signal to the open, or have the worker refuse to register media that was closed while it opened.
- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-14)
  1. False stalls (`VideoDecodeSession.ts`): `seek()` and every `decoder.decode()` in `feed()` now set `lastProgressAt` too, so a stall counts only after STALL_MS without output since the decoder last got a chunk or gave a frame, with its queue empty. Test "counts no stall while the decoder works on chunks it was just given": a fake decoder that takes chunks at once and outputs each frame 17 ms later (new `latencyMs` option in `testDecoder.ts`), clock at 5 s, scrubs to frames 10 and 50 two seconds apart, then playback to 200. Result: `stalls: 0`, `seeks: 3`, and the last chunk fed is each request's target. On the old code it fails after the first scrub (a chunk fed past frame 10).
  2. Open-GOP leading pictures:
     - `canContinue` also requires `timeUs >= runStartUs`, so a leading picture of the run's own sync sample seeks from `keyFor`'s earlier GOP.
     - That seek leaves the old run's copy of the picture in the decoder, decoded without its references. It comes out first and would land in the ring under the right timestamp, so the fix in `canContinue` alone still returned a broken frame as exact. `seek()` now records the leading pictures still pending in the superseded run (`staleLeading`, timestamp -> copies), and `output` drops their first copy. Flushes, decoder errors and closes clear the record. A decoder that skips such a picture instead costs one later copy of it: that request gets the nearest frame, marked approximate, and the next one seeks again.
     - `DecodeSessions.requestFrame` marks a result whose timestamp is not the target's as `approximate: true` (the `nearest()` fallback). `FrameService` passes it on and records no exact-latency sample for it. The `FrameResult.timeUs` and `DeliveredFrame.approximate` docs say so, and the dev bar shows "nearest N ms" in the approx style instead of "exact".
     - The fake decoder now models open GOPs: with `references` + `decodeOrder`, a leading picture comes out `corrupt` when its reference was not fed in the same unbroken run, and a key chunk first outputs the frames held before it. Test "seeks from the GOP before for a leading picture in flight, and drops the copy decoded without it" is the reviewer's probe (I0 P2 B1 | CRA4 RASL3 P5..P11, a scrub to 5 superseded while CRA4/RASL3/P5 are in the decoder, then a scrub to 3). Result: the frame at 100000 us, not corrupt, 2 seeks. Old code: 133333 us (the CRA) with 1 seek. With only the `canContinue` change: the corrupt copy.
     - Also tested: `DecodeSessions.test.ts` "marks the nearest frame approximate when the decoder cannot produce the one asked for", `FrameService.test.ts` "passes on a frame the decoder could only approximate, with no exact-latency sample".
  3. The observation (an open racing a close), fixed on both sides:
     - Worker: `DecodeSessions.close` marks the opens under way for that media, and they end with an AbortError instead of registering. Replacing an entry inside `open` uses a private `remove`, so it marks no other open. Test "drops a video closed while it opens, and keeps the others".
     - Main thread: the race had a second window. `FrameService.open` reads the file and the cached index before it posts `openVideo`, so a `release` at that point posted `closeVideo` first and the open after it. Each open now has an AbortController that `release` aborts: an open still reading never posts, and one at the worker is cancelled (the signal goes with the request). Test "cancels the open of a media released while it opens".
  - Evidence:
    - The 6 new tests fail on the old sources, with the review's symptoms, and pass with the fixes.
    - `npm run check` green: typecheck, lint, 453/453 unit tests in 59 files (+6) plus the perf test, build 323.40 kB JS (106.45 kB gzip), decode.worker 205.39 kB.
  - Headless Chrome 155 (CDP, dev server, AMD RDNA2):
    - Clips were encoded in the page with WebCodecs and wrapped by `testMp4.ts`: 240 frames at 60 fps, 1 s GOPs. This Chrome's encoder refused 4K HEVC, so the HEVC clip is 1080p.
    - A FrameService on the real decode worker did 40 random scrubs per clip with a 150 ms pause after each (the false-stall trigger), then frames 0..239 in order on a fresh session:

      | | 1080p H.264 (25 Mbps) | 1080p HEVC (40 Mbps) | 4K H.264 (80 Mbps) |
      | --- | --- | --- | --- |
      | Scrubs not exact | 0/40 | 0/40 | 0/40 |
      | Exact median / p95 | 24 / 118 ms | 34 / 120 ms | 34 / 319 ms |
      | Stalls during scrubs | 0 | 0 | 0 |
      | In order: wrong frames, seeks | 0, 1 | 0, 1 | 0, 1 |
      | Open VideoFrames after | 0 | 0 | 0 |

    - Earlier runs of the same script counted 0-2 stalls in the 1080p H.264 scrubs and 1-3 at the start of in-order runs. Those were real holds: no frame for 100 ms after the decoder's last chunk.
    - The same script on the old sources also gave 0 stalls during scrubs. These decoders output a run's first frame within the 16 ms slice, so the false stall (which needs a slower first frame, such as 4K HEVC at 84 Mbps) does not show on this machine. The unit test is the reproduction, and the browser run is a regression check.
  - Acceptance 1-4 re-run and unchanged: `VideoDecodeSession.test.ts` (0..300 with one seek, stand-ins, ring, bounds), `DecodeSessions.test.ts` (three videos, idle close after 10 s, index cache hit and re-parse), `FrameService.test.ts` (48 tests in `src/media/decode`) and `src/media/demux/sampleIndex.test.ts` all pass. Pending manual checks 1-7 above still apply. In check 1, the status can also end on "nearest N ms" for a frame the decoder could not produce.
  - follow-ups: [from Batch 06 -> Batch 08] a `getFrame` result can be approximate (the nearest frame the decoder could produce)
- review: accepted (re-review after the fixes, 2026-10-14)
  - Evidence: `npm run check` green on the first run (typecheck, lint, 453/453 unit tests in 59 files plus the perf test, build 323.40 kB JS / 106.45 kB gzip, decode.worker 205.39 kB). I put the pre-fix `VideoDecodeSession.ts`, `DecodeSessions.ts` and `FrameService.ts` back and kept the new tests and `testDecoder.ts`. The 6 new tests failed and the other 42 in `src/media/decode` passed. All 48 pass with the fixes, and the sources were restored to HEAD.
  - Review items 1 and 2 and the observation are resolved:
    1. False stalls: `seek()` (`src/media/decode/VideoDecodeSession.ts:392`) and every chunk in `feed()` (line 447) now set `lastProgressAt`. So the stall check (line 360) needs 100 ms with no chunk and no frame, with the decoder's queue empty. Real holds still count. The test "counts no stall while the decoder works on chunks it was just given" gives 0 stalls and 3 seeks, and no chunk is fed past a target.
    2. Leading pictures:
       - `canContinue` requires `timeUs >= runStartUs` (line 378). A leading picture of the run's own sync sample now seeks from `keyFor`'s earlier GOP.
       - `seek()` records the superseded run's leading pictures that are still pending (line 387). `output` drops their first copy and closes it (lines 477-486).
       - `DecodeSessions.requestFrame` marks a result that is not the requested frame as approximate (`src/media/decode/DecodeSessions.ts:92`). `FrameService` records no exact-latency sample for it (`src/media/decode/FrameService.ts:338`), and the dev bar shows "nearest N ms" in the approx style (`src/render/preview/VideoHarness.ts:155`).
       - The open-GOP test is the review's probe. It returns the frame at 100000 us, not corrupt, after 2 seeks. A decoder that skips a recorded leading picture costs one later copy of it, which is returned as approximate. The fix notes say so.
    3. The open/close race:
       - Worker side: `DecodeSessions.close` marks the opens under way, and `load` throws an AbortError instead of registering (`DecodeSessions.ts:143`).
       - Main thread: `FrameService.release` aborts the open (`FrameService.ts:242`). An open still reading files never posts, and an open already at the worker is cancelled through its signal (lines 284-285).
       - All decode methods are immediate and run in arrival order, so removing media and undoing the removal still opens it again.
  - Acceptance:
    1. Stand-ins are covered by `VideoDecodeSession.test.ts`, `FrameService.test.ts` and manual check 1. The latency deviation is accepted as in the first review: it is written down with its reason (the hardware decode rate; proxies come in Batch 24). The fix removes the false-stall slowdown that the first review held against it.
    2. "decodes frames 0..300 in order with a single seek", plus manual check 2.
    3. `DecodeSessions.test.ts`: three videos, and the idle close after 10 s. Plus manual check 3.
    4. The index cache-hit and re-parse tests and the `sampleIndex.test.ts` round trip, plus manual check 4.
    - Pending manual checks 1-7 have exact steps.
  - Do not build yet: the fix adds no compositor, playback clock, audio or proxy code. It changes only `media/decode/*`, a doc comment in `workers/protocol.ts` and the status text in `VideoHarness.ts`.
  - Literals: the fix adds no CSS and no color, font or size literal. The batch CSS still uses only tokens, the DS 1px hairline, a local z-index of 1 and `max-width: 50%`.
  - §3/§4: decoding stays in `decode.worker`. The harness writes the status to the DOM, with no React commit. Times stay integer microseconds, and dropped stale copies are closed at once (P13). Every TODO in `src/` has a `(batch NN)` tag: a grep finds none without one.
  - Observation (not blocking, not reproduced): `output` drops a leading picture of the current run only while its timestamp is still pending (`VideoDecodeSession.ts:487-497`). Suppose a superseded run outputs a frame whose timestamp is also pending in the new run. That output prunes every pending entry at or before it (line 489). A leading picture of the new run that comes out afterwards then lands in the ring as a valid frame. Two conditions make this possible: GOPs shorter than the 8-chunk in-flight window, or a re-seek to the same sync sample after a stall freed the ring. Decoders that output in POC order mostly emit the new run's earlier pictures first. Follow-up added for Batch 09.

### Batch 07: Timeline canvas foundation
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - `src/timeline/view/` (framework-free):
    - `geometry.ts`: `TimelineGeometry`. Header column, ruler, track area, rows anchored at the video/audio divider (video and caption tracks stack up from it, audio down; the caption track is always S), the "+ track" rows, `frameToX`/`xToFrame` (§6.2), the visible frame range, a binary-search `rowAt`, and the `scrollY` range.
    - `zoom.ts`: Fit (content + 2 s, at least 1 s), limits (Fit down to 0.01 px/frame, up to 400), `zoomAt` keeping a frame under an x, key zoom anchored at the visible playhead else the center (x1.25 / x0.8), scroll clamps (never before 0, at most 25% of the view past the content end), the logarithmic slider mapping, wheel delta normalization (one notch = one key step, pinch deltas smooth).
    - `ruler.ts`: levels 1/5/10 frames, 1/2/5/10/30 s, 1/5 min (plus 10/30 min and 1 h for long timelines at 0.01 px/frame). Second ticks sit on wall-clock seconds and frame ticks restart each second (29.97 matches the timecodes). Second labels are placed first; frame numbers fill the gaps only where they fit, and whole timecodes appear once the ticks are far enough apart. A label that would touch the previous one is skipped, so labels never overlap. Minor ticks subdivide at >= 8 px.
    - `clipLayout.ts`: device-pixel spans, a 1 px separator when a clip abuts the next, sub-pixel clips 1 device px wide, LOD (< 6 px body color only, < 24 px no icon or name, spec §6.4).
    - `HitTester.ts`: the full §6.18 type list as a flat result (`type`, `frame`, `trackId`, `clipId`, `addTrack`). Real logic for `clip`, `ruler`, `playheadHead` (head width + 2 px), `trackHeader` (rows and the "+ track" rows) and `empty`; the other types carry `TODO(batch NN)`.
    - `textCache.ts`: label widths measured once per font and string, plus ellipsized names per width, capped at 4096 entries. `glyphs.ts`: lucide path data (Film, AudioLines, Image, Type, Square, Captions, Plus) stroked as `Path2D`.
    - `drawContent.ts` (sunken well, header column, rows, divider, clips, content end marker, header names and glyphs, "+ track" rows, ruler) and `drawOverlay.ts` (clip and "+ track" hover, playhead line and pentagon head; 2 px while dragged).
    - Clips: rounded body, label bar (whole clip on S tracks), glyph and ellipsized name. Labels stick to the left edge of the track area when the clip starts off screen. Selected clips have an accent outline (primary +1 device px) and an accent-wash body.
    - `TimelineView.ts`: two stacked canvases at devicePixelRatio, backing stores sized inside `render`. It registers `timelineContent` and `timelineOverlay`. Subscriptions:
      - document `affects` timeline/clips/tracks/markers/captions/settings → both canvases (retires the FrameScheduler `TODO(batch 02)`)
      - session `timeline` (zoom/scroll) → both; `playhead` → overlay only; selection → content only
      - panel size, theme and DPR (shared `core/scheduler/devicePixelRatio.ts`, also used by `PreviewView`) → both
    - Input on the overlay canvas:
      - Ruler pointer down scrubs with pointer capture (no snapping, `TODO(batch 12)`).
      - Clip click selects, Shift+click toggles (`clickClipSelection` in `core/store/selection.ts`); a click on empty track area clears the selection. "+ Video track" / "+ Audio track" dispatch `track.add`.
      - Hover invalidates the overlay only when the target changes; the cursor is written only when it changes.
      - Wheel: Ctrl/Meta/Alt (and pinch) zoom at the cursor, Shift+wheel and deltaX scroll horizontally, plain wheel scrolls tracks vertically.
    - The toolbar slider draws an ephemeral zoom while dragged and writes the session once on release. Dev stats (draw counts, clips drawn / total, ms).
  - `src/timeline/edit/insertMedia.ts`: `planMediaInsert` / `insertCommands` (pure).
    - Natural durations: whole media frames, rounded down so `out <= durationUs`; images 5 s.
    - Targets: the lowest targeted, unlocked video track (V1) and the first targeted, unlocked audio track (A1). Missing ones are created with the next free name.
    - Video with audio: the video and audio clips share a new `linkGroup`. Audio-only media: one audio clip.
    - Placement: the earliest start >= playhead that is free on every target track.
    - `app/timelineController.ts` runs a plan as one `transaction("Insert clip")` and moves the playhead to the clip end. Double-click on a media card or list row inserts; double-click on the name still renames (`MediaName` stops the event).
  - `app/timelineController.ts`:
    - The `timelineView` singleton.
    - Actions: `timeline.zoomIn/zoomOut/zoomFit`; `transport.stepBack/Forward` (1, 5, 1 s), hold-to-repeat after 300 ms every 33 ms (the dispatcher suppresses OS repeats for held keys); `transport.goToStart/goToEnd` (content end); `timeline.prev/nextEditPoint` (clip starts and ends on unlocked tracks, markers, in/out: `navigationPoints`); `timeline.prev/nextMarker` (`markerFrames`).
    - Direct-DOM toolbar bindings: the zoom slider position, the editable timecode field (Enter jumps, relative forms through `parseTimecode`, Escape cancels, select-all on focus) and the total duration.
    - Dev: action `dev.generateTimeline`, `__editor.timeline.generate(clips, tracks)` (solids on new video tracks, audio clips on two new audio tracks when the project has media with sound, one undo step; tree-shaken from production) and the Timeline stats toggle (gauge) in the toolbar.
  - Toolbar: zoom -, slider, zoom +, Fit enabled; Zoom to selection and Track height stay disabled (Batches 18 and 16). `TimelinePanel` mounts the view once (the placeholder is gone).
  - Core:
    - `time.ts` exports `secondOfFrame`, `firstFrameOfSecond`, `secondsToFrames` and `usToFrameFloor`.
    - `project.ts` exports `emptyTrack` and `nextTrackName` (V1, A1, CC1, skipping names in use).
    - `queries.ts` adds `markerFrames` and `navigationPoints`.
    - `validate.ts` lets image clips outlast the still (Global deviations).
    - Session `scrollY` may be negative.
    - The Theme gains `space` (`--space-0..6`) and `timeline` (metrics, playhead, clip hover, per-kind clip colors). `__editor` is merged instead of replaced.

A double-click on a name opened the rename field and inserted nothing. Ctrl+Z three times left 4, 2, then 0 clips; Ctrl+Y three times restored them.

- deviations:
  - Image clips can outlast the still; zoom limits apply on user actions only; divider-relative `scrollY` (see Global deviations).
  - "If the spot is occupied, place it at the next free gap end": read as the earliest start >= playhead where the whole clip fits on every target track (the video and its linked audio stay aligned, nothing is overwritten).
  - Audio-only media insert one audio clip (no video clip). Inserted clips are not selected; the selection stays as it was.
  - The divider rests at half the track area (§6.2 only says it stays put); vertical scroll moves both halves together.
  - Ruler levels beyond 5 min (10 min, 30 min, 1 h) so labels keep fitting at 0.01 px/frame on long timelines, and whole timecodes on frame ticks once they fit (§6.3 asks for frame numbers between seconds; at 90+ px/frame no second boundary may be in view).
  - Clip labels stick to the visible left edge (not in the spec; long clips starting off screen would otherwise show no name).
  - Clip hover uses a 7% white wash (`--clip-hover-wash`) instead of the DS menu wash, which is invisible on clip colors.
  - The slider previews the zoom on the canvases while dragged and writes the session on release (the implementation note's "dispatches once on release"); the slider blurs on release so arrow keys go back to frame stepping.
  - Shift+Up/Down marker navigation is wired (markers can only be created from Batch 19); Up/Down include markers and in/out as §6.5 says.
  - `HitResult` is one flat struct with nullable ids instead of a union per type.

Wheel zoom and scroll (`onWheel`), document changes (insert, undo, "+ track") and the end of a scrub never redo the hit test. `renderOverlay` (lines 416-417) and `updateCursor` (line 352) keep using the old hit.
     - Wheel or trackpad scroll: the highlight moves away with the old clip, and the clip now under the pointer gets none. This is the 500-clip scroll check of Acceptance 1. Temporary Vitest probe: 50 clips of 10 frames, hover at frame 5 (`c0`), one Shift+wheel of 40 px. `scrollFrame` went to 10 and the pointer was over `c1`, but the hover stayed on `c0`.
     - "+ Video track": the new track pushes the "+" row up by one track height. The wash goes up with the row while the pointer rests on the new track's header, still with the `pointer` cursor. This happens on every click. Probe: after the click the fresh hit at (20, 150) was `trackHeader` with `addTrack: null`, but the view still held `addTrack: "video"`.
     - Spec §6.18: "The cursor is set from the hover hit each overlay frame." §0 "Premium": every state has hover feedback.
     - Fix: keep the last pointer position (cleared on `pointerleave`). Re-run `hitTester.hit` at it whenever the viewport, the tracks or the clips change and when a gesture ends; the cheapest place is the start of `renderOverlay`, or right after `writeViewport` and in the document subscription. Then invalidate and set the cursor through `setHover` as now. Add a `TimelineView.test.ts` case for wheel scroll and "+ track" under a still pointer.
  2. `src/timeline/view/drawContent.ts:115-117`: clips under 6 px are filled with the label-bar color (`colors.label`). §6.4 says "the body color still shows", and the batch notes (line 589) say "< 6 px body color only". Either use `colors.body` or write the choice down as a deviation with its reason (the stronger color keeps 1 px clips visible).

Shift+Up/Down and the in/out stops for Up/Down come from §6.5, which is in the batch's Spec refs. Panning (Hand, Space and middle drag) is left to Batch 18, as BATCHES.md asks.

The canvas code reads every color, font, size, radius and spacing from the `Theme`. The remaining numbers are behavior constants, not visual sizes: the 6/24 px LOD thresholds from §6.4, the 2 px playhead-head hit slop, the wheel line and notch sizes, and lucide's 24-unit viewBox and stroke. `fixtures.ts` is test-only.
    - §3/§4: the views are framework-free and React mounts them once. Both canvases draw only from the `timelineContent` and `timelineOverlay` render callbacks, and backing stores are sized inside `render` at DPR. Each document change maps its `affects` to content plus overlay, playhead moves redraw the overlay only, and selection changes redraw the content only. Gestures write session state only (scrub, wheel, slider once on release). "+ track" is one command, and an insert is one `transaction`. Document times stay integer frames and microseconds (`usToFrameFloor`, `secondsToFrames`, `setPlayhead` rounds). The visible-clip lookup is a binary search with a longest-clip lookback (P10), and panel sizes come from the layout store (P9). There is no media work in this batch.

- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-14)
  1. Stale hover (`src/timeline/view/TimelineView.ts`):
     - The view keeps the last pointer position over the overlay (`pointer`, from pointer down, pointer move and wheel; cleared on `pointerleave` and unmount). `refreshHover()` runs `hitTester.hit` there again, unless a gesture is running, and sets the cursor from the result.
     - It runs at the start of every overlay render. Every change that moves what lies under the pointer already redraws the overlay: wheel zoom and scroll and the slider (session `timeline`), document changes with timeline affects (insert, undo, delete, "+ track"), playhead moves (keys, timecode), panel size, theme and DPR. So hover and cursor are right in the frame that shows the change (§6.18: "the cursor is set from the hover hit each overlay frame"). The cost is one hit test (binary searches) per overlay frame.
     - `endGesture` hits again right away, so the cursor does not show the hover from before the scrub for a frame. A pointer down hits again after its action: "+ track" moves the rows, and a pen or touch down with no move before it lights the hover (and invalidates the overlay).
     - During a scrub the hover stays as it was (cursor `ew-resize`), as before.
  2. `src/timeline/view/drawContent.ts`: clips under 6 px are filled with `colors.body` (§6.4: "the body color still shows"); selected ones stay `--accent`.

The existing "redraws the content for zoom, scroll, selection and document changes" now hovers before its click: a pointer down where the pointer was not known yet lights the hover, which also redraws the overlay.

  - Review items 1 and 2 are resolved:
    1. Stale hover (`src/timeline/view/TimelineView.ts`):

The overlay is invalidated by everything that moves what lies under a still pointer: session `timeline` (wheel, keys, Fit, slider release), the slider preview, document changes with timeline affects (insert, undo, "+ track"), playhead moves, panel size, theme and DPR (lines 151-170, 262-270). Selection changes redraw only the content, but they move nothing. The render does not invalidate again, so there is no redraw loop.

### Batch 08: Compositor and preview at the playhead
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - `src/render/compositor/Compositor.ts`:
    - `evaluate(doc, frame)` walks the visible video tracks from the bottom up. On each it takes the clip at the frame with a binary search (`clipInIndex`/`lastClipStartingBy`, now exported from `timeline/model/queries.ts`) and skips disabled clips.
    - Video and image clips become `media` layers with: mediaId, still flag, media time (§5.1 `in + framesToSourceUs(frame - start, speed)`, kept inside [in, out)), opacity × fades, the crop as a texture UV rect, and one matrix.
    - The matrix composes, first to last: the crop rectangle in texture space, rotation metadata (the texture stays as coded, §9.3), flips (the content mirrors in place), the display size, then the clip transform (anchor in content fractions, scale/scaleY, rotation in clockwise degrees with exact quarter turns, position from the frame center).
    - Crop fractions cut the shown (upright, flipped) sides. Fades are 0 on the first frame of a fade-in and on the last frame of a fade-out.
    - Rect solids with a flat RGBA fill become `solid` layers. Text, captions, other shapes, keyframes and transitions are `TODO(batch 28/32/34/35/36)`.
    - `neighbors(doc, frame)` lists the edge frames of the clips just before and after the playhead on each track, within 2 s.
    - Layer structs, arrays and scratch matrices are reused between frames, with no closures.
  - `drawList.ts` gains `MediaLayer`, `CompositeLayer` and `Composite`.
  - `DrawListSnapshot` flattens the last drawn list into reused buffers: numbers, plus texture identities and their `valid` flag. A render whose list and viewport are unchanged skips the GPU draw (§9.4).
  - `src/render/preview/LayerTextures.ts` (media layers → pool textures) keeps one slot per track:
    - It snaps to the nearest frame time (`nearestPosition`, new in `sampleIndex.ts`) and draws a pool hit at once.
    - Otherwise it requests the frame through `frameService.getFrame`, with `scrub` priority while the ruler is dragged (`TimelineView.scrubbing`) and `idle` otherwise. Only the first layer of a media gets the scrub request in a pass. A superseded slot request is aborted.
    - It draws the FrameService stand-in (nearest cached frame, then a closer keyframe) in the same pass. With nothing of that media ready, it draws the slot's last layer as it was (texture, matrix, crop, opacity), so cuts never flash black.
    - A request that settles on another frame is drawn and asked again only after the layer wants another frame.
    - It pins what is drawn and calls `onChange` (invalidate `preview`) for late frames.
    - `warm()` prefetches neighbour edge frames that are not cached yet and leaves alone the media being drawn, so a scrub never cancels them.
    - Undecodable or missing media are left out with one warning.
  - `src/media/decode/ImageService.ts`: still images are decoded in decode.worker (SVG on the main thread), uploaded once with mipmaps under `imageKey` and their bitmaps closed. Loads are shared and cancelled when the project detaches.
  - `PreviewView`:
    - Subscribes to the playhead, document affects (`timeline`, `clips`, `tracks`, `media`, `settings`, `composite`), size, zoom, theme, DPR and the device.
    - Each render runs evaluate → resolve → warm → snapshot compare → draw or skip.
    - Stats: draws, skipped, ms and the per-pass layer counts (exact, stand-in, held, missing, pending, warming, failed), shown in the dev GPU stats overlay.
    - `setLayers`/`clearLayers` are gone. Export frame draws the last resolved list.
  - Removed: `ImageHarness.ts`, `VideoHarness.ts`, `pickPreviewMedia`, the dev `ScrubBar` and its CSS, `--danger-wash`, and the harness-only `containTransform`/`rotatedContainTransform` (their rotation cases moved into `Compositor.test.ts`).
  - Insert: `defaultTransform(media, frame)` in `insertMedia.ts` gives Fit through the display size (rotation metadata swaps width and height), anchored at the media center. A video's linked audio clip keeps scale 1.
  - `basicCommands.ts`: `clipPatchAffects`. Transform/opacity/crop-only patches carry the new `composite` flag (Global deviations), and undo carries the same flags.
  - `FrameScheduler` renders the dirty flags in a fixed order (`timelineContent`, `timelineOverlay`, `preview`, `previewOverlay`, ...). That fixes the Batch 05 stats-ordering follow-up.
  - `WebGpuRenderer.draw` allocates nothing on our side once warm (Batch 05 follow-up):
    - a pooled record array with two texture slots;
    - `invertInto`/`multiplyInto`/`pixelsToClipInto`;
    - reused frame/scissor rects, clear color, pass descriptor and a `Uint32Array` dynamic offset;
    - `packLayer`/`packMat3` writing element by element.

    `mat3.ts` gains `MutableMat3`, `setMat`, `multiplyInto` and `invertInto`. `time.ts` gains `framesToSourceUs`.
  - Preview chrome v1: `PanelFrame` gets a `footer` slot outside the measured body. The preview footer holds the playhead timecode (`bindPreviewTimecode`, direct DOM), the static "Auto: full" quality chip and Fit (session `preview.zoom = "fit"`).
  - Dev: `__editor.clips.list()` and `__editor.clips.update(id, patch)` (the raw `clip.update`), plus `__editor.images`.
  - Measured:
    - 551 unit tests in 72 files (+34 tests, +3 files: Compositor, LayerTextures, ImageService), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 365.07 kB JS (120.32 kB gzip, +8.51 / +3.04 kB), 35.85 kB CSS (−0.47 kB, the scrub bar CSS is gone); decode.worker 205.39 kB unchanged.
  - Headless Chrome 155. Setup: CDP, dev server, AMD RDNA2, 1600x900, OPFS projects. Test clips are H.264 encoded in the page: each frame shows a flat color and 12 stripes encoding its index, and the coded top-left corner carries a white marker. React commits were counted through the DevTools hook.
    - Acceptance 1: three PNGs (400x300 red, 300x300 green, 200x500 blue) were inserted, moved to V1..V3 at frame 0, then set through `__editor.clips.update`:
      - red: opacity 1 at (−150, −300);
      - green: opacity 0.6, scale 1.5 at (120, −100);
      - blue: opacity 0.35, rotated 30° at (0, 150).

      `renderFrame()` was compared with a Canvas2D reference built from the clip semantics (translate to center + (x, y), rotate, scale, globalAlpha):
      - 0 pixels differ by more than 1 away from edges;
      - 1,376 of 2,073,600 edge pixels differ by more than 3 (max 52), because Canvas2D antialiases quad edges and the GPU quads are not antialiased;
      - a red-only pixel is exactly (230, 40, 40).
    - Acceptance 2: clips a | b | c on V1 (540x960, 90 frames each, GOP 30), scrubbed by real ruler drags, with every preview render logged.
      - Slow scrub 60→120 (61 moves): 119 renders with 0 black and 0 wrong-media renders. The first render after the cut at 90 drew b's frame 0, already exact because it was warmed. Exact frames arrived in at most 17 ms.
      - Back 120→75: every frame exact at once.
      - Fast sweeps 0→269 and 269→0 (7 frames per move): 0 black and 0 wrong media. Every cut crossing drew the new clip's frame in its first render (exact, or a same-media stand-in). Exact frames arrived in at most 34 ms.
      - Cold frames (never decoded) on 1080x1920 H.264:
        - GOP 30: 47-98 ms;
        - GOP 60: 15-19 ms at keyframes and on the warmed cut, 52 ms 30-35 frames past a keyframe, 108 and 133 ms 59 frames past one (see deviations).
      - The first render after each settle always showed a same-media stand-in. 0 React commits in every scrub.
    - Acceptance 3:
      - A 640x360 clip got scale 1.6875 on insert. Rows above y = 656 and below y = 1264 are black, the content is centered, and the stripes decode the exact frame (110 = offset 100 + 10).
      - A 640x360 clip with the iPhone rotation-90 matrix got scale 3 and fills the 1080x1920 frame upright: the coded top-left marker is at the top right, and the stripes run down the right edge and decode the exact frame (320). The rotated clip's first render showed its warmed frame 0 as the stand-in.
    - Acceptance 4: opacity 0.5 through `__editor.clips.update` gave preview +1 draw, timeline content +0 and overlay +0. Undo, redo, a crop and a transform did the same. The same opacity again is a no-op. A rename (control) redraws the timeline content, and the preview skips its identical frame. The center pixel went from (41, 150, 200) to (20, 75, 100).
    - Regression:
      - Batch 05: `device.destroy()` → generation 2, the exact frame drawn again; PNG color exact (acceptance 1).
      - Batch 06: exact frames on scrubs (above); main-thread open VideoFrames 0.
      - Batch 07: real double-clicks on three cards made clips 0-45, 45-90, 90-135 at Fit scale 1.6875 with the playhead at 135; Ctrl+Z ×3 left 2, 1, then 0 clips, and redo ×3 restored 3.
      - `generate(500, 8)` wheel sweeps (Ctrl/Alt zoom, scroll; one event per frame): median 16.7 ms, p95 16.9-17.1 ms, max 33 ms, no long tasks, 0 commits, 0 preview renders. The first sweep right after generating had one 134 ms frame.
      - Ruler drag: 0 content draws; the overlay and the preview redraw per move. Each move first renders the previous exact frame as the stand-in (skipped, identical), then draws the exact frame about 15 ms later.
- deviations:
  - `composite` invalidation flag, Fit for every media, media time 0 = first frame: see Global deviations.
  - Rect solids with a flat fill are drawn now: the draw list's `SolidLayer` and the solid pipeline exist since Batch 05, §9.4 resolves "solids to geometry", and the dev generator makes rect solids. Shapes, gradients and radius stay in Batch 34.
  - Cut warming (not in the Build list): the edge frames of the clips within 2 s of the playhead are prefetched. Without it, a scrub that crosses into a never-decoded clip could show only the previous clip's last frame until the decoder answers. It is the scrub side of P7, and it never touches media a layer is drawing.
  - Acceptance 2's "exact frame within 100 ms" holds for 1 s GOPs (47-98 ms cold at 1080x1920) but not deep in 2 s GOPs (108 and 133 ms, 59 frames past a keyframe). That case is bound by the hardware decode rate (~2 ms/frame), as measured in Batch 06; proxies (Batch 24) are the fix. Logged as a follow-up.
  - Frames are picked by nearest frame time (§5.1), not by "shown at" (`positionAt`).
  - Flips mirror the content in place (around the media center, not the anchor). Crop applies to the media as shown.
  - The footer timecode is a read-only readout (§7.8 says editable; the timeline toolbar field is the editable one, and the transport comes in Batch 09). The quality label is static "Auto: full" (§13 wording).
  - Export frame draws the last resolved list, which may hold stand-ins while frames load.
  - The preview reads the ruler-scrub state from `TimelineView.scrubbing`, ephemeral state owned by the view running the gesture (§3.3).
  - `scripts/run-batches.sh` (the runner's 6 h timeout, an uncommitted change made before this run) is left out of the batch commit.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 08 -> Batch 09] playback through the compositor, lookahead beyond cut warming, transport in the footer; [from Batch 08 -> Batch 24] exact latency deep in 2 s GOPs; [from Batch 08 -> Batch 26] one-click Fill; [from Batch 08 -> Batch 41] offline slate for undecodable or missing media; [from Batch 08 -> Batch 44] export waits for exact frames

### Batch 09: Playback engine (video)
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-14
- notes:
  - `src/playback/PlaybackEngine.ts` (framework-free; React reads only `playing` through `subscribe`):
    - Clock (§8): `playhead = start + floor((clock - startClock) * speed * fps)` (`playedFrames` in `time.ts`), run from a `FrameScheduler` ticker. One session write per new frame. The timeline overlay, the preview and the timecode readouts follow it; the document never changes.
    - Preroll: starting from pause waits up to 0.5 s (`PREROLL_SECONDS`) until every clip under the playhead has its window decoded (15 frames, or 0.5 s at low frame rates). The clock start is taken half a display frame early, so frame boundaries fall between vsyncs and jitter neither repeats nor skips a frame.
    - Per frame, the preview evaluates the composite with `playback` priority (`PreviewView` `priority()`). `LayerTextures` then requests nothing and uses no stand-in, and a frame not decoded yet holds the track's previous layer.
    - Prefetch windows: every video clip under the playhead keeps up to 15 frames (at most 0.5 s) of its media decoded ahead, at `playback` priority. They come in 2-frame chunks, each asked for as soon as the previous one completes (see deviations). Received frames stay pinned until the playhead passes them.
    - Shuttle speeds deliver only the frames a 60 Hz screen can show (`stride`: the worker decodes the frames between but does not send them). 60 fps media in a 30 fps project deliver every other frame.
    - Backward (J): 15-frame windows ending at the playhead, asked for when half are used. The worker decodes each window forward from the keyframe before it (reverse GOP decoding): more decode work, never a main-thread wait.
    - Lookahead (P7): every 250 ms, the clips starting within 2 s get their first 4 frames at `idle` priority, so their decoder has already sought to the keyframe before the in point. This covers the play direction, plus the start of the timeline when loop wraps. Images about to appear are loaded. decode.worker closes decoders idle for 10 s (`DecodeSessions` IDLE_MS).
    - Dropped frames (1x only) are frames the clock skipped, plus frames whose first preview render lacked the exact frame of a layer (`PreviewView.firstShown`). A clip late on more than 2 frames in a row adds its media to `autoProxy` and logs a note (`TODO(batch 24)`: the proxy switch).
    - Transport:
      - play, pause and toggle;
      - the J/L ladder 1x/2x/4x/8x (`transport.ts` `shuttleSpeed`);
      - K held pauses, and J or L then steps one frame;
      - Shift+K plays around (2 s before to 2 s after the playhead, then back);
      - steps;
      - at the end of the content: stop on the last frame, or wrap when loop is on. Playing from the end restarts at 0.

      A playhead moved by someone else (Home, End, the timecode field) plays on from there. A ruler scrub pauses. The `IdleQueue` is paused while playing (Batch 01 follow-up).
  - `src/audio/AudioClock.ts`: the master clock.
    - The AudioContext (48 kHz, interactive) is created and resumed on the first pointer or key press, through capture listeners installed before the key dispatcher.
    - `now()` reads `getOutputTimestamp()`, carried forward with `performance.now()` between audio callbacks. Without output timestamps it uses `currentTime`, and before the context runs, `performance.now()`.
    - The reading is monotonic: it holds through small steps back and re-bases on large ones.
  - `src/app/playbackController.ts`: the transport actions (Space, J/K/L with hold phases, Shift+K, arrows with hold-to-repeat, Home/End, Mod+Shift+L loop), follow playhead, and the status strip binding. The stepping actions moved here from `timelineController.ts`.
  - Follow playhead (§6.2):
    - `zoom.ts` `pageToFrame` pages by one view width minus 10%, exactly when the playhead's x reaches the right edge of the track area. A frame far outside lands 10% inside the edge it comes in from, and backward pages work too.
    - `TimelineView.follow` pages while following. A horizontal wheel or scroll, or a wheel zoom, stops following until the next play; a vertical wheel does not.
    - `TimelineView.reveal` pages for keyboard jumps (arrows, Home/End, edit points, markers, the timecode field), playing or not (Batch 07 follow-up).
  - Preview chrome (§7.8, §13): the footer is now a bar (readouts left, transport centered, loop and Fit right) over a status strip.
    - Readouts: playhead timecode, duration and the fps badge, written by subscriptions (`bindPreviewReadouts`).
    - Transport: go to start, previous frame, play/pause, next frame, go to end. These are `IconButton`s running the actions; only the play/pause icon re-renders, on play and on pause.
    - Loop toggle (session `loop`).
    - Status strip: the quality chip ("Auto: full"), "Dropped N" for the last playback (reset on play), and "Pool used / budget MB" while the dev GPU stats are on. Its texts are written in the scheduler loop on engine and pool notifications.
  - Decode path:
    - `FrameService.prefetch` takes `priority` (`playback` before `idle`), `stride` and `onFrame`. `frameKeyInto` writes the pool key of a media time into a reused key. `sampleIndex.ts` adds `mediaTimePosition` (media time 0 = the first frame).
    - Frames are uploaded at most at the project's long edge (`uploadSize`). A larger frame is drawn once into a smaller texture from `importExternalTexture` (`SCALE_WGSL`, `fitWithin.ts`). Measured on 4K HEVC: 70 decoded frames/s scaled to 1080p, against 56 copied at 4K.
    - `WebGpuRenderer` keeps up to 4 spare video-frame textures from evicted frames, and uploads into one of the same size instead of creating and destroying textures (Batch 06 follow-up).
    - `VideoDecodeSession`: prefetch windows take their frames out of the ring instead of cloning them (a hardware decoder whose pictures stay referenced has to allocate new ones on the GPU thread). It also gains `stride` and `releaseRing`. `DecodeSessions` releases the ring of a session idle for 1 s (`RING_IDLE_MS`): during playback several decoders stay live for the cuts ahead.
    - Batch 06 re-review follow-up: frames of superseded runs are tracked apart from the current run (`stale`, one leading flag per copy). Before, an old run's output with a timestamp the new run had also fed pruned the new run's pending frames. The new run's leading picture then landed in the ring as valid, and `lastOutputUs` jumped ahead (the frame after the target came back). Test: "keeps the frames of a superseded run apart..."; on the old bookkeeping it returns frame 6 for 5.
  - Batch 08 review follow-ups:
    - `sinCos` writes a scratch struct.
    - `deviceSizeInto`/`frameTransformInto` write reused objects.
    - `LayerTextures` compares and copies keys instead of building `keyId` strings. Only `warm`, which does not run while playing, still builds them.
    - `FLAG_ORDER` is an `as const` array and `InvalidationFlag` is derived from it, so a new flag cannot be left out.
  - `PreviewView`: a new GPU device clears the `LayerTextures` slots. A frame request that failed while the device was being restored ("device not ready") was marked settled and never asked again, so the preview kept a dead layer. This was a race the Batch 08 run did not hit; the regression pass here did.
  - `FrameScheduler.addTicker`: tickers run at the start of each animation frame, before the views, so what they invalidate is drawn in the same frame. A registered ticker keeps the loop running.
  - Dev:
    - a playback line in the preview GPU stats (speed, dropped / frames and %, windows, received, media flagged for proxy);
    - `__editor.playback` (the engine);
    - `__editor.timeline.cuts(clips = 60, seconds = 1)`: one-second clips on V1, cycling through the project's videos, each read forward (one undo step).
  - Measured:
    - 597 unit tests in 76 files (+46 tests, +4 files: PlaybackEngine, transport, AudioClock, fitWithin), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 385.17 kB JS (126.36 kB gzip, +20.10 / +6.04 kB), 36.70 kB CSS (+0.85 kB), decode.worker 205.92 kB (+0.53 kB).
    - Headless Chrome 155 setup: CDP, dev server, AMD RDNA2 laptop GPU, 1600x900, OPFS projects. The clips are encoded in the page: a flat color, 12 stripes encoding the frame index, and a moving noise band for real-footage bitrates. "Dropped" is the engine's count. Unless noted, the clock is `performance.now()`: headless Chrome's audio device ran at 0.64-0.8x real time under load in earlier runs, which would understate the load (the audio-clock passes are listed separately).
    - Acceptance 1 (`b09-a1`): 60 one-second clips from five 1080p30 H.264 sources on V1 at 30 fps. Each source is 14 s at 10 Mbit/s with GOP 30; clip i takes source i mod 5 at in point floor(i/5) s. Final code, four warm passes (three on the performance clock, one on the audio clock): 0 / 1799 dropped each, no render without its exact frame, 2 React commits per pass (play and pause). Before the preroll change: cold 1, warm 1, 0, 0, audio 0.
    - Acceptance 2 (`b09-a2`): 4K60 HEVC (3840x2160, `hvc1.1.6.L153.B0`, 80 Mbit/s, GOP 60, 20 s) in a 1080x1920 60 fps project at Full, frames scaled to 1920x1080 on upload. Five passes with 25 s rests: 2.59%, 0.58%, 1.08%, 0.92% (performance clock) and 1.92% (audio clock). Median 1.08%; 4 of 5 under 2%.
      - Not one render lacked its exact frame. Every drop is a vsync miss: animation frames of 60-170 ms with no script time (the GPU process composites late while the hardware decoder runs at ~86% of its 4K rate). They fall mostly in the first second.
      - Late in the session, after ~30 min of back-to-back 4K playback, the same passes drifted to 3-15%. A fresh page and a fresh browser did not help; resting the machine did.
      - With the old 3-frame preroll the same source in a 30 fps project dropped 0.67-2.17%.
    - Acceptance 3, with real key events through CDP and the app's AudioClock on output timestamps:
      - L four times gives speeds 1, 2, 4, 8. At 8x the playhead advanced 364 frames in 1.517 s of clock: ratio 1.000 against the clock and against `performance.now()` (1x: 1.009).
      - K: paused at once, 0 frames moved in the next 400 ms.
      - K held + L, L, L, J: +1, +2, +3, then back to +2.
    - Acceptance 4 (P1): 30 s of playback with the GPU stats on and follow playhead paging (6 pages): 1 commit at play (the button), 0 during, 1 at pause. Every pass above, including those with dropped frames, had exactly 2 commits.
    - Acceptance 5: 180 frames in a 912 px track area.
      - The view paged at frames 180 and 342, each when the playhead's x reached 912 px (the frame before was at 906.9 px). Each page landed the playhead at 91.2 px (10%).
      - In 14 s the content canvas drew twice (the pages), against 419 overlay draws.
      - After a horizontal wheel: 0 pages in the next 8 s, with the playhead off screen. The next play paged again.
    - Regression:
      - Batch 08: a real ruler drag from frame 20 to 100 across the cuts at 30/60/90 gave 159 renders: 0 black, 0 missing, 0 content draws, 0 commits; the last render was exact.
      - Batch 05: `device.destroy()` three times gave generations 2/3/4, with the exact frame back 12-35 ms after "ready". This needed the `PreviewView` fix in the notes above; before it, the frame stayed missing.
      - Batch 07: insert, undo and wheel zoom are covered by their unit tests (all pass). The 500-clip wheel sweeps were not re-run; the timeline view changed only for follow/reveal and the wheel flags.
      - `__editor.timeline.cuts(60, 1)` made 60 contiguous clips cycling through the 5 sources (in points 0 s, then 1 s, ...) in one undo step.
- deviations:
  - P6 pinning: the engine pins the frames of its windows ahead of the playhead (up to 15 frames, 0.5 s) until the playhead passes them, and the preview pins what it draws. It does not pin everything within ±1 s. Reason: ±1 s at 60 fps is 120 frames x 8.3 MB (1080x1920) ≈ 1 GB, over the 768 MB budget. Frames just played stay in the LRU as its most recent entries.
  - Frames are uploaded at most at the project's long edge, for scrubbing too (`TODO(batch 21)`: detail-on-zoom above 100%). Reason: the preview never draws more at Fit or 100%, and 4K HEVC decodes at 70 frames/s scaled on upload against 56 when copied at 4K (see Global deviations).
  - Forward windows are 2-frame chunks requested back to back, topping the lead up to 15 frames, instead of one 15-frame window. Reason: decoding a whole window as fast as the hardware can starves Chrome's GPU process, which also composites the page. On 4K60 HEVC, 15-frame windows dropped 12-35% of frames, 2-frame chunks 1.3-4.5% (measured while building this batch).
  - Preroll: starting from pause waits for the clips' windows, up to 0.5 s (the spec names no preroll). Reason: interleaved A/B on 4K60 HEVC: a 3-frame preroll dropped 14-34 frames in the first 60 (1.2-2.9% overall); the full window dropped 7-9 (0.6-1.9%), and the clock started about 25 ms later (~85 ms after the press).
  - Prefetch windows take their frames out of the decoder's ring, and idle sessions give their ring back after 1 s. Reason: hardware decoders reallocate picture buffers while the app holds their pictures (measured 4K60 vsync misses).
  - Dropped frames count at 1x only (shuttle speeds skip frames by design). The count is clock skips plus first renders without the exact frame.
  - Arrows and K+J/K+L pause playback, then step. Home, End and the timecode field during playback play on from the new frame. The end of the content stops on the last frame unless loop is on.
  - Follow playhead: a vertical wheel does not stop following (it does not move the view horizontally); a horizontal scroll or a wheel zoom does. Keyboard jumps page the view whether playing or not.
  - Auto quality only flags media for proxy (`autoProxy` + a console note); the switch is Batch 24's (this batch's "Do not build yet"). "When paused, request the full-quality frame after 300 ms" belongs to proxies too.
  - The quality chip moved from the bar to the status strip (§13 puts the quality mode there). Pool usage shows only with the dev GPU stats on (§13: "dev toggle").
  - Acceptance 2 (< 2% dropped at Full on 4K60 HEVC) holds on a rested machine in 4 of 5 passes (median 1.08%), not in every pass. It fails once the laptop GPU has run 4K decode for half an hour (3-15%). Every frame is decoded in time; the misses come from the GPU process compositing late while the hardware decoder runs near its 4K limit. Auto quality (proxies, Batch 24) is the fix; follow-up logged.
  - The preview footer's timecode readout is read-only (§7.8: "Timecode readout (editable)"). Reason: the timeline toolbar's timecode field (Batch 07) is the editable playhead field, with the same parser and keyboard jumps; Batch 09's Spec refs take §7.8 for the transport buttons only. Batch 21, which builds the rest of §7.8, makes the readout editable with `bindTimecode`. (Added with the review fixes: the review noted it was only in the Batch 08 follow-up.)
  - `scripts/run-batches.sh` (the runner's 6 h timeout, an uncommitted change made before this run) is left out of the batch commit again.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 09 -> Batch 10] audio scheduling on `AudioClock`'s context, mute and master volume in the transport, the P1 re-check; [from Batch 09 -> Batch 11] count `IdleQueue` pausers once gestures pause it; [from Batch 09 -> Batch 19] play in/out and its loop; [from Batch 09 -> Batch 21] uploads above the project size for zoom above 100%, and the editable footer timecode readout (§7.8); [from Batch 09 -> Batch 24] Auto quality switches `autoProxy` media to proxies, 4K60 HEVC at Full is at the limit of the development machine
- design gaps: preview transport bar and status strip (details in Design system gaps)

In the console, run `__editor.timeline.cuts(60, 1)` to make 60 one-second clips on V1. Turn on GPU stats (gauge, dev) in the preview header. Press Space and let it play to the end once (cold), then press Space again with the playhead at the end (it restarts at 0). The status strip shows "Dropped 0" and the stats line "dropped 0 / 1799".
  2. 4K60 (acceptance 2): in a 1080x1920 60 fps project, insert a 4K60 HEVC phone clip (20 s or more) and play it at 1x twice. The stats line's percentage stays below 2% on a cool machine. Note the number and the machine.
  3. Transport keys (acceptance 3), with the timeline or preview focused:

  5. Follow playhead (acceptance 5): zoom the timeline so about 5 s are visible and play. Pause and play: following resumes.

- review: rejected
  1. The preview can stay on the wrong frame after a pause. `PlaybackEngine.pause` (`src/playback/PlaybackEngine.ts:238-250`) does not invalidate the preview. `PreviewView` reads its priority only at render time (`src/render/preview/PreviewView.ts:161`, from `src/app/previewController.ts:60`), and it re-renders only on playhead, zoom, document, theme, device and DPR changes (`:106-136`). While playing, `LayerTextures.resolve` holds the track's previous layer when the playhead's frame is not in the pool, and it requests nothing (`src/render/preview/LayerTextures.ts:219-222`). A pause on such a frame (K, Space or the button) leaves the playhead unchanged, so no render at `idle` priority ever runs. The exact frame is never requested, and the preview keeps showing the held layer, while the timecode shows the playhead's frame, until something else invalidates the preview.
     - At shuttle speeds this is the normal case, because windows deliver only every `stride`-th frame and the playhead usually stops between them. JKL is the tool for finding a frame to edit, and the batch goal is frame-accurate playback.
     - Reproduced in headless Chrome 155 (CDP, dev server, OPFS project `b09-a1`, 60 one-second cuts). The script plays at a speed, calls `playbackEngine.pause()` (the K path), waits 1.5 s, then compares the V1 slot's drawn key with `frameKeyInto` of the clip under the playhead. At 8x, 2 of 4 stops stayed stale, and both showed a different clip's media. Playhead 476 wanted `95f10d…` @ 3866667 us and drew `5c409a…` @ 2533333 us. Playhead 896 wanted `5c409a…` @ 5866667 us and drew `56d7dc…` @ 5933333 us. In both, `exact` was 0 of 1 layers and the preview made 0 renders after the pause. 4x (3 stops), -1x (3) and 1x (3) happened to stop on delivered frames.
     - Fix: invalidate `preview` when the engine goes from playing to paused, for example a `playbackEngine.subscribe` listener with a was-playing flag in `previewController.ts`, or a `PreviewView` dependency. The next render then resolves at `idle` priority, draws a stand-in and requests the exact frame.
     - Add a test that pauses on a held layer and asserts that the next render requests the playhead's frame. Re-run the CDP check: after 8x then K, the exact frame should be drawn within ~100 ms.

This covers K, Space, the button, a step clamped at an edge and the end of the content. `LayerTextures` then requests the playhead's frame, draws a stand-in meanwhile and the exact frame when it arrives.
