### Batch 10: Audio decoding, mixing and sync
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Resumed: the first session wrote every Build item and was cut off at ~01:25 by the Windows Update reboot, before any acceptance check. This session reviewed that code, ran the checks in Chrome and fixed what they found (the stop click, below).
  - Decoded audio (`src/audio/decode/`):
    - `DecodedAudio`: `AudioBuffer`s per media and segment, kept on the main thread in `AudioBufferCache` (512 MB LRU; the entry just stored is never evicted). Released when a media leaves the project, cleared when it closes (P13).
    - MP4/MOV/M4A audio decodes in decode.worker with WebCodecs `AudioDecoder` (`openAudio`/`decodeAudio`/`closeAudio`; `media/decode/audioTrack.ts`, `AudioTracks.ts`). Only the audio samples are read, and any range decodes on its own (2 packets of preroll for the AAC overlap). Media over 10 minutes load in ~60 s segments on demand (`segments.ts`, boundaries on multiples of 1024 frames).
    - Edit lists: `mp4Probe.ts` now reads the edit lists, the esds AudioSpecificConfig and the audio sample table. The AAC priming is skipped, a leading empty edit delays the audio, and media time 0 is the video's first frame (the Batch 08 convention). The decoder's real output rate (HE-AAC doubles it) comes from decoding the first packets.
    - At most 2 channels: quad and 5.1 are mixed down with the Web Audio "speakers" rules, in the worker and for `decodeAudioData` output.
    - Other files (mp3, aac, wav, ogg), and MP4 audio without a WebCodecs decoder, use `decodeAudioData` (OfflineAudioContext, 48 kHz) on the whole file. The import probe's buffer goes straight into the cache (`DecodedAudio.put`), so the file is decoded once (Batch 04 follow-up).
    - Worker PCM is copied into the `AudioBuffer` in slices of 1M frames, yielding between them. The worker's arrays are then transferred on to analysis.worker, with no further copy.
  - Peaks (§9.6):
    - `media/cache/peaks.ts`: level 0 holds Int8 min/max per 256 frames per channel, halved level by level down to one bucket. `cache/peaks/<mediaId>.bin` carries a header stamped with the source's size and lastModified, so a replaced or damaged file reads as null and is rebuilt.
    - `media/workers/analysis.worker.ts` (new): `peaksLevel0` per segment and `peaksFile` (pyramid + file bytes). It never decodes.
    - `media/PeaksJobs.ts`: one job at a time, each started from an idle callback (none while playing). A job reuses a valid file; otherwise it decodes segment by segment through `DecodedAudio` (the buffers stay cached for playback), writes the file, and sets `analysis.peaks` to "ready".
    - "ready" goes through the new `DocumentStore.amend`, a command (`media.analysis`, `setMediaAnalysis`) with no undo step (Batch 04 follow-up). `AnalysisScheduler` hands every audio-bearing media to the jobs; the files of media already "ready" are checked once per session (the cache is disposable).
  - Graph (`src/audio/AudioMixer.ts`, §14):
    - Per voice: source -> envelope GainNode (start/stop ramps) -> clip GainNode (gain dB, mute; `TODO(batch 37)` fades, keyframes) -> track GainNode (mute/solo, `audibleTracks`) -> bus (the master GainNode, stereo) -> monitor GainNode (master volume and mute, monitoring only) -> destination.
    - The bus also feeds a splitter and one `AnalyserNode` per channel (2048 samples, no smoothing). The meters therefore read the mix before the monitor, which is what the export will contain.
    - Every gain change is a 10 ms linear ramp from an explicit start point (`ramp`/`rampTo`; see the click fix below).
  - Scheduler (`src/audio/scheduler/AudioScheduler.ts`, §8):
    - Driven from the engine tick (`sync(mapping)`), in 50 ms steps, starting voices at most 200 ms ahead.
    - The mapping is the engine's anchor, so audio and video read the same clock. Clock readings become context times through the new `AudioClock.contextTime`, `renderLead` and `epoch`.
    - One source per (clip, segment, loop pass); `playbackRate` = the clip speed (0.25x..4x).
    - A clip's start or a segment join starts exactly. Anything else (playback starting mid-clip, a seek, audio decoded late) starts with a 10 ms fade-in, its offset moved on by the delay.
    - Pause, seek, edits and a clock re-base stop voices with a 10 ms ramp. Speeds other than 1x are silent.
    - Edits while playing: mute/solo ramp the track gains, a clip gain or mute change ramps its voice, and a moved, trimmed or removed clip stops and is scheduled again.
    - Loop: the passes after the wrap are scheduled ahead. The engine now wraps at the exact time the end frame would have started (not at the tick that sees it), so those voices are kept.
  - Engine (`PlaybackEngine`):
    - The preroll also waits for the decoded audio under the playhead (up to the 0.5 s preroll).
    - At 1x, the clock starts, and re-anchors after a seek, `lead` = render lead + 20 ms ahead, so the first samples are heard on time.
    - The 250 ms scan loads the audio of clips starting within 2 s. A pause stops the scheduler.
  - Click fix (this session): a stop after a fade-in clicked. `cancelAndHoldAtTime` adds no hold point after a ramp that has ended (Web Audio spec; Chrome does the same). The 10 ms ramp-out therefore ran from the fade-in's end, seconds earlier, and the gain dropped to ~0 at once.
    - Found in the seek recording: Home pressed after a mid-clip seek jumped 0.30 in one sample.
    - `ramp` now sets an explicit start point at the exact current value (the last ramp of each param is kept in a WeakMap). The same bug had made mute/solo instant jumps.
    - `FakeParam` (`audio/testAudio.ts`) now models the Web Audio automation rules. "ramps a voice that faded in out from full level when it stops (no click)" fails on the old code (it renders 0.005 at the stop).
    - Also fixed: the `late` counter counted voices it then skipped.
  - Preview chrome (§7.8):
    - Mute (`audio.toggleMute`, Volume2/VolumeX, pressed when muted) and the master volume `Knob` (new `ui/Knob.tsx`), both before loop and Fit.
    - Knob: drag up/down, 150 px across -60..0 dB (Shift 5x finer); arrows, Page Up/Down, Home/End when focused; double-click resets to 0 dB. While dragged it writes the DOM directly and drives the monitor gain (no React commit), and the value is stored on release.
    - Both live in the app settings (`monitorVolumeDb`, `monitorMuted`).
    - Meter: `MeterView` + `PeakMeter` (pure, tested), a canvas strip along the panel's right edge in a new `PanelFrame` `aside` slot. It redraws through the `meters` flag every frame while audio plays and until the bars and holds settle, then stops.
    - Meter scale -60..+6 dBFS, green up to 0 dBFS and red above. The hold lasts 1.5 s, then falls at 24 dB/s. Each channel has a clip LED that latches on any sample above full scale; a click on the LED row resets it.
  - Track headers (§6.12, minimal): M and S toggles on audio track headers, right-aligned on the first line (`geometry.headerToggles`, hit type `headerToggle`, accent when on, hover wash on the overlay). Each click is one `track.update` command (`updateTrack`, affects `tracks` + `audio`) and one undo step.
  - Dev (`app/audioDev.ts`, dev builds only):
    - `__editor.audio.clapTest({ seconds: 330, interval: 1 })`: a WAV of 10 ms ticks on a new audio track plus a 1-frame white solid on each tick's frame, the first one 1 s in.
    - `.syncReport()`: video minus audio per clap, measured from the analyser on the bus and `getOutputTimestamp`. It is also logged and shown as a toast at each pause.
    - `.toneTest({ gainDb: 6, levelDb: -3 })`; `.scheduler`, `.decoded`, `.meter`, `.mixer`.
  - Measured:
    - 664 unit tests in 85 files (+66 tests, +8 files: AudioScheduler, PeakMeter, AudioBufferCache, DecodedAudio, segments, PeaksJobs, peaks, audioTrack), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 413.31 kB JS (136.09 kB gzip, +27.98 / +9.68 kB), 38.44 kB CSS (+1.74 kB), decode.worker 211.94 kB (+6.02 kB), analysis.worker 3.88 kB (new). The dev tooling is not in the build.
    - Setup: headless Chrome 155 (CDP, dev server, OPFS projects), 48 kHz context: base latency 10.7 ms, output latency 40-48 ms, render lead 46-55 ms. Monitoring was muted, so nothing played aloud. The meters, the clap analyser and the AudioWorklet recorders all tap the bus, before the monitor.
    - Acceptance 1 (`b10-clap`, 30 fps): 330 s, 329 claps 1 s apart, played from 0 once. Offset = video (render time of the flash frame) minus audio (tick onset found sample-exact by a worklet, mapped to its output time by `getOutputTimestamp`).
      - First 10 s: 10.4-14.1 ms. After 5 min (30 claps): 1.4-12.3 ms. All claps: 0.4-17.5 ms, mean 8.7 ms. One frame is 33.3 ms. The dev report agreed within 2 ms.
      - No drift beyond one vsync. One voice for the whole run, no clock re-base, 2 of 9899 frames dropped, 1 React commit (play), none during playback.
      - The offset is positive (video after audio): a frame is drawn at the first vsync after its time (0-16.7 ms). The display's presentation delay comes on top and cannot be measured headless (follow-up).
    - Acceptance 2 (`b10-seek`, a 440 Hz tone at 0.5, the bus recorded sample-exact): three seeks while playing, to 20 s (the timecode-field path), to 0 (a real Home key) and to 10.4 s.
      - The old audio ramped to silence in 9.8 ms. New audio started 10.8-11.0 ms after the seek and reached full level at 11.2-21.0 ms (render clock). The device adds its output latency (46-53 ms here) to everything heard.
      - The largest sample step around the seeks was 0.0288, the tone's own slope: no click. Before the fix, the Home seek jumped 0.30. WAVs: `%TEMP%/b10/seek-*.wav`.
    - Acceptance 3: two tracks (A1 440 Hz, A2 660 Hz), each track's output recorded. The header toggles were clicked with real pointer events while playing: mute A1, unmute, solo A2, unsolo, solo A1, unsolo.
      - Each change reached silence or full level 8.8-17.3 ms after the click (the 10 ms ramp), and the other track did not change. Playback went on, with one undo step per click.
    - Acceptance 4 (`b10-peaks`): av.mp4 (H.264 + AAC, 12 s), voice.m4a (AAC, 8 s), tone.wav (PCM, 6 s).
      - All three files were written and "ready" 442 ms after the import started, with one undo step ("Import 3 media files"). Worker calls: openAudio 2, decodeAudio 2, peaksLevel0 3, peaksFile 3; the WAV reused the probe's decode.
      - The levels match the sources (top bucket +-64 / +-102 / +-32 for 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.25).
      - Reopen: no audio decode, no peaks request, files unchanged.
    - Acceptance 5 (`b10-meter`):
      - A -12 dBFS tone shows -12 dB, no clip.
      - A -3 dBFS tone with +6 dB clip gain shows +3 dBFS, and both clip LEDs latch. They are still latched 3 s after the pause (hold falling, -38.6 dB) and reset with one click (0 commits).
      - The meter stops drawing once settled. Each play/pause pair costs 2 commits (the button).
    - Knob and mute: the monitor output was recorded while a -12 dBFS tone played.
      - Dragging 75 px went from 0 to -30 dB: 0 commits during the drag, 1 at release. The output followed: -26 dB mid-drag, then -30 dB.
      - Mute gave silence, a double-click stored 0 dB (still muted), and unmute brought back 0 dB.
    - P1 with audio (Batch 09 follow-up): 330 s of playback with audio and meters running: 1 commit at play, 0 during, 1 at the end.
    - Regression:
      - Batch 09 acceptance 1 (`b09-a1`, audio clock): cold 6 / 1799 dropped, warm 0 and 1 / 1799. Every render had its exact frame; 2 commits per pass.
      - 4K60 HEVC (`b09-a2`): 72% and 22% right after the 1080p passes (a hot GPU, as in Batch 09). After a 5 min rest, interleaved, the audio scheduler detached from the engine gave 0.83% and 1.58%, attached 1.17% and 1.08%. Every render had its exact frame: no regression.
      - Batch 08: a real ruler drag from frame 20 to 100 across the cuts gave 157 renders: 0 black, 0 missing, 0 content draws, 0 commits, and the last render exact. After `device.destroy()`, generation 2 came back with the exact frame.
- deviations:
  - MP4/MOV audio decodes with WebCodecs `AudioDecoder` in decode.worker, not with `decodeAudioData` on the main thread (§14). Reasons:
    - `decodeAudioData` needs the whole file in memory (a 4K phone video is gigabytes) and cannot decode a range, so "files over 10 min decode in 60 s segments on demand" is impossible with it.
    - It ignores the edit list: phone audio would play up to ~48 ms late (the AAC priming).
    - The worker reads only the audio samples. Other formats still use `decodeAudioData`.
  - Non-MP4 audio (mp3, aac, wav, ogg) over 10 minutes still decodes whole (`decodeAudioData` has no ranges), and files over 1 GB are refused for audio. Follow-up for Batch 45.
  - The scheduler starts one `AudioBufferSourceNode` per clip (per segment and loop pass) at most 200 ms ahead, in 50 ms steps, and lets it play to the clip's end. §8 describes buffer sources in 50 ms chunks. Reasons:
    - Back-to-back chunk sources must join sample-exactly, and every join risks a click (and 20 nodes/s).
    - A source per clip is click-free, and seeks, pauses and edits still stop it with the 10 ms ramp.
    - Nothing starts more than 200 ms ahead (the 400 ms limit of the implementation notes).
    - The per-chunk gain curves of §14 come with fades and keyframes (Batch 37).
  - At 1x the clock starts, and re-anchors after a seek, `lead` ahead: render lead + 20 ms, ~70 ms on this machine. Video and audio then start together and the first samples are not cut. Playback therefore begins ~70 ms later after a press than in Batch 09.
  - "Seeking restarts audio within 50 ms" is measured on the render clock (10.8-11.0 ms here). The device's output latency (46-53 ms in headless Chrome on this machine) delays everything heard equally, the start of play included, and is not the app's to shorten.
  - The peak meter scale (-60..+6 dBFS) and its release (24 dB/s) are not in the spec. The strip is a column right of the preview body, the full height between the panel header and the footer (§7.8: "vertical along the right edge of the preview panel").
  - Master volume and monitoring mute are global app settings (IndexedDB `settings.v1`), not project data: they only concern what is heard (§14).
  - `analysis.peaks` becomes "ready" through `DocumentStore.amend` (no undo step). An undo/redo that brings back a media record also brings back "pending", and the job marks it again (Batch 04 follow-up).
- follow-ups: [from Batch 10 -> Batch 16] video-track mute and the full header toggles; [from Batch 10 -> Batch 17] load the peaks for the waveforms; [from Batch 10 -> Batch 37] clip gain curves per scheduled chunk, audio scrub from the decoded audio; [from Batch 10 -> Batch 38] silence detection in analysis.worker; [from Batch 10 -> Batch 43] the export mixdown on the same graph; [from Batch 10 -> Batch 45] long non-MP4 audio decodes whole, display latency
- design gaps: audio meter strip, master volume knob, track header M/S toggles (details in Design system gaps)
- pending manual checks:
  1. Clap test with real output (acceptance 1): in a normal Chrome window with speakers or headphones, open a 30 fps project. In the console run `await __editor.audio.clapTest()`, then press Space. Each tick should come with a white flash. At the end (5.5 min) the toast reads "start max N ms · after 5 min max N ms · 1 frame = 33.3 ms": both maxima below 33.3. For the display's own delay, film the screen and speaker with a phone in slow motion: flash and tick within one frame.
  2. Seeks (acceptance 2): import a music file (or a sustained tone) and insert it. While it plays, press Home, type timecodes in the toolbar field and press Up/Down. Audio resumes at once at the new place, with no click or pop.
  3. Mute/solo (acceptance 3): two audio tracks with different sounds. While playing, click M and S in the A1/A2 headers: each change is heard at once, and Ctrl+Z undoes it.
  4. Peaks (acceptance 4): import an MP4 with sound, an MP3 and a WAV. The project folder's `cache/peaks/` has one `.bin` per media. Close and reopen the project: the files keep their timestamps, and no new ones appear.
  5. Meters (acceptance 5): run `await __editor.audio.toneTest({ gainDb: 6 })` and play. The meter goes red above 0 dBFS and both clip LEDs light; they stay lit after a pause. A click on the LEDs turns them off. A plain clip at moderate level moves the green bars only.
  6. Master volume: while playing, drag the knob down (quieter at once) and click mute (silent). The meter keeps moving: it shows the mix, not the monitor. The knob and mute state survive a reload.
  7. Phone footage: a video recorded on a phone (AAC with an edit list) plays with the lips in sync.
  8. P1 with audio: React DevTools Profiler, 30 s of playback with sound: one commit at play, none during, one at pause.

  - Evidence: `npm run check` is green: typecheck, lint, 664/664 unit tests in 85 files plus 1 perf test, and the build (413.31 kB JS / 136.09 kB gzip, 38.44 kB CSS, decode.worker 211.94 kB, analysis.worker 3.88 kB). This matches the notes. `dist/` contains no `clapTest` or `syncReport`, so the dev tooling stays out of the build.
  - Acceptance (each item also has a pending manual check with steps, and the CDP measurements are in the notes):
    1. Clap test:
       - Tests: `AudioScheduler.test.ts` "starts the clip under the playhead at the anchor...", "starts a clip exactly at its cut, only once it is within 200 ms" and "schedules the next loop pass ahead of the wrap...". `PlaybackEngine.test.ts` "waits in the preroll for the audio under the playhead, then starts the clock its lead ahead" and "wraps a loop at the exact time...". `AudioClock.test.ts` "maps clock readings to context time...".
       - Audio and video share one mapping: a voice starts at `contextTime(origin + s)` (`src/audio/scheduler/AudioScheduler.ts:275`), and the frame shows at the same clock reading.
       - CDP: offsets of 0.4-17.5 ms over 330 s, with no drift. The display's own delay is pending manual check 1 and a Batch 45 follow-up.
    2. Seek:
       - Tests: "restarts on a seek..." and "ramps a voice that faded in out from full level when it stops (no click)". `FakeParam` models the automation rules, and this test fails on the old `ramp`. Also `PlaybackEngine.test.ts` "re-anchors a seek with the lead...".
       - CDP: the new audio started 10.8-11.0 ms after the seek on the render clock, and the largest sample step was 0.0288.
       - Measuring on the render clock, with the device output latency on top, is a logged deviation with a sound reason.
    3. Mute/solo:
       - Tests: "applies mute and solo to the track gains with a ramp, at once", the `audibleTracks` test, `basicCommands.test.ts` "sets track flags as one undo step, mute and solo flagged for the audio engine", `HitTester.test.ts` "finds the mute and solo toggles..." and `TimelineView.test.ts` "toggles mute and solo from the audio track header...".
       - CDP: each click took effect in 8.8-17.3 ms.
    4. Peaks:
       - Tests: `PeaksJobs.test.ts` "builds and writes the peaks of every audio-bearing media after import, then marks them ready with no undo step", "reuses a valid peaks file on reopen without decoding" and "rebuilds a missing file...". `peaks.test.ts` covers the pyramid, level 0 built in segments, and the source stamp check.
       - Video files with sound are included (`hasAudio`).
    5. Meters:
       - Tests: `PeakMeter.test.ts` "latches the clip indicator above full scale until reset" and "holds the peak for 1.5 s...".
       - The analysers tap the bus before the monitor gain (`src/audio/AudioMixer.ts:83-91`), so the +6 dB clip latches whatever the knob says.
       - CDP: +3 dBFS shown, both LEDs latched, and one click reset them with 0 commits.
  - Build list: everything is present.
    - Decode:
      - MP4/MOV audio decodes by range with the worker's `AudioDecoder`: edit lists, AAC priming, the real HE-AAC output rate and a preroll. Other files use `decodeAudioData`.
      - Media over 10 minutes load in 60 s segments, the cache is a 512 MB LRU, and the probe's buffer is reused.
      - Decoding in the worker instead of with `decodeAudioData` is a logged deviation with sound reasons (memory, ranges, edit lists).
    - Scheduler:
      - Voices start 200 ms ahead, in 50 ms steps. The chain is envelope -> clip gain (dB, mute) -> track gain -> bus -> monitor.
      - Every gain change is a 10 ms ramp from an explicit start point. `playbackRate` is the clip speed, and every other transport speed is silent.
      - One source per clip, instead of one per 50 ms chunk, is a logged deviation.
    - Chrome:
      - Mute and the knob sit before loop and Fit. While dragged, the knob writes the DOM, and it makes one settings update on release.
      - The meter strip is in the new `aside` slot, and audio track headers have M/S toggles.
    - Sync tooling: `__editor.audio.clapTest()`, `.syncReport()` and `.toneTest()`, in dev builds only.
  - Do not build yet: nothing from it was built.
    - No waveform drawing: the peaks are only written (`TODO(batch 17)` in `PeaksJobs.ts`).
    - No rubber bands, fades, normalize or ducking (`TODO(batch 37)`). The envelope only does the 10 ms start and stop ramps.
    - No audio scrub: the scheduler is silent at every speed but 1x, and nothing reads the session's `audioScrub` flag.
  - Literals: none outside the tokens file.
    - The new CSS uses tokens only, plus the DS 1px hairline borders. The new tokens (`--meter-*`, `--knob-size`, `--track-toggle-*`) are in `tokens.css`.
    - The meter and the M/S toggles take their colors, sizes and fonts from `Theme` (`theme.meter`, `theme.timeline.toggleSize/toggleGap`, `fontOf`).
    - The px values in `src/timeline/view/fixtures.ts` belong to a test fixture. The flash solid's `{r:1,g:1,b:1,a:1}` in `audioDev.ts` is document data in a dev tool. Lint passes.
  - §3/§4:
    - P1: React renders the mute button and the knob only when the settings change. A knob drag writes the DOM and the monitor gain directly.
    - P3: the meter is a framework-free canvas on the scheduler's `meters` flag. It redraws only while audio plays or the bars settle. The track toggles are drawn on the content canvas, and their hover on the overlay.
    - P2, §3.3: each M/S click is one `track.update` command. `analysis.peaks` becomes "ready" through `amend`, with no undo step. The knob lives in the app settings, not in the document.
    - P4: the document gains no floats, only track booleans and the analysis enum. The scheduler's seconds exist only at runtime.
    - P5: MP4 audio decodes in decode.worker, and the peaks are built in analysis.worker from transferred PCM. `decodeAudioData` on the main thread is what §14 prescribes.
    - P13: decoded audio is released when its media leaves the project and cleared when the project closes. See observation 2.
  - TODOs: every new TODO has a `(batch NN)` tag (16, 17, 19, 24, 37, 38, 41, 42, 43). No `TODO(batch 01..10)` is left in `src/`.
  - Earlier rejections: none for this batch.
  - Observations (not blocking; follow-ups added for 1-3):
    1. A track removed while its clips sound is cut off with no ramp.
       - `AudioScheduler.onDocument` (`src/audio/scheduler/AudioScheduler.ts:390`) calls `mixer.updateTracks` first. That disconnects the removed track's node at once (`src/audio/AudioMixer.ts:117`), before the voice loop ramps the voices out.
       - Repro: insert an audio file into a project with no audio track (the insert creates A1), press play, then press Ctrl+Z while it sounds. The clip and A1 go in one undo step, and the sound stops abruptly.
    2. Closing a project does not close the worker's audio tracks. `DecodedAudio.reset()` (`src/audio/decode/DecodedAudio.ts:153`) sends no `closeAudio`; only `release` does (`:145`). decode.worker therefore keeps up to `KEEP` = 8 parsed sample tables and `File` references of the closed project (`src/media/decode/AudioTracks.ts:10`). The amount is bounded, but P13 wants the release to be explicit.
    3. `DecodedAudio.atMostStereo` (`src/audio/decode/DecodedAudio.ts:212`) mixes more than 2 channels down in one synchronous pass on the main thread. Only `decodeAudioData` output with more than 2 channels (5.1 WAV or OGG) goes through it, but a 10-minute file is ~29M sample frames in one long task.
    4. The WebCodecs path keeps the source sample rate (`src/media/decode/AudioTracks.ts:83`, for example 44.1 kHz), where §14 says 48 kHz. Web Audio resamples on playback and the peaks file stores its rate, so nothing sounds or draws differently. The Batch 10 deviation could say so in one line.
    5. `src/audio/index.ts:1` (a Batch 01 placeholder) still says "offline mixdown, scrub grains (Batch 10)". Those come with Batches 43 and 37.

### Batch 11: Selection and moving clips
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Selection (§6.7, `core/store/selection.ts` `clickSelection`):
    - Plain click: the clip and its linked partners (Linked selection on). Shift toggles the clip and its partners, Mod toggles the clip alone, Alt selects the clip alone. The clicked clip becomes the primary.
    - A press on a clip that is already selected waits for the release: a drag moves the whole selection, a click without a drag then applies (Batch 07 follow-up). A press on an unselected clip selects at once, so the drag moves the new selection.
    - Clips on locked tracks take no selection (§6.12); a press on one acts as on empty space.
    - Marquee: a drag on empty track space past the 4 px dead zone. It selects every clip it touches (intersect, not contain) on unlocked tracks, with their linked partners; Shift adds. The rectangle is on the overlay and the proposed selection on the content canvas; the session is written once, on release (the Properties panel subscribes to the selection, P1).
    - `selection.selectAll` (Mod+A: clips on unlocked tracks, the primary kept) and `selection.deselect` (Mod+Shift+A), timeline and preview scopes.
    - Undo restores the selection from before the move (the Batch 02 mechanism). Every move is dispatched with the moved clips (or the copies) selected, the dragged clip primary.
  - Pure edit (`timeline/edit/move.ts`, fully unit tested):
    - `moveClips(doc, ids, deltaFrames, trackMap, mode, { duplicate, newTracks, newId })` returns `noop`, `refused` or one `clip.move` command (one undo step) with the placed ids. The delta is clamped at frame 0. A move onto itself is a no-op (no undo entry), in both modes.
    - Overwrite: each stationary clip under a dropped one loses the covered part: trimmed, removed when fully covered, split when covered in the middle (three pieces for two separate covers). Media clips move in/out by the cut frames at their speed.
    - Insert: the moved block (earliest start to latest end) opens a gap at its start on every unlocked track. Clips crossing that frame are split and the right part shifts with the rest. Locked tracks never move.
    - Refusals: clips on a locked track (`lockedSource`) or a locked destination (`lockedTarget`), in both modes.
    - Duplicates get new ids; copies that shared a link group (or group) share a new one, a lone copy is unlinked. Moving clips never overwrite each other (they leave their place first); a copy may overwrite its original.
    - `sliceClip`: keyframes partitioned by frame and re-based (an edge that did not move keeps the keyframes past it); a new edge drops that side's fade, transition and text animation. Split pieces of one link group that start at the same frame (cut at the same frame, whatever their lengths) are linked to each other under a new group; a lone piece is unlinked.
    - Lanes (`laneOf`, `clampLaneDelta`, `laneTarget`, `mapLanes`): video and audio tracks numbered outward from the divider. A vertical move shifts every clip by the same lane delta within its kind, so V1 -> V2 takes A1 -> A2 (§6.8 "same track delta"). Each kind is clamped at its first lane; caption clips never change track. Lanes past the last track become new tracks (V3, A4..., in lane order, at the default places).
    - `applyMoveRequest` (lane delta -> track map -> `moveClips`), `nudgeClips`, `moveToAdjacentLane`.
    - Collision lookups: per-track `ends` (sorted, since clips never overlap) + `clipsOverlapping`, a binary search on the ends: O(log n + k) per clip.
  - Drag gesture (`timeline/view/gestures/moveClips.ts`, `MoveGesture`):
    - 4 px dead zone (`--timeline-drag-threshold`). The proposal (delta, lane deltas, mode, duplicate, ghosts, new track lanes, overwritten spans, insert frame, refusal) is recomputed only when its outcome changes; the overlay draws it. The document is untouched until the drop, which sends one `MoveRequest` to the controller (P2).
    - The anchor (pressed clip) follows the row under the pointer within its kind. Above the top video track (or below the bottom audio track) means a new track; the other kind's side clamps to lane 0. Shift keeps every clip on its track, Alt duplicates, Mod inserts. Modifier keys pressed or released mid-drag update the ghost at once (window key listeners while dragging; a lone Alt key is preventDefault-ed so Windows does not focus the browser menu).
    - Esc: `app.cancel` -> `TimelineView.cancelGesture()` (declined when nothing runs, so popovers still get Esc). A document edit under a drag (an undo key) cancels it.
  - Overlay (§6.17 order): new track rows (header with the name it will get), ghosts at 70% (kind colors, accent outline; red when refused), hatching over the overwritten spans, the insert line with an arrow, the marquee, the playhead, the `Δ +00:00:12` tooltip (mm:ss:ff).
  - Auto-scroll (§6.6) for scrub, move and marquee: within 24 px of the track area's edges or past them, 12 px per 60 Hz frame at the edge and up to 3x past it, scaled by frame time. A drag that proposes new tracks gets extra vertical room so auto-scroll brings their rows into view; the view settles back into range after the drag.
  - Keys: `edit.nudgeLeft/Right(5)` (Alt+Left/Right 1 frame, Alt+Shift 5: overwrite, coalesceKey `clip.nudge`, so a burst is one undo step; handled even with nothing selected because Alt+Left is the browser's Back). `edit.moveToTrackAbove/Below` (Alt+Up/Down): to the adjacent track if the spans there are free; the primary clip's kind sets the direction and linked audio follows with the same lane delta; no tracks are created.
  - Refusals are toasts: locked track (error), occupied adjacent track (info).
  - Dev: `__editor.tracks.list()` / `.update("V2", { locked: true })` (track flags for the checks until the Batch 16 header buttons).
  - IdleQueue: named pausers (`"playback"`, `"gesture"`), so one resume never undoes another's pause (Batch 09 follow-up). Scrub, move and marquee pause it.
  - Measured:
    - 714 unit tests in 87 files (+50 tests, +2 files: `move.test.ts`, `autoScroll.test.ts`), plus the perf project.
    - Build: 437.34 kB JS (143.79 kB gzip, +24.03 / +7.70 kB), 38.68 kB CSS (+0.24 kB); workers unchanged.
    - Setup: headless Chrome 155 over CDP (real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`/`dispatchKeyEvent`, modifiers included), dev server, OPFS projects `b11-a1`..`b11-a5`, `b11-perf`; 1600x900.
    - Acceptance 1 (`b11-a1`): "short" (V2, 0..60) dragged onto the middle of "long" (V1, 0..300) at frame 120. Mid-drag: hatch [120, 180), 0 document changes. Drop: `long@0+120 short@120+60 long@180+120`, one undo step "Move clip". Ctrl+Z: `long@0+300`, short back on V2.
    - Acceptance 2 (`b11-a2`): V1 a/b, V2 locked (L 60..120), V3 c (30..90) and m (400..430), A1 a 20 s WAV bed. m dragged to V1 at 60 with Ctrl at the drop: V1 `a@0 m@60 b@120`, V3 `c@30+30 c@90+30` (split and shifted), A1 `bed@0+60 bed@90+540` (split, the gap opened), the locked V2 unchanged. Ctrl+drop onto V2: red ghosts, refused, toast "Can't drop clips onto a locked track / V2 is locked..."; a plain drop onto V2: refused, layout and history unchanged.
    - Acceptance 3 (`b11-a3`): p (V1) and q (V2) selected with click + Shift+click, p dragged up one row: `V2 p@10`, `V3 q@50` (offsets kept). q (now on the top track) dragged above it: the proposal has a new lane "V4", drawn as a row with the ghost in it (auto-scroll revealed it); the drop creates V4 with q and puts p on V3; Ctrl+Z removes V4.
    - Acceptance 4 (`b11-a4`): x and y selected, Alt+drag by 100 frames: originals stay, copies at 100 and 130, the copies selected, "Duplicate clips". A drag then a real Escape mid-drag: no proposal, the release changes nothing, the history is unchanged.
    - Acceptance 5 (`b11-a5`): a marquee from empty space (V1 frame 95) to V2 frame 30 selects k1, k2, k3 (all partly covered); the session showed no selection during the marquee. The selection moved by 40 frames, then Ctrl+Z: layout restored and the selection still k1, k2, k3.
    - Acceptance 6 (`b11-perf`): `generate(500, 8)`, 200 clips selected (the earliest by start, across 8 tracks), one dragged for 4 s with lane changes every second (real mouse events, ~30-38 moves/s over CDP). rAF baseline idle: 16.66 ms mean.
      - Fit (all 200 ghosts on screen): 240 frames, mean 16.66 ms, max 17.5 ms, 0 frames over 25 ms; overlay mean 0.69 ms, max 1.3 ms; proposal mean 0.18 ms, max 0.8 ms; 0 React commits; 0 content draws.
      - 1 px/frame (labels and hatching): mean 16.66 ms, max 18.1 ms, 0 over 25 ms; overlay max 2.1 ms. With Ctrl/Alt toggled during the drag (insert line, duplicates): max 17.8 ms, overlay max 3.2 ms.
      - The drop (one command over 200 clips, dev validation included): 7-9.6 ms, 1 React commit (the history store).
    - P1: 0 commits during every drag and marquee; the only commits are the click selection at the press (Properties panel) and the drop.
    - Regression (Batch 10): seeks while playing restarted audio 21.5-32.2 ms after the seek on the render clock (within 50 ms), largest sample step 0.0288 (the tone's own slope, no click); M/S toggles took effect in 8.7-9.8 ms with one undo step each.
    - Regression (Batch 09, audio clock): 60 one-second 1080p30 cuts 7 / 0 / 13, then 0 / 0 / 0 of 1799 dropped, every render exact, 2 commits per pass.
    - 4K60 HEVC read 8.8-38% right after the 1080p passes and the drag runs, and 6% after a 10 min rest, so it was A/B-tested: the Batch 10 commit and this tree served alternately on the same origin (same OPFS project), one pass each, 25 s apart. Batch 10: 4.17 / 2.75 / 1.75%; Batch 11: 0.33 / 2.59 / 4.42% (means 2.9% and 2.5%). No regression; the spread is the GPU heat noted since Batch 09.
- deviations:
  - Insert collisions with locked tracks: locked tracks never shift and nothing lands on them, so the only insert refusal is a locked destination (or source) track. It is reported by an error toast, as in overwrite mode.
  - Insert splits the clips crossing the insert frame on every unlocked track and shifts the right parts (Premiere's insert with sync locks on), so all unlocked tracks stay in sync. The gap is the moved block's span (earliest start to latest end).
  - Mod+click toggles the clip alone (§6.7 "add without linked partner"), so a second Mod+click removes it.
  - Alt on an already-selected clip keeps the selection, so Alt+drag duplicates all of it; Alt+click without a drag then selects only that clip (Premiere's Alt behavior).
  - A click on empty space clears the selection on release, not on press (a press there may start a marquee). Mod does nothing special for the marquee.
  - Keyframes are partitioned by frame on overwrite trims and splits, with no boundary keyframes: eases are opaque until Batch 35.
  - Alt+Up/Down never create tracks: each kind stops at its outermost existing track, and the move is refused when a destination span is taken.
  - Dragging above the top video track proposes the anchor's next lane (one new track for a single clip); a multi-track selection whose top clip is not the anchor may need more than one new track, all shown as rows.
  - The ruler scrub also auto-scrolls near the edges and pauses the idle queue (§6.6 lists the playhead; §3.4 pauses while dragging).
- follow-ups: [from Batch 11 -> Batch 12] snapping in the drag, hold S, snap name in the tooltip; [from Batch 11 -> Batch 16] group selection, track header selection, locked-track hatching, sync badges after offsets; [from Batch 11 -> Batch 32] caption words on split, new video rows over the caption row; [from Batch 11 -> Batch 35] boundary keyframes on split; [from Batch 11 -> Batch 46] tooltip shadow on canvas
- design gaps: drag visuals (ghosts, refused ghost, hatching, insert line, marquee, new track rows, drag tooltip), drag dead zone and auto-scroll band tokens (details in Design system gaps)
- pending manual checks:
  1. Split (acceptance 1): drag a short clip onto the middle of a longer one on another track. While dragging, the part it will cover is hatched and the tooltip reads `Δ +mm:ss:ff`. After the drop the long clip is two clips around it; Ctrl+Z gives back the single clip.
  2. Insert (acceptance 2): lock a track (no lock button until Batch 16; in a dev build's console: `__editor.tracks.update("V2", { locked: true })`). Drag a clip and hold Ctrl before releasing: a gold insert line shows, and later clips on every unlocked track shift right; the locked track does not move. Dropping onto the locked track turns the ghost red and shows a toast.
  3. Tracks (acceptance 3): select clips on V1 and V2, drag them up one row: both move up, same offsets. Drag above the top video track: a new track row appears with the ghost in it (the view scrolls to show it), and the drop creates the track.
  4. Duplicate and cancel (acceptance 4): Alt+drag a clip: the original stays and a copy lands at the drop. Start another drag and press Esc before releasing: nothing changes, and Ctrl+Z still undoes the duplicate (the cancelled drag left no undo step). Press and release Alt alone during a drag: the browser menu must not take the focus (Windows).
  5. Marquee (acceptance 5): drag on empty track space across parts of several clips: all touched clips light up gold while dragging and stay selected; move them and press Ctrl+Z: they are still selected.
  6. Performance (acceptance 6): in a dev build run `__editor.timeline.generate(500, 8)`, select 200 clips (Mod+A selects all 500, also a fine test) and drag: the ghosts follow at 60 fps; React DevTools Profiler shows no commit during the drag.
  7. Playback regression (Batch 09, 4K60): on a rested machine the 4K60 HEVC project plays with about 1% dropped (Batch 10's rested numbers); headless runs here stayed at 0.3-4.4% for both Batch 10 and Batch 11 code (GPU heat).
  8. Nudge and Alt+Up/Down: with a clip selected and the timeline focused, Alt+Left/Right moves it 1 frame (Shift: 5), overwriting what it lands on; a burst of nudges is one Ctrl+Z. Alt+Up/Down moves it to the next track when free.

- review: rejected
  1. `src/timeline/view/drawOverlay.ts:159-176` (`drawHatch`, called at line 133): the hatch loop runs over the whole overwritten span in CSS px, never clamped to the visible track area, so its cost grows with zoom x overlap length instead of the view width (§6.17 BINDING "off-screen clips are skipped entirely", P10, P1/P14 60 fps drags). Measured in headless Chrome 155 with the same loop (6 px gap, 52 px rows, overhead of a 10-segment span subtracted, about 2 ms): a 30 s overlap at 100 px/frame is 15,000 segments (~2 ms); a 2 min overlap at 400 px/frame (the §6.2 zoom limit) is 240,000 segments (~20 ms per ghost per overlay frame); a 10 min music bed at 400 px/frame is 1.2 M segments (~100 ms). Clamped to the view, the same 10 min case is 247 segments. Reproduce: zoom the timeline in fully, drag a long clip so it overlaps another long clip on the destination track, and watch the overlay frame time (dev stats chip). Fix: intersect [x0, x1) with `geometry.area` (both the clip rect and the loop bounds, keeping the grid anchored by x so the pattern does not crawl) and return when the span is empty.
  2. `src/timeline/edit/move.ts:298` (`Edit.relinkPieces`): split pieces are grouped by `linkGroup + start + duration`, so the pieces of a linked pair cut at the same frame are unlinked whenever the two clips end at different frames. Reproduced with a throwaway test: `lv` V1 [0,100) and `la` A1 [0,120) linked as `L`, clip `m` (V2 at 300, 10 frames) moved with `moveClips(p, ["m"], -240, {v2 -> v1}, "insert")`. The left halves keep `L`, but the right pieces V1 [70,110) and A1 [70,130) both come out with no link group, so they no longer select or move together (§6.12). This contradicts the batch's own note ("pieces cut at the same frames from one link group are linked to each other"). In Batch 11 such pairs come from an overwrite that trims one side (a drop on the audio tail) or an Alt-dragged offset. Each clip gives at most one piece starting at a given frame, so keying on `linkGroup + start` keeps the right pieces linked (insert: all start at `from + length`; overwrite: all start at the cover's end) without ever linking two pieces of the same clip. Add a `move.test.ts` case for the pair above, in insert and in overwrite mode.

The lines stay on the same x grid (`floor((left - height) / gap) * gap`), so the pattern does not crawl. The line count now follows the view width, not the zoom or the overlap length.

### Batch 12: Timeline snapping
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Snap index (`src/timeline/snap/snapIndex.ts`, replaces the `index.ts` stub, fully unit tested):
    - Candidates in the §6.9 priority order: playhead, clip starts and ends on every track (locked ones included), markers, the in and out points, frame 0. The playhead is passed in at query time; in the index it would need a rebuild per frame during playback. Keyframes and transition edges are `TODO(batch 35)` and `TODO(batch 36)`.
    - `buildSnapCandidates`: one pass over clips and markers into packed float64 keys (`(frame * 8 + kind) * 2^21 + entry`) and one numeric typed-array sort (no comparator calls), then frame, kind and ref arrays.
    - `SnapIndex.get(project, inPoint, outPoint)` rebuilds lazily: on the first query after `project.clips`, `project.markers` or the in/out points changed (compared by reference, the document is immutable). Never per pointer move, never on a document change by itself.
    - `resolveSnap(candidates, edges, { thresholdPx, pxPerFrame, exclude, playhead })`: a binary search per edge into the ±threshold window. The smallest screen distance wins, then the priority, then the earlier edge, then the lower frame. `exclude` takes clip (or marker) ids. `snapLabel` names the target: "End of b", "Playhead", "Marker: Drop", "In point", "Timeline start".
  - Clip drags (`MoveGesture.update(..., snap)`): the block's leading and trailing edges are tested at the raw fractional delta, and the closer one lands on the candidate. The delta is then clamped at frame 0; a trailing-edge snap that would push the block below 0 is dropped. The dragged clips are no candidates. While Alt duplicates, the originals stay put and are candidates, so a copy snaps right after its original. The proposal carries `snap`, and the ghost is drawn at the snapped position, so it sticks until the pointer leaves the threshold. A playhead move during a drag (playback) re-proposes.
  - Ruler scrub (§6.3): the playhead snaps only while Shift is held and snapping is on, to every candidate but the playhead. Shift or S pressed or released mid-scrub re-snaps at once. The wheel during a scrub now moves the playhead with the view, as auto-scroll does.
  - Toggle (`toggle.snap`: S and the toolbar magnet) flips session `snap` (persisted in session.json). The magnet is enabled and shows the state through its own one-flag subscription: one React commit per toggle. Holding S during any drag (clip move, scrub, marquee) inverts snapping until it is released, without touching the session (0 commits). The drag's window key listener reads S in the capture phase (see deviations). A key repeat (S pressed before the drag) does not hold.
  - Overlay: a 1 px `--accent` snap line through the full timeline height (ruler and tracks, not the header column) at the snapped frame, after the hover and before the ghosts (§6.17 order). The drag tooltip shows the target's name after the delta, in the caption UI font and `--accent`; a Shift scrub shows the name alone.
  - Fixed during the browser check: the binary search window lacked the distance test's float tolerance. A pointer exactly 8 px away (edge 62.666666666666686 for 60 + 8/3 at 3 px/frame) found no candidate. The window now uses the same epsilon; the unit test fails on the old code.
  - Test isolation: `TimelineView.test.ts` unmounts every view after each test (a drag left open kept its window key listeners into the next test).
  - Measured:
    - 743 unit tests in 89 files (+24 tests, +1 file `snapIndex.test.ts`), plus `snapIndex.perf.test.ts` in the perf project.
    - Snap index rebuild, 500 clips on 8 tracks + 20 markers (1023 candidates), Node: median 0.10 ms, cold 0.60-0.77 ms; one query 1.1 µs. In Chrome (`generate(500, 8)`, 1001 candidates): median 0.1 ms, p95 0.2 ms, max 0.7 ms (0.1 ms timer resolution).
    - Build: 442.44 kB JS (145.64 kB gzip, +4.95 / +1.75 kB), 38.74 kB CSS (+0.06 kB); workers unchanged.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP, dev server, real mouse and key events, OPFS projects `b12-a1` and `b12-perf` (`%TEMP%/b12/acc.mjs`).
      - Acceptance 1: b (V1, 200..260) dragged so its start is 8 px after a's end (60). At 3 and 0.5 px/frame, and at 12 px/frame (d on V2, 80..100, view scrolled to 30), the proposal snapped to 60 ("End of a"). The overlay column at frame 60 was `--accent` in the ruler, in the tracks and at the bottom, and the tooltip read `Δ -00:04:20` + `End of a` (accent). 3 px closer: same delta (sticky). 9 px: free (-137 / -122 / -19 frames), no line. Far away: no snap. React commits during the drags: none apart from the press's click selection.
      - Acceptance 2: the magnet click turned snapping off (aria-pressed false, 1 commit). Mid-drag at 8 px: free (-137). S down: snapped (-140) with the line; 8 auto-repeats: still snapped; S up: free (-137) at once, no line; Shift+S: snapped (read as S, not Shift+S). 0 commits during the drag, the magnet stayed off, session `snap` stayed false. S outside a drag turned snapping back on (magnet pressed).
      - Acceptance 3: a ruler press 8 px before b's start: playhead 197 without Shift, 200 with Shift (line in the ruler, label "Start of b"), back to 197 at once on Shift up, 197 at 9 px. Snapping off: Shift alone 197, Shift + S held 200. The playhead head dragged with Shift near a's end: 60.
      - Acceptance 4: the unit perf test and the browser numbers above.
    - Regression (Batch 11, `%TEMP%/b11/acc.mjs`): acceptance 1-5 give the same layouts, history labels, refusals, toasts and selections as recorded. The 500-clip drag with 200 selected (`perf.mjs`): Fit mean 16.66 ms, max 17.5 ms, 0 frames over 25 ms, overlay mean 0.81 ms (max 1.6), proposal update mean 0.20 ms (max 0.6). At 1 px/frame with modifiers: max 18.1 ms, overlay max 1.8 ms. 0 commits, 0 content draws.
    - Regression (Batch 10, `%TEMP%/b10/a23.mjs`): seeks while playing restarted the audio 10.7-11 ms after the seek (full level by 21 ms), largest sample step 0.0288 (the tone's own slope, no click). Mute/solo took effect in 8.7-9.8 ms.
- deviations:
  - During a drag the drag's window key listener reads S in the capture phase and stops it, so it never reaches the KeyDispatcher (§3.6: a single KeyDispatcher listens on window). Reason: S must act as a modifier while Shift (the scrub's snap key, the move's keep-track key), Alt or Mod is held, and Shift+S and Mod+S are other commands (linked selection, save). Key-ups pass on, so the dispatcher releases an S pressed before the drag. Outside drags S goes through the dispatcher (`toggle.snap`).
  - The drag matches S as the dispatcher does: by the letter typed, or by the key position when no letter comes (Option+S types "ß" on macOS).
  - While Alt duplicates, the originals are snap candidates. §6.9 excludes "the clips being dragged"; with Alt the copies move and the originals stay.
  - A scrub snap's line lies under the 2 px active playhead in the track area (both are on the same frame). The gold shows in the ruler above the playhead head and in the label.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 12 -> Batch 13] trim snapping through `resolveSnap`; [from Batch 12 -> Batch 19] marker and in/out handle drags snap; [from Batch 12 -> Batch 35] keyframe candidates; [from Batch 12 -> Batch 36] transition edges; [from Batch 12 -> Batch 46] scrub snap line under the playhead, tooltip type mix
- design gaps: snap threshold and line tokens, snap line and the target name in the drag tooltip (details in Design system gaps)
- pending manual checks:
  1. Snap (acceptance 1): drag a clip so its start comes near another clip's end, at two zoom levels (`=` and `-`). Within 8 px it jumps onto the edge: a gold line runs through the whole timeline and the tooltip reads the move plus "End of <clip>" in gold. Small moves keep it there; moving away lets it go.
  2. Hold S (acceptance 2): turn snapping off with the magnet (or press S with the timeline focused). Drag a clip near an edge: no snapping. Hold S: it snaps at once. Release S: it lets go at once. The magnet does not change during the drag.
  3. Ruler (acceptance 3): drag on the ruler near a clip edge: the playhead moves freely. Hold Shift: it jumps onto the edge, with the name under the ruler. Release Shift: free again.
  4. Real keyboards: during a clip drag hold Shift and S, then Ctrl and S: snapping inverts, no save dialog opens, nothing else toggles. On macOS, Option+S during an Alt-duplicate drag.
- review request: docs/PROGRESS.md src/app/timelineController.ts src/core/theme/theme.ts src/styles/tokens.css src/timeline/snap/index.ts src/timeline/snap/snapIndex.perf.test.ts src/timeline/snap/snapIndex.test.ts src/timeline/snap/snapIndex.ts src/timeline/toolbar/TimelineToolbar.tsx src/timeline/view/TimelineView.test.ts src/timeline/view/TimelineView.ts src/timeline/view/drawOverlay.test.ts src/timeline/view/drawOverlay.ts src/timeline/view/fixtures.ts src/timeline/view/gestures/moveClips.ts
- review: accepted. `npm run check` green (743 unit tests in 89 files, 2 perf tests, build 442.44 kB JS); the snap perf test re-run alone gave cold 0.62 ms, median 0.10 ms. Acceptance 1-3 are covered by `TimelineView.test.ts` (8 px snap at 4 and 1 px/frame with line, label and release; hold S both ways with the shortcuts never reached; Shift-only ruler snap) and `drawOverlay.test.ts`, run in the browser and listed under pending manual checks 1-3. Acceptance 4 is covered by `snapIndex.perf.test.ts`. Nothing from Do not build yet (no trim snapping; keyframes and transition edges are `TODO(batch 35)` and `TODO(batch 36)`). The only new literals are in `tokens.css` and the test token fixture. One command per drop (integer `deltaFrames`), the hold on S never writes the session, the snap line is drawn on the overlay through the scheduler. Every TODO has a batch tag. Minor, not blocking: a window blur while S is held mid-drag leaves `snapHeld` set until the drop, because the key-up is lost (the KeyDispatcher releases its keys on blur, the drag listener does not).
- docs: timeline.md (snapping: snap clips into place, turn snapping on or off, hold S for one drag, Shift-snap the playhead in the ruler; wheel during a ruler drag), keyboard-shortcuts.md (Snapping), troubleshooting.md (two Timeline entries, limitations), whats-new.md, workspace.md, README.md, projects-and-saving.md, preview-and-playback.md in docs/user-guide/ (2026-10-15)

### Batch 13: Trimming I: regular and ripple trims, trim to playhead
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Pure edit (`src/timeline/edit/trim.ts`, fully unit tested in `trim.test.ts`, 35 tests):
    - `planTrim(doc, clipIds, edge, { ripple, linked })` resolves the delta-independent part once per gesture: the trimmed clips (the given ones and, unless `linked: false`, their partners on unlocked tracks), the clips a ripple shifts, and the range of deltas every limit allows. `resolveTrim(plan, delta)` clamps the request into it and builds one `clip.trim` command (only clip records change; trims never reorder a track). `trimEdge(doc, clipId, edge, delta, { ripple })` is the two together; `applyTrim` gives the document the command leaves (the live preview and the overlay draw it).
    - Limits, each an interval of deltas, reported by reason (`mediaStart`, `mediaEnd`, `minDuration`, `neighbor`, `ripple`, `timelineStart`): the 1-frame minimum; the media bounds of video and audio clips (`headFrames`, `maxDurationFrames`, computed with `framesToSourceUs` so a trim and its reverse restore in and out exactly, also at speed != 1 within 1 us; none for images, text, solids, captions); frame 0; the neighbor clip (regular); the ripple collisions (ripple: the gap before the first shifted clip of every unlocked track); each partner's own (both stop when the partner hits its bound first). `mediaBound` says when a trimmed edge sits on its media bound (red edge).
    - Ripple: the in point keeps its position (the content slides under it), the clips after the edit point on every unlocked track shift by the change in duration; locked tracks never move.
    - Clip changes: in trims move `start` (regular) and `in`; out trims move `out` by the delta, capped at the media end; stills keep `in` with `out = in + duration`. Video and audio keyframes follow the source on in trims (out-of-range keys kept); fades are clamped to the new duration. Hooks: `TODO(batch 36)` transitions, `TODO(batch 32)` caption words, `TODO(batch 34)` animated images, `TODO(batch 42)` the follow-source setting.
    - `trimToPlayhead(doc, playhead, "start" | "end")` (Q/W, see deviations) and `extendToPlayhead(doc, clipId, playhead)` (E: the nearest edge, regular trim with partners).
  - Hit testing (`HitTester.ts`): `clipEdgeIn` / `clipEdgeOut` for the outer `--timeline-trim-zone` (8 px) inside each clip edge, a third of a narrow clip at most; where clips abut the left side is the left clip's out point and the right side the right clip's in point. None on locked tracks.
  - Gesture (`timeline/view/gestures/trimClip.ts`, `TrimGesture`): a press on a trim zone becomes a trim past the 4 px dead zone (a click without a drag selects the clip as a body click would; the selection is not changed by a trim). The edge follows the pointer from the press; Mod or Ripple mode ripples, Alt trims the clip without its partners (both update mid-drag from the drag's key listener). The dragged edge snaps through `resolveSnap` to every candidate but the clips the trim moves (the trimmed ones and, when rippling, the shifted ones); a snap the limits do not reach does not hold. One plan per (ripple, linked) combination; the proposal is recomputed only when its outcome changes. The release sends one `TrimRequest`; Esc or a document change cancels. Auto-scroll is horizontal.
  - Live feedback: the preview panel shows the proposed document at the new edge frame (the first included frame of an in trim, the last of an out trim) through `PreviewView`'s new `override` dep, with scrub priority (P8); the trim pauses playback. The overlay redraws the rows the trim changes (the trimmed clips' old and new extents, a ripple's rows from the first shifted clip on) from the proposed document, then the ghost edge bar (accent; danger at a media bound), the snap line and the tooltip `Δ -00:00:10 · 00:01:00` with the snap name and `Media start` / `Media end`. Bracket cursors `[` / `]`, gold for the ripple variant (Ripple mode on, or Mod held: the view reads Mod while hovered). The hovered clip stays lit on its trim zones.
  - Keys and toolbar (`timelineController.ts`): `edit.rippleTrimPrev` (Q), `edit.rippleTrimNext` (W), `edit.extendToPlayhead` (E), one command each, with an info toast when a limit stopped them short (reason, clip or track named). `toggle.rippleMode` flips session `ripple` (persisted); the toolbar Ripple mode button shows it through its own one-flag subscription.
  - Sync badge (minimal, for acceptance 5): `linkOffset(doc, clipId)` (queries) and a `+N` / `-N` chip on the label bar of offset linked clips, on the content canvas and in the trim preview.
  - Measured:
    - 792 unit tests in 90 files (+49 tests, +1 file `trim.test.ts`), plus the 2 perf tests.
    - Build: 456.85 kB JS (150.12 kB gzip, +14.41 / +4.48 kB), 38.80 kB CSS (+0.06 kB); workers unchanged.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP (real mouse and key events), dev server, OPFS projects `b13-a`, `b13-q`, `b13-perf`, `b13-shot`, a 360x640 30 fps H.264 + AAC test file whose frames carry their index in stripes (`%TEMP%/b13/page.js`, `acc.mjs`, `qaudio.mjs`, `perf.mjs`, `shot.mjs`).
      - Acceptance 1: the out point of a linked 300-frame pair dragged to frame 200: the preview showed stripe 199 52 ms after the move, then 149 31 ms after a move to 150; the document unchanged and 0 React commits during the drag. Release: both clips 200 frames, one undo step "Trim clips" (on top of "Insert clip"); the preview back on the playhead's frame; undo restores 300.
      - Acceptance 2: x (V1 0..100) out point dragged 60 frames left with Ctrl, V3 holding p 40..80 and q 110..130, V4 locked: clamped to -30 (limit `ripple` on q), V1 `x@0+70 y@70+60`, V2 `t@90+20` (u unchanged), V3 `q@80+20`, V4 `L1@100+50` unchanged, "Ripple trim". The toolbar Ripple mode button (aria-pressed true) rippled a plain drag by -20 the same way.
      - Acceptance 3: k (V1 100..200, in 30) out point dragged far left: 1 frame; zoomed to 40 px/frame and back to 4, dragged back to 100 frames: `k@100+100(in 30)`. Its in point pulled 60 frames left: clamped at -30 (`mediaStart`), the overlay pixel at the edge `229,84,90` (`--danger`), the tooltip "Media start", the preview showed stripe 0 (152 ms); release: `k@70+130(in 0)`.
      - Acceptance 4: k1 (0..150) and k2 (150..300) playing at 1x; Q at frame 57: `k1@0+93(in 57) k2@93+150`, the playhead to 0, playback on (frame 43 1.5 s later), "Ripple trim start to playhead". Over 102 renders the source frame on screen never went back and never skipped (largest step 1). With a linked pair (`qaudio.mjs`): Q at 55 trimmed video and audio (in 55), W at 34 cut both tails, the audio clock kept running and playback went on; no console errors.
      - Acceptance 5: the linked pair's out point dragged 50 frames left: both 250 frames. With Alt, 20 more: video 230, audio 250; `linkOffset` -20 / +20, and the content canvas drew "-20" and "+20" in `--danger` (`%TEMP%/b13/a5-badge.png`).
      - Ripple over 500 clips on 8 tracks (`generate(500, 8)`, Ctrl-drag of an out point for 3 s, ~27 moves/s): at Fit (480 clips shifted) frames mean 16.66 ms, max 17.5 ms, 0 over 25 ms, overlay mean 0.58 ms, max 2.6 ms; at 1 px/frame (491 shifted) max 17.9 ms, overlay max 1.1 ms. 0 React commits, 0 content draws. The release (one command over ~490 clips with dev validation) and undo + redo: 10-12 ms.
    - Regression (Batch 11, `%TEMP%/b11/acc.mjs`): acceptance 1-5 give the recorded layouts, history labels, refusals, toasts and selections.
    - Regression (Batch 12, `%TEMP%/b12/acc.mjs`): acceptance 1-4 as recorded (snaps at 3, 0.5 and 12 px/frame with the gold line and "End of a", hold S both ways, Shift ruler snap); snap index rebuild median 0.1 ms, p95 0.2 ms (one 5.1 ms max).
- deviations:
  - Q / W use one edit point (the latest start, the earliest end of the clips under the playhead on targeted tracks), trim the clips with their edge there plus their linked partners, and ripple every unlocked track; clips spanning the edit point are left alone; clips starting at the playhead are not trimmed. After Q the playhead moves to the edit point (the picture continues); W leaves it. Reasons in Global deviations.
  - E acts on the primary selected clip (or the first selected) with its linked partners (§6.10 "the selected clip").
  - Ripple edit points per track and in-point ripples as in Global deviations; locked clips cannot limit a ripple because nothing ever moves onto their tracks (tested: locked tracks neither move nor limit).
  - The sync badge shows the start offset, else the end offset (Alt-trims produce a badge as §6.10 says); Batch 16 settles the rule with "Move into sync".
  - Keyframes follow the source for video and audio clips only, out-of-range keys kept; fades clamped to the new duration (§6.10 is silent on fades).
  - A trim drag pauses playback; the view reads Mod over the timeline for the ripple cursor without handling keys.
  - Trim zones shrink to a third of a clip narrower than 24 px, so a narrow clip can still be moved.
- follow-ups: [from Batch 12 -> Batch 13] done (trim edges snap through `resolveSnap`, hold S, snap line and name); [from Batch 13 -> Batch 14] junction roll zone, rate stretch edges, reuse of the trim bounds; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 16] target toggles for Q/W, Move into sync and the offset rule; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 32] caption words on trims; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 34] animated images; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 36] transitions on trims; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 42] keyframes-follow-source setting; [from Batch 13 -> Batch 46] trim tooltip over the header, content-end line under the trim preview
- design gaps: trim zone, ghost edge, trim preview, trim tooltip, bracket cursors, sync badge (details in Design system gaps)
- pending manual checks:
  1. Live edge frame (acceptance 1): drag the out point of a clip: while dragging, the preview shows the clip's last included frame (an in point: the first included frame). Release: one Ctrl+Z gives the clip back.
  2. Ripple (acceptance 2): put clips on several tracks, hold Ctrl and drag an out point left: the later clips on every unlocked track follow live and stop where a clip on another track would overlap. Lock a track (no button until Batch 16; dev console `__editor.tracks.update("V2", { locked: true })`): it never moves. The toolbar Ripple mode button does the same without Ctrl; the trim cursor is gold then.
  3. 1 frame and media start (acceptance 3): drag an out point far left (1 frame), zoom in and drag it back. Drag the in point of a clip that starts at its media start further left: the edge and "Media start" in the tooltip turn red and the clip does not grow.
  4. Q during playback (acceptance 4): play, press Q: the clip under the playhead loses its head up to the playhead, the later clips move left, the playhead jumps to the cut and the picture and sound go on without a pause. W cuts the tail and playback moves on to the next clip.
  5. Linked and Alt (acceptance 5): drag the out point of a video with sound: video and audio trim together. Hold Alt and drag again: only that side; both clips show a red +N / -N badge.
  6. Real cursors: the `[` / `]` bracket cursors (SVG data URLs) show on Windows and macOS, gold while Ctrl/Cmd is held over an edge or Ripple mode is on.

### Batch 14: Trimming II: roll, slip, slide, rate stretch, edit points
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Pure edits (`src/timeline/edit/trim.ts`, unit tested in `trim.test.ts`, +16 tests):
    - The plan is generalized to per-clip moves (`ClipMove`: `in`, `out`, `move`, `slip`, `stretchIn`, `stretchOut`) with the Batch 13 interval limits: an edge stops at the facing edge of its neighbor unless that edge moves with it, so a roll's two sides and a slide's neighbors never limit each other. `planTrim` keeps its signature and behavior (the 35 Batch 13 tests pass unchanged); ripple stays a trim option.
    - `planRoll`/`rollEdit(doc, leftId, rightId, delta)`: the left clip's out and the right clip's in (and their partners') move together; total duration unchanged; limited by both media bounds and the 1-frame minimum.
    - `planSlip`/`slip(doc, clipId, delta)`: `in` and `out` move by the same source delta (positive = later frames), keyframes stay on their source frames; video and audio clips only (`tailFrames` bounds the end).
    - `planSlide`/`slide(doc, clipId, delta)`: the clip (and its partners) move; the abutting previous clip's out and next clip's in trim by the same delta; a gap side takes the change, bounded by the clip beyond it or frame 0.
    - `planStretch`/`rateStretch(doc, clipId, edge, delta)`: the edge moves, `speed = speed * oldDuration / newDuration`, `in`/`out` unchanged, 0.25x..4x (`minSpeed`/`maxSpeed` limits), keyframe frames scaled, fades clamped; non-media clips trim.
    - Media bounds are per clip and side (`bounds`): a roll reports which side sits on its bound. `planEdit(doc, spec)` replays a gesture's `EditSpec` (the controller dispatches `TrimRequest`s of every kind through it). Commands `clip.trim`, `clip.roll`, `clip.slip`, `clip.slide`, `clip.rateStretch`: one undo step each ("Rolling edit", "Slip clip(s)", "Slide clip(s)", "Rate stretch clip(s)").
  - Hit testing: `junction` hits on the 6 px band centered on a cut between abutting clips on unlocked tracks (`HitResult.junction`: left, right, frame; `clipId` is the side under the pointer). Tools change what a hit does (§6.18): Select trims edges, rolls cuts with Mod+Alt (cursor switches while hovering), selects the edit point on a click on a cut; Slip (Y) and Slide (U) drag clip bodies (and edges); Rate stretch (R) drags edges, its bodies only select. `tool.select/slip/slide/rateStretch` set `activeTool`; the toolbar tool buttons show it (one-flag subscriptions). Razor and Hand stay for Batches 15 and 18.
  - Gesture: `TrimGesture` takes an edit kind (trim, roll, slip, slide, stretch). Edges and the slid clip's two edges snap (`resolveSnap`, excluding what the edit moves); slips follow the pointer as in Premiere (dragging right shows earlier frames). Alt edits one side of a link (except rolls, which Mod+Alt starts). One plan per (ripple, linked), one request at the release, Esc and document changes cancel, 0 React commits during the drag.
  - Previews: `TrimProposal.previewSplit`: roll = the left clip's new last frame | the right clip's new first frame, slip = the clip's new first | last frame, slide = the left neighbor's new last | the right neighbor's new first frame. `PreviewView` renders it through a second compositor, `LayerTextures.resolveSplit` (one pass over both panes, its own slots per pane, one scrub request per media so the panes never supersede each other) and `Renderer.drawPanes` (one render pass, each pane clipped to its half). Trims and stretches keep the single edge frame.
  - Overlay: ghost edges per edited edge (red only on the side at its media bound), the selected edit point's brackets (roll: `][` in `--danger`; ripple side in `--accent`; also on linked partners' cuts at the same frame while Linked selection is on), tooltips per kind (slip: new source in; stretch: new speed and the speed limits).
  - Edit point selection: `SessionStore.editPoint` (see Global deviations), drawn only while no drag moves clips; Mod+Shift+A, clicks on clips or on empty space clear it.
  - Up/Down: already wired since Batch 07 (`navigationPoints`: clip starts and ends on unlocked tracks, markers, in/out); verified across tracks in the browser. `Mod+Shift+.` (`timeline.centerPlayhead`) centers the view on the playhead within the scroll range.
  - Speed badge: `0.5x` / `2x` (`formatSpeed` in `core/document/time.ts`) on video and audio clips whose speed is not 1, left of the sync badge, on the content canvas and in the trim preview.
  - Audio `playbackRate` = clip speed since Batch 10; a stretch while playing restarts the voice at the new rate (pitch follows) and to the new end (`AudioScheduler.test.ts`).
  - Measured:
    - 823 unit tests in 90 files (+31), plus the 2 perf tests. Build: 469.03 kB JS (153.91 kB gzip, +12.18 / +3.79 kB), 38.83 kB CSS (+0.03 kB); workers unchanged.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP (real mouse and key events), dev server, OPFS projects `b14-a` and `b14-shot`, the Batch 13 stripe-coded 360x640 H.264 + AAC file; split panes read back by redrawing the preview's panes into an offscreen target and decoding the stripes of each half (`%TEMP%/b14/page.js`, `acc.mjs`, `shot.mjs`).
      - Acceptance 1: A (0..100, media 0..100) and B (100..200, media 150..250) at 3 px/frame. Over the cut the cursor is the out bracket, the roll cursor while Ctrl+Alt is held, the out bracket again on release. A Ctrl+Alt drag +20: the split showed A's last frame 119 | B's first frame 170 (49 ms), at -10: 89 | 140 (11 ms); 0 React commits during the drag. Release: `A@0+120(in 0) B@120+80(in 170)`, total 200, one undo step "Rolling edit"; the preview back to one frame. B with 10 frames of head: the roll stopped at -10 (`mediaStart` on B), the overlay pixel right of the cut `229,84,90` (`--danger`), left of it `217,165,33` (`--accent`), tooltip "Media start"; A on its media end: a roll right stayed at 0 (`mediaEnd` on A), the left side red, the right side gold, "Media end".
      - Acceptance 2: Y: slip cursor; dragging S (100..150, media 100..150) 10 frames left slipped the source +10, the split showed 110 | 159, tooltip `Δ +00:00:10 · In 00:03:20`; release `S@100+50(in 110)`, "Slip clip" (1 commit: the press's click selection). U: slide +10 showed L's new last frame 109 | R's new first frame 160; release `L@0+110 S@110+50(in 100) R@160+90(in 160)`: L +10 and R -10, "Slide clip".
      - Acceptance 3: R on a linked 300-frame video + audio pair at 1.2 px/frame: the out edge dragged to 600: both 600 frames, speed 0.5, in 0 and out 10 000 000 us unchanged, "Rate stretch clips", tooltip `Δ +00:10:00 · 00:20:00 · 0.5x`, both clips badged `0.5x`. Playing from 1 s: the audio voice runs at playbackRate 0.5 and the 440 Hz test tone measured 222 Hz on the mix bus (after undo: rate 1, 436 Hz). Ctrl+Shift+. at playhead 400: scrollFrame 248 (expected 248).
      - Acceptance 4: V1 a 0..100, b 100..160, V2 c 40..130, V3 d 20..70: Down from 0 stopped at 20 40 70 100 130 160 (then stayed), Up back at 130 100 70 40 20 0; with V2 locked Down skipped 40 and 130 (20 70 100 160). A click on the a|b cut selected the edit point (both sides, clip selection empty) and drew red brackets on both sides of the cut; Ctrl+click selected the right (ripple) side in gold only; a click on a clip selected it and dropped the edit point.
    - Regression (Batch 13, `%TEMP%/b13/acc.mjs`): acceptance 1-5 as recorded (live edge frames 199/149, ripple layouts, 1-frame trim and back, media start red, Q while playing with 101 renders never going back, Alt trim offsets ±20 with badges). 500-clip ripple (`perf.mjs`): Fit mean 16.66 ms, max 17.7 ms, 0 over 25 ms, overlay max 1.3 ms; 1 px/frame max 17.8 ms; 0 commits, 0 content draws.
    - Regression (Batch 12, `%TEMP%/b12/acc.mjs`): acceptance 1-4 as recorded (snaps at 3, 0.5 and 12 px/frame with the gold line and "End of a", hold S both ways, Shift ruler snap, index rebuild median 0.1 ms).
    - Playback through the new render path (Batch 09 `b09-a1`, 60 one-second 1080p30 cuts): cold 5 dropped, warm passes 0 / 1799 dropped each, 2 React commits per pass (play and pause), no render without its exact frame.
- deviations:
  - Edit point selection in its own session channel, not in `Selection` (§3.3 fixes the kinds); not persisted.
  - Junction band: a plain drag trims the side under the pointer, Mod+Alt rolls, a click selects the edit point (roll; ripple side with Mod or Ripple mode); §6.10's hit-priority sentence read as §6.4's inside-the-clip zones.
  - The split preview shows two whole frames, each fitted in its half (Premiere's trim monitor), as a preview override field rather than inside `Compositor.evaluate`.
  - Renderer: layer matrices mapped to target px on the CPU, per-record scissors (`drawPanes`).
  - Slip direction as in Premiere (drag right = earlier source); slipped keyframes follow the source.
  - Slide with a gap side; rolls and slides take linked partners with their own neighbors.
  - Rate stretch: speed from the duration ratio, 0.25x..4x, proportional keyframes, clamped fades, non-media clips trim, no ripple.

Drag: the preview shows the left clip's last frame and the right clip's first frame side by side, the two durations change together. Drag toward a clip that has no more media: it stops, its half of the cut turns red and the tooltip says Media start / Media end.
  2. Slip and slide (acceptance 2): press Y and drag a clip: its position stays, the preview shows its new first and last frames. Press U and drag a clip between two others: it moves, the left neighbor gets longer by what the right one loses; the preview shows their new edge frames.

Acceptance 1 is covered by the `rollEdit` tests in `trim.test.ts` (total duration kept, both media bounds with the side that hit reported in `bounds`, 1-frame minimum, partners' cut), the overlay test (red only on the bound side), the view roll test (Mod+Alt cursor, `previewSplit` 59 | 60, document untouched, one request), run in the browser, manual check 1. Acceptance 2 by the `slip` / `slide` tests (in and out by the same delta, neighbors trimmed symmetrically, neighbor bounds, gap side), the Slip/Slide view tests (both new edge frames in `previewSplit`), `PreviewView.test.ts` (two frames side by side, back to one) and `LayerTextures.test.ts` (one pass, one scrub request per media), manual check 2. Acceptance 3 by the `rateStretch` tests (doubled duration gives 0.5x, in/out unchanged, partners, 0.25x..4x) and `AudioScheduler.test.ts` (the voice restarts at playbackRate 0.5); pitch measured in the browser (222 Hz), manual check 3. Acceptance 4 by `queries.test.ts` (`navigationPoints` across tracks with markers and in/out, locked tracks skipped), the edit point click view test, the bracket overlay test and `sessionStore.test.ts`, manual check 4. My own probe (not committed): 400 random projects x 25 random trims, ripples, rolls, slips, slides and stretches (linked and Alt, off-grid media end, speeds 0.5 and 2) all left valid documents through a validating store, with the roll, slip and stretch invariants holding. Nothing from Do not build yet (no speed UI in properties; keyframes only scaled proportionally, `TODO(batch 35)`). No color literals; the junction band is a token (`--timeline-junction-zone`); the new cursors reuse the 24-unit SVG geometry noted in the Batch 13 review (listed under Design system gaps). One command per release (`clip.roll`, `clip.slip`, `clip.slide`, `clip.rateStretch`), integer frames (speed is not time), the split preview drawn in the scheduler's preview render in one pass, the edit point in session state (written deviation), tool buttons on one-flag subscriptions. Every TODO has a batch tag. The Batch 13 note on §6.10's hit-priority sentence now has its written deviation. Minor, not blocking: (1) speed is recomputed from the current speed on every stretch, so separate drags drift: 13 -> 23 -> 13 frames leaves speed 0.9999999999999999 (the clip then shows a `1x` badge), and a full stretch after that gives 0.24999999999999997, just under `MIN_SPEED`. Snapping near-integer speeds (or computing the speed from (out - in) / duration) belongs with the Batch 27 speed UI, which reuses this math. (2) A stretched linked pair with a sync offset gets the same frame delta on each side, so the two sides end up at different speeds.
