### Batch 15: Split, delete, gaps, compact
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Pure edits (`src/timeline/edit/`, unit tested):
    - `split.ts` (`split.test.ts`, 10 tests): `clipsToSplit(doc, frame, scope, { linked })`: the selection's clips under the frame, the targeted unlocked tracks' (nothing selected) or every unlocked track's, plus linked partners under the frame. `splitClips(doc, ids, frame, newId)`: one `clip.split` command ("Split clip(s)"). The left piece keeps the id; the right piece gets a new one, inserted after it in the track's list. `sliceClip` (Batch 11) moves in/out at the clip's speed, partitions keyframes and fades, and drops the transition and text animation on the cut side: the transition removal hook. Right pieces of one link group form a new group; a lone right piece is unlinked. A frame on a clip's start or end splits nothing. `selectionAfterSplit`: both pieces of selected clips stay selected, the primary moves to the right piece.
    - `gaps.ts` (`gaps.test.ts`, 12 tests; `gaps.perf.test.ts`): `closeGaps(doc, gaps, { removed })` is the one ripple behind close gap, compact and ripple delete (see Global deviations):
      - Each track closes its own gaps by their own length.
      - Passive unlocked tracks: a clip moves by the most any one source track has closed before its start (never the sum of gaps apart in time; fixed after the review), limited to the largest safe amount by their clips that would collide, with the limiting clip reported.
      - Locked tracks neither move nor limit.
      - One sweep over the gaps by end (the passive tracks' clips walked alongside), one pass per source track, one command (only `start` fields change, plus the removed clips).

      Also in `gaps.ts`: `closeGap(doc, gap)` (`gap.close`, only while the gap still is one); `gapAtPlayhead(doc, frame)`; `compactGaps(doc, trackIds?)` (`gap.closeAll`, "Close all gaps"; default: the targeted unlocked tracks).
    - `delete.ts` (`delete.test.ts`, 9 tests): `deletionSet` (linked partners unless Linked selection is off; locked tracks never); `deleteClips` (`clip.remove`, gaps stay); `removedSpans` (runs of touching removed clips); `rippleDelete` (`clip.rippleDelete`: the removal and the ripple in one command, "Ripple delete clip(s)").
  - Session: `SessionStore.gap` (`GapSelection`), its own channel like the edit point; a gap, a clip selection and an edit point exclude each other. Mod+Shift+A and empty clicks clear it. The controller clears it when an edit moves or closes it.
  - View: `HitTester` returns `gap` hits (the empty span before a clip on an unlocked track, from the previous clip or frame 0); after the last clip it is still empty space.
    - Gaps: a click selects the gap (a drag from it is still a marquee). The overlay draws the hovered gap in the hover wash and the selected one in the accent wash with a dashed accent outline.
    - Razor tool (C, toolbar): a razor cursor over the track rows and a 1 px cut line at the snapped frame over the clip under the pointer and its linked partners (every unlocked track's with Shift), drawn over the snap line. The snap line, the target's name in the tooltip and the snapping are as for drags (§6.9). A press cuts (`onRazor`); a Shift+press anywhere in the rows cuts every unlocked track.
    - Right-click on a clip (selected first unless the selection holds it) or a gap (selected) asks for its menu (`onContextMenu`).
    - `clipLabelRect(clipId, minWidth)` places the rename field, paging the view to the clip when it is off screen.
  - `app/cutController.ts`: `edit.split` (Mod+K), `edit.splitAll` (Mod+Shift+K), `tool.razor`, `edit.delete` (Delete/Backspace; in the media panel it still asks to remove media), `edit.rippleDelete` (Shift+Delete), `edit.closeGap` (G), `edit.closeAllGaps` (Shift+G), `edit.closeAllGapsOnTrack` (gap menu), `edit.rename` (F2: the primary clip; the media panel's rename in its scope). Delete or G on a selected gap closes it. A ripple that stopped short gets an info toast naming the clip and track in the way ("Ripple delete stopped short", "Gap closed partly", "Can't close the gap", "Some gaps stayed open"). Every edit is one command; split keeps the selection rule above, deletes clear the selection.
  - Toolbar: Split, Delete, Ripple delete, Close gap and Close all gaps are enabled; Razor shows its pressed state (one-flag subscription).
  - Context menus (`timeline/menus/TimelineContextMenu.tsx`, React, rendered only while open):
    - Clip menu: Cut/Copy/Paste (disabled until Batch 39), Split at playhead, Delete, Ripple delete, Rename, and a row of 8 label swatches. The current label is checked; clicking it again removes the label.
    - Gap menu: Close gap, Close all gaps on track.
    - Rows run the command ids with their shortcuts shown. Escape or an outside press closes the menu, and the keyboard goes back to the timeline. `ContextMenu` gained a swatch row entry.
  - Inline rename (`timeline/menus/ClipRenameField.tsx`): the DS input over the clip's label bar, placed by direct style writes. Enter or blur commits one "Rename clip" step, Escape cancels, and a scroll or zoom commits.
  - Label colors: `LABEL_COLORS` (8) in `types.ts` (`LabelColor` is now that union; `validate.ts` rejects others), tokens `--label-*`. A labeled clip is drawn in its color (body and label bar, `fillClip`), drag ghosts included. The setter `setLabelColor` is one undo step ("Label clip(s)").

- review: rejected
  1. `src/timeline/edit/gaps.ts:183-191` (passive tracks), with `:152-182` (source tracks) and `:115-150` (the passive limit check): when gaps on different tracks do not overlap in time, every passive track moves by the sum of all the clusters' amounts, but each source track moves only by its own gaps. The content after the gaps then loses sync, and the summed amount also causes collision limits that should not happen. This breaks §6.11 ("closes the resulting gaps by shifting later clips on all unlocked tracks") and the module's own aim (lines 6-7: "keeping their content in sync with what moved"). I reproduced it with temporary Vitest probes (not committed); all tracks are targeted unless noted:
     - (a) V1 A 0+100, B 100+100, C 200+100, D 300+100, E 400+100; V2 F, G, H, I, J at the same frames; A2 K 500+100. `rippleDelete(["B", "I"])`: E and J now start at 300, and so does K. K used to start where E and J ended; now it overlaps them by 100 frames. Each source track closed 100 frames; A2 moved 200.
     - (b) The same layout with A2 Z 150+140 and K 450+100: `closed` is [100, 60] and `limit` is K on A2, so the toast says "Ripple delete stopped short". Moving K by 100 (what V1 and V2 each closed) fits in its 160 free frames. Instead K moved 160, more than any track closed.
     - (c) Compact (Shift+G) with A2 untargeted: V1 a 0+100, b 200+100, b2 300+300; V2 c 0+300, d 400+100, d2 500+100; A2 e 600+100. Afterwards b2 and d2 end at 500, but e starts at 400 (it used to start after both).
     - (d) A realistic case: delete a linked video/audio pair at 100..200 on V1/A1 and a title at 300..350 on V2, with a sound effect on A2 at 500 on the next video cut. The video cut moves to 400 and the sound effect to 350.
     `gaps.test.ts:105` ("gaps apart in time each move the other tracks by their own length") asserts this rule, but in a layout where no source track has content after the other track's gap, so the problem doesn't show. Fix: a passive clip must not move more than the source tracks have closed before it. For example, its shift could be the most that any one source track has closed in total before the clip's start; for overlapping gaps (acceptance 2) this gives the same result as today. The collision check (`room`) must use that amount. Add tests for (a)-(c), and update the module comment and the Global deviation "Ripple with gaps on several tracks".
  2. `src/ui/ContextMenu.module.css:82` (`transform: scale(1.15)`) and `:91` (`box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px var(--bg-sunken), 0 0 0 2px var(--accent)`): the swatch hover scale and the active swatch's ring are size literals outside `src/styles/tokens.css`. The design gap is logged, but the values belong in tokens (e.g. `--swatch-hover-scale`, `--swatch-active-ring`), like this batch's other gap values (`--timeline-cut-line-width`, `--timeline-gap-dash`, `--clip-label-*-alpha`).

- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-15)
  1. Ripple across tracks (`gaps.ts`): `closeGaps` is now one sweep over the gaps by end; clusters are gone. A clip on a passive unlocked track moves by the most any one source track has closed before its start (gaps ending at or before it), so gaps apart in time no longer add up. The collision cap uses that amount: the first passive clip after a gap may move at most the previous clip's shift plus the free space between them, and that caps every source track's total there. Overlapping gaps (acceptance 2) give the same results as before. The module comment, the Global deviation "Ripple with gaps on several tracks" and this entry's notes and deviation were updated.

(review case a: E, J and K at 300/300/400), "checks collisions against what the clip really moves ..." (case b: closed [100, 100], no limit, K at 350; and a limit between the gaps: closed [50, 100], limit k), "moves an untargeted track by the most ..." (case c: e at 500 where b2 and d2 end); `delete.test.ts` "deletes apart in time on several tracks ..." (case d: the next video cut and the sound effect both at 400).

- review: accepted (re-review after the fixes, 2026-10-15). `npm run check` green (869 unit tests in 93 files, 3 perf tests, build 488.00 kB JS / 159.63 kB gzip, 41.39 kB CSS). Both problems of the rejected list are fixed:
  1. `src/timeline/edit/gaps.ts:105-148`: one sweep over the gaps by end; a passive clip moves by the most any one source track has closed before its start, and the cap (`room`, line 119) uses that amount. The review's cases are tests: (a) `gaps.test.ts` "keeps a clip after gaps apart in time in sync" (E, J, K at 300/300/400), (b) "checks collisions against what the clip really moves" (no limit, K at 350; plus a real limit between the gaps), (c) "moves an untargeted track by the most any one track closed" (e at 500), (d) `delete.test.ts` "deletes apart in time on several tracks" (cut and sound effect both at 400). The old test now asserts u at 370, not the summed 350. The module comment, the Global deviation and this entry's notes were updated. My own probe (not committed) compared `closeGaps` with an independent brute force (greedy by gap end, each gap the largest amount that leaves no overlap on any track): 4000 random projects (2-5 tracks, random locks and targeting), each with Compact on the targeted tracks, Compact on a random track set, a random ripple delete and a random Close gap, about 16 000 edits, 2423 of them limited. Closed amounts, new starts and `limit` presence all matched. Every result passed a validating store, locked tracks never moved, and the reported blocker always ended flush against the clip before it.
  2. `src/ui/ContextMenu.module.css:82,91` use `--swatch-hover-scale` and `--swatch-active-ring` from `tokens.css`; the design gap entry names them.

Nothing from Do not build yet (no lift/extract, no delete-track UI; the clipboard rows are disabled placeholders with `TODO(batch 39)`). No color literals in the batch's files; the remaining `1px` borders and rings are the DS hairline and input focus ring, as elsewhere in the code. One dispatch per action, and label changes are one transaction. Every TODO has a `(batch NN)` tag, and no `TODO(batch 15)` is left.
  - Minor, not blocking: during Compact, a targeted track with no gaps of its own is a passive track, so it follows (and can limit) the other tracks' ripple. As soon as it has any gap, it is a source track and ignores them. Example: V1 a 0+100, b 200+100 and V2 c 0+200, d 200+200, both targeted. Shift+G closes nothing (limit d on V2). Add a 1-frame gap at 400 on V2 (e at 401) and V1 closes all 100 frames while d stays at 200. This follows the written deviation's rule; Batch 16 (compact on selected tracks, already a follow-up) should decide whether targeted tracks in a compact should ever limit each other.

### Batch 16: Linking, groups, track management
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Pure edits (unit tested):
    - `timeline/edit/tracks.ts` (`tracks.test.ts`, 11 tests):
      - `reorderTrack` (`track.reorder`, "Reorder track"): a video or audio track moves among the tracks of its kind; that kind's slots are rewritten, so the divider and the other kinds stay put. Video z-order follows.
      - `addTrackNear`: above or below a track of the same kind, else where new tracks of the kind go.
      - `deleteTracks` (`track.delete`): refused when a track is locked; clips left alone in a link group or group lose it.
      - `deleteEmptyTracks`; `setTrackFlag` (one command id per flag: `track.target`, `track.lock`, `track.mute`, `track.solo`, `track.hide`); `muteOthers`; `lockAll`.
      - `setTrackHeights` (`track.height`, applied with `amend`), `cycleHeight`, `nextHeightForAll`.
    - `timeline/edit/links.ts` (`links.test.ts`, 9 tests): `toggleLink` (`clip.unlink` / `clip.link`), `groupClips` / `ungroupClips` (`clip.group` / `clip.ungroup`), `moveIntoSync` (an overwrite move by the sync offset, "Move into sync"). Clips on locked tracks are never changed.
    - `timeline/model/queries.ts`:
      - `linkOffset` settles the sync rule (Global deviations): by source for clips of one media, by start otherwise.
      - `selectionPartners`: the group and, with Linked selection, the linked partners, transitively, unlocked only.
      - Also `groupOf` and `linkGroupOf`.
    - `delete.ts`: a deleted clip's group goes with it. `gaps.ts`: `closeGaps` takes extra `sources`; Compact passes its tracks, so a compacted track with no gap stays put (the Batch 15 review's open point).
    - `locks.test.ts` (5 tests): the lock audit, V2 locked.
      - Not selected (Mod+A, partners, groups).
      - Not moved: drags off and onto it, insert drops, nudges, Alt+Up/Down and Move into sync are refused.
      - Not trimmed, rolled, slipped, slid or stretched (Q/W/E included); not split or deleted.
      - Not linked or grouped, and its track is not deleted.
      - Ripple trim, ripple delete, close gap, Compact and W skip it. A locked track never limits a ripple, while the same track unlocked does.
  - Store and session:
    - Track heights are session state: `amend`, no undo step; `carryTrackHeights` keeps them through undo/redo.
    - Selection: `Selection.trackIds` for kind `track`, with `selectTracks`, `trackClickSelection` and `selectedTrackIds`. `filterSelection` drops tracks that are gone; session.json keeps `trackIds`.
  - View:
    - Geometry: `HEADER_TOGGLES` per kind, `headerToggles` / `headerToggle` / `headerName` / `reorderLane`; `HitTester` finds every toggle.
    - Header press: a click selects the track (Shift/Mod toggle, Mod+Shift selects its clips).
    - A vertical drag past the dead zone reorders the track:
      - `trackDrag` gesture: the overlay draws the dragged header at the pointer and an accent line where it lands; vertical auto-scroll.
      - Esc and document changes cancel; one `onReorderTrack` on release.
    - Double-click renames (`onRenameTrack`); right-click opens the track menu (the track is selected first); header hover wash.
    - Clicks, marquee and right-clicks select groups (`partners` = `selectionPartners`).

  - `app/trackController.ts` (new):

  - Track selection is `Selection.trackIds` (kind `track`).
  - Compact: every given track closes its own gaps, so a gapless one neither follows nor limits.
  - Groups: any member selects the group (Alt/Mod+click take one clip); deleting a member deletes the group; split pieces keep the group.
  - Link rules (audio + a video-track clip, one per track); height cycling rules; track menu wording and rules (see Global deviations).
  - Acceptance 1, "collisions with it limit ripples": a ripple never moves a clip to another track, so a locked clip can only meet moving content when a move, nudge, Alt+Up/Down or insert drop targets its track. Each of those is refused (`locks.test.ts`). Locked tracks neither move nor limit ripples, as in the Batch 13 and 15 deviations. This is the closest achievable version, tested.

Acceptance 1 is covered by `locks.test.ts` (V2 locked: not selected by Mod+A, partners or groups; moves, nudges, Alt+Up/Down, insert drops and Move into sync refused; no trim, roll, slip, slide, stretch, split, delete, link, group or track delete; ripple trim, ripple delete, close gap, Compact and W skip it and a locked track never limits a ripple). "Collisions with it limit ripples" cannot happen because ripples never change tracks, and the moves that could collide are refused; this is logged as a deviation, as in Batches 13 and 15. Acceptance 2 is covered by the first `links.test.ts` case (unlink, nudge the audio 12 frames, relink: `linkOffset` +12 / -12; Move into sync puts it back at 100; one undo step). The by-source sync rule for clips of one media is logged under Global deviations. Acceptance 3 is covered by `tracks.test.ts`: `reorderTrack(v1, 2)` changes `Compositor.evaluate` order to c2, c3, c1, and one undo restores the project. Acceptance 4 (inline rename, reload) and 5 (target toggle with Mod+K: `split.test.ts` targeted cases plus `setTrackFlag`) were run in the browser and are listed under pending manual checks with steps. Nothing from Batch 17 was built: no filmstrips or waveforms, and the link badge is a follow-up. No color, font or size literals in the diff: the new tokens are in `tokens.css` and reach the canvases through `Theme` (`lockHatch`, `inactiveOpacity`). Architecture: header drawing, hover, drag ghost and insert line are canvas-only through the scheduler; a track drag commits one `track.reorder` on release (0 React commits measured); heights use `amend` with no undo step (deviation logged); time stays in integer frames (`linkOffset` rounds); no media work was added. Every new TODO has a batch tag, and no TODO(batch ≤16) is left in `src`. Non-blocking notes: `drawContent.ts` imports `audibleTracks` from `audio/AudioMixer.ts`, so the timeline view depends on the audio module (could move to `timeline/model` later). Mod+L on a pair whose partner is on a locked track leaves that partner alone in its link group, which is harmless (its offset reads 0).

- notes:
  - Thumbnail sheets (unit tested):
    - `media/cache/thumbSheets.ts` (`thumbSheets.test.ts`, 10 tests): the §9.6 levels (LOD0 every 2 s, LOD1 every 0.5 s, LOD2 every 5 media frames), `thumbLayout` (160 px tall at the upright aspect, 16 per sheet in 4 x 4 cells), sheet times, the nearest thumbnail of a source time, `chooseLevel`, and `index.json` read/validate/write (version, source LOD, file stamp, layout; a stale or damaged index reads as null).
    - Worker side: `media/decode/thumbSheet.ts` (`ThumbSheetMaker`): analysis.worker's own `DecodeSessions` (one live decoder, 2 s idle close), frames at `idle` priority, drawn upright (`drawUpright`, shared with the image posters), closed at once; WebP + `ImageBitmap`. New analysis.worker methods `thumbSheet` (takes the source LOD for Batch 24), `decodeSheet` (cached sheets) and `closeThumbs` (immediate).
    - `media/ThumbnailStore.ts` (`ThumbnailStore.test.ts`, 10 tests), one per project:
      - follows the document's videos; one job at a time from the IdleQueue: the sheets the timeline's last content pass wanted and the cards' first sheets first, then LOD0 and LOD1 of each video (eager), after which `analysis.thumbs` becomes "ready" with `amend` (no undo step); LOD2 only when shown;
      - a job loads a listed sheet (`decodeSheet`) or has the worker make it and writes `cache/thumbs/<id>/<lod>-<sheet>.webp` and `index.json` (nothing in read-only projects);
      - decodes yield: the IdleQueue's pause (playback, drags) and a preview frame going to its decoder cancel the running sheet; a sheet starts only after 250 ms without a preview decode (`FrameService.decodesInFlight` / `onDecodeRequest`, new);
      - LRU of 256 sheets / 192 MB closing what it evicts, never the sheets on screen or a card's first sheet; eager sheets nobody shows are closed after writing; stats (decoded, cached, failed, resident, MB).
    - `IdleQueue.onPauseChange` (tested).
  - Peaks: `PeaksJobs` keeps the peaks it reads or builds (`get`, `onLoaded`), `prioritize` moves the media on screen to the front, `forget` drops removed media (2 new tests). `AnalysisScheduler` routes media to peaks and thumbnails (rewritten, 4 tests).
  - Drawing (`timeline/view/clipVisuals.ts`, `clipVisuals.test.ts`, 6 tests; `drawContent.ts`):
    - Filmstrips per video clip at M/L heights and at least 48 px wide (spec §6.4): tiles from the clip's start, each the frame at its left edge's source time (in point, speed), from the coarsest level that gives each tile its own frame; a tile whose sheet is not in memory shows another level's (coarser first) or the body color.
    - Waveforms per audio clip at M/L: per device pixel column, the peak of the source samples it covers from the level closest to one bucket per CSS px (interpolated below a bucket), mirrored, 0 dB (`TODO(batch 37)`).
    - Image clips tile their poster; solid clips show a swatch; text and caption clips their (first) line; disabled or muted clips are dimmed and hatched.

No color, font or size literals: `--clip-waveform` and `--clip-disabled-hatch` are in `tokens.css` and reach the canvas through `Theme` (`timeline.waveform`, `timeline.disabledHatch`); body text uses `fontOf(theme, "caption")`; and 160 px, 48 px and 100 ms are spec values (§9.6, §6.4, §6.17). Architecture: the decoding happens in analysis.worker (its own `DecodeSessions`, at `idle` priority, frames closed at once, WebP decoded back in the worker), and the main thread only draws transferred `ImageBitmap`s. Evicted and dropped sheets are closed (P13). `analysis.thumbs` goes through `amend` (no undo step). Thumbnail times are integer us. Content redraws for arrivals go through `scheduler.invalidate("timelineContent")` and none happen during playback (P3); card pictures are painted through the `mediaThumbs` view; and React only gets a store set on attach and detach. No TODO is missing its batch tag, and none of batch 17 or earlier is left in `src`. Non-blocking notes: (1) Pending manual check 2 says "Shift+Z / Fit", but Shift+Z is Punch zoom (`src/core/keyboard/keymap.ts:68`, off until Batch 27). The timeline fit is `\` or the toolbar's "Zoom to fit". (2) The overlay's trim preview draws filmstrips without the content pass's `imageSmoothingQuality = "medium"` (`drawContent.ts:87-88`), so it uses the browser default "low" quality while a trim is dragged.

- pending manual checks:
  1. Zoom handles and thumb (acceptance 1): put a few clips on the timeline (or dev builds: `__editor.timeline.generate(500, 8)`). At the bottom of the timeline, drag the right end of the scrollbar thumb (the dot; the cursor becomes a double arrow) to the left: the view zooms in and its left edge does not move. Drag the left end: it zooms with the right edge fixed. Drag the middle of the thumb: the view scrolls. Click the empty part of the bar: the thumb jumps there.
  2. Vertical scrollbar: add tracks until they do not fit (Ctrl+Alt+Up makes them taller), then drag the thumb at the right of the tracks: the rows scroll.
  3. Zoom to selection (acceptance 2): select two or three clips (click, Shift+click), press Shift+\ (Shift+Backslash): they fill the view with a little room on each side. Press \ to see everything again.
  4. Timecode field (acceptance 3): click the timecode at the right end of the timeline toolbar, type +30 and press Enter: the playhead moves 30 frames. Type 1:05:10 and Enter: it jumps to 1 min 5 s 10 frames. Type something wrong (1:x5) and Enter: the field turns red and stays open; Esc puts the time back. Click `ff` before the timecode under the preview: times switch to milliseconds (field, preview, ruler); type 1:05.5 and Enter; click `ms` to switch back.
  5. Shortcuts (acceptance 4, dev builds): click an empty part of the timeline and press M, ; (semicolon), X, Ctrl+C: each shows a toast naming the batch that brings it.
  6. Panning: press H and drag anywhere on the tracks (the hand cursor); press V to go back. Drag with the middle mouse button. With playback stopped, hold Space over an empty part of the tracks and drag; a short Space press there still plays. Press = and - a few times: the zoom glides instead of jumping. Ctrl+Shift+. or the Center playhead button (in the toolbar's ... menu when the window is narrow) centers the view on the playhead.

Acceptance 1 is covered by `scrollbars.test.ts` (right handle: left edge fixed and the thumb's end under the pointer; left handle: right edge fixed; the end follows the pointer's travel while the drawn thumb sits at its minimum; Fit and 400 px/frame limits; thumb drag maps back to the scroll) and `TimelineView.test.ts` (a real thumb drag scrolls with the zoom unchanged, a right-handle drag zooms in and out with `scrollFrame` unchanged, `ew-resize` on the handle, trough jump, vertical thumb), plus pending manual checks 1 and 2. Acceptance 2 by `zoom.test.ts` (`zoomToSpan`: 10% of the view free on each side, centered, within the zoom limits and scroll range), `TimelineView.test.ts` (`zoomToFrames`) and `queries.test.ts` (`clipsSpan`); `\` is the existing Fit; pending manual check 3. Acceptance 3 by `time.test.ts` (`+30` from 100 gives 130, `-15`, `+2.`, right-aligned `m:ss:ff` fields as in `1:05:10`, ms mode, invalid input gives null; the field marks it `aria-invalid`), pending manual check 4. Acceptance 4: `planned.test.ts` checks that every planned id is a keymap id with a later batch and that the toast names it; a script I ran over `keymap.ts`, every `actions.register` call in `src/` and `planned.ts` found 88 bindings in the timeline and global scopes, 61 registered, 27 planned, 0 missing and none both planned and registered (the recorded numbers); pending manual check 5. Nothing from `Do not build yet` was built: markers and in/out are only `planned.ts` entries that name Batch 19. No color or font literal outside `tokens.css`: the scrollbars draw with theme colors, `space[0]` and `radius.pill`, the new sizes are `--timeline-scrollbar-size` / `--timeline-scrollbar-min-thumb`, and the CSS uses `--danger-wash`, `--type-badge` and `--focus-ring` (the `1px` rings and hairlines follow the DS's 1px borders, as elsewhere). §3/§4: the scrollbars are drawn on the content canvas, with hover and drag states on the overlay; the eased zoom is a scheduler ticker; pan and scrollbar drags write only session state (no document command), one write per move; the zoom slider and timecode readouts are direct DOM writes, and the only new React component (the format chip) re-renders only when the format toggles; no React selector reads the timeline zoom or scroll; frames stay integer in the document. The Batch 18 TODOs (Hand tool, Center playhead button, timecode format and validation) are removed, and there are no untagged TODOs in `src/`. There are no earlier rejections.

### Batch 19: Markers, in/out range, loop, lift/extract
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-15
- notes:
  - Model and edits (unit tested):
    - `core/commands/markerCommands.ts` (`markerCommands.test.ts`, 4 tests): `marker.add`, `marker.update` (label, color, frame; a new frame re-sorts it; undo labels Move / Rename / Color marker), `marker.remove`; markers stay sorted by frame, flag `markers` only. `validate.ts`: marker invariants (unique ids, integer frames >= 0 in order, label colors).
    - `timeline/model/inOut.ts` (`inOut.test.ts`, 8 tests): `markIn` / `markOut` (the out point is the exclusive end: O at F stores F + 1; a point on the wrong side clears the other), `lastFrameOf` (Shift+O), `dragIn` / `dragOut` (one frame at least), `markedRange` (in defaults to 0, out to the content end), `selectionSpan` (now shared with Zoom to selection), `tracksTopDown`, `clipToMark` (X: a selected clip under the playhead, else the topmost on a targeted track, else the topmost).
    - `timeline/edit/liftExtract.ts` (`liftExtract.test.ts`, 7 tests): `rangeTracks` (targeted, unlocked), `liftRange` (clips inside go, edges trimmed with `sliceClip`, a spanning clip split, right pieces relinked with split's `relink`, now exported), `closeExtracted` (`closeGaps` over the range with the targeted tracks as sources). Tests run Extract as the controller does: one transaction, one undo step; untargeted tracks follow, a spanning clip stays, a collision limits and is named, locked tracks never move.
    - Session: `SessionStore.marker` (`selectMarker`, exclusive with clips, edit point and gap) and `setInOut` (2 tests); session.json drops an out point at or before the in point (1 test).
    - Playback: `PlaybackEngine` plays within bounds: a range played on its own (play around; play in to out, looping while loop is on), else the loop range (in/out when set) with loop on, else [0, content end); a play outside the loop range starts on its first frame (backward: its last); wraps are frame-exact both ways. `playInToOut()`. `AudioScheduler`: `PlayMapping.start`, loop passes of [start, end) with origins `origin0 + p * span`. 4 new engine tests (75 frames of a 30-frame loop in order, out point never shown; backward wrap; play in to out once / looping / whole timeline; the audio mapping re-anchored on the in point), 1 scheduler test (the next pass starts at the in point's media position).
  - View (`TimelineView.ts`, 5 new tests; `HitTester.ts`, 3 new tests):
    - Hits: `marker` (the ruler's bottom band, the nearest), `inHandle` / `outHandle` (the upper band, within the 8 px zone), both before the playhead head.
    - Content: markers in the ruler (`traceMarker`), the dragged one left out. Overlay: the range wash and brackets (first, under everything), marker states (selected, hovered, dragged), the tooltip's `name` (marker label before the timecode).
    - Gestures: `pressMarker` (selects at once) / `markerDrag` (snaps, itself excluded; one `onMoveMarker` on release; auto-scroll; S inverts; Esc cancels; a removed marker ends it), `rangeDrag` (a second `SnapIndex` without the dragged point; the session written once on release). Double-click a marker: `onEditMarker`; right-click on the ruler: the `ruler` menu request; `markerAnchor` places the editor (paging to the marker).
  - App and chrome:
    - `app/markerController.ts`: `marker.add` (M; on a marker there, its editor), `marker.edit`, `marker.delete`, `range.markIn/markOut/clearIn/clearOut/clearInOut/markClip/toSelection`, `edit.lift` / `edit.extract` (transactions; toasts for no range, no targeted track, a limited ripple; then the range clears and the playhead goes to the in point); a removed marker (undo) drops its selection and editor. `timelineController`: `range.goToIn` / `range.goToOut`, the view callbacks. `playbackController`: `transport.playInToOut`. `cutController`: Delete removes a selected marker.
    - `timeline/menus/MarkerPopover.tsx` (inline editor: label, swatches, Delete; Enter, an outside click or a scroll commits, Esc cancels), `labelSwatches.ts`, `ui/Swatches.tsx` (the clip menu's swatch row now uses it), the ruler/marker menu in `TimelineContextMenu.tsx` (Add marker here, Edit, Delete, Clear in/out, Set in/out to selection), `panels/properties/MarkerSection.tsx` (label, color, time, delete), the preview's Play in/out button, the toolbar's Add marker enabled, keymap Shift+M / Mod+Shift+M, `planned.ts` without Batch 19.

The expected differences: M, `;` and `'` now act instead of toasting "coming in Batch 19" (`;` and `'` with no range say "Set an in or out point first"), and the keymap audit counts 73 registered and 15 planned (was 61 / 27), 0 missing.
- deviations:
  - The out point is the exclusive end (O at F stores F + 1, Shift+O goes to F); a point on the wrong side clears the other.
  - In/out stay session state: not undoable; Lift and Extract clear the range and put the playhead on the in point.
  - Lift / Extract: the in point defaults to 0 and the out point to the content end; Extract closes the range on every targeted unlocked track with the Batch 15 ripple rule; one transaction each.
  - Loop with a range: every play loops the range and starts at its first frame when outside it; play in to out once (loop off) or looping (loop on); no range: the whole timeline.
  - One marker per frame: M on a marker opens its editor. New markers are green.
  - Marker selection in its own session channel, selected on the press; Delete deletes it.
  - Ruler hit order: markers and in/out handles before the playhead head; the ruler menu's Add marker adds at the clicked frame.
  - Shift+M / Mod+Shift+M are extra keys of the §18 marker jumps (timeline scope).
  - The in/out wash and handles are drawn on the overlay; markers on the content canvas.

Reload the page and open the project again: the markers and the name are still there.
  2. In/out loop (acceptance 2): press I at one frame and O at a later one: the range is shaded, with brackets in the ruler. Turn on Loop (the repeat button under the preview) and press Ctrl+Shift+Space (or the brackets button next to Loop): playback repeats the range; watch the first and last frames: nothing before the in point or after the out frame shows. Turn Loop off and press it again: it plays the range once and stops on the out frame.
  3. Lift and Extract (acceptance 3): mark a range across a clip with I and O, click an empty part of the timeline, press ' (apostrophe): the range is gone and what follows moves left. Ctrl+Z restores it in one step. Mark again and press ; (semicolon): the range becomes a gap. Turn off a track's target toggle (the crosshair in its header) first to leave that track alone.

- deviations:
  - Detail renders cover the visible part of the frame; the 4096 px cap applies to that part and to uploads; renders at the view scale draw straight onto the canvas.
  - Quality modes without proxies: reduced = no detail beyond the project's pixels; Auto reduced while playing or scrubbing and 300 ms after; Proxy always reduced until Batch 24; 1/2 and 1/4 during playback only (uploads too).
  - Uploads follow the render scale (project long edge x max(1, scale)); a bigger decode replaces the smaller cached texture.
  - Pan as the project point at the canvas center, clamped; Fit centered; zoom 5%-3200%; menu and Mod+0/1/2 around the canvas center; Hand and Space pans on the canvas.
  - Title-safe 80% inside action-safe 90%; no separate frame border item; squares and custom grid definitions; safe-zone numbers from ad templates; rulers along the canvas edges; overlays hidden during the split preview.
  - Fullscreen fills the window (not the Fullscreen API), not persisted; Esc after gestures.
  - Zoom, Fit, Quality, Overlays, Snap, Guides, Fullscreen in the header; menu buttons instead of native selects (P1).
  - KeyDispatcher fall-through for scope bindings not built yet.
  - The Guides toggle is state only (Batch 23 draws smart guides).

    - `timeline/edit/arrange.ts` (`arrange.test.ts`, 5 tests): `arrangeLayers` (Bring forward / Send backward, command `clip.arrange`): the clip to the adjacent video track when it is unlocked, visible (not hidden or muted) and free over the clip's span, else a new video track just past it (past the top or bottom track at the ends); several clips one by one, furthest in the direction first; time, links and groups kept.
    - `render/compositor/Compositor.ts` (+1 test): `evaluate(doc, frame, transforms)`, the gesture-aware option (a canvas gesture's ephemeral transforms replace the clips' own).
    - `render/preview/canvasLayers.ts` (`canvasLayers.test.ts`, 6 tests): `canvasLayersAt` (video tracks bottom to top, the enabled clip at the frame with a content box; hidden and locked tracks skipped, muted kept, the Batch 16 follow-up), quads with a gesture's transforms, `pointInQuad`, `layersUnder` (top first), `quadIntersectsRect` (separating axes), `layersInRect` (marquee), `quadsBounds` (group box).
    - `render/preview/gizmo.ts` (`gizmo.test.ts`, 14 tests): handles in box coordinates, `gizmoBoxOf` (one layer: its visible quad, turned with it; several: the axis-aligned group box), `hitGizmo` (corner handles, edge handles on sides at least `--gizmo-min-edge` on screen, the body, rotate zones 16 px outside the corners), `resizeCursor` (the handle's direction on screen to 45°), and the gestures: `moveGesture` (whole px, Shift on the dominant axis), `scaleGesture` (one layer in its own axes: corners uniform from the opposite corner, Shift per axis, edges one axis writing `scaleY`, Alt from the center, 100% snap within 2%, at least 1%; a group from its box: positions scaled from the fixed point, each member's scale by the box factors along its own axes), `AngleTracker` (pointer angle unwrapped across ±180°) and `rotateGesture` (one layer around its anchor, a group around its box center; `snapRotation`: Shift snaps to 45° multiples within 3°, else 0.1°), `anchorGesture` (the anchor under the pointer, the position following so the layer stays put).
    - `render/preview/CanvasEditInput.ts` (`CanvasEditInput.test.ts`, 8 tests): pointer and modifier input on the overlay canvas. Click selects the top layer with its partners (group, linked partners with Linked selection on), Shift toggles them, Mod toggles the layer alone, Alt+click cycles through the layers under the pointer (alone); a press inside the gizmo box moves the selection (a click there picks the top layer); empty space: a marquee (Shift / Mod add), written once on release, else a click that clears. Handles scale, the corner zones rotate, Alt over the anchor marker drags the anchor (a click there is the Alt+click). Drags start past `--canvas-drag-threshold` and pause playback; the gesture's `transforms` are ephemeral (the preview and the overlay draw them), one `setTransforms` command on release; Shift / Alt change a running gesture at once (keys or pointer events); Esc, a window blur or a document change under the drag cancel it. Hover outline, cursors (resize by direction, `canvasCursors` rotate SVG, move, crosshair over the anchor with Alt), right-click selects the layer and opens the menu. `scene()` describes what the overlay draws. Rect read once per enter and after a resize (P9).

### Batch 23: Canvas snapping, smart guides, proximity measurements
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Snapping (unit tested):
    - `render/preview/snap.ts` (`snap.test.ts`, 22 tests): `buildCanvasSnap` collects the candidates in the §7.4 priority order: the frame's center and edges, plus its thirds while the Thirds grid shows (priority 0); the safe rectangles on screen (1); the other visible layers' bounding box edges and centers (2); and equal spacing, the midpoint of the room between every two layers one after the other on an axis (3). Lines are sorted by value, and a query binary-searches the reach. `CanvasSnapIndex` keeps the candidates and rebuilds them only when its sources differ by value: the other layers' bounds, the frame, thirds and the safe rectangles. It counts `rebuilds` and `queries`.
    - Resolution: `nearestLine` takes the nearest line within reach of any tested value, and ties go to the higher priority. `snapMove` tests the moved box's edges and center on the free axes, x first. Equal spacing applies only when the box fits the room, both layers overlap it across the axis, and no other such layer sits between them. `snapHandle` snaps a scale handle on its free axes; a uniform corner takes the nearer target and the other axis follows the diagonal. `isAxisAligned` gates the scale snap. The box sticks while the raw position stays within reach.
    - Guides: `moveGuides` and `handleGuides` show lines across the frame for frame and safe targets. A layer line spans the box and the layers on it, with the distance to the nearest layer on each side, once per layer. Equal-spacing gaps are spans, and a layer-line distance they already show is dropped. `measureGuides` gives the four sides to the nearest layer in line, else to the frame edge. `formatDistance` labels in whole px, else one decimal.
    - `gizmo.ts` (+1 test): `moveDelta` holds the rounding and the Shift axis lock, and returns the free axes. `moveGesture` takes a `snap` offset added after the rounding. `scaleGesture` takes `snap` for the 100% snap (off when snapping is off). `canvasLayers.ts`: `CanvasLayer.muted`.
    - `CanvasEditInput.ts` (+7 tests):
      - `snapping` is the session's `snap`, inverted while S is held during a drag. S is read in the capture-phase modifier listener with `preventDefault`/`stopPropagation`; repeats are ignored and the key-up passes on, as on the timeline. `isSnapKey` moved to `core/keyboard/keys.ts`.
      - `updateDrag`: a move snaps through `snapMove` and keeps `moveGuides`. A scale aims the gesture at the snapped handle point and keeps `handleGuides`. Rotation keeps its Shift snap; anchor drags do not snap.
      - Candidates come from `snapCandidates(held)`: the frame, thirds while the grid is Thirds, `safeZoneOn(...).safe` plus title-safe and action-safe while shown, and the other non-muted layers while `preview.guides` is on.
      - `scene().guides` holds the drag's guides, or the measure mode: Alt over the canvas, no press or drag, a selection, Smart guides on. `snapStats` gives the counters. Alt entering or leaving the canvas redraws.
  - Views and app:
    - `PreviewOverlayView` draws `scene.guides`: 1 px `--accent` lines and spans with `--canvas-guide-cap` end caps (crisp on device px), under the handles. Distance chips are drawn after the anchor and before the readout. The guides are part of the flattened scene comparison (P3).
    - `PreviewStatsOverlay` adds a line `canvas snap: candidates rebuilt N · queries M` (`canvas` dep = `canvasInput`, wired in `previewController`).
    - Tokens `--canvas-snap-threshold` (6px), `--canvas-guide-width` and `--canvas-guide-cap`, read as `Theme.gizmo.snapThreshold/guideWidth/guideCap`.
    - The reach in project px is `snapThreshold * dpr / placement.transform[0]`, about 15.6 project px at Fit in the test layout.

- deviations:
  - With Smart guides off, other layers and equal spacing are not snap targets and Alt measures nothing. The frame and safe targets still snap with the Snap toggle, and their lines are always drawn (§0 "every snap is visible").
  - Thirds are targets while the grid overlay shows Thirds: the Overlays menu's Thirds is §7.4's "thirds" toggle. Title-safe and action-safe edges are targets while shown, alongside the platform safe rectangle (the mask regions' own edges are not).
  - Layers are targets by the axis-aligned bounds of their visible quads, so a turned layer counts by its bounding box. Muted layers are not targets: they are out of the picture, though selectable. Layers on locked tracks are targets and measure neighbours (in the picture, though not selectable; fixed after the review).
  - The S toggle and hold-S also govern the §7.3 100% scale snap (one snap state). Shift's 45° rotation snap stays, since Shift is an explicit modifier, and is the only rotation snap (§7.4 "rotation (Shift)"). Anchor drags do not snap.
  - A snapped axis lands exactly on its target, which can be a fractional project px; unsnapped axes stay whole px (Batch 22).
  - A scale snaps the dragged edge only while the box is axis-aligned: no turn or a quarter turn, and group boxes. A uniform corner snaps along the nearer target's axis, and the other axis follows the aspect.
  - Equal spacing covers only the middle case (§7.4 "when moving the middle one"). The box is centered in the room between two layers that both overlap it across the axis, with no other such layer between them and gaps over 0. A series is not extended past its end.
  - Guides show every target the moved box meets exactly, including on an axis Shift holds. A layer line spans the box and every layer on it, with the distance to the nearest layer on each side (once per layer). A distance already shown by an equal-spacing gap is not repeated.
  - Measure mode: each side measures to the nearest layer overlapping the selection across it, else to the frame edge, so both are never drawn on one line. A box overhanging the frame gets a negative distance. A group measures from its box. It needs Smart guides on, and the anchor marker still shows with one layer.
  - Candidates are compared by value at each query and rebuilt only when the other layers' bounds, the frame or the overlays shown differ. Dragging another layer is a different candidate set, so it rebuilds once.

  1. Layers on locked video tracks are not snap targets and are not measured by Alt, although they are in the picture. §7.4 (BINDING) says "other visible layers at the playhead", and the compositor draws locked tracks: `src/render/compositor/Compositor.ts:117` skips only hidden and muted tracks. But `snapCandidates` (`src/render/preview/CanvasEditInput.ts:633`) takes its rects from `this.layers()` = `canvasLayersAt`, which skips locked tracks for selection (`src/render/preview/canvasLayers.ts:40`). Measure mode (`CanvasEditInput.ts:322`) uses the same list. No deviation note covers this: the Batch 23 deviations (`docs/PROGRESS.md:2322`) only exclude muted layers. §6.9 explicitly keeps locked tracks as timeline snap candidates. Reproduced with the `CanvasEditInput.test.ts` setup, s1 on V1 at x -300 and s2 dragged from (540, 1360) to (244, 1360). With V1 unlocked, s2 snaps to x -300 with 3 guide lines and a 300 px span. With V1 locked, s2 stays at x -296 with no guides, though s1 is still composited. A locked background or logo, the usual alignment reference, gets no smart guides. Fix: build the candidate rects (and the measure-mode neighbours) from the composited layers at the playhead: video, not hidden, not muted, enabled, locked included, minus the held or selected ones. Keep selection on `canvasLayersAt`, and add a test with a locked-track target (snap plus Alt measure).

### Batch 24: Proxy workflow

- notes:
  - Engine (unit tested):
    - `media/proxy/proxyFormat.ts` (`proxyFormat.test.ts`, 7 tests): `proxySize` (short edge 360/540/720, never above the source, even sides), `avcLevel` / `proxyCodecs` (Main at the lowest level holding size and rate: `avc1.4d401f` for 960x540 at 30 fps, `4d4020` at 60; then High, then Constrained Baseline), `halvingSteps`, `wantsProxy` (rules "heavy" = above 1080p short edge or above 30 fps, the default; "large"; "all"), PROXY_GOP 15, PROXY_BITRATE 3 Mbps.
    - `media/proxy/ProxyMaker.ts` (`ProxyMaker.test.ts`, 8 tests, fakes for decoder/encoder/muxer): the sample index (cached or parsed), every sample fed in decode order from the first sync sample with the decoder and encoder queues kept under 4, each frame scaled, encoded with a keyframe every 15 frames and muxed; the file's bytes stream out as `{ kind: "data", position, bytes }` progress, `{ kind: "progress", done, total }` every 10 frames; source times kept (the muxer starts them at 0), rotation into the track matrix; pause (stops feeding), abort, encoder and decoder failures; every frame closed.
    - `media/proxy/browserProxy.ts` (worker only): the VideoEncoder wrapper, `halvingScaler` (OffscreenCanvas 2D, successive 2x bilinear steps), `loadSampleIndex`, `createMp4Writer` (mp4-muxer `StreamTarget`, 1 MiB chunks, no fast start, `firstTimestampBehavior: "offset"`). `media/workers/proxy.worker.ts`: `makeProxy`, `pauseProxy` (immediate), one job at a time. Protocol: `WorkerKind` "proxy", `closeVideo` takes an optional lod; the `proxy` worker factory in `app/services.ts`. The `media/proxy/index.ts` placeholder is gone.
    - `media/ProxyJobs.ts` (`ProxyJobs.test.ts`, 12 tests): the document's `proxy.status` is the queue. Videos with no proxy decision become "pending" when automatic proxies are on and the rule picks them (`amend`, no undo step; on import, for older projects, on a rule change); one job at a time, oldest first; the bytes written at their positions into `cache/proxies/<id>.mp4.part` (`FsDir.openWritable`), renamed to `<id>.mp4` (`FsDir.rename`: `move()` where the browser has it, else copy and remove), then "ready" with the size; a failure marks "failed" (not retried this session); Remove proxy cancels a running job, deletes the files and marks "none" (never made again on its own); a media removed or the project closed cancels its job and deletes the `.part`; paused (worker `pauseProxy`) while the IdleQueue is paused, no job starts meanwhile; on open the `.part` / `.crswap` files of interrupted jobs are deleted, pending videos start over and ready proxies whose file is gone are made again; read-only projects make none. `progress` store of the running job; `generateIfUndecided` for playback falling behind.
    - `core/commands/mediaCommands.ts` `setMediaProxy` (command `media.proxy`, through `amend`; +1 test); `core/document/validate.ts` checks the proxy's status, size and path (+2 cases); `project/appSettings.ts` `proxyAuto` (default on), `proxyRule` ("heavy"), `proxyShortEdge` (540), `proxySettings()` (+1 test); `project/fs.ts` + `memoryFs.ts` `openWritable` / `rename`, `project/mediaFiles.ts` `PROXIES_DIR`, `proxyPath`, `proxyPartPath`, `sampleIndexPath(id, lod)`, `openWritableAt`, `renameFileAt` (+3 tests); `TexturePool.deleteMedia(id, lod?)` (+1 case).
    - `media/decode/FrameService.ts` (+4 tests): `lodFor(mediaId, wanted)` / `hasProxy` (the document says ready and the proxy did not fail to open; a proxy that fails to open or cannot be decoded is not used again until released), `release(mediaId, lod?)` (only the proxy's sessions and textures), `uploadAtLeast` request option.
    - `render/preview/detail.ts` (+5 tests): `wantedLod(mode, activity, behind)` (Auto: proxy while playing or scrubbing, full when settling or idle; Proxy always; Full never; 1/2 and 1/4 proxy only for a media that fell behind while playing), `qualityLabel(..., proxy)` reads `Auto: proxy` when a layer drew a proxy frame, `layerDetail`, `exportLongEdge` (Export frame: a video's own size, or twice its drawn size when drawn under half, at most 4096).
    - `render/preview/LayerTextures.ts` (+7 tests): one lod per media per pass (`wantedLod` dep, routed by `frames.lodFor`); the other lod's frame at the same time stands in (a proxy frame while the full one decodes); a cached full frame at least as big as the proxy is drawn as exact where the proxy is wanted (a smaller one, e.g. from a 1/4 playback, only stands in); requests, warming and opens per lod; a proxy that does not open never marks the media failed; detail refreshes for full frames only; `stats.proxy`; `resolveExact` (Export frame: exact full frames at `exportLongEdge`, pinned while drawn).
    - `render/preview/PreviewView.ts` (+3 tests): each pass resolves with the quality mode's lod for its activity; a scrub or trim whose frame has not moved for 300 ms is at rest (§7.5); the status text after the frames are resolved; `behind` dep; `renderFrame` evaluates the playhead with its own compositor and draws `resolveExact` (overlays never in it).
    - `playback/PlaybackEngine.ts` (+3 tests): a lod per stream (`quality` dep and `wantedLod(..., "playing", behind)`, routed by `frames.lodFor`), a stream starts over when its lod changes; windows, pins and the preroll check on the stream's lod; `onBehind` when a media falls more than 2 frames behind.
    - `media/ThumbnailStore.ts` (+3 tests): a source per video (the proxy once ready, the source file when the proxy file is missing); a source change keeps the shown sheets, starts the index over and makes LOD0/LOD1 again after the other eager work; `regenerate` (Regenerate thumbnails) does the same ignoring the cache.
  - App and UI:
    - `app/previewController.ts`: `proxyReady` from the document; the frame source opens ready proxies and their own index files; the engine's `quality` and `onBehind` (→ `requestProxyIfUndecided`); `behind` for the preview; `watchPriority` also hears the IdleQueue resume (the end of a timeline scrub or trim), so Auto asks for the full frame at once; a proxy leaving "ready" releases its sessions and textures.
    - `app/mediaController.ts`: `ProxyJobs` per project (files, settings, IdleQueue), `proxyProgress` store and `bindProxyProgress` (direct DOM writes), `generateProxies` / `removeProxies` / `regenerateThumbnails` (actions `media.generateProxy`, `media.removeProxy`, `media.regenerateThumbnails`; a read-only project shows a toast), `requestProxyIfUndecided`, thumbnails from the proxy, `__editor.proxies` in dev builds.

A running job stayed at 15% during a 4 s playback and resumed after it. React commits: 1 per run (the transport's play/pause state).

- deviations:
  - Proxy settings are global app settings (IndexedDB `settings.v1`), shown in the Project section: §5.2's `Project` type is kept verbatim (no settings field for them), and which footage is too heavy depends on the machine, not the project. The switch governs automatic proxies only; pending jobs still run when it is turned off, and Generate proxy works with it off.
  - Resume = start over: an MP4 being muxed cannot be continued, so the `.part` of an interrupted job is deleted on open and the pending video is made again from frame 0 (acceptance 3: "or restarts cleanly").
  - Jobs pause while the IdleQueue is paused: playback and also timeline drags, scrubs and trims (§9.5 names playback and export), so the scrub's decodes come first.
  - Auto asks for the full frame as soon as playback pauses or a scrub or trim ends (the proxy frame stays on screen until the full one is decoded), where §8 says "after 300 ms of inactivity"; a scrub held still is at rest after 300 ms (§7.5). Acceptance 2's "switches to full within 300 ms" holds up to about 45 frames past a keyframe in the 1 s GOPs of the 4K60 HEVC test file; deeper frames took 308-414 ms (the hardware decode rate, ~7 ms per frame, as measured since Batch 06).
  - The "more than 2 frames behind → proxy" rule (§8, the Build's automatic downgrade): Auto already plays the proxy of every video that has one, so a video with no proxy decision that falls behind gets one queued (`onBehind`); 1/2 and 1/4 switch a video that falls behind to its proxy for the rest of the playback; Full never switches.
  - H.264 Main is tried first, then High and Constrained Baseline for encoders without Main. Frames are scaled by successive 2x bilinear halvings (the Build's "pick the faster that keeps quality", numbers in the notes), and keep their coded orientation with the source's rotation in the proxy's track matrix.
  - Thumbnails: a video's LOD0/LOD1 sheets are made again on any source change, also back to the source file after Remove proxy (the index records one source); the shown sheets stay until replaced.
  - Export frame decodes each video's exact full frame at its own size (or twice its drawn size when drawn under half, at most 4096 px) instead of drawing the textures last shown.
  - Remove proxy also cancels a running job (no separate Cancel row or card button).
  - `mp4-muxer` is marked superseded by Mediabunny upstream; it is used because spec §3.1 names it.

### Batch 25: Properties panel framework and core sections
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Engine (unit tested):
    - `timeline/edit/transform.ts` (+4 tests): `quickTransforms(project, ids, action)` applies fit, fill, center, flipH, flipV or reset to the selected layers as one `clip.transform` command. Fit, Fill and Center skip layers with no content box, and locked clips are skipped. `withAnchor(transform, size, anchor)` moves the anchor and keeps the picture in place, as the gizmo's Alt anchor drag does.
    - `timeline/edit/clipProps.ts` (`clipProps.test.ts`, 7 tests):
      - `setOpacities` (`clip.opacity`): composite only, clamped to 0..1, skips locked clips and non-layers.
      - `setEnabled` (`clip.enable`).
      - `clipTime`, `retimeDelta` and `retimeClips` serve the Clip section's start, end, duration and source in/out. They are regular trims through `trimEdge`: no ripple, linked partners included while Linked selection is on, applied one after another and merged into one `clip.trim` command. The result reports the first limit hit.
    - `timeline/edit/duplicate.ts` (`duplicate.test.ts`, 3 tests): `duplicateClip` (`clip.duplicate`) copies the clip onto its own track, in the first free span at or after its end that holds it, else after the track's last clip. The copy is unlinked and ungrouped; a clip on a locked track gives null. Helper: `freeSpotAfter`.
    - `core/commands/projectCommands.ts` (+1 test): `setResolution(w, h, { scaleLayout })` and `scaledTransform` scale positions per axis, and scale and scaleY by the smaller ratio. Locked tracks are scaled too.
    - `core/store/sessionStore.ts` (+1 test): `setSectionCollapsed`.
    - `project/appSettings.ts` (+1 test): `recentColors` (hex only, at most `MAX_RECENT_COLORS` = 8) and `pushRecentColor`.
    - `ui/colorMath.ts` (`colorMath.test.ts`, 2 tests): `rgbToHsv` and `hsvToRgb`.
  - UI framework:
    - `ui/NumberField.tsx` (rewritten; `NumberField.test.ts`, 10 tests):
      - It is bound through a `NumberBinding`: `read`, `subscribe`, `set`, and `change(delta, "step" | "scrub" | "commit" | "cancel")`.
      - The input is uncontrolled. The bound value is written into the DOM from the subscription, so a value change never renders React (P1).
      - Label scrub: pointer capture after a `--field-scrub-threshold` (3 px) dead zone; Shift 10x, Alt 0.1x; clamped as it goes; Esc cancels. While scrubbing, the field gets `data-scrubbing` (gold label and edge) and the page shows an ew-resize cursor.
      - Typing: Enter or blur commits, Esc reverts, "3,5" reads 3.5. Up/Down step ±`step` (Shift 10x).
      - Units, min/max, precision, and a `--` placeholder for mixed values. A click on the label without a drag focuses the field.
      - Helpers: `valueBinding` (plain values: steps and scrub releases set the value) and `useValueBinding` (React state; used by the New project dialog's W/H).
    - `ui/ColorField.tsx` + `ColorField.module.css`: a swatch (checkerboard under the color) and the hex field. The swatch opens a popover picker (a portal, fixed under the swatch, menu material) with:
      - a saturation/value square and hue and alpha strips. Drags paint the picker by direct DOM writes, report `onPreview` live, and send one `onChange` on release;
      - a hex field and R G B A number fields, bound to the picker's own store (scrubs preview live);
      - the recent colors, and an EyeDropper button when `window.EyeDropper` exists.
      Esc or a click outside closes it.
    - `ui/Toggle.tsx` gets `mixed` (`aria-checked="mixed"`, the thumb halfway; a click turns all on). `ui/IconButton.tsx`'s `icon` is optional, for text chips.
  - Live edits (P2):
    - New `app/liveEdit.ts` store with `transforms`, `opacities` and `background`.
    - `Compositor.evaluate(..., opacities)`; `PreviewView` deps `opacities` and `background`.
    - `CanvasEditInput` dep `live`: its `transforms` are the gesture's, else the field scrub's, so the gizmo follows a scrub.
    - `previewController` invalidates `preview` and `gizmo` on every change.
  - Panel (`panels/properties/`):
    - `context.ts` (`context.test.ts`, 4 tests): `propertiesContext` (marker, none, track, or clips with their primary and the layer ids; cue → `TODO(batch 32)`), `contextKey`, `sectionIds` (the section registry keyed by selection kind) and `shared`.
    - `layerProps.ts` defines the layer number properties: position in px from the frame center; scale W/H in %, with the lock; rotation in degrees; anchor in %, keeping the picture in place; opacity in %.
    - `bindings.ts` (`bindings.test.ts`, 6 tests): `layerBinding`.
      - read: the value the selected layers share (live edit included), else null.
      - set: one command. step: coalesce key `properties.<id>`. scrub: `liveEdit` only. commit: one command, then the live edit is cleared. cancel: the live edit is cleared.
    - `Section.tsx`: a header button with a chevron; the collapsed state lives in `collapsedSections`; `Row` lays out a label and its controls. `Section.module.css` replaces `ProjectSection.module.css`.
    - `useShared.ts`: `useClipsShared` and `useTracksShared`, narrow selectors that return a primitive, MIXED or NONE.
    - Sections:
      - `ClipSection`: name, label color, enabled, start/end/duration, source in/out.
      - `TransformSection` and `OpacitySection`.
      - `TrackSection`: kind, height and switches can be mixed; the name row is shown for one track only.
      - `MarkerSection`: moved into the framework.
      - `ProjectSection`: the background as a `ColorField` with a live preview, the resolution flow placeholder, bound W/H fields, and disabled Batch 42 rows (Still duration, Transition, Cache with Clear cache).
    - `QuickActions.tsx` is the §17 row, in these groups: Fit, Fill, Center, Flip H/V, Reset | Fade in/out, Freeze, Punch zoom | 0.5x 1x 1.5x 2x | Mute, Save preset, Update preset | Dissolve, Dip black, Dip white | Duplicate, Delete. Which buttons are available comes from one selector (a bit mask). Planned chips are disabled; in dev builds their tooltip reads "<label>: coming in Batch NN".
    - `PropertiesPanel.tsx` renders only when the context key changes (selection kind, ids, primary, marker). The quick row sits above the scroll area. The sections are keyed by the context, so each selection gets fresh bindings. The header shows "N clips" or "N tracks".
  - Actions (`app/propertiesController.ts`):
    - Registered: `transform.fit`, `fill`, `center`, `flipH`, `flipV`, `reset` (on the selected layers; `canvas.resetTransform` is gone); `edit.duplicate` (Mod+D, removed from `core/keyboard/planned.ts`); `clip.toggleEnabled`.
    - Helpers used by the sections: `retime`, `renameClips`, `setClipsEnabled`, `changeResolution` and `changeFrameRate` (the conversion flow placeholder), `changeBackground`, `previewBackground`, `rememberColor`.
    - `reportTrimLimit` is now exported from `timelineController`, so typed times get the trim toasts.
    - The canvas menu gets Fit, Fill, Center, Flip horizontal, Flip vertical and Reset transform. The clip menu gets Enable/Disable after Ripple delete.
  - Tokens: `--properties-section-header`, `--field-scrub-threshold`, `--picker-width`, `--picker-square-height`, `--picker-strip-height`, `--picker-thumb-size`, `--picker-thumb-ring` (added by the fixes), `--picker-light`, `--picker-dark`, `--picker-hue`, `--picker-checker`.

- deviations:
  - Field scrubs write ephemeral values (`liveEdit`) and commit one command on release. §11 asks for coalesced commands during the scrub; P2 (binding) keeps gestures out of the document, so a scrub neither autosaves nor renders the app bar. Either way a gesture is one undo step. Arrow steps coalesce (`properties.<prop>`, 300 ms).
  - Transform fields:
    - Position shows the stored numbers: project px from the frame center, so typing 100 sets exactly 100.
    - Scale has W and H fields in % with an aspect lock between them. The lock starts closed unless a selected layer is scaled unevenly; while locked, the other axis follows by the same factor. The range is 1% to 10000%.
    - Rotation is in degrees, anchor in %, opacity in %.
    - Anchor edits keep the picture in place (the gizmo's Alt anchor drag), instead of moving the layer.
  - Clip times:
    - End is the exclusive end (Start + Duration).
    - Start trims the in point (the end stays); End and Duration trim the out point; Source in/out trim by the typed source time at the clip's speed.
    - These are regular trims (no ripple). Linked partners follow while Linked selection is on. They clamp at media and neighbors, with the trim toasts.
    - With several clips, each is trimmed to the typed time, in one undo step.
  - Multi-selection: Transform and Opacity show when any selected clip is a layer, and edit the layers (a linked audio partner is skipped). The name field renames every selected clip. A mixed switch sits halfway; a click turns all on.
  - Project settings:
    - Resolution changes go through the conversion flow placeholder and scale the layout (locked tracks too), replacing Batch 03's "keep".
    - The frame rate still changes only without clips or markers.
    - Default durations, the default transition duration and Cache / Clear cache are disabled rows naming Batch 42.
  - Duplicate copies the primary clip alone: unlinked, ungrouped, into the next free span after it on its track, then selected. Mod+D works in the timeline and preview scopes. The canvas menu's Duplicate row stays a placeholder until Batch 39.
  - The one-click transforms are `transform.*` ids shared by the quick row and the canvas menu. They act on every selected layer, not only those at the playhead.
  - The clip menu's Enable/Disable (`clip.toggleEnabled`) is built here with the Clip section's switch, ahead of Batch 41's TODO.
  - Recent colors are a global app setting.
  - The color picker shows the project background live while dragged.

  1. P1 (BINDING; CLAUDE.md "zero React commits during ... field scrubbing"): scrubbing the color picker's R, G, B or A label re-renders React on every move. `channelBinding`'s `scrub` phase (`src/ui/ColorField.tsx:119-121`) calls `pick(..., "preview")` (`:89-97`). The store change runs `paint` (`:180-195`), and `paint` calls `setHex` whenever `dragging` is false (`:192`). Only the square and strip drags set `dragging` (`:205`); a channel scrub never does. Measured with a temporary Vitest probe (since deleted): the swatch opened, the R label pressed and moved 10 times past the dead zone under a `Profiler` gave 11 commits during the moves. Repro in the app: select nothing, open the Background swatch, drag the picker's R label; React DevTools Profiler shows one commit per move. Fix: treat a channel scrub as a drag. Set `dragging` on the first `scrub` and clear it on `commit` / `cancel`, or write the hex input's text straight into the DOM while previewing and call `setHex` only when a pick ends. Add a `ColorField` test: scrub a channel label under a `Profiler`, 0 commits until the release, then one `onChange`.
  2. P2 (ephemeral state must end with the gesture): Esc during a drag on the picker's square or strips leaves the live background in place. `useDismiss` (`src/ui/ColorField.tsx:198`) closes the picker on Escape, and the picker unmounts with no cleanup. `onPreview(null)` is never sent: the last `onPreview` was a color (`:206`), so `previewBackground` keeps `liveEdit.background` set (`src/app/propertiesController.ts:106-108`). The preview then draws a background the document does not have (`src/render/preview/PreviewView.ts:318-319`) until another live edit happens to clear it. The same happens if anything unmounts the picker mid-drag (a selection change by keyboard). Measured with a temporary Vitest probe: pointerdown on the square, then Escape gives `open: false`, one preview `{r:1,g:1,b:1,a:1}`, last preview not null, 0 `onChange`. - notes:
  - Engine (unit tested):
    - New `timeline/edit/crop.ts` (`crop.test.ts`, 15 tests):
      - Geometry in media px: `cropSize` (the media as shown), `cropRect` / `rectCrop`, `clampCrop` (fractions in 0..1, at least `MIN_CROP_PX` = 1 media px left on each axis).
      - `withCropEdge(crop, size, edge, value, lock)`: one edge, clamped; with the lock the visible aspect stays (the other axis follows around its center and shifts to stay inside the media; the edge stops where the other axis reaches the media).
      - `cropToFrame(transform, size, frame)`: the crop that makes the visible area the frame at the current transform, or, when the layer does not cover the frame, the largest area with the frame's aspect inside the part of the frame the layer covers.
      - Commands: `setCrops` (`clip.crop`, composite only, skips locked clips, solids and no-ops), `quickCrops(project, ids, "reset" | "frame")` ("Reset crop", "Crop to frame").
      - Acceptance 1 as a test: Crop to frame, then Fill: the visible quad is exactly the frame's corners (scale 1920/1080).
    - `timeline/edit/transform.ts`: `layerContent(project, clip, crop?)` takes an ephemeral crop. Fit, Fill and Center already used the cropped box (Batch 25); a test now fits a half-cropped clip.
    - `render/compositor/Compositor.ts`: `evaluate(..., crops)` reads ephemeral crops (P2). The crop UV with rotation metadata and flips was already built in Batch 08; `Compositor.test.ts` gets a pixel reference test (+2 tests): a numbered 8 x 6 coded texture is turned upright by hand (each metadata rotation), mirrored (flips), cropped and placed (with and without a quarter-turn transform), and the compositor's matrix and UV rectangle must sample the same coded pixel at every visible pixel's center.
    - New `render/preview/cropGizmo.ts` (`cropGizmo.test.ts`, 8 tests): `contentPoint` (the inverse of `layerPoint`), `cropGesture` (edges to the pointer, Alt symmetric, the lock: corners scale from the opposite corner by the pointer's projection on the diagonal, edges move the other axis around its center), the readout (`L 480 · 1440 × 1080`), `CropModeState`.
    - `render/preview/gizmo.ts`: `hitGizmo(..., crop)` has no rotate zones in crop mode.
    - `render/preview/canvasLayers.ts`: `canvasLayersAt(..., crops)`: the hit test and the gizmo use the ephemeral crop (+1 test).
  - Canvas (`render/preview/CanvasEditInput.ts`, `CanvasEditInput.crop.test.ts`, 7 tests):
    - Crop mode: `cropMode` store (target, aspect lock; `app/previewController.ts`), `enterCropMode` / `exitCropMode`, `cropTarget`. A double-click on a video or image layer selects it alone and enters crop mode; a double-click on it again leaves. It ends when the selection is not that layer alone any more, the clip goes or its track locks.
    - In crop mode the box gets crop handles: a handle drag is a `crop` gesture (snapping, Shift/lock, Alt), writes `liveEdit.crops` every move (the compositor, the canvas layers and the Crop fields read it), and commits one `clip.crop` ("Crop layer") on release. Esc cancels the drag, a second Esc leaves crop mode (`app.cancel`: timeline gesture, canvas gesture, crop mode, fullscreen). A body drag still moves the layer; a click on the layer keeps the selection.
    - Crop edges snap to the frame's edges, center and thirds (their own `CanvasSnapIndex`) while snapping is on (S inverts while held) and the layer is axis-aligned; the guide is drawn.
    - `PreviewOverlayView.drawCrop`: the whole media's box dashed in `--accent-dim`, corner brackets and edge bars inside the visible box (`--text-primary` over a `--bg-sunken` outline), turned with the layer; the scene comparison includes crop mode.

- deviations:
  - Crop to frame (§11) keeps the transform. When the layer covers the frame, the visible area is the frame exactly; otherwise it is the largest area with the frame's aspect inside the part of the frame the layer covers (so a fitted 16:9 clip in a 9:16 frame is cut to its 9:16 middle and Fill then fills the frame exactly, acceptance 1). A turned layer gets the largest such rectangle centered in the turned frame, then inside the media (never outside either; not always the largest possible); the frame's aspect follows the nearest quarter turn. A layer outside the frame is left as it is.
  - Crop mode (§7.3 "crop handles as a gizmo mode"; Build "toggle in the Crop section header or double-click a video layer with the crop tool chip"): the header toggle and a double-click on a video or image layer enter it; the "crop tool chip" is a chip over the canvas while crop mode is on (Crop · Reset · Done). One layer at a time; a double-click selects it alone (a group's other layers leave the selection). It ends on Esc (after a running gesture), Done, a double-click on the layer, a selection that is no longer that layer alone, the clip leaving, or its track locking. In crop mode a body drag moves the layer (no dragging the media inside the crop window), Alt is the symmetric crop (no anchor marker, no measure mode), and the box has no rotate zones.
  - The aspect lock is the Crop section's toggle, shared with the canvas handles, and Shift inverts it during a handle drag (as Shift inverts the corners' uniform scale, §7.3). It keeps the current visible aspect in media px: a field or an edge handle moves the other axis around its center (shifted to stay inside the media); a corner scales from the opposite corner (or the center with Alt).
  - Crop edges snap to the frame's edges, center and thirds (the Build's "frame edges and thirds"), always including the thirds (moves and scales snap to them only while the Thirds grid shows, Batch 23), only while snapping is on and the layer is axis-aligned; no other layers or safe zones.
  - Crop units: media px of the media as shown (rotation metadata applied), or % of its side; px by default, the choice kept for the session (not persisted). A crop always leaves at least 1 media px on each axis (a crop to nothing would drop the layer from the picture and the canvas).
  - A crop handle drag writes `liveEdit.crops` (where Crop field scrubs write theirs), not a gesture-local map as transform gestures do: the Crop fields follow the drag live (acceptance 2 "fields match").
  - Crop fractions cut the sides as shown after the flips (Batch 08's compositor rule): flipping a cropped clip mirrors the picture inside the same crop window on screen.

Acceptance 2 is covered by `CanvasEditInput.crop.test.ts`: the west handle drag writes `liveEdit.crops` while the document stays unchanged, the release commits one `clip.crop` "Crop layer" step, one undo restores it, and Esc drops the drag with no commit. `bindings.test.ts` shows the Crop fields reading the live crops, and the canvas drag writes the same store. The test's sampling model matches the shader (`out.uv = mix(uv.xy, uv.zw, t)`, `src/render/gpu/shaders.ts:47`; media layers carry their flips in the matrix, not in `flipX`). The CropChip and the header toggle render only when crop mode starts or ends, and the fields update outside React (P1). ### Batch 27: Speed, duration, freeze frame, punch zoom
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Engine (unit tested):
    - `core/document/types.ts`: `isFreeze(clip)`, a video clip with `speed = 0`. `validate.ts` allows speed 0 only on video clips and skips the duration x speed invariant for them (`out > in`, `out <= media` still hold).
    - New `timeline/edit/speed.ts` (`speed.test.ts`, 10 tests):
      - `setSpeeds(project, ids, speed | (clip) => speed, { link, ripple, linked })` is one `clip.speed` command ("Change speed").
      - The speed stays exactly as asked, clamped to 0.25x..4x. Linked (the default), `in`/`out` stay and the duration follows, rounded to whole frames; keyframes scale with the duration and fades are clamped. Unlinked, the duration stays and `out` follows from `in`. When the media runs out first, the clip is shortened and the result reports `mediaEnd` (§6.19 "clamp, warn").
      - A clip and its partners (with Linked selection, or when both are selected) change together, once. Groups are processed latest end first.
      - Ripple reuses the ripple trim's out-point plan (`planTrim(..., { ripple: true })`): what follows each member on its track, and what follows the clip's end on every other unlocked track. A shift to the left stops where a track would overlap (a gap stays, `ripple`). Without ripple, a slower clip stops at the next clip: the speed stays and the out point comes in (`neighbor`).
      - `setSpeedDurations(...)` ("Change duration") serves the Speed section's duration. Linked, it is a rate stretch of the out point; `planStretch` takes a `ripple` option (the Rate stretch tool still never ripples). Unlinked, and for freeze frames, it is a trim of the out point. Both ripple with the option.
      - Helpers: `clampSpeed`, `hasSpeed`.
    - New `timeline/edit/freeze.ts` (`freeze.test.ts`, 7 tests):
      - `freezeFrame(project, frame, selection, newId)` (`clip.freezeFrame`, "Freeze frame"):
        - Targets the selected video clips under the playhead, else the topmost enabled video under it on a targeted, unlocked, visible track.
        - Splits each target there together with its linked partners (always, so the sound stays in sync).
        - Inserts a `FREEZE_SECONDS` (2 s) clip between the halves: speed 0, `in` = the source time shown at the playhead, `out` = one project frame later. The freeze keeps the clip's look (name, label color, transform, crop, opacity) and drops the time-based parts (fades, keyframes, transitions, link, group).
        - Ripple: every clip from the playhead on, on every unlocked track, shifts right by 2 s. A clip spanning the playhead without being split (a music bed) stays where it is.
        - When the playhead is at a clip's start, nothing is split and the freeze goes before the clip.
        - A miss returns "none" or "notUnderPlayhead" (selected, but not under the playhead).
      - `punchZoom(project, frame, selection, factor, newId, { linked })` (`clip.punchZoom`, "Punch zoom"):
        - Targets the selected layers under the playhead, else the topmost video or image.
        - Splits them there (linked partners with them while Linked selection is on, as Split at playhead) and multiplies the right pieces' `scale` (and `scaleY`) by the factor around their anchor.
        - A clip that starts at the playhead is zoomed whole.
    - Freeze frames elsewhere:
      - `trim.ts` `hasSource` is false for them, so they have no media bounds; slip skips them; Rate stretch trims their edge; an in-point trim keeps `in`/`out`. Also exported here: `scaleKeyframes`, `editCommand`.
      - `move.ts` `sliceClip`: both pieces of a split freeze keep `in`/`out`.
      - `queries.ts` `linkOffset`: a freeze is in sync when it starts with its partner.
      - `clipProps.ts` `hasSourceRange`: no Source in/out rows for a freeze.
      - `AudioScheduler` never schedules a speed-0 clip.
      - The timeline's stretch tooltip shows 1x for clips without speed.
    - `PlaybackEngine`: no change needed. A freeze's source time is constant, so its prefetch window is one frame. The new test (`PlaybackEngine.test.ts`, +1) plays 80 frames through a freeze: one window `[2 s, 2 s]`, no drops.
    - `project/appSettings.ts` (+1 test in `recentProjects.test.ts`): `punchZoomScale` (1.2, kept in 1.05..3) and the reserved `punchZoomAnimated` (false, `TODO(batch 35)`).
