- deviations:
  - Speed (§11, §14):
    - The speed is kept exactly as asked. Linked, the duration rounds; it is never derived back from a rounded duration.
    - Without ripple, a slower clip stops at the next clip and its out point comes in (Premiere's behavior), with a toast; a faster clip leaves a gap.
    - Ripple moves what follows as a ripple trim of the out point does. A shift to the left stops at the first overlap, and a gap stays.
    - (fix) A clip and its linked partners get the same speed, and each is retimed from its own start and source range, so it gets its own duration. Partners that differ (an L cut, a speed set with Linked selection off) keep their own `in`/`out`. Partners that start apart (a J cut) keep their starts, so the pair shows a sync offset afterwards; "Move into sync" puts it back.
    - (fix) Ripple shifts what follows by the largest change of duration among the members. The clips after them stay in sync and none overlaps. An L cut's overlap shrinks or grows with the speed, which can leave a gap on the sound's track.
    - (fix 2) A clip with less than one frame of media left after its `in` at the new speed keeps its speed, linked or not, and the change reports `mediaEnd` (toast "Limited by the media end"). Its partners still change. §6.19 asks to clamp and warn; a clamped `out` would leave `out - in` short of `duration x speed`, and a later rate stretch to a slower speed then breaks §5.2. A speed that fills the one frame was not used: it is always below the one asked and can be below the current one. So `out` always follows `in` exactly after a speed change.
    - The Speed section's options are per session (not persisted): duration link on, Ripple on by default, so a speed-up closes up on a short's timeline.
    - The Build's "ripple checkbox" is the DS switch.
  - Speed field scrubs show the value in the field and apply once on release. There is no live retiming of the timeline or the preview during the scrub (P1/P2); the Rate stretch tool is the live way.
  - The Speed section's Duration is a rate stretch of the out point when linked, and a trim of the out point when unlinked or for a freeze. Both ripple with the option. The Clip section's Duration stays a regular trim.
  - Freeze frame (Build "media clip with speed = 0"):
    - `out` is one project frame after `in`. Linked partners are always split, so the sound stays in sync even with Linked selection off.
    - The ripple covers every unlocked track from the playhead on.
    - The target is the selected video under the playhead, else the topmost targeted visible video. Selected clips that are not under the playhead get a toast instead of another clip being frozen.
    - Freeze frames trim like images (no media bounds), never slip, and stretch as a trim. A split gives two holds of the same frame.
  - Punch zoom:
    - The factor is the app setting `punchZoomScale` (1.2; 1.05..3), applied to `scale` and `scaleY` around the anchor.
    - Linked partners are split while Linked selection is on (as Split at playhead).
    - The target is the selected layers under the playhead, else the topmost video or image. A clip starting at the playhead is zoomed whole.
    - `punchZoomAnimated` is a reserved app setting (false, read by nothing).
  - "Speed..." in the clip menu opens the Speed section with its speed field focused (no dialog).

- review: rejected
  1. `src/timeline/edit/speed.ts:111-122` and `:139` (`retimeGroup`): a group (a clip and its linked partners) gets one shared change of duration. That change is computed from the primary only (`:111`), then lowered to each member's media room at the new speed (`:115-120`), then raised again to every member's 1-frame minimum (`:122`). When the partners differ in duration or speed, `:122` can push a member past its media room. `:139` then clamps `out` to the media end, so `out - in` no longer matches `duration x speed`, and the document is invalid. Reachable in the UI: import a 10 s video with sound and put it on the timeline. With Linked selection off, select only the video and click 2x (video 150 frames at 2x, sound 300 frames at 1x). Turn Linked selection on, select the video and type 400 in Speed. Result (unit repro, `setSpeeds(p, ["v"], 4, { link: true, ripple: true, linked: true })`): the video collapses to 1 frame (out 133 333 us), and the sound gets 151 frames at 4x with out clamped to 10 000 000 us. `validateProject` reports `clip "a": out - in = 10000000 us, expected duration * speed = 20133333 us`, and a validating `DocumentStore` throws `DocumentInvariantError` (dev builds). `limit` is null, so no toast. Production builds would commit and save the invalid document. My probe (600 random projects x 12 random `setSpeeds` / `setSpeedDurations` / `freezeFrame` / `punchZoom` calls, 3846 commands through a validating store) hit this 72 times, every time in a linked group whose members differed in duration or speed. Partners differ after a speed change with Linked selection off, after an Alt-trim (L/J-cut) or after a one-sided Alt rate stretch.
  2. Same root cause, a valid document but a wrong one, contradicting this entry's notes ("Linked (the default), `in`/`out` stay") and §6.19 "clamp, warn". An L-cut: the video 0-240 (source 0-8 s) and its sound Alt-trimmed to 0-300 (source 0-10 s, the media end) of a 10 s media. 2x on the video gives the video 90 frames with out 6 000 000 us (2 s of its picture lost; 120 frames with out 8 s was expected) and the sound 150 frames. `limit` is null, so no toast. Partners whose spans differ also get their own source range changed (the sound's `out` moves) while "linked" promises in/out stay. Fix: retime each member from its own `in`/`out` (duration = round(member span / speed), each clamped to its own media with a `mediaEnd` limit), or write down and test a rule that keeps the pair's offset without breaking a member's source range or the §5.2 invariant. Never apply a minimum-duration clamp after the media clamp without re-checking the media.
  3. `src/timeline/edit/speed.test.ts` has no case with partners that differ (sync offset, L-cut, a speed set with Linked selection off and then changed with it on), so neither problem is caught. Add these, including a media-end partner, and assert `validateProject(next)` is empty.
  - Checked and fine: `npm run check` green (typecheck, lint + CSS color check, 1299 unit tests in 132 files, 3 perf tests, build 721.13 kB JS / 232.49 kB gzip; the >500 kB chunk warning is the logged Batch 45 follow-up). Acceptance: 1 by `speed.test.ts` (2x on 300 frames gives 150 with in/out kept, the linked sound follows, ripple on every unlocked track, a gap without ripple, one "Change speed" step, undo) plus `AudioScheduler.test.ts` (playbackRate = speed), the builder's browser run (437 -> 875 Hz) and manual check 1. 2 by `freeze.test.ts` (split, a 60-frame hold of the source time shown at the playhead, the sound split and rippled so it is silent, undo) and `PlaybackEngine.test.ts` (one `[2 s, 2 s]` window, 0 drops), plus the browser run and manual check 2. 3 by `freeze.test.ts` (split, right piece x1.2, one "Punch zoom" step, undo restores), plus the preview readback and manual check 3. My probe found no problem in `freezeFrame`, `punchZoom` or `setSpeedDurations`: every document was valid with no overlaps, undo was exact, every freeze was 60 frames at the playhead, and the picture before the playhead was unchanged while what followed moved by 60 frames (within the 1 us split rounding). The Batch 27 entry has no Do not build yet list, and nothing from later batches was built: Reverse has no row or field, `punchZoomAnimated` is read by nothing, and no settings panel exists. No color, font or size literals: the new CSS is `--space-*` tokens, the snowflake is a lucide path in `glyphs.ts` drawn through `Theme`, and the new design gaps are written up. §3/§4: the Speed section fields bind outside React (`speedBinding.ts`), and a scrub applies once on release (written deviation). The quick row follows the selection only, never the playhead (P1). Every edit is one command, time stays integer frames and us, the badge is drawn in the content pass, and no media work was added on the main thread. Every new TODO has a batch tag (35, 36, 42), and no `TODO(batch 27)` is left in `src`. Non-blocking: the Speed section's Duration (linked) reuses `stretchedClip`, so the speed is still derived as `speed * old / new` (the Batch 14 drift note: 13 -> 23 -> 13 frames drifts off 1x).
- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-16)
  1. and 2. `retimeGroup` (`src/timeline/edit/speed.ts`) retimes each member of a linked group from its own start and source range. It no longer shares one change of duration computed from the primary:
    - Linked: duration = round((out - in) / (frame x new speed)), at least 1 frame and within the member's own media. Only the rounding frame can be lost at the media end, so there is no warning. `out` follows `in` exactly, so `in`/`out` stay within that rounding and a later rate stretch keeps the §5.2 invariant. No minimum-duration clamp runs after the media clamp any more.
    - Unlinked: each member keeps its duration, clamped to its own media at the new speed with a `mediaEnd` limit (§6.19 "clamp, warn").
    - Without ripple, each member stops at its own next clip (`neighbor`).
    - Ripple: what follows shifts by the largest change of duration among the members (0 for a member already at the speed). No member runs into what follows on its track, and the clips after stay in sync. A shift to the left still stops where another track would overlap.
    - Members already at the asked speed are left as they are.
    - Results: the review's repro now gives the video and the sound 75 frames each at 4x, both with in/out 0 / 10 000 000 us, and a valid document. The L cut gives the video 120 frames (out 8 s) and the sound 150 frames (out 10 s, the media end), with no toast.
    - Rules written under this entry's deviations (marked "(fix)").
  3. `src/timeline/edit/speed.test.ts`, +5 tests:
    - The review's repro on 10 s media: a speed set with Linked selection off, then changed with it on. Both partners get 75 frames at 4x with in/out 0 / 10 s, `validateProject` is empty, and there is no limit.
    - An L cut with the sound on the media end and a synced pair B/b after it (b starts 60 frames after B): A 120 frames (out 8 s), a 150 (out 10 s), B at 120 and b at 180 (they move together); both pairs stay in sync.

- review: rejected
  1. `src/timeline/edit/speed.ts:128-133` and `:146` (`retimeGroup`): when not even one frame fits between `in` and the media end at the new speed (`maxDurationFrames` is 0), `room` is forced to 1 and `out` is clamped to the media end. The clip is then left with `out - in` short of `duration x speed` by up to almost a whole source frame. That is valid by itself, because the tolerance is one source frame at the new speed. But any later rate stretch to a longer duration keeps `in`/`out` and the expected span while lowering the speed, so the tolerance shrinks below the gap and the document becomes invalid. This affects both the Rate stretch tool (`planStretch` / `stretchedClip`, `src/timeline/edit/trim.ts:491-497`) and the Speed section's linked Duration (`setSpeedDurations`). This is the same hole the fix-attempt note describes for a byte-exact `out`, left open for the one-frame clamp. Unit repro on 10 s media:
    - A 1-frame clip at 1x with in 9 966 667 us (frame 299) and out 10 000 000 us. `setSpeeds(p, ["v"], 2, { link: true, ripple: true, linked: true })` commits duration 1, speed 2, out 10 000 000 us, with `limit` null.
    - Then `setSpeedDurations(next, ["v"], 2, LINKED)` throws `DocumentInvariantError`: `clip "v": out - in = 33333 us, expected duration * speed = 66667 us (within 1 frame)`. `resolveTrim(planStretch(next, "v", "out", { linked: true }), 1)` gives the same issue.
    - The unlinked variant fails too. A 2-frame clip on the media's last 2 frames, set to 4x unlinked, becomes 1 frame with `out` clamped (`mediaEnd` toast). A linked Duration of 4 frames then throws.
    - In the UI: import a 10 s video and trim a clip's in point until only the media's last frame is left. Click 2x in the quick row, then type Duration 00:00:02 in Edit Properties > Speed, or drag the edge one frame with the Rate stretch tool (R). Dev builds throw, and production builds commit and save an invalid document.
    - My probe ran 6000 random documents with in points near the media end: 47 003 commands mixing `setSpeeds`, `setSpeedDurations`, rate stretches and in-point trims, all through a validating `DocumentStore`. It found 108 invariant failures, all with this same `out - in` message, and every one in a run that included a speed change. Runs without a speed change had none.
    - Fix: never leave `out - in` further from `framesToSourceUs(duration, speed, fps)` than its rounding. For example, when one frame does not fit at the asked speed, give that member the speed that makes its remaining source fill one frame, or leave the member unchanged. Either way, report `mediaEnd`.
  2. `src/timeline/edit/speed.test.ts:235-243` (`randomProject`): `in` is drawn from frames 0..250 of the 300-frame media, so no clip ever starts within the last 4 source frames, which the clamp in problem 1 needs. The random test cannot reach that problem. Add a unit test for the repro above: a speed change on a clip at the media's last frame, then a linked Change duration and a Rate stretch, asserting `validateProject(next)` is empty. Let the random generator place in points within the media's last few frames and apply rate stretches after speed changes.

### Batch 28: Text engine
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Model (`core/document/types.ts`, unit tested):
    - `TextContent = { value, box: { mode: "auto" | "fixed", width } }`: plain text with `\n` breaks, and its box.
    - `TextStyle`: family, weight (1..1000), italic, size, fill (`RGBA | Gradient`), stroke / shadow / background (each with `enabled`), case, letter spacing (px), line height (x), align, vertical anchor.
    - `Gradient = { from, to }` (vertical two-stop) is defined now; `isRgba` tells the fills apart.
    - `validate.ts` checks text clips: a string value, a box width > 0, a size, line height and weight the layout can use (+1 test). Fixture `makeTextClip`.
    - New `src/text/style.ts` (`style.test.ts`, 5 tests): `DEFAULT_TEXT_STYLE` (Inter 700, 96 px, white, centered; stroke, shadow and box off but preset), `DEFAULT_TEXT_BOX`, `applyTextCase` (title case not after apostrophes), `firstLine`, `isGradient`, `fillColor`, memoized `contentKey` / `styleKey`, `TEXT_LIMITS`.
    - New `src/text/fonts.ts` (`fonts.test.ts`, 7 tests): `fontShorthand` (with the DS body font as fallback), `quoteFamily`, `parseFaceStyle` ("Bold Italic" -> 700 italic), `nearestFace` (CSS font matching order), the project font manifest (`parseFontManifest` / `formatFontManifest`), `fontExtension` (sfnt tag), `usedFamilies`.
  - Engine (`render/text`, unit tested with an approximate measurer):
    - `layout.ts` (`layout.test.ts`, 11 tests): lines (explicit breaks; a fixed box wraps words, breaks words wider than the box between characters, drops the spaces at a wrap), slots `fontSize x lineHeight` with the em box centered, alignment, case, letter spacing (not after the last character), the content box (the block plus the background padding), per-line or block background boxes, glyph bounds per line (measured ink), and the raster rect (backgrounds, ink grown by the stroke, its shadow grown by 1.5 blurs, whole px + 1).
    - `layoutCache.ts`: `textLayout(content, style)` memoized per object pair (WeakMaps), a version bumped by `invalidateTextLayouts` (fonts loaded), the canvas measurer (`ctx.letterSpacing`, `fontKerning`, one `measureText` per run) or an approximate one without OffscreenCanvas, the fallback font from the theme.
    - `raster.ts`: `rasterizeText` (OffscreenCanvas at the scale; font size, spacing, stroke, padding, radius and shadow scaled by hand; lines measured once at that size and aligned; background boxes in one path; stroke drawn twice as wide with its inside cut away; fill solid or a vertical gradient over the text block; one shadow pass of stroke and fill through a scratch canvas; 4096 px cap) and `buildTextAtlas` (words or lines packed, each with stroke, fill and shadow, built for Batch 31).
    - `atlas.ts` (`atlas.test.ts`, 4 tests): `packShelves`, `atlasUnits` (word positions from prefix advances, rects with stroke and shadow).
    - `TextRasterCache.ts` (`TextRasterCache.test.ts`, 9 tests): GPU textures by content + style + scale bucket (`textScaleBucket`: 0.25..4, rounded up to 2^(1/8) steps; mipmaps only when drawn at under 2/3 of the raster), one slot per clip (identity checks: an unchanged clip costs no work), shared rasters for equal texts, a held raster while a font loads, a 128 MB LRU budget that never evicts what this or the last pass drew, re-raster after a lost device, `dropFamily` when project fonts change, `exact()` for Export frame. Stats: rasters, bytes, rasterizations, the clips rasterized by the last render and its time. New rasters use a layout measured now (a font that just loaded may not have reached the cached layouts).
    - `bundledFonts.ts`: 8 OFL variable families (Inter, Montserrat, Nunito, Oswald, Unbounded, Playfair Display, Caveat, JetBrains Mono), latin and latin-ext subsets, upright and italic where the family has one: 34 woff2 files (1.4 MB) in `render/text/fonts/<family>/` with each `OFL.txt`, from the Fontsource 5.3.0 builds; bundled as hashed assets through `import.meta.glob`.
    - `FontLibrary.ts`: installs the bundled FontFaces (unloaded until a text uses them), `source(family)` (bundled / project / system / missing: a family that measures like the generic fallbacks is missing), `ready(style, text)` (`FontFaceSet.check`, starts the load when not), `load`, project faces (`setProjectFaces` / `clearProjectFaces`), change events (loads, project faces).
    - `localFonts.ts` (dynamic import, 0.68 kB chunk): `queryLocalFonts` faces with weight and italic from their style names, grouped by family, cached for the session.
  - Compositor and preview:
    - `drawList.ts`: `TextCompositeLayer` (model of the layout box, text, style, the layer's own scale, its detail).
    - `Compositor.evaluate(..., texts)`: text clips become text layers (layout box matrix, flips mirror inside the box, fades, live texts of a scrub).
    - `LayerTextures`: text layers resolve through the raster cache at the render scale times the layer's scale, drawn over the raster rect with its crop; `resolveExact` (Export frame) rasterizes at project size; `stats.texts`.
    - `detail.ts`: a text layer's detail is its raster cap (4, 4096 px) over its scale, so a zoom re-rasterizes it (§7.1).
    - `PreviewView`: passes the render scale (lowered like the offscreen 4096 px cap) and the panes' view scale for a split; `texts` and `loadFonts` deps; Export frame waits for fonts and lays out again when it had to.
    - `Renderer.uploadImageBitmap` takes an `OffscreenCanvas` too.
  - Canvas (spec §7.2):
    - `layerContent` returns a text's layout box (the gizmo box, Fit / Fill / Center).
    - `canvasLayersAt(..., texts)`: text layers carry `hits`: each line's glyph bounds padded by `TEXT_HIT_PADDING` (4 project px) and the background boxes, flips included; `layersUnder` and marquees use them (`hitQuads`).
    - `CanvasEditInput` caches key on the live texts and the layout version, and redraw when layouts change.
  - Edits (`timeline/edit/text.ts`, `text.test.ts`, 9 tests):
    - `keepAnchored`: when an edit changes the box's size, the point the alignment and the vertical anchor name (left / center / right, top / middle / bottom) stays where it was on the frame (rotation and scale included).
    - `setTexts` (`clip.text`, one undo step, coalesce key; `clips` in `affects` only when the timeline shows the change), `textEdit`, `newTextClip`, `textClipName`, `addTextClip` (`clip.addText`: the topmost unlocked video track free over the span, else a new top video track).
  - UI:
    - New `panels/properties/TextSection.tsx` + `TextSection.module.css`, right after Clip (`context.ts` `textIds`): content area (typing edits live, one step per burst), font field and picker, weight (the family's range) and italic, size, fill (Solid / Gradient chips, one or two color fields), stroke (switch, width, color), shadow (switch, X / Y, blur, color), background (switch, per line / block, padding X / Y, radius, color), alignment, vertical anchor, letter spacing, line height, case chips, box (Auto / Fixed) and width.
    - `textProps.ts` + `textBinding.ts` (`textBinding.test.ts`, 4 tests): number fields bound outside React; a label scrub writes `liveEdit.texts` and the moved boxes into `liveEdit.transforms` (P2), one command on release.
    - `FontPicker.tsx`: the font field (family in its own font, Missing chip) and its popover: search (Enter picks the first match), Favorites (stars, app setting `favoriteFonts`), Used in project, Bundled, System (Show system fonts asks once; 140 families here). Previews: each row gets its family's font when it scrolls into view.
    - New `app/textController.ts`: installs the fonts, the fallback, the redraws on font changes, `editTexts` / `previewTexts` (color picker drags), the dev command `dev.addText`, `__editor.text`.
    - New `app/fontController.ts`: `systemFonts`, `requestSystemFonts`; `attachFonts` / `detachFonts` (projectController): reads `fonts/fonts.json` and registers the faces; after text edits, copies the system face each clip uses into `fonts/` and writes the manifest.
    - `liveEdit.ts`: `texts`, `patchLiveEdit`.
    - Timeline: a text clip's body shows the first line of its text; a missing font gets a `triangle-alert` badge in `--danger` (`TimelineView` `fonts` dep, the trim preview too).
    - Dev builds: a text-cursor button in the timeline toolbar adds a text clip (`dev.addText`); the dev GPU stats show a `text` line (drawn, waiting, rasters, MB, rasterized).
  - Measured:
    - `npm run check` green: 1372 unit tests in 140 files (+68 tests, +8 files), 3 perf tests. Build 772.98 kB JS (249.59 kB gzip; was 721.03 / 232.50, see the Batch 45 follow-up), 63.48 kB CSS, the 0.68 kB `localFonts` chunk, 34 woff2 assets.

- deviations:
  - The text box lives in `TextContent` (`{ value, box }`): §5.2 keeps `TextClip`'s fields as listed and §10.3 keeps the box apart from the style (presets).
  - `Gradient` (`{ from, to }`, vertical) is defined in this batch for text fills; solids still draw plain colors only (Batch 34).
  - Vertical anchor (§10.1 "top/middle/bottom"): there is no fixed box height to align in, so the anchor names the edge that stays in place when the box changes size; the alignment does the same across. Text edits move the position to keep that point; center / middle (the default) grows evenly.
  - Raster scale = the render scale times the layer's own scale (larger axis), rounded up to 2^(1/8) steps, clamped to 0.25..4, then the 4096 px cap (spec: the detail factor clamped 0.25..4). Reason: a title scaled up on the canvas stays sharp. A text layer's detail (§7.1) is that cap over its scale.
  - Glyph hit testing pads by 4 project px and also hits the background boxes; marquees use the same areas.
  - Text layers snap (§7.4) with their content box, as their gizmo; glyph-bound and baseline snapping are a follow-up.
  - The Text section sits right after Clip (§11 lists it after Audio): it is where a text clip is edited.
  - Rasterization runs on the main thread inside the preview render (§3.1 lists the text rasterizer among the main-thread canvas classes; P5 covers media work), so an edit shows in the same frame. Rasters are GPU only; the optional `cache/text/` is not used.
  - System fonts are listed on a click ("Show system fonts": the permission prompt needs a user gesture) and kept for the session; favorites are an app setting.
  - Project fonts: only the face each clip's weight and italic pick is copied (not the whole family), described by `fonts/fonts.json`, registered under the family's name (so it wins over an installed font of that name) while the project is open. Read-only projects copy nothing.
  - Bundled fonts carry the latin and latin-ext subsets only; other scripts draw with the fallback font.
  - Typing in the content area commits per keystroke; keystrokes within 300 ms of each other are one undo step.
  - Stroke "outside": drawn twice as wide with the inside cut away, round joins. The shadow is one pass of the stroked and filled text; the background casts none. The gradient spans the text block. Per-line boxes cover each non-empty line's slot plus the padding; overlapping boxes fill once. Letter spacing is in project px.
  - Fonts load when a text needs them (that text appears a frame or two later); no IdleQueue font preloads (§3.4's example list).
  - The per-word atlas is built but drawn by nothing until Batch 31; backgrounds stay in the layer raster.

  - `npm run check` is green: typecheck, lint plus the CSS color check, 1372 unit tests in 140 files, 3 perf tests, and the build (772.98 kB JS / 249.59 kB gzip, 63.48 kB CSS, the 0.68 kB `localFonts` chunk, 34 woff2 assets; the >500 kB chunk warning is the logged Batch 45 follow-up).
  - 1. Every acceptance item is covered:

Also the builder's `acc.mjs` diff against Export frame (2.51 / 255 at Fit, 3.16 at 400%), and pending manual check 1.
    - Acceptance 2 (a size change re-rasterizes only that layer, within one frame): `TextRasterCache.test.ts` "re-rasterizes only the clip whose style changed (P11)" (`stats.lastRasterized`). `textBinding.test.ts` covers the typed size (one undo step) and the scrub (live only, then one command). Also `acc.mjs` (6.4 ms, in the same frame) and pending manual check 2.
    - Acceptance 3 (a system font copied into `fonts/`, used after reload): `fonts.test.ts` covers `nearestFace`, the manifest round trip and the sfnt extension. The copy runs through `copyUsedFaces` (`src/app/fontController.ts:136`) and the reload path through `attachFonts` -> `setProjectFaces`. The builder checked it with real Local Font Access over CDP (Georgia-Bold.ttf). Pending manual check 3 gives the exact steps.
    - Acceptance 4 (hits on glyph bounds, not empty box corners): `textLayers.test.ts` "selects on the glyph bounds padded by 4 px, not on the empty corners of the box", plus flips, scale, background boxes and live text. Also `acc.mjs` with real clicks, and pending manual check 4.
  - 2. Nothing from `Do not build yet` was built:
    - Inline editing: no caret or selection code, `TODO(batch 29)` in `TextSection.tsx`.
    - Presets: no preset model or UI, `TODO(batch 30)`.
    - Animations: only the per-word atlas the Build asks for, drawn by nothing, `TODO(batch 31)` in `raster.ts:179`.
    - Captions: `TODO(batch 32)` in the compositor.
    - `+ Title` / `+ Subtitle`: the text clip comes only from the dev command and the dev-only toolbar button.
  - 3. No color, font or size literal outside the tokens file:
    - `TextSection.module.css` uses tokens only, plus the `1px` hairline borders used everywhere.
    - The new sizes are tokens in `tokens.css` (`--font-picker-width`, `--font-picker-list-height`, `--text-content-height`), with design-gap notes.
    - Previews fall back to `var(--font-ui)`, and the text fallback font comes from the Theme.
    - The RGBA values in `style.ts` and `TextSection.tsx` are content colors (the default text style), not UI.
    - The timeline badge uses `theme.color.danger`.
  - 4. §3/§4 hold:
    - React is used for panel chrome only (the Text section and the font picker). Layout, rasterization and the raster cache are framework-free and run inside the preview's scheduler pass. The picker's lazy previews are DOM writes from an IntersectionObserver.
    - Label scrubs and color drags write `liveEdit.texts` / `liveEdit.transforms` only and commit one `clip.text` command on release (P2). Typing coalesces by key (a logged deviation).
    - New clips use integer frames (`addTextClip` rounds, and `secondsToFrames` sets the duration).
    - No media work was added on the main thread. Text rasterization is the §3.1 main-thread rasterizer (a logged deviation), and the font listing is a dynamic import (P12).
    - Rasters are cached by content + style + scale bucket, so editing one layer re-rasterizes only that layer (P11). Textures are destroyed on eviction, clear and device loss (P13).
  - 5. Every TODO in the diff has a `(batch NN)` tag, and no `TODO(batch 28)` is left in `src`.
  - 6. There were no earlier rejections.
  - Non-blocking notes:
    - `wrapParagraph` (`src/render/text/layout.ts:117-124`): in a fixed box, a paragraph that starts with spaces, whose first word does not fit after them, gets an extra empty first line.
    - `setTexts` does not rename the clip: a dev clip keeps the label "Text" while its body shows the edited first line.
    - `keepAnchored` uses the layout measured at edit time. If a newly picked family is still loading, the box changes size again when it loads, without re-anchoring.
    - A font load bumps `fontsVersion`, which re-renders the mounted font field. That can happen once during playback, when a text whose font is not loaded yet first appears.
    - Raster keys do not include the fallback stack. This is moot while the theme is dark only.

### Batch 29: Text UX: one-click titles and subtitles, inline editing, text tool
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Engine (unit tested):
    - `render/text/layout.ts`: each `TextLine` has `start` / `end`, its place in the text as drawn (case applied, line breaks included); the spaces a wrap drops sit after `end`.
    - New `render/text/caret.ts` (`caret.test.ts`, 15 tests):
      - Caret stops are grapheme boundaries (`Intl.Segmenter`), measured the way the rasterizer draws: at the raster scale (font size times the scale, divided back), each line aligned by its width at that size as `raster.ts` aligns its runs.
      - A stop's x is where the next grapheme's advance starts, measured with the pair before it so kerning counts, less half the letter spacing.
      - Also: `caseMap` (source and display indices, grapheme by grapheme when a case changes lengths, "ß" upper is "SS"), `caretAt` (downstream at a wrap unless `upstream`), `selectionRects` (line slots, a sliver past a break), `indexAtPoint`, `verticalMove` (goal x), `lineEdge` (Home / End), `wordAt`.
    - `raster.ts`: `TextHighlight` (rects in content px, a CSS color) is drawn between the background boxes and the text; `rasterRect` grows the raster over it.
    - `TextRasterCache.resolve(..., highlight)`: a highlighted raster is the slot's own (no key, never shared, destroyed when the slot draws something else or ends). +1 test.
    - `TextCompositeLayer.highlight` and `LiveText.highlight` carry it through `Compositor.writeText` and `LayerTextures`. +1 test.
    - `PreviewView.textRenderScale`: the device px per project px the last render rasterized text at.
    - `text/style.ts` (`style.test.ts`, +3 tests):
      - `DEFAULT_TITLE_STYLE`: Inter 800, 120 px, line height 1.1, a soft shadow.
      - `DEFAULT_SUBTITLE_STYLE`: Inter 600, 56 px, line height 1.3, a 70% black box per line (padding 24 / 10, radius 14), bottom anchor; `DEFAULT_SUBTITLE_BOX` is fixed at 900 px.
      - `titleDefaults(kind, frame)` scales them by min(width, height) / 1080, with `TODO(batch 30)` presets.
      - `scaleTextStyle(style, k)` scales the font size (0.1 px steps, within the limits) and every px value with it.
    - `timeline/edit/text.ts` (`text.test.ts`, +9 tests):
      - `addTitle(project, { kind, playhead, matchClip, below })` gives one `clip.addText` command, "Add title" / "Add subtitle".
      - Track: the topmost video track when it is unlocked, shown and free over the span, else a new top track.
      - `titleDuration`: 3 s, or the rest of the topmost video clip under the playhead.
      - Position: the Title centered, or 40 px under a selected text clip (centered under it); the Subtitle's bottom edge at 78% of the frame height.
      - `setTexts` takes an edit's own transform (`TextEditInput`). A clip still named after its text keeps being named after it ("Title" becomes "hello"; a name the user gave stays; the Batch 28 review note).
    - New `render/preview/textGizmo.ts` (`textGizmo.test.ts`, 6 tests):
      - `textSizeGesture`: a corner sizes the font and every px value; a fixed box's width scales too; the opposite corner (Alt: the center) stays.
      - `textWidthGesture`: a side sets a fixed box width, the text reflows; the opposite side (Alt: the center) stays, at the height the vertical anchor names.
  - Inline editing: new `render/preview/InlineTextEditor.ts` (`InlineTextEditor.test.ts`, 11 tests, jsdom).
    - The textarea:
      - A transparent `<textarea data-scope="textEditing">` in the preview host, placed over the text block by a CSS matrix (content px to canvas CSS px, flips and turns included). It has the layout's font shorthand, line height, letter spacing, alignment and `pre` / `pre-wrap`.
      - It takes no pointer input and draws nothing (no text, caret, selection color or focus ring). It provides keys, IME, the clipboard and undo of the typing.
      - A capture scroll guard puts back the panels the browser scrolls to show its caret.
    - Draft and display:
      - The draft, selection and composition are the editor's. `live()` gives the draft over the clip's shown text (a field scrub's live values included), its box moved by `keepAnchored` and the selection highlight. It is memoized, so the preview and the canvas caches see stable objects.
      - The caret (blinking `--text-caret-blink` while focused, steady otherwise, hidden with a selection) and the IME underline are a `scene()` for the overlay, computed by `caret.ts`.
    - Keys: Up / Down / Home / End (Shift extends) follow the drawn lines through `caret.ts`, keeping a goal x and the end-of-wrapped-line affinity. Everything else is the textarea's own; `keyup`, `select` and `selectionchange` sync the selection.
    - Pointer: `press` places the caret (Shift extends; a second press within 500 ms and 4 CSS px selects the word, a third everything), `drag` selects, `release`.
    - Ending: `end(commit)` makes one "Edit text" `clip.text` command when the draft differs. A press or focus outside the textarea and the kept areas commits.
    - It also ends when the clip leaves the selection, the playhead leaves the clip or its track hides (committed), and when the clip goes or locks (no command).
  - Canvas (`CanvasEditInput`, `gizmo.ts`, `PreviewOverlayView`; `CanvasEditInput.text.test.ts`, 5 tests):
    - One selected text layer's gizmo has corner and side handles only (`hitGizmo(..., text)`, `scene.textBox`). Corner drags are `textSize` and side drags `textWidth`: live through `CanvasEditInput.texts` and the gesture transforms, then one `clip.text` command ("Resize text" / "Change text box width"). Multi-selections scale as before.
    - A double-click on a text layer selects it alone and edits it with the caret at the click. While editing, a press on the edited box (grown by half a handle hit) goes to the editor; a press elsewhere commits first, then acts. The cursor is `text` over the box.
    - The scene while editing: the edited box outlined in `--accent` (no handles, no hover) plus the caret and underline. The overlay draws the caret (`--text-caret-width`) and underline in `--accent`.
  - App:
    - `previewController`: `textEditor` (deps: the placement, the text render scale, the clip as shown with `liveEdit`, the commit).
      - Kept areas: the overlay canvas, `[data-scope="properties"]` and dialogs, menus and listboxes (the font picker, color picker and canvas menu).
      - `onEditing` gives the keyboard to `textEditing` (also after a press on the text, which the preview scope took first) and back to the preview.
      - `liveTexts()` / `liveTransforms()` merge a canvas text handle drag or a field scrub with the draft, memoized; the preview and the canvas read them.
    - `textController`:
      - `add.title` (T) and `add.subtitle` (Shift+T) commit an open edit, pause playback, call `addTitle` (`below`: the selected text clip, primary first; `matchClip`: the new app setting `matchClipUnderPlayhead`, true) and select the clip. Then they edit it with everything selected.
      - `text.edit` (Enter, timeline and preview): the selected text clip, the playhead moved onto it when elsewhere; declined without one.
      - `text.commit` (Mod+Enter) and `text.cancel` (Esc) both commit and exit.
      - The five ids are out of `planned.ts`.
    - `canvasController`: Esc (`app.cancel`) also ends inline editing, after a running gesture (with the focus in the properties panel).
    - Timeline toolbar: `+ Title` and `+ Subtitle` are enabled; the dev text button is gone (`dev.addText` and `__editor.text.add` stay for scripts).
    - Text section: the content area shows the draft while its clip is edited on the canvas; focusing it commits that editing first.
    - Theme bridge `gizmo.caretWidth`, `caretBlink` and `textSelection`; tokens `--text-caret-width`, `--text-caret-blink` and `--text-selection`.
  - Measured:
    - `npm run check` green: 1424 unit tests in 144 files (+52 tests, +4 files), 3 perf tests. Build 797.95 kB JS (257.74 kB gzip; was 772.98 / 249.59), 63.93 kB CSS.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP on the dev server, 1600 x 900 at dpr 1. Scripts in `%TEMP%/b29/`: `acc.mjs` 23/23, `extra.mjs` 15/15, `shot.mjs`, `smoke.mjs`, `page.js` (`__b29`), `setup.mjs`.
    - Acceptance 1 (project `b29-a1`):
      - T at frame 45: editing in 31-34 ms, "Title" selected, scope `textEditing`.
      - Typing "hello" then Esc gives a 90-frame title "hello" (named "hello") at 45, centered (box center 540, 960) on V1, the empty track.
      - 7 keys, 238-285 ms of app time from T to the committed text.
      - Undo: "Edit text", then "Add title". A second T over the first goes onto a new V2.
    - Acceptance 2 (project `b29-a2`; the composite screenshot with the overlay hidden, ink edges at half coverage, against the overlay's caret):
      - "IIIII" (symmetric glyphs): each interior caret within 0.10 px of the middle of the gap between the stems drawn, at Fit (view scale 0.385) and at 200% (view panned).
      - "hello" (kerning, unequal sidebearings): every caret inside its gap at Fit and 200% (offsets from the gap middle ≤ 0.6 px at Fit, ±2.8 px at 200%: the letters' own sidebearings).
    - Acceptance 3 (project `b29-a3`):
      - "hello" typed, caret at 3. A real click into Font size, 150, Enter: still editing, draft "hello", caret 3, the caret 27% taller; the document has 150 and still "Title".
      - A click on the text gives the keyboard back, and the carets sit inside the gaps at 150 px. Esc commits "hello" at 150.
    - Acceptance 4 (project `b29-a4`): Shift+T edits "Subtitle", all selected. A two-line subtitle sits at y 1331.8..1497.4 (the lower third from 1280; bottom at 78%), with a box per line, a fixed 900 px box and the bottom anchor. Screenshots: `a4-subtitle.png`, `s3-subtitle.png`.
    - Extra (`extra.mjs`):
      - Keys and presses: Mod+Enter commits. Enter edits the selected clip, all selected. A title made with a text clip selected sits 40 px under it. A double-click edits with the caret at the click, and a double press selects the word, the scope staying `textEditing`. Enter inserts a line break; Up, End and Shift+Down follow the lines.
      - The highlight is in the composite (9351 gold pixels with the overlay hidden). IME through `Input.imeSetComposition`: one underline segment, then the committed characters replace the composition. A press on the timeline commits.
      - Handles: a corner drag gives 140.9 px at scale 1, "Resize text", with the top-left fixed; a side drag gives fixed 500 px, "Change text box width"; no top or bottom handles.
      - A title over a 150-frame video at 40 lasts 110 frames on a new V2.
      - Typing 10 keys on the canvas: 10 React commits, the content field showing the draft.
    - The browser checks found three bugs, fixed before this commit:
      - The global `:focus-visible` ring drew a box around the transparent textarea.
      - A press on the edited text left the keyboard scope on the preview (Esc and Mod+Enter would not have reached the editor).
      - T, then Shift+T, stacked the subtitle under the title.
    - Regression: Batch 28 `acc.mjs` 16/16. Its `extra.mjs` used the dev button this batch removes: the copy `%TEMP%/b28/reg_b29.mjs` uses `+ Title` (size 120), 6/6. Batch 27 `acc.mjs` 33/34 (the known project-switch warning, [from Batch 27 -> Batch 47]).
- deviations:
  - The caret is drawn on the overlay canvas from the rasterizer's own geometry, not in the raster: a blink would re-rasterize the layer twice a second (P11). The selection highlight is in the raster (§7.7), as the clip's own raster.
  - Esc (`text.cancel`, §18 "cancel") commits and exits like Mod+Enter, as §7.7 and §10.2 say ("Esc keeps the clip with the typed content"). Undo takes an edit back.
  - An inline edit is one undo step ("Edit text"), apart from "Add title" / "Add subtitle".
  - Text box handles (§7.3):
    - Corners size the font and every px value of the style with it, and a fixed box's width ("keeping layout": the same look, larger).
    - Side handles set the box width; an auto box becomes fixed at that width, Figma style.
    - No top or bottom handles. Shift does nothing, Alt works from the center, and the drags do not snap (§7.4): see [from Batch 29 -> Batch 46].
    - With several layers selected, the group scales as before.
  - The 40 px stacking applies to Titles (the Build's "instead of at the center"). A Subtitle always goes to the lower third, so T then Shift+T, with the title still selected, gives a subtitle under it in the lower third.
  - Title placement (§10.2 "topmost video track, new track if the spot is occupied"): the topmost video track when it is unlocked, shown and free over the title's span, else a new top track, even when a lower track is free (the title stays on top of the picture).
  - The Subtitle's bottom edge sits at 78% of the frame height (the lower third, §10.2), anchored at the bottom so more lines grow upwards, in a fixed 900 px box (5/6 of the frame width). On 9:16 the platforms' safe zones end at about 65% (Batch 21's overlay); the spec asks for the lower third.
  - Default sizes scale with the project frame: min(width, height) / 1080.
  - "Match clip under playhead" counts the topmost video clip (kind video) on a shown video track. The app setting `matchClipUnderPlayhead` (on) has no switch until Batch 42.
  - Click-away (§7.7 "clicking elsewhere commits"): presses and focus in the properties panel, the popovers, menus and dialogs keep the edit ("the properties panel text fields stay live"). Focusing the Text section's content field commits the inline edit first (one editor of the content at a time); anything else commits.
  - Enter (`text.edit`) with a selected text clip off the playhead moves the playhead onto the clip, so the canvas shows it, and selects all its text.
  - While a text is edited on the canvas, the Text section's content area shows the draft: one React render of that field per key (typing is not in P1's list; the canvas renders no React).
  - A text clip still named after its text follows its text when edited (the Batch 28 review note); a name the user gave stays.

  - 4. EDITOR_SPEC §3 and §4 hold:
    - React is chrome only. `InlineTextEditor`, `textGizmo` and `caret.ts` are framework-free.
    - Typing on the canvas renders only the Text section's content field, once per key: a logged deviation, and typing is not on P1's list. `extra.mjs` x14 counted 10 commits for 10 keys.
    - One command per gesture. An inline edit is one "Edit text" command at its end. A handle drag shows live through `CanvasEditInput.texts`, then commits one `clip.text` on release.
    - Time stays in integer frames: `titleDuration` uses `secondsToFrames`. The batch adds no media work.
    - The canvases draw through the scheduler. The caret blink only invalidates `gizmo` and never re-rasterizes (P11). A highlighted raster belongs to its slot and is destroyed when the slot changes.
  - 5. Every TODO in `src/` has a `(batch NN)` tag (a grep over `src/` found none untagged).
  - 6. This entry has no earlier `review: rejected` list.
  - Notes (not blocking):
    - `src/render/preview/InlineTextEditor.ts:102-103`: the double-press slop (4 CSS px, 500 ms) is a constant in code. The drag, snap and scrub thresholds live in `tokens.css`; the timeline's `HEAD_SLOP` is a constant in code too.
    - `InlineTextEditor.ts:239` and `:545`: if the editor unmounts or the clip leaves the document mid-edit (for example a project switch), editing ends with no command. An uncommitted draft is dropped.
    - `InlineTextEditor.ts:568-573`: `rasterScale` caps with `rasterRect(layout)`, without the highlight, but the raster grows over the highlight (`raster.ts` `rasterRect`). At the 4096 px raster cap, the caret can be measured at a slightly different scale from the one drawn.

### Batch 30: Text presets library
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Model (unit tested):
    - `TextPreset` (`core/document/types.ts`): id, name, style, box, animIn / animOut, defaultDuration (whole ms), position, applyPosition, tags, favorite, createdAt, updatedAt.
    - New `text/presets.ts` (`presets.test.ts`, 18 tests):
      - Sizes are stored for a 1080 px short side: `presetScale`, `presetLook` (style via `scaleTextStyle`, fixed box width, the position only when `applyPosition`); nothing is scaled on 1080 px projects.
      - `presetFromClip`, `presetUpdatedFromClip` (name, flags and creation kept), `presetDiffers` (style, fixed box width, animations; content and position are the clip's own).
      - `defaultPresetName` ("Oswald 140"), `thumbnailText` (the name, `Aa` over 12 characters), `uniquePresetName`, `duplicatePreset`, `sortPresets`, `searchPresets` (name, tags, family; every word), `favoriteChips` (8: global first), `mergePresets` (newer `updatedAt` wins).
      - Files: `sanitizeTextStyle` / `sanitizePreset` (every value checked, defaults for bad ones), `formatPresetsFile` / `parsePresetsFile` (an export, a list or one preset; newer versions refused), `presetFileName`, `exportFileName`.
    - New `text/starterPresets.ts`: 12 starters in the bundled fonts: Title and Subtitle (Batch 29's defaults, the default presets), Bold outline, Lower third (applies its position), Caption bold, Highlight, Minimal mono, Big number (gradient), Elegant serif, Handwritten, Neon glow, Breaking. Each has a position (the frame center; the Subtitle one line in the lower third), used once Apply position is on.
    - `timeline/edit/text.ts` (`text.test.ts`, +4 tests): `applyPreset` (`clip.applyPreset`, "Apply "Name"": style, box, animations and `presetId` on the unlocked text clips; content, timing and position kept, the position taken when the preset applies it), `linkPreset` (`clip.linkPreset`), `addTitle({ look, label })` (a preset's look, id and default duration; its position or the Title / Subtitle placement), `newTextClip` takes `presetId` and animations.
    - `timeline/edit/dropMedia.ts` (`dropMedia.test.ts`, +2 tests): a drop item is media or a text template (`TextDropItem`): a text clip on the video lane, through the same staging, ghosts, overwrite / insert rules and request.
    - New `project/presetFiles.ts` (`presetFiles.test.ts`, 4 tests): `projectPresetsDir` (`presets/text/`, made on the first write), `readPresetFolder` (`*.json`, other files skipped, the newer of two copies), `PresetFolderWriter` (writes presets whose `updatedAt` changed, removes files of presets that left, one write at a time).
    - App settings `defaultTitlePreset` / `defaultSubtitlePreset` (the starter ids; null = built-in; +1 test); session `mediaPanel.presetScope` (global / project, in session.json).
  - App (`app/presetController.ts`):
    - `presetLibrary` store: global presets from IndexedDB `textPresets.v1` (the starters put there on the first start), the open project's from `presets/text/` (attached and detached with the project; written while it is writable), the library folder state.
    - Library folder (handle in IndexedDB `presetLibraryFolder.v1`): choosing one merges both ways and writes every global preset as `<id>.json`; later changes are written; on start it syncs when the browser still grants access, else the strip asks for a click (Sync); Stop forgets the folder.
    - Actions: Save as preset (the primary selected text clip's look, a unique name, into the tab's scope, the clip linked: one undo step "Save preset "Name""), Update preset (toast: other clips keep their style until it is applied again), Apply (the selected text clips, else a new clip), Add (`addTitleClip` with the preset's look: at the playhead, edited at once), rename, duplicate, delete, favorite, apply position, default Title / Subtitle (T / Shift+T use them through `setDefaultTitleLook`), import / export (`showOpenFilePicker` / `showSaveFilePicker`, else an input and a download link).
    - Hover: a hovered card shows the preset on the selected text clips through `liveEdit` (texts, and transforms for Apply position); it ends on leave, scroll, a drag, a selection change, a click.
    - Drags: `presetDragSource` for `pressDragCard` (`mediaGestures.ts` now takes any `CardDragSource`): over the timeline the media drop ghosts, released there one "Add clip" command; over the canvas a new clip at the playhead centered on the point.
    - Actions `preset.save` and `preset.update` (the quick row); dev `__editor.presets`.
  - UI:
    - New `panels/media/PresetsTab.tsx` (+ CSS): header actions New from selection, Import, Export; a tool row with Global / Project and the search; the name row; cards (thumbnail, name, badges: favorite, default title, default subtitle; tooltip with the gestures); the card menu (Apply / Add at playhead, Use as default title (T), Use as default subtitle (Shift+T), Favorite, Apply position, Rename (in place), Duplicate, Export…, Delete); the library folder strip (Global scope); empty, loading and read-only states.
    - New `render/text/presetThumbnails.ts`: the name (or `Aa`) laid out and rasterized by the text engine in the preset's look, fitted to the card with a margin, cached by look + text + size + layout version (96 entries); a font still loading draws nothing, the font change redraws (`fontsVersion`).
    - New `panels/media/PresetNameField.tsx`: prefilled with font + size, all selected; Enter saves, Esc or blur cancels.
    - Quick row: `Save as preset` live (a text clip selected), `Update preset` with the preset's name and the tooltip `Update "Name": saves this clip's style into the preset. Other clips that use "Name" keep their own style until you apply the preset to them again.`; disabled when the clip has no preset, its preset is gone or read-only, or the style is the preset's.
    - Timeline toolbar: up to 8 favorite chips after the add buttons (`presetChips` item), following the library live; the overflow fitting measures again when they change.
  - Measured:
    - `npm run check` green: 1453 unit tests in 146 files (+29 tests, +2 files), 3 perf tests. Build 831.49 kB JS (268.47 kB gzip; was 797.95 / 257.74), 68.06 kB CSS.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP on the dev server (5181), 1600 x 900 at dpr 1. Scripts in `%TEMP%/b30/`: `acc.mjs` 32/32, `extra.mjs` 23/23, `p1.mjs`, `smoke.mjs`, `page.js` (`__b30`), `setup.mjs`.
    - Acceptance 1 (project `b30-acc`): T, "Promo", Esc; styled Oswald 600 140 px red with an 8 px black stroke. A real click on Save as preset opened the name field focused with "Oswald 140" selected; "Red promo" + Enter saved it (global), linked the clip ("Save preset "Red promo""), and the card appeared: its 118 x 66 thumbnail had 682 red and 469 black pixels, centered. Favorite from the card menu: the chip appeared; a click on it at frame 150 made a title with exactly that style, linked, editing at once ("Add "Red promo"").
    - Acceptance 2: Update was disabled while the clip matched; after a green 120 px change its label and hover tooltip read exactly the text above. A click wrote green 120 into the preset (the thumbnail turned green); the other clip kept red 140; a click on the card with that clip selected re-applied it (green 120, content and timing kept, one step "Apply "Red promo"").
    - Acceptance 3: Use as default subtitle on Highlight (badge shown); Shift+T made "Subtitle" with the Highlight style, linked, its bottom at 78% (y 1498).

- deviations:
  - Project presets are files in `presets/text/<id>.json` (spec §9.7 and the Build), not entries of `Project.textPresetsLocal` (§5.2, kept and always empty): a preset library is not document editing, so like the global presets they are outside the undo history. Save as preset's link (`presetId`) and Apply are commands.
  - Preset sizes are stored for a 1080 px short side and scale with the project (Batch 29's defaults did so): a preset looks the same on a 4K project. A fixed box width scales by the same factor (Batch 29 sized the Subtitle's box from the frame width: on 16:9 the default subtitle box is now 900 px, was 1600).
  - `TextPreset` adds `applyPosition` (the spec's "apply position" option) and `updatedAt` (the library folder sync). `defaultDuration` is whole milliseconds (presets cross projects of any fps); nothing sets it yet.
  - Applying a preset replaces the box (auto / fixed width) with the style: the box is part of the look (§10.3 stores it).
  - Apply keeps the clip's position as it is (X / Y unchanged), not the anchored edge, unless the preset applies its position.
  - New clips from a preset (chips, a card with no text clip selected, a drop) say "Title" and are edited at once like `+ Title`; they use the Title placement (40 px under a selected text clip), or the preset's position. Shift+T keeps the Subtitle placement with the default Subtitle preset.
  - Save as preset names are made unique within their scope ("Name 2"); the preset goes into the tab's scope (global when the project is read-only or none is open).
  - Default Title / Subtitle are app settings by preset id; a missing one (deleted, or a closed project's) falls back to the built-in look. Choosing the default again turns it off (built-in).
  - The library folder sync is a union by id where the newer `updatedAt` wins; a preset deleted on one machine comes back from another machine's copy. It runs on start (when the browser keeps the access) and on Sync; it lives in a strip at the bottom of the Global scope until the settings panel (Batch 42).
  - Import puts presets into the shown scope; a preset with a known id replaces it (an export imported again changes nothing).
  - The Presets header (§17) keeps New from selection, Import and Export as icon buttons; the Global / Project toggle and the search are a tool row under it (the media panel header is too narrow for five controls next to the tabs).
  - Rename is in the card menu and edits in place; a double-click on a card applies twice (click = apply), so it does not rename.
  - The starters are seeded into the global presets once (first start), then the user's to change or delete; there is no restore yet (follow-up).

- review: rejected
  1. Preset card thumbnails are drawn outside the `FrameScheduler`. This breaks spec §3.4 and P3, both BINDING ("Nothing renders outside this loop", "All canvases render only when invalidated, through the single scheduler"), and no deviation is recorded for it. `src/panels/media/PresetsTab.tsx:67-80` (`PresetThumb`) calls `drawPresetThumbnail` (`src/render/text/presetThumbnails.ts:42-54`) straight from a `ResizeObserver` callback, once per card, and again from the effect on every preset or `fontsVersion` change. That call lays the text out, rasterizes it and draws it into the card's on-screen canvas. Every other on-screen canvas draws in the scheduler loop: the timeline, the preview overlay, the meters, and the Media tab's cards through `PosterPainter` (`src/panels/media/PosterPainter.ts:29`, `register("mediaThumbs", ...)`). In practice, opening the tab rasterizes every card at once in one observer pass. A splitter drag that resizes the grid re-rasterizes every visible card at each new size, outside the scheduler's one-render-per-frame rule. Fix:
     - Draw the thumbnails through a painter registered with the scheduler, as the Media tab does. The observer and the preset or font changes only record the canvas's size and preset, then call `scheduler.invalidate(...)` (for example `mediaThumbs`). `render()` draws each dirty card once per frame.
     - Add a unit test in the style of `PosterPainter.test.ts`: a burst of size changes draws once per frame, and a detached canvas is no longer drawn.
  2. `src/panels/properties/TextSection.tsx:49` still says `TODO(batch 30): Save as preset.` Save as preset is built (the quick row in `QuickActions.tsx`, and the Presets header), so this hook is stale and points at a finished batch. Remove it.
  - Not blocking (worth fixing with 1): see `src/app/presetController.ts:267-285` (`attachPresets`) and `:117-139` (`commit`). A project preset saved or imported before `attachPresets` has finished reading `presets/text/` is added to the empty list. The queued write then runs with a writer that already `know`s the files just read. It writes only the new preset and deletes the files of all the others. The read's `setLibrary({ project: ... })` (`:280`) also drops the new preset from the tab. The window is short (one folder read after a project opens), but the result is deleted preset files. Fix: make project commits wait for the read, or build the list inside the queued write from what the read found.
  - Checked and fine:

- fix-attempt: done (2026-10-16)
  1. Preset card thumbnails draw in the FrameScheduler loop (spec §3.4, P3):
    - New `src/panels/media/PresetThumbPainter.ts`, framework-free like `PosterPainter`, registered on `mediaThumbs`. `show(canvas, preset)`, `resize(canvas, w, h)` and `redrawAll()` (fonts) only record the card and call `scheduler.invalidate("mediaThumbs")`. `render()` draws each dirty card once, at its latest preset and size. A card with no size yet (or a hidden one, 0 x 0) is not drawn. `detach(canvas)` drops a card and its pending draw.
    - The instance is `presetThumbs` in `presetController.ts`. Its draw function calls `drawPresetThumbnail` with the device pixel ratio and margin read at draw time. A `fontsVersion` change calls `presetThumbs.redrawAll()` (subscribed in `installPresetController`).
    - `PresetThumb` (`PresetsTab.tsx`) draws nothing itself. Its ResizeObserver hands the size to `presetThumbs.resize`, an effect hands the preset to `presetThumbs.show`, and unmounting detaches the canvas. It no longer subscribes to `fontsVersion`, so a font load no longer re-renders every card through React.
    - New `PresetThumbPainter.test.ts` (5 tests), in the style of `PosterPainter.test.ts`:
      - A burst of sizes on two cards draws nothing outside the frame, then once each at the last size.
      - Nothing is drawn until both the preset and a size are known, and a hidden card is skipped.
      - A changed preset draws again, and `redrawAll` draws every card once.
      - A detached card is not drawn, even with a draw pending.
      - `dispose` unregisters.
  2. `src/panels/properties/TextSection.tsx`: the stale `TODO(batch 30): Save as preset.` is removed. No `TODO(batch 30)` is left in `src`.
  3. (non-blocking item) The project preset race in `presetController.ts`:
    - The read in `attachPresets` now merges what it found with the presets already in the list (`mergePresets`, the newer copy of an id wins). A preset saved or imported during the read stays in the tab.
    - The queued project write (`commit`) writes the list as it is when the write runs (`list("project")`), not the snapshot taken at commit time. The write after the read therefore holds the files just read and adds the new one, instead of deleting the others.
    - New `src/app/presetController.test.ts`, with the services mock of `projectController.test.ts`. It gates the project root's folder lookups so the read is pending, imports a preset into the Project scope, then opens the gate. The tab and `presets/text/` must end with all three presets. The test fails on the old code (the tab shows `[a, b]`) and passes with the fix.

### Batch 31: Text animations
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Model (unit tested):
    - `TextAnim` in `core/document/types.ts`: `id` (the 12 of §10.4, `TEXT_ANIM_IDS`), `duration` (frames), `perUnit` (char / word / line), `stagger` (frames), `ease`. `PresetTextAnim`: the same in milliseconds, for presets.
    - New `text/anim.ts` (`anim.test.ts`, 15 tests):
      - Per animation: chip label, unit (block, char, word, line), own ease, first length (`TEXT_ANIMS`). Eases `TEXT_EASES`: linear, easeIn, easeOut, easeInOut. An out animation's own ease out becomes ease in.
      - `activeAnim`: the in animation from the clip's first frame (progress 0: nothing shown yet, as fades) and the out one to its last (progress 1). `animLength` caps each at half the clip, so they never overlap; under 2 frames nothing plays. Animations the engine cannot play (no id, no whole-frame duration) are ignored (`isPlayable`).
      - Units: the duration is the whole animation. Unit i starts `stagger` frames after unit i - 1 and plays `D - (n - 1) * stagger`. A stagger over D / n is cut to it; none gives D / (n + 1.5) (each unit overlaps 1.5 others). Out animations play the units last first. A typewriter types by count (`typedCount`) and erases from the end.
      - Shapes (`writeUnitShape`) from the eased presence `a` (1 = at rest):
        - fade and wordFade: opacity.
        - Slides: 0.6 em of travel. In comes from the far side, out leaves in the named direction (slideUp out moves up).
        - pop and wordPop: a back-out scale from 0.4, opacity reaching 1 by a third of the way.
        - scaleBounce: from 0 with two damped bounces.
        - lineReveal: slides a line's height inside its place (masked).
        - blurIn: a Gaussian blur of 0.2 em at the start, and the opacity.
      - `newTextAnim` keeps the previous animation's duration, stagger and ease. Also `sanitizeTextAnim` / `sanitizePresetAnim`, `toPresetAnim` / `fromPresetAnim` (ms <-> frames through `time.ts`; 29.97 rounds back to the same frames) and `sameAnim`.
    - New `timeline/edit/textAnim.ts` (`textAnim.test.ts`, 3 tests): `setTextAnims` (`clip.animation`, one undo step, coalescing key; null removes; locked, non-text and unchanged clips skipped; affects `clips` for the badge) and `animEdits` (per clip from its own animation).
    - `text/presets.ts` (+2 tests): presets store animations in ms (`presetFromClip`, `presetUpdatedFromClip`); `presetLook` gives them in the project's frames; `presetDiffers` compares in frames; `sanitizePreset` checks them. The frame argument now carries the fps.
    - `text/starterPresets.ts` (+1 test): Lower third (slide right in, fade out), Caption bold (word pop), Neon glow (blur in) and Breaking (slide up) have animations; Title and Subtitle none, so T and Shift+T stay still. `upgradeStarters` gives the animations to starters stored before this batch and never changed since (`updatedAt` still the bundled one). The preset controller applies it once when the library loads.
    - `timeline/edit/text.test.ts` (+1 test): a preset's animations reach a selected clip (Apply) and a new clip (`addTitle` with its look), in the project's frames (a 60 fps project gets 30 frames for 500 ms).
  - Rendering:
    - `render/text/atlas.ts` (+2 tests): `char` units (graphemes, spaces left out), each unit's `offset` in its line and its middle (`originX/Y`, what a pop scales about); `lineStops` (each line's grapheme boundaries in content px).
    - `raster.ts`:
      - `rasterizeText(..., part)`: `backgrounds` draws the background boxes alone over the same rect.
      - `buildTextAtlas` draws each unit where the layer raster draws it: its line aligned at the raster size, its prefix measured at it. A text at rest looks the same from either.
    - New `render/text/animUnits.ts`: the units and stops cached per layout object (the compositor allocates nothing per frame).
    - `TextRasterCache` (+4 tests):
      - Parts besides the raster: `resolveAtlas` (a cell crop and rect per unit) and `resolveBackground`, keyed apart. `ResolvedText` gives `scale` (texture px per content px) and `units`.
      - `warm(clipId, part, ...)` makes a part ahead of time; its slot keeps it while passes warm it.
      - `exact(..., part)` for Export frame.
      - A clip's own raster slot is still keyed by the clip id alone.
    - `Compositor`:
      - Text layers carry `anim: TextAnimFrame | null`: `block` (one state moving the layer raster), or an atlas kind with one state per unit and the background opacity (the leading unit's).
      - A typewriter's characters use the line atlas, cut at the last typed grapheme (`cut`), so the glyphs are the static text's exactly.
      - A text edited inline (`LiveText.editing`) shows at rest. `evaluate(..., anims)` takes live animations (a field scrub).
      - `upcomingTexts(doc, frame, reach)` lists the text clips showing in the window and the parts they draw.
    - `LayerTextures`: a block animation draws the raster moved (scale about the block's middle, offset, opacity, blur in texture px); a per-unit one draws the background raster, then a pooled texture layer per cell. A masked line is cropped to its place; a cut line is cropped at its x. Export frame does the same with exact rasters. `resolve(..., upcoming)` warms the window's parts: all while idle, one per pass while playing or scrubbing (then it asks for another pass).
    - `TextureLayer.blur` (sigma, texture px); `DrawListSnapshot` compares it.
    - `WebGpuRenderer`: a blurred record is blurred first, in the draw's command encoder. Two passes of a separable Gaussian (`BLUR_WGSL`): horizontal into a pooled texture, then vertical into another; sigma is capped at 16 texture px (`MAX_BLUR_SIGMA`, 3 sigma reach). It is then drawn from the second texture with its quad grown by the reach. Pooled textures go in 64 px steps and are freed after 120 unused draws; the uniforms use their own 256-byte-slot buffer.
    - `PreviewView`: `anims` dep (live animations); text clips within `TEXT_LOOKAHEAD_SECONDS` (5 s) of the playhead are warmed.
    - Tests in `render/text/textLayers.test.ts` (+7):
      - Evaluation at the frame: units in, at rest, a block out, at rest while edited inline.
      - Slide offset and opacity, blur in texture px.
      - Units over the boxes, a pop scaled about its middle.
      - A typewriter's cut, a line reveal's crop.
      - Upcoming parts warmed (all idle, one per pass playing).
      - Export frame units.
    - `PlaybackEngine.playRange(from, to, returnTo)` (+1 test): plays a range once, then puts the playhead back (Play around's mechanism).
  - App and UI:
    - New `app/textAnimController.ts`:
      - `chooseTextAnim`: a chip sets the animation on the selected text clips, one step "In animation: Word pop" / "Remove out animation". Choosing a clip's own animation again only previews it.
      - `previewTextAnim` (spec §10.4 "preview on click"): inline editing commits first. An in animation plays from the clip's start for its length + 10 frames (`PREVIEW_TAIL_FRAMES`, within the clip); an out one from 10 frames before it to the clip's end. Then the playhead returns.
    - New `panels/properties/AnimationSection.tsx`, after Text:
      - The In row and the Out row: None + 12 text chips (tooltip "In: Word pop. Click to preview").
      - Duration and Stagger in seconds (two decimals, one frame per arrow press and per px of scrub). Stagger is disabled for whole-text animations and the typewriter.
      - Easing (Auto with the animation's own named, then the four eases).
      - The half-clip hint.
      - Duration, stagger and easing commits replay the animation.
    - New `panels/properties/animBinding.ts` (`animBinding.test.ts`, 3 tests): the field bindings, bound outside React. A label scrub writes `liveEdit.anims`, so the preview at the playhead follows with no React render; then one command on release. Only clips with that animation take part. An automatic stagger reads as the delay it plays with (`shownStagger`).
    - `liveEdit.anims`; `previewController` passes them to the preview and marks the inline draft `editing`.
    - Timeline: the `sparkles` badge on a text clip with an animation (`glyphs.ts`, `drawContent.ts`).
  - Measured:
    - `npm run check` green: 1497 unit tests in 151 files (+38 tests, +3 files), 3 perf tests. Build 857.10 kB JS (277.00 kB gzip; was 832.56 / 268.88), 68.15 kB CSS.
    - Browser: headless Chrome 155 over CDP on the dev server (5181), 1600 x 900 at dpr 1. Scripts in `%TEMP%/b31/`: `acc1.mjs` 9/9, `acc2.mjs` 17/17, `extra.mjs` 8/8, `gap.mjs`, `page.js` (`__b31`), `setup.mjs`.
    - Acceptance 1 (project `b31-acc1`):
      - The title: 12 words, Montserrat 800 84 px with a 6 px stroke and a shadow, a fixed 900 px box (4 lines), frames 30..149, word pop in 0.8 s, fade out 0.5 s, over a full-frame solid.
      - At frame 0 (idle) the raster and the word atlas were warmed (2 rasterizations). Moving the playhead into the animation rasterized nothing.
      - Frame 40: 7 words drawn; frame 53: all 12 word quads.
      - Two 6 s playbacks from frame 0 through the title: 361 frames each, rAF mean 16.66 ms, p95 17.2 / 16.9 ms, max 18.3 / 17.6 ms, none over 20 ms. 0 text rasterizations (cache stats constant: 2 rasters, 2 rasterizations), 0 React commits between the Play and Pause clicks.

- deviations:
  - Duration semantics (§10.4 `duration`, `stagger`): the duration is the whole animation, the units' delays included, so the field reads what plays. Without a stagger the units spread over it (each overlapping 1.5 others); a longer stagger than duration / units is cut.
  - "Clamped to half the clip duration when needed": each animation plays over at most half the clip (in and out never overlap), at playback. The stored duration stays as typed (a later trim or extend needs no rewrite), and the section shows a hint.
  - Out animations play the units last first (a typewriter erases from the end) and keep their names' direction (§10.4: slideUp out moves up and fades). Their default ease is the mirror of the in one (ease out -> ease in).
  - Easing ids are `linear`, `easeIn`, `easeOut`, `easeInOut`. An animation without one uses its own (Auto in the section); `EaseId` stays a string until Batch 35.
  - Whole-text animations (fade, slides, pop, blurIn, scaleBounce) move the layer raster, backgrounds included; the per-word and per-line ones use the atlas (§10.1).
    - Under per-unit animations the background boxes are a raster of their own, at the leading unit's opacity, not moved (the atlas leaves backgrounds out, §10.1). Shadows are per unit, so overlapping shadows of neighboring words darken a little while they animate.
    - A typewriter reveals graphemes by count, spaces included, with no fade. Its characters are cut out of the line atlas cells, so the glyphs (kerning, strokes) are exactly the static text's; per-char atlas cells would let a thick stroke overlap the previous letter.
    - `perUnit: "char"` on other animations uses character cells (model only: the section does not choose units; the word and line variants are their own chips).
  - Shapes: slides travel 0.6 font sizes; pop goes from 0.4 with an overshoot; bounce from 0 with two damped bounces. The blur starts at 0.2 font sizes, capped at 16 texture px in the renderer (§10.4 "cap the blur radius").
  - Presets store animation times in milliseconds (`PresetTextAnim`), as `defaultDuration` does; they come back in the project's frames.
  - Starter animations (Lower third, Caption bold, Neon glow, Breaking). Starters stored before this batch and never changed take them on load (`upgradeStarters`, date kept).
  - Preview on click: an out animation plays from 10 frames before it to the clip's end (§10.4 names the in case). Choosing the active chip again previews without a command; None removes without a preview.
    - Duration, stagger and easing commits replay the animation, so a change shows at once even with the playhead off the clip. A label scrub shows live at the playhead.
    - A chip or field during inline editing commits the edit first.
  - Durations are shown in seconds (two decimals, a frame per step) and stored in frames.
  - Pre-rasterization (P11 "pre-rasterizes per word/line once"): text clips within 5 s of the playhead get their raster, atlas and background parts ahead: all at once while idle, one per pass while playing or scrubbing. So playing into an animation rasterizes nothing; a clip more than 5 s ahead of where playback started is rasterized during playback, before it shows.
  - A text edited inline shows at rest; the gizmo, canvas hit testing and snapping use the text at rest.
  - Animations that cannot play (from older builds or files) are ignored at evaluation rather than reported by `validateProject`; preset files are sanitized.
  - The Animation section follows Text (§11 lists it after Text).

Both are small per-frame allocations, against the "allocation-free" comments (`anim.ts:2`, `LayerTextures.ts` header).

### Batch 32: Captions track, captions editor, SRT/VTT
- status: done
- date: 2026-10-16
- notes:
  - Model (unit tested):
    - `core/document/types.ts`: `CaptionStyle` (`text: TextStyle`, `position` bottom / center / top / custom, `y`, `maxWidth`, `maxLines`, `highlight` with `mode` none / color / background / scale / colorAndScale, `color`, `inactiveColor`), `CaptionTrackData = { style }`, `CaptionWord` (frames relative to the cue start). Schema version unchanged (projects had no caption data).
    - `validate.ts` (+2 tests): at most one caption track, above every video track; caption styles (a text style, position, max width, integer max lines, highlight mode and colors); cue text a string, words with integer frames start < end.
    - New `text/captions.ts` (`captions.test.ts`, 13 tests): the default style (scaled to the project), `captionPlace` / `captionMiddleY` (where a position puts the caption), word count, search, `cueLine`, `wordRanges` (words found in the text in reading order), `cueBoundary` / `cueSlice` (timeline splits: at the first right word with word timings, else at the space nearest the frame's share of the characters; a single word keeps the text on both sides), `splitCueAt` (Enter: the text at the caret, timing by the share of characters, or at the first right word), `mergeCues`, `evenWords` (generated karaoke), `activeWordIndex`.
    - New `text/subtitles.ts` (`subtitles.test.ts`, 11 tests): SRT and VTT parse (BOM, CRLF / CR, missing cue numbers, blank lines, `,` or `.` before the ms, hours left out, 1-3 digit fractions, blocks that lost their blank line, SRT tags and `{\...}` codes dropped; VTT header, NOTE / STYLE / REGION blocks, identifiers, settings, tags and entities, inline timestamps as word timings) and write (SRT numbered blocks, VTT header and escaping, ms rounding, empty cues left out, blank lines inside a text closed up).
    - New `timeline/edit/captions.ts` (`captions.test.ts`, 18 tests): `addCaptionTrack` (`track.addCaption`), `addCueAt` (`caption.add`, creating the track), `setCueText` (`caption.text`), `splitCue` (`caption.split`), `mergeWithPrevious` (`caption.merge`), `clearCues` (`caption.clear`), `cuesFromSubtitles` + `importCues` (`caption.import`, replaces the cues, overlaps resolved), `exportCues`, `setCaptionStyle` (`caption.style`), `cueMoveRange` / `clampCueDelta` (moves stop at the cues that stay; copies go to the nearest free spot within their length). Round trip: a 200-cue SRT at 29.97 fps imported and exported gives the same cues on the frame grid, every time within 1 ms of the file's. A 200-step random move / copy run never leaves an overlap.
    - `move.ts`: an overwrite move clamps its delta for cues (`clampCueDelta`; null: no-op); `sliceClip` slices a cue's text and words. `trim.ts`: a dragged cue's trims are regular even in Ripple mode; in trims re-base the words.
  - Rendering:
    - New `render/text/captionLayout.ts`: `captionLook` (cached per style and text: an auto box up to the max width, else wrapped there, drawn smaller in 10% steps down to half size to keep within the max lines; the inactive and active looks of a highlight) and `highlightUnits` (per timed word: its units in the word atlas, the box behind it, its middle; cached per layout and word list).
    - `Compositor`: the caption track (drawn after every video track) adds its cue at the frame as a text layer in the track's style at its position; with word timings and a highlight it carries `karaoke` (`CaptionHighlightFrame`: the active word's look, unit range, scale, box). Live cue texts and live caption styles (`LiveCaptions`). `upcomingTexts` lists the cues in the next 5 s with their raster and, with a highlight, both word atlases (`HIGHLIGHT_SLOT`).
    - `LayerTextures`: a highlighted cue draws its background boxes, the other words from its word atlas, the highlight box (a pooled solid layer) and the active word from the highlight look's atlas grown about its middle; the same for Export frame (`exactText`).
    - `PreviewView`: `captions` dep; redraws on the `captions` affect. Tests in `render/text/captionLayers.test.ts` (11): placement of the four positions, hidden / disabled / empty cues, wrapping and fitting, live text and style, each highlight mode, upcoming parts, the drawn layers of a scale and a box highlight.
  - App and UI:

New `CaptionStyleSection.tsx` and `captionBinding.ts` (`captionBinding.test.ts`, 3 tests). `context.ts` (+1 test): a selection with cues, or with the caption track, adds `captionStyle`. `PropertiesPanel` reveals and focuses a section on request. `textProps.ts`: style props also read and write a bare style.

Fonts: caption styles count as used families (font picker, `fonts/` copies).

Export (stubbed picker): `b32-a1.srt` with 200 blocks, the same cues on the frame grid and the same ms times as the file; `b32.vtt` chosen by name gives a WEBVTT file with the same cues. Undo removed the cues and the track, redo put them back.

Typing "Hello brave new world": the draft in `liveEdit.cues`, the cue's raster made from it in the next frame (the preview showed it, `a2-typed.png`), the document and the undo history unchanged. Enter after "Hello brave": "Hello brave" 45 + 20 frames and "new world" 65 + 16 (36 frames, 11 of 20 characters: round(19.8)), one step "Split cue", the right half focused with the caret at 0. Mod+Backspace merged it back (36 frames, caret 12). Esc put the text back (no step), Tab committed ("Edit caption") and selected the next cue's text, Delete on a focused row deleted its cue, `+ Cue` at frame 10 made a cue at 36 for 9 frames (up to the next cue) with "Caption" selected; a click elsewhere committed the typed text.

No overlaps, document valid throughout.

Scrubbing through all four words rasterized nothing. The exact render (Export frame's) had 3535 yellow pixels on word 2 and none with the mode None.
