- deviations:
  - Caption track data (§5.2 "style + cue ids"): `captionTracks[trackId]` holds the style only; the cue ids are the track's `clipIds`, so the order and membership have one source of truth. A caption track without data draws in the default style.
  - Cue selection (§3.3 kind `cue`): cues are selected as clips (kind `clips`), so move, trim, delete, split, label and the Clip section work on them unchanged; the `cue` kind stays in the type (session files) and is never made.
  - Word timings are frames relative to the cue's start (as keyframes), so a moved cue keeps them; in trims re-base them and words outside the new span are kept (a trim back shows them again).
  - "No overwrite and no push on this track" (§10.5): an overwrite move or a nudge of cues stops at the cues that stay; an Alt-drag copy goes to the nearest free spot no further than the copied block's length from the drop, else the drop is refused (`occupied`, red ghost); a dragged cue's trims are always regular (Ripple mode and Mod ignored). Insert moves (Mod) and ripples started on other tracks (ripple trims, ripple delete, Q / W) still shift the caption track like any other unlocked track, splitting a cue that crosses the insert point, so captions stay in sync with the picture.
  - Splits: Enter splits by the share of characters (§10.5); when the cue has word timings on both sides it splits at the first right word's start instead. A timeline split (razor, Mod+K) cuts the text where the first right word starts, or at the space nearest the frame's share of the characters; a single word stays whole on both sides. Merges join the texts with a space.
  - Cue list keys (§10.5 lists Enter, Mod+Backspace, Delete, Tab): Shift+Enter inserts a line break; Shift+Tab goes to the previous cue; Esc puts the text back and leaves the field (the Number field's Esc); blur commits. Typing is a live draft (`liveEdit.cues`, shown on the canvas at once) committed as one "Edit caption" step, not one step per key.
  - `+ Cue at playhead`: when the playhead is inside a cue the new cue starts at its end (and after the cues touching it), and it is at most 2 s, up to the next cue; it reads "Caption", selected in the list (typing replaces it, as `+ Title`).
  - Import (§10.5 "create cues"): the file's cues replace the track's cues (one undo step; the toast counts the replaced ones); times go to the nearest frame (at least one frame long); a cue starting inside the previous one cuts it there, two starting on the same frame become one (their texts on two lines). VTT inline timestamps (karaoke) are read as word timings. Export: one button; SRT, or VTT when the chosen name ends in `.vtt` (the save picker offers both); times to the nearest ms; empty cues are left out.
  - Caption layout (§10.5 max width, max lines): a cue keeps an auto box up to the max width (its background fits the text), wraps at the max width beyond it, and is drawn smaller (10% steps, down to half size) when it has more lines than the max.
  - Positions: Bottom puts the caption's bottom edge at 78% of the frame height (the Subtitle's place), Top its top edge at 18%, Center its middle at the center, Custom its middle at Y (project px from the center). Choosing Custom starts it where the preset showed a one-line caption.
  - Word highlight (§10.5 "draws the active word from the atlas"): the other words take the inactive color in every mode; Color and Both draw the active word in the highlight color; Box puts a box in the highlight color behind it (square corners until Batch 34's shapes), the word in the text's own fill; Scale and Both grow it 1.15 x about its middle. The cue is drawn from two word atlases (the other words' look and the active word's) over its background boxes. Between words, and while its text is being typed, nothing is highlighted.
  - The Caption style section shows for a selection with cues and for a selected caption track, after Clip (or Track); it has no vertical anchor (captions sit at their position). Style (the Captions header) selects the caption track unless cues are selected, opens the section, scrolls to it and focuses it.
  - Applying a preset to the caption track (Apply or hover with cues or the track selected and no text clip) replaces its text style only: position, max width, lines and highlight stay; the preset's box width is not used.
  - Clear all asks nothing (§0 one click); Undo brings the cues back.
  - Cue list virtualization (P1 "no React renders on scrolling"): the list renders the rows in view and a block around them, and renders again when the view crosses a block of 16 rows, not on every scroll step; the cue under the playhead is marked through the DOM, so playback renders nothing.
  - Caption cues are not on the canvas (no selection or gizmo; Batch 53 adds it).

Expected: both files open in a text editor with the same cues and times; importing the saved file again gives the same cues.
  2. Editing (acceptance 2): click into a cue's text in the list and type. Expected: the preview shows the new text while you type; the timeline label updates when you leave the field.   3. Dragging (acceptance 3): drag a cue on the CC row into its neighbor, then far past it. Expected: the spoken word lights up (yellow, a yellow box, grown, both) while the other words stay white. Importing a VTT with inline timestamps (`Never <00:00:00.700>drink ...`) highlights the same way.

Expected: the captions move and wrap at once; a long cue past the max lines is drawn smaller. Click a card in Media > Presets. Expected: the captions take its font and colors and keep their position.

- review: rejected
  1. A cue's typed text is lost, and the preview keeps showing it, when its row unmounts while it is being edited. This breaks acceptance 2 (the canvas shows text the document never gets) and P2 (the ephemeral draft is never committed or cleared). In `src/panels/media/CaptionsTab.tsx:65-149` (`CueRow`), the draft is committed only by `onBlur` (`:137-141`) and the Enter / Mod+Backspace / Tab / Esc keys. There is no cleanup when the row unmounts, and `onBlur` does not run when the focused textarea is removed. Two ordinary actions remove it:
     - Scrolling the list: the virtualized slice (`CaptionsTab.tsx:265-267`) drops a row more than one block of 16 rows outside the view.
     - Switching the media panel tab: `src/panels/MediaPanel.tsx:23` unmounts `CaptionsTab`, and the PanelTabs buttons prevent mousedown, so the field never blurs.
     - In both cases `liveEdit.cues` (set by `draftCue`, `src/app/captionController.ts:78-80`) stays set. The compositor draws the draft (`Compositor.writeCaption` reads `live.texts`) while the document keeps the old text, with no undo step. This lasts until another cue is typed into. Export (Batch 43) would render the document's text, so the output would differ from the preview.
     - Reproduced in headless Chrome on a fresh dev server (5181), with 200 cues imported. `%TEMP%/b32/rev_unmount2.mjs`: click cue 3's text, Ctrl+A, type "Typed then scrolled away", scroll the list 100 rows down, click the timeline. Result: `liveEdit.cues` still holds the draft, the preview's raster slot reads "Typed then scrolled away", the document reads "Cue number 3 says hello", and the last undo step is still "Import captions". `%TEMP%/b32/rev_tab.mjs`: the same, but clicking the Media tab instead of scrolling, gives the same result.
     - Fix: a row that unmounts with an unhandled draft commits it (`commitCue(id, latest value)`, from a ref in an effect cleanup), so the step is "Edit caption" and `liveEdit.cues` is cleared. Keeping the edited cue's row in the rendered slice also helps, but it does not cover the tab switch. Cover it with a test, or with a recorded browser run plus a step in pending manual check 2 (type, scroll far away or switch tabs, then check the timeline label and Undo).
  2. `src/panels/media/CaptionsTab.module.css:108` (`height: calc(2 * var(--type-body-line) + 2 * var(--space-0) + 2px)`): the `+ 2px` (the field's two 1px borders) is a size literal outside `src/styles/tokens.css`. It is the only non-hairline px value in any CSS module. The Batch 21 review rejected the same kind of value (`MenuButton.tsx` `rect.bottom + 2`). Fix: a token in `tokens.css` (for example `--cue-text-height`, named in the Captions tab design-gap entry), or `box-sizing: content-box` so the 1px borders sit outside the two-line height.
  - Not blocking: `src/panels/properties/CaptionStyleSection.tsx:96-97` repeats the default highlight yellow and white of `src/text/captions.ts:29-31` as mixed-value fallbacks. Exporting them from `captions.ts` would keep one source.

A 29.97 fps SRT with a BOM, CRLF line ends and two-line cues is imported (200 cues on the track, one step) and exported, then read back: the same cues on the frame grid, each time within 1 ms. `subtitles.test.ts` covers parsing and writing. The list UI is pending manual check 1.
    - Acceptance 2: proportional Enter splits are covered by `splitCueAt` / `splitCue` tests (36 frames, 11 of 20 characters = 20). The live canvas text is covered by `captionLayers.test.ts` ("shows the cue list's live text") and pending manual check 2, apart from problem 1.
    - Acceptance 3: `cueMoveRange` / `clampCueDelta`, nudges, moves mixed with other clips, Alt copies, regular trims under Ripple mode, and a 200-step random move/copy run through a validating store all keep the cues apart (`captions.test.ts`). Pending manual check 3.
    - Acceptance 4: `captionLayers.test.ts` covers the color, scale and box modes, `None`, no word timings, the upcoming word atlases, and the drawn quads and box in `LayerTextures`. Pending manual check 4.
